# 5th Metatarsal Base Fracture

*Foot & Ankle · 1 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — among the **commonest foot fractures** · base fractures = **25% of all metatarsal fractures** · **90% are zone 1**

**Who** — **athletes, military recruits, manual labourers** · runners, dancers

### Anatomy & blood supply

**Regions** — tuberosity → base → metadiaphysis → diaphysis → neck → head

**Base + tuberosity** — **cancellous, highly vascular** · tuberosity = insertion of **peroneus brevis + lateral band of plantar fascia**

**Metadiaphysis** — **no tendon attachments** · **vascular watershed** between **metaphyseal vessels** and **diaphyseal nutrient artery** (terminal capillaries)

**Diaphysis** — dorsal curve in distal ⅓ · **peroneus tertius** inserts dorsally

**Shear force (OB)** — fracture lies in the **hypovascular zone between peroneus brevis and peroneus tertius insertions** → tendons shear across fracture site

**Function** — lateral border of forefoot · **lever during push-off**

**Mimics** — **open apophysis** or **os peroneum** → comparison radiographs

![Watershed zone between metaphyseal arteries and the nutrient artery](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7031-shows-blood-supply.jpg)

### Genes & lab markers

**CMT (cavovarus → 5th MT stress fracture)** — **duplication on chromosome 17**\
✗ Wrong options: absent dystrophin (muscular dystrophy, equinovarus) · PITX1 (clubfoot) · PTT insufficiency (flatfoot) · tarsal coalition

**Nonunion / refracture labs** — **vitamin D deficiency (hypovitaminosis D)** · **thyroid** (hormonal) deficiency

**Female athlete triad (TO)** — disordered eating + **menstrual irregularities** + ↓ BMD → ↑ metatarsal stress fractures (TO)

## 2 Mechanism

| Zone | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Plantarflexion + hindfoot inversion** → avulsion by **peroneus brevis**, **lateral band of plantar fascia** or **long plantar ligament** |
| **2** | **Forefoot adduction** (Orthobullets, OB) · **strong abduction force to forefoot → bending moment at metaphyseal–diaphyseal junction** (TO) — read the options · acute inversion injury (OB) |
| **3** | **Repetitive microtrauma** → **stress fracture** |

**Associated conditions** — **Lisfranc / midfoot injury** · **lateral ankle ligament laxity** · **cavus foot + varus hindfoot**

**Hindfoot varus / cavus** — **overloads lateral column** → stress fracture distal to 4/5 articulation

## 3 Presentation

**Symptom** — **pain over lateral border of forefoot**, worse with WB · stress fracture: **antecedent pain**, rapid ↑ workload / training change

**Exam** — point tenderness at base · rare **skin tenting (zone 1)** · **varus hindfoot**, **cavus**, **lateral shoe-tread wear**, **5th MT head callosity**

**Check** — lateral ligament instability · is varus hindfoot correctable · **pain with resisted eversion** (peroneal weakness)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray AP, lateral, oblique foot** | **Diagnosis**; location + displacement decide fixation |
| X-ray chronicity | **Intramedullary sclerosis + no periosteal callus** = chronic · callus forms **medially first → laterally** |
| **Plantar fracture gap** | **Poor prognosis for nonop union** |
| Cavovarus signs | **Plantarflexed 1st MT**, **high Meary angle** |
| **Bone scan** | Suspected stress fracture, equivocal X-ray → **diaphyseal uptake** |
| **MRI** | Equivocal X-ray / scan → **high-signal stress reaction + oedema** |
| **CT** | **Healing in delayed / nonunion** or after fixation · **not at time of injury** (X-ray suffices) |
| **Screw planning (TO)** | **AP = screw diameter** · **lateral = screw length** (TO) |

### Lawrence & Botte anatomic zones (also called Torg anatomic zones)

| Zone | Name | Location | Key facts |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Pseudo-Jones** | **Proximal tubercle avulsion**; may enter **cubometatarsal (5th TMT) joint**; **proximal to 4–5 intermetatarsal joint** | Well vascularised; nonunion rare, **usually asymptomatic (fibrous)** |
| **2** | **Jones** | **Metaphyseal–diaphyseal junction**; **enters 4th–5th intermetatarsal articulation** | **Watershed**; acute; **nonunion 15–30%** |
| **3** | **Proximal diaphyseal** | **Distal to 4–5 articulation**; **proximal 1.5 cm of diaphysis (TO)** | **Stress fracture in athletes**; cavovarus, sensory neuropathy; ↑ nonunion |

![Anatomic zones 1–3 of the proximal 5th metatarsal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7031-illustration-of-the-torg-anatomic-classification-zone-1-incl.jpg)

**Jones location** — **1.5–3 cm** from proximal tip (OB) · **\~2.5 cm** distal to base (OB) · intra-articular at 4–5 **intermetatarsal** facet; does **not** extend distal to it or into metatarsocuboid joint

**Joint entered** — **Jones → intermetatarsal (4–5) joint** · **avulsion → tarsometatarsal joint** (OB) · one OB explanation calls the Jones joint "4th–5th tarsometatarsal" — read the options

**Zone 2 vs 3 (TO)** — some studies: **no distinction needed**, treatment + outcome the same (TO)

### Torg radiographic classification (stress fractures, zone 3)

| Type | Orthobullets | TO | Age |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Narrow line, no IM sclerosis** | **Acute fracture** | **Acute** |
| **II** | **Widened line + IM sclerosis** | **Periosteal reaction, widening, some IM sclerosis** | **Delayed union** |
| **III** | **Widened (obliterated) canal, no callus** | **IM sclerosis + blunted fracture edges** | **Nonunion** |

## 5 Management

```
Proximal 5th MT fracture
├─ Zone 1 → protected WB, stiff-soled shoe / boot
│      └─ rotational displacement / skin tenting → fixation
├─ Zone 2, non-athlete / recreational → NWB short-leg cast 6–8 wk
│      └─ failed 6–8 wk nonop or displaced → IM screw
├─ Zone 2, elite / competitive athlete → early IM screw
└─ Zone 3 → NWB cast ~8 wk
       └─ athlete, cavovarus, sclerosis (Torg II–III), not healing 8–12 wk → IM screw ± graft
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative | Operative (IM screw) |
|---|---|
| **Zone 1** without rotational displacement — **even elite athletes / dancers** | **Zone 1 with rotational displacement or skin tenting** · gross displacement / articular involvement in young active (TO) |
| **Zone 2 in non-athlete / recreational athlete** (e.g. 45-yo recreational soccer) | **Zone 2 in elite / competitive athlete** (collegiate, Olympic, professional) — **even acute + nondisplaced** |
| **High-school / recreational athlete** until **6–8 wk nonop failed** | **Non-elite after failed 6–8 wk** nonop or **unacceptable displacement** |
| **Zone 3 acute**, low demand | **Zone 3 in athletes, cavovarus, sclerosis / nonunion (Torg II–III)** · **not healing at 8–12 wk (TO)** · occupation can't tolerate NWB (TO) |

### Zone 1 avulsion – weight bearing in a hard-soled shoe

**Treatment** — **protected / immediate WB in stiff- (hard-) soled shoe, postoperative shoe, walking boot or short-leg walking cast**, advance as pain allows

**Outcome** — **union by 8 wk** · fibrous unions rarely symptomatic · early RTW, symptoms up to **6 months**

**Soft dressing (TO)** — compressive dressing + WB in cast boot → **shorter recuperation, better foot score than cast** (TO)

**Elite dancer, zone 1** — **protected WB, stiff-soled shoe, gradual return**\
✗ Wrong options: immediate return to competition · NWB short-leg cast (unnecessary) · IM screw (true Jones only) · resection + peroneus brevis advancement

**57-yo, inversion off curb, zone 1** — **immediate WB in postoperative shoe**\
✗ Wrong options: acute fragment excision · IM screw · NWB cast · K-wires

**Which pattern → immediate WB in boot?** — **zone 1**\
✗ Wrong options: **Lisfranc** (unstable) · **displaced 1st MT** fracture (no intermetatarsal ligament → surgery) · zone 2 (NWB if nonop)

**Symptomatic zone 1 nonunion** — **fragment excision + peroneus brevis reattachment**\
✗ Wrong options: acutely

**Zone 1 ORIF (TO)** — rarely; **displaced intra-articular in highly competitive** · plate, screw or **tension-band** (TO)

### Acute Jones (zone 2) – IM screw in athletes vs casting

**Nonop** — **NWB short-leg cast 6–8 wk** (Orthobullets, OB) · then WB cast until union (TO) · advance WB with callus (\~4–6 wk) · **NWB cast > walking cast** for healing

| Zone 2 outcome | Value |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion, nonop** | **15–30%** (Orthobullets) · **10–30%** (OB) · **25%** (OB) · **up to 33%** (OB) · **> 30%** in some studies (OB) — read the options |
| **Union with cast** | **72–93%** (OB) · **healing rate 75%** (TO) |
| **Refracture after nonop** | **33%** (Orthobullets) · **\~30%** (OB) |
| **Union with IM screw** | **Approaching 100%** |
| **Reoperation after ORIF (Yates)** | **8.5%** — refracture, persistent nonunion, symptomatic hardware |

**What IM screw achieves** — **improved union rate** (primary advantage) · **faster / decreased time to union** · **earlier return to play / work** · minimises nonunion + prolonged activity restriction · better patient-reported scores in athletes

**What it does not do** — ↓ pain · ↓ HO · ↑ ROM · ↓ cost · **eliminate refracture** · avoid hardware complications · ↓ sural irritation (**more** with surgery) · prevent midfoot arthritis (not a sequela)

**✗ Casting traps** — **> 95% union** with cast (false) · **asymptomatic fibrous nonunion** with cast (that's zone 1; zone 2 nonunion usually symptomatic) · "casting unacceptable" (false — still valid if avoiding surgery) · "ORIF ≈ casting" (false) · "ORIF high hardware failure" (false)

**Bone graft** — **not required** in primary fixation · use in **revision / nonunion / refracture** (± BMAC)

**✗ Other wrong answers for athlete Jones** — **K-wires** (not supported) · **tubular plate** (long lines into zone 3 / multifragmentary diaphyseal) · **MRI** · **taping + immediate RTS** · **Brostrom** · **Lisfranc repair** · **midfoot fusion**

**TO answer key (TO)** — painful 5th MT base stress fracture in runner: **"All correct"** = **internal fixation always required** + **more in females with menstrual abnormalities** + **RICE is first treatment** (TO) · conflicts with OB/Orthobullets (cast for non-athletes) — read the options

**RICE (TO)** — rest, ice, compression, elevation = **primary treatment in all fractures** (TO)

**Competitive athletes (TO)** — **early ORIF to ↓ disability time** (TO)

### Stress fractures, delayed union & nonunion – fixation

**Zone 3 nonop (TO)** — chronic (canal obliteration, sclerosis) → **NWB casting \~8 wk** may work (TO) · Orthobullets: NWB 6–8 wk (often 6–7 wk) · **ESWT** union ≈ internal fixation (reports)

**Refracture common in zone 3 (TO)** — risk factors: **cavovarus**, **hypovitaminosis D**, **metatarsus adductus**, **curved 5th MT with prominent base** (TO)

**Two proven operations, zones 2–3 (TO)** — **IM screw (5.5 mm)** · **corticocancellous inlay bone graft** after clearing canal of sclerotic bone (TO)

**Delayed union after 6 wk cast, widened line (15-yo)** — **percutaneous IM screw**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF (not needed) · **electrical stimulation alone** · more casting · hard-sole shoe

**Zone 3, 11 wk, sclerotic edges, minimal callus** — **ORIF ± bone grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · bone stimulator (controversial) · continued cast / boot

**HS quarterback, refracture after 8 wk NWB boot** — **IM compression screw**\
✗ Wrong options: plate · fragment excision (zone 1) · NWB + stimulator · midfoot fusion

**Zone 3, triathlete wants running at 6 wk** — **IM screw**\
✗ Wrong options: boot / hard-soled shoe / early PT (zone 1) · NWB cast (non-athletes)

**Nonunion risk factor (dancer, 4 months)** — **fracture location** (zone 2–3)\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs · high calcium · early WB · female sex

**Nonunion treatment** — **revision IM screw + bone graft** · plate = salvage after failed IM screw

### IM screw technique & failure

**Position** — supine, bump under hip, fluoroscopy

**Approach** — short incision **proximal to tuberosity**, parallel to sole · blunt dissection past **sural branches** · between **peroneus longus + brevis**

**Start point** — **"high and inside"** = **superior + medial** on tuberosity · K-wire not past MT curvature

**Screw** — **> 4 mm diameter** · **4.5, 5.5 or 6.5 mm partially threaded** (solid or cannulated) · **largest diameter the canal accepts** (max fill + compression, ↓ refracture) · **5.5 mm** (TO)

**Check** — **all threads cross fracture** · no distal cortical perforation · tap measures length

| Screw error | Result |
|---|---|
| **< 4.5 mm** (not including 4.5) | **Delayed / nonunion** |
| **Too long** | **Straightens curved shaft** → **fracture gapping + distal / medial cortical perforation** → failure, nonunion |
| **Too short** | **No compression** |
| **Prominent head** | **Sural nerve irritation** |

![Too-long screw straightening the shaft and gapping the fracture](../../MCQ/attachments/q219330-illa-screenshot-2023-12-19-at-5-07-22-pm.jpg)

**Rehab** — **NWB 1–3 wk** → protected WB (from \~2 wk) → **running + impact at 6 wk** if pain-free + **radiographic callus** · RTP average **4–6 wk**

**Failure risk factors** — **elite athletes** · **return to sport before radiographic union** · **early unprotected WB in athletes** · **too-long screw**\
✗ Wrong options: female · age < 20 or > 40 · solid vs cannulated · **4.5 mm** or **> 4.5 mm** screw · no bone graft · no ultrasound stimulator · partially threaded

**Bent 5.0 mm screw at 12 wk** — cause = **return to play before radiographic union** · fix = **remove + larger IM screw**

**NFL QB, failure at 8 wk** — **too long partially threaded screw**\
✗ Wrong options: WB at 2 wk · impact at 6 wk · no BMAC · largest screw

**IM screw vs plate** — IM **more rigid** + **less hardware prominence** (OB) · plate **equivalent strength** in early data (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Plate (3 mm, plantar-lateral)** — same indications; **salvage after failed IM screw**; reduces plantar gap / rotation

### Association with cavovarus foot (CMT)

**Cavus foot** — **↑ refracture** + ↑ nonunion even after successful treatment\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ nonunion · earlier / later RTS

**Varus hindfoot** — overloads lateral column → **zone 3 stress fracture** distal to 4/5 articulation

**Refracture treatment** — internal fixation **+ correct cavovarus** · **leave screw in until end of athletic career** (removal ↑ refracture)

### Iselin disease (apophysitis) (TO)

**Definition (TO)** — **apophysitis (osteochondritis) of 5th metatarsal base** · benign, **self-limiting** · **young adolescent sportspersons** (TO)

| Osteochondritis site (TO) | Eponym |
|---|---|
| **5th MT base** | **Iselin** |
| **2nd MT head** | **Freiberg** |
| **Navicular** | **Köhler** |
| **Calcaneal tuberosity** | **Sever** |
| **Lower pole patella** | **Sinding-Larsen–Johansson** |
| **Tibial tubercle** | **Osgood–Schlatter** |
| **Lateral part of medial femoral condyle** | **Osteochondritis dissecans** |
| **Femoral head** | **Perthes** |
| **Lunate** | **Kienböck** |
| **Capitulum** | **Panner** |
| **Vertebral ring epiphysis** | **Scheuermann** |
| **Central bony nucleus of vertebral body** | **Calvé** |

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | Zone 2 **15–30%**; zones 2–3 (watershed); **screw < 4.5 mm**; vitamin D / thyroid deficiency → **revision IM screw + graft** |
| **Failure of fixation** | **Elite athlete**, **RTS before radiographic union**, **screw length** (too long → gapping) → revision fixation |
| **Refracture** | **33% zone 2 nonop**; cavovarus, stress fracture, vitamin D, **screw removal** → fix + correct cavovarus |
| **Painful hardware** | Rare; prominent head → **modified shoewear** |
| **Sural nerve injury** | **Dorsolateral branch within 2–3 mm of tuberosity** · prevent: **tissue protector + countersink head** |
| **Chronic pain** | Zone 1 nonunion after nonop → **fragment excision + peroneus brevis reattachment** |

## X vs Y

| | **Zone 1 (pseudo-Jones)** | **Zone 2 (Jones)** | **Zone 3 (diaphyseal stress)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Tuberosity | **Metadiaphysis** | **Proximal diaphysis** |
| Joint | 5th TMT (cubometatarsal) | **4–5 intermetatarsal** | Distal to 4–5 joint |
| Mechanism | **PF + inversion** | **Forefoot adduction** (abduction — TO) | **Repetitive stress** |
| Blood supply | Good | **Watershed** | Watershed |
| Nonop | **WB hard-soled shoe** | **NWB cast 6–8 wk** | **NWB cast \~8 wk** |
| Athlete | Still nonop | **Early IM screw** | **IM screw** |

## How it's asked

**"Elite dancer, tuberosity avulsion — management?"** → **protected WB in stiff-soled shoe, gradual return**

**"Zone 1 avulsion in 57-yo — treatment?"** → **immediate WB in postoperative shoe**

**"Which pattern → immediate WB in walking boot?"** → **zone 1 (pseudo-Jones)**

**"Zone 1 vs zone 2 — true?"** → **zone 1 (pseudo-Jones) → hard-soled shoe, allowed to weight bear as tolerated**

**"Fastest union + return to sport, collegiate lineman Jones?"** → **intramedullary screw fixation**

**"Professional basketball player, Jones — quickest return?"** → **IM screw**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · taping

**"Primary advantage of fixing a Jones fracture?"** → **improved union rate**

**"45-yo recreational soccer, Jones?"** → **NWB short-leg cast**

**"Collegiate runner, acute metadiaphyseal fracture?"** → **acute meta-diaphyseal fracture → IM screw**

**"Which patient needs early surgery?"** → **collegiate soccer player, acute nondisplaced zone 2**

**"IM screw vs cast for Jones — true?"** → **decreased time to union**

**"Operative vs casting in Jones — true?"** → **shorter time to union, quicker return to work**

**"Proposed benefit of IM screw?"** → **faster radiographic union + earlier return to play**

**"Zone III in triathlete wanting to run at 6 wk?"** → **IM screw**

**"Delayed union after 6 wk cast in 15-yo?"** → **percutaneous screw fixation**

**"Collegiate varsity athlete, Jones on X-ray?"** → **internal fixation**

**"Recurrent pain after nonop Jones in HS quarterback?"** → **IM compression screw**

**"Jones in cavus vs planus foot?"** → **higher risk of refracture**

**"Zone III, 11 wk, sclerotic edges?"** → **ORIF ± bone grafting**

**"Risk factor for nonunion?"** → **fracture location**

**"Bent screw after release to play?"** → **return to play before radiographic union**

**"Risk factor for failure after fixation?"** → **return to sport before radiographic union**

**"Higher failure proportion after IM screw?"** → **early unprotected WB in athletes**

**"IM screw for zone 3 — true?"** → **screw diameter as large as possible**

**"Failure at 8 wk in NFL QB?"** → **too long a partially threaded screw**

**"Screw diameter linked to nonunion?"** → **< 4.5 mm**

**"Best view for screw diameter / length?"** → **AP / lateral** (TO)

**"Associated with 5th MT stress fracture + cavus foot?"** → **duplication on chromosome 17 (CMT)**

**"Painful 5th MT base stress fracture in runner — correct?"** → **all correct** (fixation, female menstrual abnormality, RICE) (TO)

**"Iselin disease is?"** → **osteochondritis of 5th metatarsal base** (TO)

**"Freiberg disease is?"** → **osteochondritis of 2nd metatarsal head** (TO)

# Achilles Tendon Rupture

*Foot & Ankle · 2 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Incidence** — **18 : 100,000 / yr** · **missed in up to 25%**

**Demographics** — **men** · **30–40 yr** (Orthobullets) · **males 30–39** (TO)

**Classic patient** — episodic athlete, **"weekend warrior"** (basketball, tennis, pickleball, volleyball)

| Risk factor | Source |
|---|---|
| **Fluoroquinolones** | Orthobullets, TO |
| **Steroids** — local injections + systemic | Orthobullets, TO |
| **Hypercholesterolaemia**, **gout**, **RA** | TO |
| **Long-term dialysis**, **renal transplantation** | TO |
| **Chronic tendinopathy with Haglund disease** | TO |
| Foot / ankle malalignment, training errors, degenerative tendon | TO |

**Haglund deformity ↑ TA rupture** — true (TO)

**"Antithrombolytics have a role"** — the **incorrect statement** in the TO list (TO)

### Anatomy

**Achilles** — **largest** (Orthobullets) / **strongest** (OB) tendon in body · confluence of **soleus + medial and lateral gastrocnemius**

### Tendon blood supply (TO)

| Fact | TO answer |
|---|---|
| **Three sources** | **Musculotendinous junction**, **osteotendinous junction**, **paratenon** (**paratenon = most important**) |
| **Major artery** | **Posterior tibial artery** (also Orthobullets) |
| Minor | **Peroneal artery** — small contribution to mid-section |
| **Most abundant zone** | **At the tendon insertion** (✗ Wrong options: mid-section between MTJ and bone–tendon junction) |
| **Least vascularised zone** | **2–6 cm above calcaneal insertion**, at all ages → poor repair |
| **Main surface of supply** | **Anterior (deep) surface** (✗ Wrong options: posterior · medial · lateral) — branches loop round / pass through to posterior surface |

### Sural nerve

**Course** — **lateral to the TA** · accompanied by **small saphenous vein**, which lies **just lateral** to the nerve

**Protect** — **skin incision slightly medial to TA** (TO, Orthobullets) · injury → **heel + lateral foot numbness** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: saphenous · tibial · lateral plantar nerve

![Axial T1 MRI – A peroneus longus, B peroneus brevis, C small saphenous vein, D sural nerve, E FHL](../../MCQ/attachments/q214033-a-sural-nerve.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **sudden forced plantarflexion** · **violent dorsiflexion of a plantarflexed foot** · **push-off with weight-bearing forefoot while extending knee** (TO) · **sudden unexpected dorsiflexion** (TO) · **eccentric load** (OB)

**Site** — **4–6 cm above calcaneal insertion** (Orthobullets, OB) · **3–6 cm above os calcis**, watershed (TO) · least vascular zone **2–6 cm** (TO) — read the options

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **"pop"** · weakness, difficulty walking, heel pain · ecchymosis

**Inspection** — **↑ resting ankle dorsiflexion prone with knees bent** (loss of resting tension) · calf atrophy (chronic)

**Palpation** — **palpable gap**

**Motion** — **weak plantarflexion** · **↑ passive dorsiflexion** · chronic: **unable to toe off / toe walk**

![Loss of resting tension – affected foot rests in more dorsiflexion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7021-picture-1-resting-tension.jpg)

### Diagnosis – Thompson test & ultrasound

| Test (TO) | Method | Positive = rupture |
|---|---|---|
| **Thompson (Simmonds) squeeze** | Prone, feet over couch edge; squeeze calf **just distal to maximal girth** | **Little / no plantarflexion** vs other side · **sensitivity 0.89–0.93** |
| **Matles** | Prone, **actively flex knees to 90°** | Foot falls to **neutral or slight dorsiflexion** |
| **O'Brien needle** | **25-gauge needle** in midline of proximal tendon, passive ankle ROM | **Needle doesn't move** (moves = continuity) |

**"Thomas test is a reliable test"** — TO uses "Thomas / Simmonds" for the calf squeeze (true statement, TO) · **Thomas test = hip flexion contracture**, **Thessaly = meniscus** (OB) — read the options

**Movement tested against resistance (TO)** — options inversion + PF / dorsiflexion / plantarflexion / eversion + DF — **answer key not provided by the source** · Orthobullets: **weakness to ankle plantarflexion**

### Differential – xanthomas (TO)

**Bilateral swellings over Achilles (TO)** — options rheumatoid nodules / tophaceous gout / **tendoachilles xanthomas** / ganglion — **answer key not provided by the source** (module filed as xanthomas) · hypercholesterolaemia = rupture risk factor (TO)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **Rule out other pathology** (✗ Wrong options: IOC) |
| **Ultrasound** | **Investigation of choice** after + Thompson (TO) · **complete vs partial** rupture (Orthobullets) · as good as MRI for **tendinopathy + full-thickness tear** (TO) |
| **MRI** | **Highest accuracy** (TO) · **superior for partial tears + types of tendinopathy** (TO) · **equivocal exam**, **chronic ruptures**, gauging **gap size** · retracted tendon edges |
| Complete tear | **US = MRI** (TO) |
| No | **Venography**, **CT** (TO) |

### Diagnostic criteria – ultrasound criteria for nonoperative treatment (TO)

| Position | Criterion |
|---|---|
| **Maximal plantarflexion** | **Gap < 5 mm** |
| **Neutral** | **Gap < 10 mm** |
| **20° plantarflexion** | **> 75% tendon apposition** |

### Acute vs chronic – definition

**Chronic** — **> 3–4 wk** (OB) · **> 4 wk** (OB, JAAOS algorithm) · **> 6 wk** (other OBs) · Orthobullets: acute = **< 6 wk** · TO: chronic **4–6 wk** — read the options

### Chronic sleeve avulsion – diagnosis

**Chronic Achilles avulsion (sleeve) rupture** — old cast-treated rupture + new fall · X-ray: **calcified / ossified distal tendon moved away from insertion** · MRI: **chronic avulsion from insertion**\
✗ Wrong options: HO of proximal tendon · untreated midsubstance · gout · musculotendinous junction

### Medial gastrocnemius tear (tennis leg)

**Mechanism** — **simultaneous knee extension + ankle dorsiflexion** · athletes

**Presentation** — sudden **medial proximal calf** pain + ecchymosis · US / MRI confirm (often unnecessary)

**Treatment** — **conservative first line** → **excellent outcomes, most return to activity within 3 months**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery · quarter need surgery (nonop failure rare)

**Myotendinous Achilles rupture** — nonop boot + PT from 3 wk → complete healing (Orthobullets: more proximal injuries → nonop)

## 5 Management

```
Acute Achilles rupture
├─ Sedentary, frail, proximal / myotendinous, or preference → functional bracing in equinus + early rehab
├─ Active, wants surgery → open end-to-end or percutaneous repair + early functional rehab
└─ Deep infection after repair → debride necrotic tendon, no reconstruction, IV antibiotics 6 wk
```

```
Chronic rupture – gap after debridement
├─ < 2 cm → end-to-end repair
├─ 2–5 cm → V-Y advancement ± FHL transfer
└─ > 5 cm → FHL transfer ± V-Y / turndown
       └─ Defect too big for local tissue (> 10 cm) → allograft
```

```
Chronic / neglected rupture > 4 wk (JAAOS, OB)
├─ Avoid sacrificing tendons / donor morbidity → synthetics
├─ Gap 3–5 cm, viable ends → V-Y or local turndown ± FHL
└─ Gap > 5 cm or unhealthy tissue → FHL / FDL transfer ± dermal matrix
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Acute, preference for nonop · **sedentary** · **medically frail** · **proximal (musculature) injuries** | **Functional bracing / cast in resting equinus (20° PF)** + early functional rehab |
| **Acute (< \~6 wk)** | **Open end-to-end repair** |
| **Cosmesis concern** | **Percutaneous repair** |
| **Chronic, defect < 3 cm** | **V-Y advancement** (Orthobullets) |
| **Chronic, defect > 3 cm** | **FHL transfer ± V-Y advancement** — **requires functioning tibial nerve** (Orthobullets) |
| Defect < 3 cm after debridement + < 3 months | **Direct end-to-end repair** (TO, OB) |

### Operative vs nonoperative acute treatment

| Outcome | Surgery vs nonop with **functional rehab** |
|---|---|
| **Re-rupture** | **Lower with surgery vs cast 6 wk** (classic) · **equal with early functional rehab** (Willits, Soroceanu) · mixed (OB) |
| Re-rupture rates | **\~2% op vs 10–40% nonop** (Orthobullets) · 2.3% vs 3.9% (meta-analysis) · op **3–4%** (TO) · **1–2%** (OB) |
| **Complications** | **↑ with surgery** (\~2×; 4.9% vs 1.6%) — wound, infection, sural |
| Strength, ROM, calf girth, SMFA at 12 mo | **No difference** · **equivalent PF strength** (some ↑ PF strength with surgery; no ↑ DF strength) |
| DF ROM at 6 mo | Not improved — some studies ↓ in op group |
| **Functional outcomes** | **No significant difference** |
| **DVT** | **No difference** (7–10%) |
| **Return to work** | **Quicker with surgery** |
| **Return to sport** | **Quicker with surgery** (one OB) · **similar** (another OB) — read the options · TO: \~60% after surgery |
| Nerve injury | **Higher with surgery** |
| Cost | **Higher with surgery** |
| Satisfaction, ultimate strength | Same (even vs cast) |

**Cast 6 wk nonop vs surgery** — **higher risk of re-rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ satisfaction · less return to sport · ↓ strength · ↑ skin problems

**Re-rupture: greater in conservatively managed cases** — true (TO)

**↓ Re-rupture after repair** — **early functional rehabilitation protocol** (WB in boot \~2 wk + ROM)\
✗ Wrong options: delayed WB > 6 wk (stiffness) · gastrocnemius fascial flap augmentation · biological / PRP augmentation · percutaneous technique

**Nonop rehab** — start **within 48 h** · strict adherence · supervised PT · functional bracing → low re-rupture esp. **> 40 yr, women**

**Post-op immobilisation** — **20° plantarflexion for 4–6 wk** (↓ skin tension, protects repair) · **functional bracing beats casting** (↓ adhesions, ↓ complications)

### Open vs percutaneous / minimally invasive repair – wound complications & sural nerve

| Outcome | **Open** | **Percutaneous / MIS** |
|---|---|---|
| **Wound complications / infection** | **Higher** (superficial infection 6.0%) | **Lower** (0.4%) |
| **Overall complications** | **Higher** | Lower |
| **Local scarring** | More | **Less** |
| **Sural nerve injury** | Nerve seen + protected (0%) | **Higher** (3.4%) — blind needle passage (Orthobullets, OB) · **no difference** (other OBs) — read the options |
| Re-rupture, PF strength, calf circumference | Same | Same |
| Return to sport / running, outcome scores | Same | Same |
| Return to work | — | **More rapid** (Orthobullets) |
| 1-year function | Same | Same |

**Open technique** — incision **just medial to tendon** (sural) · incise **paratenon** · heavy **non-absorbable** suture

### Repair techniques – eponyms (TO)

| Eponym | Technique |
|---|---|
| **Ma and Griffith** | **Percutaneous repair of acute rupture** |
| **Lynn** | Repair + **fanned-out plantaris** over it (injury **< 10 days**) |
| **Lindholm** | **Two flaps** from proximal tendon + gastrocnemius aponeurosis **turned down 180°** to distal tendon |
| **Maffulli** | **Peroneus brevis transfer** (loop across defect) → **NOT an acute repair technique** (TO answer) |
| Teuffer | **PB loop** when end-to-end impossible |
| White and Kraynick | Teuffer modification: **PB through calcaneal tuberosity** + plantaris through ends |
| Turco and Spinella | PB through **midcoronal slit** in distal stump |
| **Bosworth** | **Gastrocnemius-soleus turndown** graft |
| **Abraham and Pankovich** | **V-Y tendinous flap** |
| **Wapner** | **FHL tendon transfer** |

### Wound complications – most common complication & risk factors

**Most common complication of surgical repair** — **skin healing problems / wound complications** (**5–10%**, poor posterior blood supply)\
✗ Wrong options: infection (common, not commonest) · neurological injury (rare after open) · re-rupture (1–2%) · stiffness · loss of motion (nonop)

**Risk factors** — **tobacco (most common)** · **steroid use** · **female sex** · **diabetes** · **open technique**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ BMI · immediate surgery · male · age > 40

**≥ 1 of diabetes / tobacco / steroid** — **> 40%** complications vs 6% without

### Infected repair – debridement

**Deep infection + re-rupture (5 cm)** — **debridement of necrotic + infected tendon, no attempt at reconstruction** → **culture-specific IV antibiotics 6 wk** → later soft-tissue cover + reconstruction\
✗ Wrong options: primary repair · V-Y · turndown · FHL transfer (not with active infection)

### Chronic rupture – V-Y advancement, turndown & FHL transfer by gap size

| Gap (after scar debridement) | Treatment | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **\~1–2 cm** / **< 2 cm** | **End-to-end repair** | OB (most) |
| < 2–3 cm | Direct repair | OB review |
| **< 3 cm** (+ < 3 months) | **Direct repair or V-Y alone** | Orthobullets, OB, TO |
| **2–5 cm** | **V-Y advancement (gastroc-soleus) ± FHL** · V-Y fills 2–5 cm | OB |
| **> 3 cm** | **FHL transfer ± V-Y** (muscle weak, scarred) | Orthobullets, OB |
| **> 5 cm** | **FHL transfer** ± V-Y / turndown · ± allograft | OB |
| **> 10 cm** | **Allograft** (not suitable for local transfer) | OB · (> 5 cm in another OB) |

**4 cm chronic defect** — **V-Y ± FHL augmentation** (one OB) · **open FHL transfer** (another OB) — read the options

**Gap is measured after scar debridement** — 1.5 cm + 1.0 cm scar = **2.5 cm → V-Y lengthening + end-to-end**\
✗ Wrong options: insertional tear · 0.5 + 1.0 = 1.5 cm → end-to-end alone · 1.5 cm no scar · 3.0 + 2.5 = 5.5 cm → combined procedures

**V-Y advancement** — **V apex at musculotendinous junction**, limbs diverge to exit tendon · cut **superficial tendinous portion only**, muscle intact · avoids sacrificing a normal muscle–tendon unit

**Gastrocnemius turndown** — **slip of central third of gastrocnemius tendon** bridges gap · augment with **FHL** (proximity + vascularity)

**FHL transfer technique** — excise degenerate ends · **release FHL at knot of Henry** · tenodese distal FHL stump to FDL · reroute **through calcaneus** → FHL + triceps surae both act on calcaneus · open / extended approach\
✗ Wrong options: percutaneous · limited open

**Calcaneal attachment** — **posterior = greater force** (longer lever arm, advocated) · anterior = more plantarflexion range · transtendinous = transosseous outcomes

| Why FHL is preferred |
|---|
| **Axis of contractile force replicates Achilles** |
| **Fires in phase** with gastroc-soleus |
| **Location / harvest avoids NV bundle** |
| **Expendable** · **stronger** than FDL / PB · **biomechanically strong** bridge |
| Close + well-vascularised · preserves foot muscle balance |

**FHL anatomy** — origin **interosseous membrane + distal 2/3 fibula** · **lateral to FDL at ankle** · **dorsal to FDL at knot of Henry** · inserts **base of 1st distal phalanx** · **tibial nerve**

| Option | Tendon | Why wrong |
|---|---|---|
| **1st distal phalanx, tibial nerve** | **FHL** ✓ | — |
| 1st proximal phalanx | FHB | Wrong insertion |
| 1st distal phalanx (dorsum), deep peroneal | EHL | Anterior |
| 5th MT base, superficial peroneal | **Peroneus brevis** | **Eversion weakness**, doesn't replicate pull, historical, worse longevity |
| Medial cuneiform + 1st MT base, deep peroneal | Tibialis anterior | **Out of phase** (anterior compartment) |
| Medial to FDL, inserts navicular | **Tibialis posterior** | **Chopart weakness, ↓ inversion, pes planus** |
| Plantar to FHL at knot of Henry | **FDL** | Not adjacent, less power (described 1991; FHL 1993) |
| Lateral femoral condyle origin | **Plantaris** | Not robust (**absent in 10%**), must be supplemented |
| — | Peroneus longus, EDL | No (PB acceptable, not PL) |
| — | **Synthetic / carbon fibre** | **Foreign body reaction**, longevity, no proven benefit |
| — | Xenograft, hamstring autograft | Poor outcomes / not used |

**After FHL transfer** — **only significant change: ↓ hallucal phalangeal pressure** · AOFAS MTP-IP **96.4** · no transfer metatarsalgia · no ↑ 1st MT head pressure\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ short flexor recruitment

**Hallux after FHL transfer** — **residual hallux PF / IP flexion weakness** (Orthobullets, OB review) · **no functional hallux deficit** (OB study) — read the options

**Strength** — near-normal maximal strength, **↓ endurance** · torque deficit \~35% · FHL hypertrophy · main benefit = pain relief

**FHL for tendinosis / insertional** — when **> 50% of tendon involved**

**✗ Chronic traps** — **equinus casting** (retracted ends won't appose) · **primary repair in max PF + stretching** (equinus contracture, re-rupture) · end-to-end repair (contracted gastroc-soleus, gap too big) · percutaneous / limited open

![V-Y advancement of gastrocnemius-soleus aponeurosis](../../MCQ/attachments/q3841-illa-vy.jpg)

![FHL transfer to the calcaneus](../../MCQ/attachments/q3841-illb-fhl-transfer.jpg)

### Open insertional rupture on Haglund / insertional tendinopathy

**Open insertional rupture, prior steroid injections, Haglund on X-ray** — **Haglund excision + gastrocnemius recession + Achilles repair + FHL transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: open primary repair alone (distal disease) · functional rehab (never studied for insertional / tendinopathy) · bone-block allograft (> 10 cm) · percutaneous

**Chronic tendonitis + Haglund** — debridement of osseous spur + repair

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Re-rupture** | **Higher nonop (\~10–40% vs 2%)** classically · **no difference** with functional rehab (Level 1) → treat with **surgical repair** |
| **Wound healing** | **5–10%** · **smoking = most common** risk factor · female, steroids, open technique |
| **Deep infection** | Debride necrotic tendon + **6 wk culture-specific antibiotics** |
| **Sural nerve injury** | **Higher with percutaneous** approach |
| **DVT** | **7–10%**, **no op vs nonop difference** |
| FHL harvest | Hallux weakness, harvest morbidity |

## X vs Y

| | **Acute midsubstance** | **Chronic (> 4–6 wk)** | **Sleeve avulsion** | **Tennis leg** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Site | 4–6 cm above insertion | Retracted, scarred stumps | **Insertion** (ossified tendon pulled away) | **Medial gastrocnemius head** |
| Exam | + Thompson, gap | + Thompson, ↑ resting DF, can't toe-walk | Weak squeeze response | Medial calf pain, ecchymosis |
| Treatment | Nonop rehab or repair | **By gap: end-to-end / V-Y / FHL** | Reconstruction | **Conservative** |

## How it's asked

**"Incorrect statement for TA rupture?"** → **antithrombolytics have a role** (TO)

**"Greatest advantage of surgery + early ROM vs cast 6 wk?"** → **lower rate of re-rupture**

**"Cast 6 wk + PT — applies to conservative treatment?"** → **higher risk for re-rupture**

**"Nonop with early functional rehab vs surgery — expected?"** → **no significant difference in functional outcomes**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ DVT · ↓ re-rupture · earlier RTS · ↑ complications

**"Reported with surgery vs functional rehab?"** → **increased complication rates**

**"Shown to decrease re-rupture after repair?"** → **early functional rehabilitation protocol**

**"Reason to recommend minimally invasive repair vs functional rehab?"** → **quicker return to work**

**"Operative vs nonop outcomes?"** → **higher nerve injury, quicker return to sport, quicker return to work**

**"Not a technique for acute TA repair?"** → **Maffulli** (TO)

**"Nerve at risk in TA repair?"** → **sural nerve** (TO)

**"Percutaneous vs open — clearly reduces?"** → **wound infection**

**"Benefit of limited-open technique vs open?"** → **decreased local scarring**

**"Sural nerve on axial MRI?"** → **D** (lateral, beside small saphenous vein)

**"Open vs percutaneous — open results in?"** → **increased rate of postoperative wound complications**

**"Disadvantage of open vs MIS?"** → **higher complication rate**

**"Why percutaneous over open?"** → **lower incidence of wound complications**

**"Increases wound complications after repair?"** → **tobacco use**

**"Most common complication of surgical repair?"** → **skin healing problems**

**"Most common complication to warn of before open repair?"** → **wound complication**

**"Chronic rupture, 5 cm gap after debridement?"** → **gastrocnemius turndown augmented with FHL transfer**

**"Sub-acute rupture with loss of resting tension?"** → **FHL transfer + sliding V-Y advancement of gastroc-soleus aponeurosis**

**"Tendon used to augment V-Y — insertion + nerve?"** → **1st digit distal phalanx, tibial nerve**

**"6 mo neglected, 4 cm defect at surgery?"** → **open repair with FHL transfer**

**"Open insertional rupture, steroid injections, Haglund?"** → **Haglund excision, gastrocnemius recession, Achilles repair, FHL transfer**

**"Best tendon transfer for chronic rupture?"** → **flexor hallucis longus**

**"6 mo injury, 4 cm defect on MRI — reconstruction?"** → **V-Y advancement with or without FHL augmentation**

**"Single-incision FHL transfer — expect?"** → **significantly decreased hallucal phalangeal pressure**

**"Chronic rupture, + Matles — other test + best procedure?"** → **Thompson test, V-Y advancement with tendon transfer**

**"6 wk old, 3 cm defect — procedure likely needed?"** → **V-Y advancement of the Achilles**

**"Intra-op finding supporting V-Y + end-to-end?"** → **1.5 cm defect + 1.0 cm interposed scar**

**"Anatomy of best local autograft after 3.5 cm scar resection?"** → **lateral to FDL at ankle, dorsal to FDL in midfoot**

**"2-yr neglected rupture, 3 cm retraction?"** → **gastrocnemius-soleus V-Y advancement ± FHL transfer**

**"Fever + drainage 4 mo post-repair, 5 cm re-rupture?"** → **debridement of necrotic + infected tendon, no reconstruction**

**"Old cast-treated rupture, ossified tendon pulled off insertion?"** → **chronic Achilles avulsion (sleeve) rupture**

**"Medial gastrocnemius head rupture — expected first-line outcome?"** → **excellent with conservative care, return within 3 months**

**"Bilateral swellings over the Achilles?"** → **answer key not provided by the source** (module: xanthomas) (TO)

**"Heel pain walking upstairs, weak Thompson — investigation of choice?"** → **USG** (TO)

**"Movement tested against resistance in TA tear?"** → **answer key not provided by the source** (TO)

**"False about TA blood supply?"** → **most abundant zone is the mid-section** (it is the insertion) (TO)

**"TA mainly supplied from which surface?"** → **anterior surface** (TO)
