# Achilles Tendonitis

*Foot & Ankle · 3 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Three conditions

| Condition | Who | Site | Key feature |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Insertional Achilles tendonitis** | **Middle-aged + elderly, tight heel cord** | **At insertion** | Spur, **intratendinous calcification**, progressive bony enlargement |
| **Retrocalcaneal bursitis + Haglund deformity** | **Young** | Bursa **between anterior Achilles + posterior calcaneus** · **posterosuperior calcaneal tuberosity** | Pain **anterior to + 2–3 cm proximal to insertion** |
| **Noninsertional (midportion) tendinopathy** | **Active, middle-aged men** | **2–6 cm proximal to insertion** | Thickening, fusiform swelling |

**Noninsertional** — thought **more common** · insertional **less common** (OB)

### Pathophysiology

**Insertional** — repetitive trauma → inflammation → **cartilaginous then bony metaplasia**

**Noninsertional** — **abnormal vascularity / poor blood supply 2–6 cm proximal** + **repetitive micro-tears**

**Risk factors** — overuse · **dorsiflexor / plantarflexor imbalance** · poor blood supply · genetic · **fluoroquinolones** · inflammatory arthropathy

**Inflammation?** — **degeneration without inflammation** (tendinosis; asymptomatic degeneration from microtrauma + ageing) (OB) · "inflammation and thickening" (another OB) — read the options

### Histology

**Insertional** — **disorganised collagen + mucoid degeneration**, **few inflammatory cells**

**Tendinosis** — **anaerobic degeneration** in poorly vascularised segment

## 3 Presentation

**Insertional** — **posterior heel pain**, swelling, burning, stiffness · **shoe pressure pain** (aversion to closed-heel shoes) · **midline tenderness at insertion** · bony prominence

**Retrocalcaneal bursitis** — **fullness + tenderness medial and lateral to tendon** · **pain with dorsiflexion**

**Noninsertional** — pain, swelling, warmth, **worse with activity**, difficulty running · **thickening + tenderness 2–6 cm proximal** · pain through whole ROM

**Painful arc sign** — **tender intratendinous nodule moves with active ROM** = **tendinosis** (vs **paratenonitis** — stays still)

**Dorsiflexion** — most have **limited passive dorsiflexion**

**Silfverskiöld test** — DF improves with **knee flexion** → **gastrocnemius contracture** · no improvement → **Achilles contracture**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Lateral foot X-ray** | **Bone spur + intratendinous calcification (enthesophyte)** (insertional) · **Haglund deformity** = enlarged posterosuperior tuberosity |
| **MRI** | **Intrasubstance intermediate signal** (disorganised tissue) · **thickened tendon** · % degeneration guides surgery · rarely needed for Haglund |
| **Ultrasound** | Degeneration · **colour / power Doppler ↑ intratendinous blood flow** (neovascularity) · chronic rupture = **hypoechoic gap between ends** |
| **MRI diffuse thickening, no focal area** | → **tendon transfer** indication |

![Insertional calcification and spur at the Achilles insertion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7022-32a-moved.jpg)

![Haglund deformity — enlarged posterosuperior calcaneal tuberosity](../../MCQ/attachments/q218623-b-haglund.jpg)

### Noninsertional classification

| Type | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Achilles tendinosis** | **Tendon thickening**; anaerobic degeneration |
| **Achilles peritendonitis** | **Inflammation of tendon sheath** |
| **Paratenonitis** | **Inflammation of paratenon** |

**Brisement** — for **peritendinitis**, not tendinosis

## 5 Management

```
Achilles tendinopathy
├─ First line → eccentric strengthening, NSAIDs, heel lift, shoe change
│      (avoid steroid injection — rupture)
├─ Failed nonop, < 50% degenerated → debridement ± Haglund resection
│      └─ gastrocnemius contracture → + gastrocnemius recession
└─ > 50% degenerated / diffuse / > 55 yr → debridement + FHL transfer
       └─ Achilles contracture (no Silfverskiöld change) → + Achilles lengthening
```

### Operative criteria

| Finding | Add to debridement |
|---|---|
| **Haglund deformity** | **Calcaneal exostectomy / calcaneoplasty** (prevents recurrence) |
| **< 50% degeneration** | **Side-to-side repair** / suture anchor; no transfer |
| **> 50% degeneration** (or debrided) | **FHL (or FDL, PB) transfer / augmentation** |
| **DF improves with knee flexion** | **Gastrocnemius recession** |
| **DF does not improve with knee flexion** | **Achilles lengthening** |
| **> 55 yr**, **diffuse MRI thickening**, **subacute rupture** on tendinopathy, heavier patients | **Tendon transfer** |
| Chronic symptoms **> 6 months** without improvement | Surgery considered |

### Noninsertional tendinopathy – eccentric loading & PRP

**First line** — **eccentric strengthening** (cornerstone, **heavy-load eccentric gastrocsoleus**) + rest, NSAIDs, heel lift, shoes, orthoses for overpronation, PT **3–6 months** · nonop **65–90% successful**

**Not for chronic tendinopathy** — stair climbing · rope jumping · concentric · isometric

**Eccentric = heel drop** — stand on step, heels off, **slowly lower below step** · **force remains constant as the muscle lengthens** · **closed-chain** · enhances dorsiflexion · most helpful in **later phases** of rehab / after diminished activity

![Heel drop — eccentric loading on a step](../../MCQ/attachments/q215782-illa-heel-drop.jpg)

| Exercise (✗ Wrong options: traps) | Definition / example |
|---|---|
| **Eccentric ✓** | **Force constant while muscle lengthens** · heel drop |
| **Concentric** | **Muscle shortens**, tension ∝ external load |
| **Isometric** | **Constant length + tension**, no movement · **standing calf-raise hold**, pushing a wall |
| **Isotonic** | **Constant force through ROM** |
| **Isokinetic** | **Constant velocity (angular) through varied resistance** · Cybex |
| **Plyometric** | **Jump training** · may aggravate |
| **Single-leg stance** | **Proprioception** → ankle instability |
| **Heel walk** | **Dorsiflexor** strengthening |

**Why eccentric works (theory)** — absence of concentric phase disrupts lengthening / shortening cycle → shear in tendon + injury to collagen (exact mechanism unknown)

**Doppler neovascularity, intact tendon, 4 months** — **eccentric closed-chain exercises**\
✗ Wrong options: **steroid** (rupture) · CT chest / abdomen / pelvis · **PRP + delayed arthroscopic debridement** (level IV, not before eccentrics) · resection + FHL + gastrocsoleus turndown (chronic rupture)

**PRP** — **added to eccentrics may improve outcomes in non-responders** (OB, noninsertional) · listed as nonop option (Orthobullets) · **no evidence** for PRP in tendinosis (OB, insertional) — read the options

**Orthotic + eccentrics** — **no better than eccentrics alone**

**Adjuncts studied** — orthotics, PRP, **ESWT**, **topical glyceryl trinitrate**, **polidocanol**, blood, hyaluronic acid, pulsed light, NSAIDs, steroids · GTN, **prolotherapy**, **aprotinin** = evolving, little evidence

**Steroid injection** — **avoid → Achilles rupture** (Orthobullets, OB) · one OB lists corticosteroid injection among nonop options — read the options

**Severe** — cast or removable boot

**Diagnosis from first-line eccentrics** — **Achilles tendinopathy**\
✗ Wrong options: **Baxter nerve** (stretching, PT, NSAIDs) · **calcaneal stress fracture** (NWB) · **ATFL tear** (PT + proprioception) · **syndesmotic sprain** (NWB cast / CAM boot)

### Insertional tendinopathy – debridement, Haglund resection & FHL transfer

**Nonop** — **heel sleeves + pads (mainstay)** · **small heel lift** · eccentric PT + gastrocsoleus stretching · **locked-ankle AFO 6–9 months** if others fail · **no steroid** · Haglund: ice, external padding · high nonop failure rates

**Surgery (< 50% removed)** — **retrocalcaneal bursa excision + debridement + calcaneal prominence resection** · tendon detachment → calcaneoplasty → reattachment

**Surgery (> 50% removed)** — **suture anchor repair + FHL (FDL, PB) augmentation + primary closure**\
✗ Wrong options: **side-to-side** (for < 50%) · **tibialis posterior** transfer · wound VAC / staged closure · closure alone

**Incisions** — **midline, lateral or medial J-shaped**

**Haglund + calcifications, failed nonop, loss of function** — **debridement + calcaneal exostectomy + possible FHL transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: debridement alone · Arizona brace · steroid · ankle arthrodesis

**Haglund, normal DF (10°), 30% degeneration** — **debridement + exostectomy alone**

| Scenario (OB) | Correct plan |
|---|---|
| Normal heel, DF 10°, **60%** | Debridement + **FHL** (no exostectomy) |
| Normal heel, DF −10° → 10° with knee flexion, 60% | Debridement + FHL + **gastrocnemius recession** |
| **Haglund**, DF 10°, 60% | Debridement + FHL + **exostectomy** |
| **Haglund**, DF 10°, **30%** | **Debridement + exostectomy alone** ✓ |
| Haglund, DF −10° not improved with knee flexion, 30% | Debridement + **Achilles lengthening** + exostectomy |

**Haglund + gastrocnemius contracture, 40% debrided** — **calcaneoplasty / Haglund excision + gastrocnemius recession** ("I and III")\
✗ Wrong options: FHL (< 50%)

**FHL transfer (overweight)** — **↑ ankle plantar flexion strength**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ AOFAS · ↑ SF-36 · **↓ great-toe plantar flexion** (not seen) · no difference

**Recovery (older, enthesopathy, debridement + ostectomy)** — **12 months (a year or more)** · young / pure tendon pathology faster

### Chronic noninsertional disease – debridement with FHL transfer

| Operation | Indication | Success |
|---|---|---|
| **Percutaneous longitudinal tenotomy** | **Mild–moderate** | — |
| Tendon stripping (suture strips anterior Achilles adhesions) | Adhesions | — |
| **Dorsal wedge calcaneal osteotomy + debridement** | **< 50%** involvement | — |
| **Open excision + tubularisation** | **Moderate–severe** | **70–100%** |
| **Tendon transfer (FHL, FDL, PB)** | **> 50% degeneration**, **> 55 yr**, **diffuse MRI thickening**, **subacute rupture** | — |

**Fusiform swelling 12 months, > 50% width** — **debridement + FHL transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: side-to-side (inadequate strength) · steroid · brisement · walking cast

**Subacute rupture on tendinosis, 4 cm gap** — **debridement + augmentation with tendon inserting on plantar great-toe distal phalanx (FHL)**\
✗ Wrong options: primary suture-anchor repair (fails) · plantar 1st MT head · FDL plantar lesser-toe distal phalanges, not MT heads / dorsal

**Large-gap options** — **V-Y myotendinous flap**, free tendon graft, synthetic graft, local transfer (**PB, FDL, FHL**) · small tear → debridement + primary repair

**FHL transfer outcome (Den Hartog)** — ↑ ankle–hindfoot scores, mostly good / excellent, **no hallux functional deficit**

| Tendon | Insertion |
|---|---|
| **FHL** | **Plantar aspect of great-toe distal phalanx** |
| **FDL** | **Plantar distal phalanges of lesser toes** |

## X vs Y

| | **Insertional** | **Noninsertional** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **At insertion** | **2–6 cm proximal** |
| Age | Middle-aged + elderly | Active middle-aged |
| X-ray | **Spur, calcification, Haglund** | Often normal |
| Nonop | **Heel sleeves / pads**, heel lift, AFO | **Eccentric loading** ± PRP |
| Surgery | **Debridement + exostectomy** ± FHL | Tenotomy / excision + tubularisation ± FHL |

| | **Tendinosis** | **Paratenonitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Painful arc | **Nodule moves** with ROM | Fixed |
| Treatment | Eccentrics, debridement | **Brisement** |

## How it's asked

**"Exercise to add after a period of reduced activity?"** → **eccentric**

**"Most effective PT for chronic noninsertional tendinopathy?"** → **eccentric strengthening**

**"Later-phase strengthening in Achilles tendinitis?"** → **eccentric**

**"Exercise brochure for midportion tendinosis?"** → **Figures B and D — eccentric exercises most likely to relieve pain + return him to previous activity level**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery first

**"Describe the most effective PT technique?"** → **force remains constant as the muscle lengthens**

**"Most effective exercise for posterior heel pain?"** → **heel drop**

**"Doppler ↑ blood flow, intact tendon — next step?"** → **eccentric closed-chain exercises**

**"First-line eccentric strengthening — diagnosis?"** → **Achilles tendinopathy**

**"PRP in noninsertional tendinopathy — true?"** → **PRP + eccentrics may improve outcomes in non-responders**

**"Haglund + calcification, failed nonop?"** → **debridement + calcaneal exostectomy + possible FHL transfer**

**"55% debrided — after suture-anchor repair?"** → **FHL augmentation + primary closure**

**"Recovery after debridement + ostectomy in 65-yo?"** → **12 months**

**"FHL transfer vs no transfer?"** → **increased ankle plantar flexion strength**

**"When is debridement + exostectomy alone right?"** → **Haglund, DF 10° knee extended, 30% degeneration**

**"40% debrided, Haglund, DF ↑ with knee flexion?"** → **calcaneoplasty + gastrocnemius recession (I and III)**

**"Fusiform swelling, > 50% degenerated?"** → **debridement + FHL tendon transfer**

**"4 cm gap on chronic tendinosis — best function?"** → **tendon inserting on plantar great-toe distal phalanx (FHL)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Acquired Spastic Equinovarus Deformity

*Foot & Ankle · 4 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Etiology

**Causes** — **cerebrovascular accident (CVA)** · **traumatic brain injury (TBI)** → **upper motor neuron** injury

**Commonest physical finding after CVA** — **spastic equinovarus** deformity of foot and ankle\
✗ Wrong options: flaccid · calcaneovalgus · equinovalgus

**Associated** — joint contractures · **knee hyperextension in stance**

### Pathophysiology

| Component | Overactive muscle |
|---|---|
| **Equinus** | **Gastrocnemius–soleus complex** |
| **Varus** | **Tibialis anterior** (relative overactivity) · lesser: **FHL, FDL, tibialis posterior** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — deformity · difficulty with gait

**Exam** — **spastic equinovarus** · **↑ tone** · **hyperreflexia**

![Spastic equinovarus foot after stroke](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7000-equinovarus.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**: spastic equinovarus foot + prior CVA / TBI

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **AP, lateral, oblique** of foot and ankle

## 5 Management

```
Spastic equinovarus after CVA / TBI
├─ During neurologic recovery → PT, botulinum / phenol, AFO
└─ Fixed contracture after recovery, not braceable → surgery
       ├─ Equinus → Achilles lengthening (TAL)
       ├─ Varus → SPLATT
       └─ Recurrence despite soft-tissue surgery → osteotomies / fusions
```

### Nonoperative (first line)

**Therapy** — stretching + strengthening · maintain joint ROM · splinting

**Injections** — **phenol blocks** · **botulinum toxin**

**AFO** — use while in **bed or wheelchair**

**Timing** — **neurologic recovery 6–18 months after CVA** · **years after TBI** → operate only after recovery period

### Operative criteria

| Indication for surgery |
|---|
| **Fixed contractures persist after neurologic recovery** and **not braceable** |
| Functional deficits |
| **Skin problems** secondary to deformity / braces |
| Recurrence despite proper soft-tissue procedures → **osteotomies + fusions** |

### Post-stroke equinovarus – SPLATT with TAL

**Equinus** — **Achilles tendon lengthening (TAL)** (open / percutaneous) or **gastrocnemius recession**

**Varus** — **split anterior tibialis tendon transfer (SPLATT)**\
✗ Wrong options: medializing calcaneal osteotomy + FHL transfer (that's flatfoot)

**SPLATT technique** — tibialis anterior split → **lateral half to cuboid** through drill hole, sutured · ± lengthening / dorsal transfer of **PTT**

![SPLATT – lateral half of tibialis anterior to cuboid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7000-splatt-2.jpg)

**Bridle procedure** — **posterior tibialis tendon** through **interosseous membrane** to dorsum → dual anastomosis to **tibialis anterior** + rerouted **peroneus longus** (in front of lateral malleolus)

## 6 Complications

**Hindfoot valgus** — overcorrection from **inadvertent PTT lengthening**

### Prognosis after CVA

| Outcome | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Normal ambulation** | **25%** |
| **Some level of ambulation** | **75%** |
| Neurologic recovery | **6–18 months** (CVA) · years (TBI) |

## X vs Y

| | **SPLATT** | **Bridle** |
|---|---|---|
| Tendon moved | **Lateral half of tibialis anterior** | **Tibialis posterior** |
| Route / insertion | **Cuboid** (drill hole) | Through **interosseous membrane** → dorsum, anastomosed to TA + rerouted PL |
| Corrects | **Varus** (spastic) | Foot drop / varus |

## How it's asked

**"Foot deformity after stroke?"** → **spastic equinovarus**

**"Post-stroke, correctable contracture, brace skin problems — add to TAL?"** → **SPLATT**\
✗ Wrong options: medializing calcaneal osteotomy + FHL transfer

**"Varus after CVA — prime mover?"** → **tibialis anterior** (relative overactivity)

**"Equinus after CVA — cause?"** → **gastrocnemius–soleus overactivity**

**"Surgery indicated when?"** → **fixed contracture after neurologic recovery, not braceable**

**"Neurologic recovery after CVA takes?"** → **6–18 months**

**"Hindfoot valgus after SPLATT?"** → **inadvertent PTT lengthening**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ankle Arthritis

*Foot & Ankle · 5 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & etiology

**Incidence** — **30 / 100,000 / yr** · prevalence **\~1%** · less common than hip / knee OA

**Post-traumatic** — **commonest, 75–80%** · mostly **ankle fractures**; rest = **chronic (lateral) ligamentous laxity**

**Primary OA** — **< 10%**

**Other causes** — **RA / inflammatory**, osteonecrosis, neuropathic, septic, gout, haemophilia

**Risk factors** — prior ankle fracture · chronic ligamentous laxity · inflammatory arthropathy

**Pathoanatomy** — nonanatomic fracture healing → altered contact forces + load bearing → cartilage loss on **talar body + plafond** → narrowing, subchondral sclerosis, **eburnation**

### Anatomy

**Joint** — **ginglymus** (tibia, talus, fibula) · talar dome **biconcave with central sulcus**

**ROM** — **dorsiflexion 20°** · **plantarflexion 50°**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain with weight-bearing · stiffness · locking / catching

**Exam** — variable effusion · pain + ↓ ROM vs contralateral · angular deformity (trauma) · crepitus

**Localising sign (OB)** — pain through tibiotalar arc · **painless subtalar inversion / eversion** → isolated tibiotalar disease (→ no need to fuse subtalar)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing AP, lateral, mortise** | Diagnosis: **joint space loss**, subchondral sclerosis + cysts, eburnation, angular deformity |
| **CT** | **Surgical planning** (arthrodesis + arthroplasty); quantify healing of a fusion nonunion |
| **MRI** | Focal cartilage disease · **↑ sensitivity in early disease** · cartilage injury, subchondral marrow oedema, ligament tears |
| Labelled WBC scan | Infection workup in fusion nonunion |
| Scanograms / contralateral films | Malalignment planning before revision |

### Takakura classification (mortise view, varus-type OA)

| Stage | Radiograph | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | Osteophytes + early sclerosis, **no narrowing** | ![Takakura I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7037-stage-i.jpg) |
| **II** | **Narrowing of medial joint space**, no subchondral contact | ![Takakura II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7037-stage-ii.jpg) |
| **IIIA** | Obliteration **at medial malleolus**, subchondral bone contact | ![Takakura IIIA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7037-stage-iiia.jpg) |
| **IIIB** | Obliteration **over roof of talar dome**, subchondral contact | ![Takakura IIIB](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7037-stage-iiib.jpg) |
| **IV** | Obliteration with **complete tibiotalar contact** | ![Takakura IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7037-stage-iv.jpg) |

| Stage | Treatment (OB) |
|---|---|
| **I** | Conservative / symptomatic |
| **II, IIIA** | **Supramalleolar osteotomy** (joint preserving) |
| **IIIB** | SMO **poor results / higher failure** → TAA or arthrodesis |
| **IV** | **Arthrodesis** or **TAA** (whole joint) |

### Talar osteonecrosis (differential)

**Late talar head osteonecrosis (rare)** — **talonavicular + subtalar** pain (selective injections) · X-ray / CT = advanced degenerative change of TN + subtalar joints

**MRI** — **↓ T1, ↑ T2 with dark serpiginous line**

**✗ Look-alikes** — **PVNS** (erosions + hemosiderin) · **chronic osteomyelitis** (fluid collections, bony destruction) · talar neck / head fracture (no fracture line)

## 5 Management

```
Ankle arthritis
├─ Mild / first line → NSAIDs, activity change, Arizona brace, rocker sole, steroid injection
├─ Anterior osteophytes, isolated impingement → arthroscopic debridement + exostectomy
├─ Asymmetric (Takakura II–IIIA), malaligned, near-normal ROM → supramalleolar osteotomy
├─ Young, moderate–severe PTOA, good ROM → distraction arthroplasty
└─ End-stage (IIIB–IV)
       ├─ Young high-demand labourer, obese, neuropathic → ankle arthrodesis
       └─ Elderly, low demand, inflammatory, contralateral fusion → total ankle arthroplasty
```

### Nonoperative management – bracing, rocker sole & injection

**First line** — activity modification, **bracing to immobilise ankle**, NSAIDs · **intra-articular corticosteroid = mainstay injection** · PRP / hyaluronic acid **no consensus** (PRP not proven)

**Untried nonop (OB)** — **steroid injection + cushioned rocker-bottom shoe** (± cane)\
✗ Wrong options: opiates + limited WB 6 wk · cast NWB 12 wk · triple / pan-talar arthrodesis

**Arizona (gauntlet) brace** — **immobilises ankle + hindfoot** → best brace for tibiotalar OA at any age · PT may worsen pain

| Orthosis / shoe | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Single rocker sole** | **End-stage tibiotalar (or subtalar) arthritis with limited motion**, or after ankle / subtalar fusion · ↑ toe-off propulsion, ↓ heel-strike pressure, ↓ ankle motion needed |
| **Double rocker sole** | **Midfoot prominences (Charcot)**, midfoot OA |
| **Severe angle rocker** | **Toe-tip ulcers** |
| **Negative heel rocker** | **Fixed ankle dorsiflexion** deformity |
| **CROW boot** | Charcot / midfoot |
| **Lateral hindfoot post** | **Cavovarus** foot |
| **Hinged AFO** | **Foot drop** — does **not** immobilise ankle |
| Rocker sole (wrong option) | Fixed planovalgus |

### Arthroscopic debridement & distraction arthroplasty

**Debridement + anterior tibial / dorsal talar exostectomy** — **isolated anterior impingement** from anterior osteophytes · joint preserving · open option (plan future approaches) · **not for advanced arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: won't correct alignment

**Distraction arthroplasty** — **young**, moderate–severe **post-traumatic** OA · offloading → biologic repair · **needs good pre-op ROM** · static / hinged ex-fix or spatial frame ± microfracture, chondroplasty, orthobiologics · outcomes controversial

### Supramalleolar osteotomy – indications (varus, asymmetric arthritis)

**Indications** — **near-normal ROM** · **minimal talar tilt** · **varus heel alignment** · **medially focused (asymmetric) arthritis = Takakura II / IIIA** · tibiotalar malalignment, eccentric wear · young active patient wanting high activity

**Biggest change in ankle forces** — **tibial osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: deltoid / lateral ligament reconstruction · calcaneal osteotomy · PL → PB transfer (adjuncts only)

| Deformity | Osteotomy |
|---|---|
| **Varus** | **Medial opening wedge** (or lateral closing wedge) |
| **Valgus** | **Medial closing wedge** (or lateral opening wedge) ± **oblique fibular lengthening osteotomy** |
| Dome | Less translation / length change; technically hard |
| Multiplanar / large | **Gradual correction with spatial frame** |

**Fibular osteotomy** — outcomes **same** whether or not needed

**Medial opening wedge best for** — **symptomatic medial narrowing + varus alignment**\
✗ Wrong options: stage IV valgus → fusion · stage I → conservative · lateral narrowing + neutral alignment → look at knee / hip · IIIB → poor results

| Preoperative talar tilt | Five-year survival rate |
|---|---|
| **4–10°** | **85%** |
| **> 10°** | **65%** (higher failure; also Takakura IIIB) |

**Contraindications** — **< 50% preserved tibiotalar surface** · **age > 70** · hindfoot instability not fixable by ligament reconstruction · significant vascular disease · **inflammatory arthritis** · **neuroarthropathy** ·\
✗ Wrong options: **age < 50 is NOT** a contraindication

**Complications** — **reoperation for hardware removal commonest**, then nonunion

### Operative criteria – arthrodesis vs TAA

| | **Ankle arthrodesis** | **Total ankle arthroplasty** |
|---|---|---|
| Indications | Moderate–severe **PTOA or inflammatory**, refractory · **young, high-demand labourers** · malalignment (+ osteotomy) · comorbidities | PTOA or **inflammatory (RA)** · **elderly**, low demand · ? concomitant TN / subtalar fusion · **contralateral ankle fusion** · wants motion (hiking, uneven ground) |
| Relative contraindications | **Vascular compromise (ABI 0.4, claudication)** — poor wound + fusion healing | **Obesity** (✗ Wrong options: not absolute, BMI 35) · **young labourers** · severe deformity |
| Absolute contraindications | — | **Active infection** · inadequate bone stock / **severe osteoporosis** · **talus osteonecrosis** · **Charcot / neuropathic joint** · **peripheral neuropathy** |
| Other contraindications (OB) | — | Uncorrectable deformity · ankle instability · neurologic muscle imbalance · severe malalignment |

### Arthrodesis vs total ankle arthroplasty – gait, adjacent joints

**Not TAA contraindications** — post-traumatic arthritis (commonest indication) · inflammatory arthritis / RA · age > 50 · reconstructible ligament damage · **correctable coronal deformity (up to 20–30°)** via osteotomy / soft-tissue balancing (TO: **> 10–15° = contraindication** — read the options) · **preserved subtalar motion = favourable**

**Peripheral neuropathy** — no protective sensation → microtrauma, instability, wounds, infection, **early loosening**

**Arthrodesis** — **gold standard** for end-stage OA · reliable pain relief + ADLs · **union \~90%** (Orthobullets) · **93–94% with cancellous screws**, 74% overall (OB)

**Arthrodesis position** — **neutral dorsiflexion**, **5–10° external rotation** (10° OB / TO), **\~5° valgus** (0–5° OB), **talus \~5 mm posterior**

**Young labourer (28 yo, BMI 31, construction)** — **ankle fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: low-profile TAA · bipolar allograft · interposition · arthroscopic debridement (too advanced)

**Healthy 66-yo hiker, subtalar pain-free** — **TAA**\
✗ Wrong options: TTC nail (spare subtalar) · tibiotalar fusion → younger · distraction → younger, moderate

**Large OCD + 5° varus, 41 yo** — joint / cartilage preservation first

| Function (TAA vs arthrodesis) | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Gait** | TAA patients: **superior gait mechanics compared to AA post-operatively** — **↑ stride length, cadence, stride / gait velocity** → arthrodesis = **↓ stride length** (✗ Wrong options: hip abduction · leg length) |
| **Slopes / stairs** | TAA **higher scores walking uphill, upstairs, downstairs** |
| Uneven surfaces at 12 mo | **Both improve** vs pre-op |
| Gait function at 12 mo | Arthrodesis **improves** it (✗ Wrong options: "loss of gait function") |
| **Bilateral arthrodesis** | Bad for gait → TAA for the second ankle |

**TAA outcomes** — up to **90% good–excellent at 5–10 yr** (Orthobullets) · **\~90% survivorship at 10 yr** (OB) · **> 80% survivorship at 5 yr if < 50 yo** (Orthobullets) · survivorship ↓ if **< 54 yo** (OB) · **70–98% at 3–6 yr, 80–95% at 8–12 yr** (OB, Easley) · **5-yr up to 60%, reoperation up to 30%** (another OB) — read the options · long-term data pending

**Arthrodesis long-term** — **ipsilateral midfoot / hindfoot degeneration**\
✗ Wrong options: revision surgery · hardware failure · walking aids · knee / hip DJD

**Subtalar arthrosis after arthrodesis** — **50% at 10 yr** (Orthobullets) · **> 50% within 10 yr** (OB) · **higher after AA than TAA**\
✗ Wrong options: "rare after well-done fusion"

### Arthrodesis & arthroplasty technique

**Arthrodesis approaches** — anterior · **transfibular (8–10 cm distal fibula resected)** · mini-open (burr) · arthroscopic

**Arthrodesis fixation** — transarticular compression screws · bridge plate · ex-fix · **hindfoot retrograde nail (TTC)**

**TAA approach** — anterior, **between tibialis anterior + EHL** · protect **SPN, DPN, anterior tibial artery**

**TAA design** — fixed vs mobile bearing · adjuncts: **TAL / gastrocnemius recession**, foot osteotomies, ligament reconstruction

### Failed fusion or talar AVN – revision arthrodesis

**Workup** — exclude **infection + metabolic** causes · plain films · CT (bony healing) · **labelled WBC scan** · scanograms if malaligned

**Nonunion, subtalar pain-free** — **revision ankle arthrodesis + bone graft** with **stable compression fixation** (transfibular approach + compression screws) · **spare subtalar** (young)

**✗ Nonunion traps** — external / internal bone stimulator · **dynamisation → instability** · PRP · bone grafting alone · hardware removal + **IM rod / hindfoot nail (TTC)** when subtalar is normal

**Revision union** — **≥ 85%**

## 6 Complications

| Procedure | Complication |
|---|---|
| **Arthrodesis** | **Subtalar arthrosis 50% at 10 yr** · **nonunion 10%** |
| Nonunion risk factors | **Smoking**, adjacent joint fusion, **failed previous arthrodesis**, **AVN** |
| **TAA** | **Intra-op medial malleolus fracture** (prophylactic pinning) · PJI · aseptic loosening · **periprosthetic cysts** · wound + deep infection · osteolysis · periprosthetic fracture |
| Periprosthetic cysts | Infection or aseptic loosening · small asymptomatic → **observe** · large / progressive / painful → **debridement + bone graft** or **revision TAA** |
| **SMO** | Hardware removal (commonest reoperation) → nonunion |

### TAA complication – syndesmotic nonunion

**Pain 10 mo after TAA, stable ligaments, syndesmotic screws** — **syndesmotic nonunion** → **↑ tibial component loosening**\
✗ Wrong options: poly wear loosening · lateral ligament failure · "normal at 10 mo" · wrong procedure

![Syndesmotic nonunion after total ankle arthroplasty](../../MCQ/attachments/q583-b-tar-non.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Arthrodesis** | **TAA** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | **Young labourer**, obese, neuropathic, vasculopath | **Elderly**, RA, low demand |
| Gait | ↓ stride length | **↑ stride length, cadence, velocity**, uphill |
| Adjacent joints | **Subtalar 50% at 10 yr**, midfoot / hindfoot DJD | Preserved |
| Talar osteonecrosis | Option | **Contraindicated** |

| | **Supramalleolar osteotomy** | **Fusion / TAA** |
|---|---|---|
| Takakura | **II–IIIA** (asymmetric, medial) | **IIIB–IV** (whole joint) |
| Talar tilt | Minimal (> 10° → 65% survival) | Any |
| Ages | Young OK (< 50 not CI); **> 70 CI** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Single rocker sole best for?"** → **end-stage tibiotalar arthritis with limited motion**

**"Double rocker sole for?"** → **midfoot prominences (Charcot)**

**"Untreated tibiotalar OA — next step?"** → **steroid injection + cushioned rocker-bottom shoe**

**"Nonop tibiotalar OA, which brace?"** → **Arizona brace**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral hindfoot post · hinged AFO · CROW

**"Young active woman, varus asymmetric OA — return to sport?"** → **supramalleolar osteotomy**

**"Medial opening wedge SMO most appropriate?"** → **medial narrowing + varus alignment**

**"Greatest change in ankle forces?"** → **tibial osteotomy**

**"Best indication for SMO?"** → **varus heel alignment**\
✗ Wrong options: 30% surface · age > 70 · inflammatory · neuroarthropathy

**"True about surgical options in a labourer?"** → **SMO 5-yr survival 65% if talar tilt > 10°**

**"TAA contraindication?"** → **neuropathic joint disease**

**"TAA contraindication (BMI 35, 12° correctable, PTOA)?"** → **peripheral neuropathy**

**"Long-term after ankle fusion?"** → **ipsilateral midfoot / hindfoot degenerative changes**

**"Arthrodesis vs TAA gait?"** → **decreased stride length**

**"28-yo labourer, BMI 31, PTOA?"** → **ankle fusion**

**"66-yo hiker, subtalar spared?"** → **total ankle arthroplasty**

**"Best ankle fusion candidate?"** → **45-yo construction worker, end-stage OA**

**"TAA vs AA true (RA patient)?"** → **TAA superior gait mechanics**

**"TAA vs AA true?"** → **TAA greater scores walking uphill**

**"Pain 10 mo after TAA with syndesmotic screws?"** → **syndesmotic nonunion**

**"Ankle fusion nonunion at 12 mo, subtalar normal?"** → **revision ankle arthrodesis + bone grafting**

**"Distracted fusion, loose distal fixation, 34 yo?"** → **revision fusion with stable compression + bone graft**

**"TN + subtalar pain, T1 dark / T2 bright serpiginous line?"** → **late osteonecrosis of talar head**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ankle Arthrodesis

*Foot & Ankle · 6 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Indications & causes

**Definition** — fusion of the **tibiotalar joint**; commonest for **end-stage ankle arthritis**; open or arthroscopic

**Commonest cause** — **post-traumatic arthritis** · **> 60%** of end-stage tibiotalar arthritis (OB)

**Other indications** — post-infection · chronic instability · **AVN of the talus** · inflammatory arthropathy · primary OA · **neuropathic arthropathy** · tumour resection · **salvage of failed ORIF** · **salvage of failed TAA**

**Talar AVN** — **trauma = commonest cause (75%)**, mainly talar neck / body fractures · early → core decompression, non-vascularised or vascularised bone graft · late (structural change) → **arthrodesis**

**Hindfoot OA causes** — calcaneus / talus fractures · RA · OA · end-stage PTTD · tarsal coalition · **Charcot-Marie-Tooth (cavus)**

**Talar neck fracture** — **varus malunion \~30%** → abnormal ankle loading → arthritis

### Effect of hindfoot fusion on motion (talonavicular)

| Joint fused | Effect on motion |
|---|---|
| **Talonavicular** | **Eliminates almost all hindfoot motion — > 90% ↓** (greatest cumulative effect) |
| **Subtalar (talocalcaneal)** | **↓ TN motion 74%** · **↓ CC motion 44%** |
| **Calcaneocuboid** | **↓ TN motion 33%** (OB) · **67%** (another OB) — read the options · ↓ subtalar 8% (little effect) |
| Naviculocuneiform | Little effect on hindfoot |
| Cuboid–5th MT | Limited effect |
| **Tibiotalar** | **Little effect on hindfoot ROM** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Decides |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing X-rays** ankle + foot | End-stage tibiotalar arthritis · coronal deformity (varus) · triple malunion (residual subtalar valgus, forefoot abduction) |
| **CT** | **Hindfoot (subtalar) degeneration** → spared = **isolated tibiotalar** · involved = **TTC** |
| **MRI** | **Talar AVN** — broad-based → TTC |
| **Aspiration** | Prior septic ankle — **no growth, WBC 9,800 = no active infection** → arthrodesis (✗ Wrong options: repeat I&D) |
| Post-op X-ray | **Tibial stress fracture at proximal interlocking screw** of hindfoot nail |

**Clinical localisation** — pain with **passive DF / PF** = tibiotalar · pain with **inversion / eversion** + sinus tarsi pain = **subtalar** · **Coleman block** rigid hindfoot = fixed

## 5 Management

```
End-stage ankle arthritis, failed bracing / NSAIDs / injections
├─ Subtalar spared → TIBIOTALAR ARTHRODESIS (screws)
├─ + Subtalar arthritis / broad talar AVN / bone loss → TTC ARTHRODESIS
│      (lateral transfibular · retrograde IM nail · femoral head allograft if bone loss)
├─ Infection / draining hardware → REMOVE HARDWARE + I&D → STAGED ARTHRODESIS
└─ Isolated subtalar arthritis, ankle painless → SUBTALAR ARTHRODESIS
```

### Operative criteria – arthrodesis vs TAA

| Favours **arthrodesis** | Favours **TAA** |
|---|---|
| **Young, high-demand / labourer** (construction worker, 36–45 yo) | **Elderly, less active** |
| **Peripheral neuropathy** (↑ TAA failure) · insensate foot | Preserved sensation |
| **Severe PVD** | — |
| **Charcot / neuropathic arthropathy** | — |
| **Osteonecrosis of talus** (TAA contraindicated) | Good talar bone stock |
| **Active or prior infection** (↑ periprosthetic infection) | No infection history |
| **Severe deformity** · soft-tissue compromise | Correctable deformity |
| **Gold standard**; most reliable pain relief | Keeps ROM → better cadence, **↓ adjacent joint degeneration**; reasonable 10-yr survivorship |

**Tibiotalar fusion** — **highest success rate** for post-ORIF tibiotalar arthritis\
✗ Wrong options: osteochondral allograft · interpositional soft tissue · arthroscopic debridement + microfracture · TTC

**Supramalleolar osteotomy** — only for **coronal deformity > 10°** with **mild / moderate** arthritis\
✗ Wrong options: end-stage

### Optimal fusion position

| Plane | Position |
|---|---|
| **Sagittal** | **Neutral (0°) dorsiflexion / plantarflexion — plantigrade** |
| **External rotation** | **5–10° ER** (Orthobullets, OB) · **10° ER** (OB, TO) · **ER \~5°** (TO explanation) — read the options |
| **Coronal** | **5° hindfoot valgus** (Orthobullets, TO) · **0–5° valgus** (OB) · **5–10° valgus** (another OB) — read the options |
| **Translation** | **5 mm posterior talar translation** under the plafond |

![Tibiotalar arthrodesis with crossed screws](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7052-ankle-fusion.jpg)

**Why each** — neutral flexion ↓ knee extension in gait + **MCL laxity** · slight valgus prevents **lateral column overload** · posterior talus ↓ **lever arm of foot**

**Avoid (TO)** — **extension** → excess pressure + **intractable heel pain** (slight flexion tolerated) · **any varus** · **anterior talar translation** → **"vaulting" gait + knee pain** (TO)

**Wrong positions** — **plantarflexion** → equinus gait, poor swing-phase clearance ("never the answer"; goal = plantigrade) · **too much DF or valgus (20°)** → altered gait, ↑ knee stress · **internal rotation** → abnormal foot progression angle · 0° ER · varus → poor outcome, adjacent joint degeneration

**Hindfoot arthrodesis** — aim **5° hindfoot valgus**

### Indications – tibiotalar vs tibiotalocalcaneal vs subtalar fusion

| Scenario | Answer | ✗ Traps |
|---|---|---|
| Post-traumatic ankle OA, 36-yo labourer, **CT: hindfoot spared** | **Tibiotalar arthrodesis with screws** | Arthroscopic debridement + steroid (adjunct only) · TAA · TTC IM / extramedullary |
| Varus end-stage ankle OA, **mild subtalar changes**, diabetic, no neuropathy | **Isolated tibiotalar fusion + slight posterior talar translation** | > 5° PF · IR · combined ST fusion · triple |
| End-stage ankle OA + **peripheral neuropathy**, no subtalar pain | **Tibiotalar arthrodesis** | TAA (↑ failure) · arthroscopy · subtalar fusion |
| **Prior septic arthritis + talar AVN**, aspirate sterile | **Ankle arthrodesis** | Repeat I&D · **TAA (contraindicated in talar AVN)** · SMOT · triple (fixed adult flatfoot) |
| Talar neck nonunion + **AVN + talar head collapse** | **TTC arthrodesis with femoral head allograft** | Steroid injection · TAA · subtalar fusion alone · triple |
| **Broad-based talar AVN** + tibiotalar + subtalar arthritis | **TTC via lateral transfibular approach** | Anterior approach (no subtalar access) · ankle fusion alone · TAA · **talectomy without arthrodesis** |
| **Pes cavus**, sinus tarsi pain, painful inversion / eversion, rigid on Coleman block, ankle painless | **Subtalar arthrodesis** | Broström–Gould (instability) · hindfoot scope + os trigonum excision (posterior impingement) · Chrisman–Snook (advanced instability) · **triple (TN + CC + ST arthritis)** |
| Isolated hindfoot arthritis from **chronic pes planus** | **Subtalar arthrodesis** | — |

### Techniques & fixation

**Arthroscopic arthrodesis** — **only if minimal deformity**

**Open** — **transfibular approach when deformity present** · fixation = **screws** · **plate + screws** · **external fixation**

**Staged approach** — **clear infection before definitive internal hardware**

**TTC fixation options** — Steinmann pins · plates + screws · ex-fix · **retrograde IM nail**

**Retrograde IM nail** — **load-sharing**; **↑ bending stiffness + rotational stability** vs plate-and-screw · useful with distal tibial / talar bone loss or when screw fixation suboptimal

![Tibiotalocalcaneal fusion with retrograde hindfoot nail](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7052-hindfoot-nail-moved.jpg)

**Nail entry point (TO)** — crossing of **bimalleolar (medial malleolus) bisector** (coronal) **+ sagittal line from tip of 2nd toe to centre of heel** (TO)

**Lateral transfibular approach** — dual joint preparation (**tibiotalar + subtalar**) + **local autograft** (talar osteonecrosis)

**TTC indications** — end-stage **ankle + subtalar arthritis** · **Charcot neuroarthropathy** · **hindfoot bone loss** (failed TAA, failed arthrodesis) · **osteonecrosis / global AVN of talus** · severe acute trauma · RA · flail ankle

### Contraindication – peripheral vascular disease

| Retrograde nail TTC | Items |
|---|---|
| **Absolute contraindications** | **Active infection** · **severe (profound) peripheral vascular disease** · **severe tibial malalignment** |
| **Relative contraindication** | **Normal subtalar joint** — may be sacrificed in non-compliant patients + **diabetic Charcot** for stability |
| **Indications (✗ Wrong options: as contraindication)** | **Osteonecrosis of talus** · **bone loss from failed TAA / arthrodesis** · **severe peripheral neuropathy with Charcot** |

### Salvage of infection/failed TAA – staged arthrodesis

**Infected TAA + varus instability + talar bone loss** — stage 1: **explant + antibiotic cement spacer + IV antibiotics** → stage 2: **ankle + hindfoot (TTC) arthrodesis with structural allograft**

**✗ Infected TAA traps** — revision TAA ± structural allograft (allograft remodels incompletely, fails under shear) · ankle arthrodesis in situ / with allograft (**talar bone loss → poor talar purchase**)

**Failed TAA salvage** — **conversion to fusion** or **transtibial amputation**

**Draining wound over old hardware + post-traumatic OA** — **removal of hardware, I&D, staged arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: keep hardware · simultaneous arthrodesis · simultaneous arthroplasty

**Infected post-traumatic OA salvage** — arthrodesis or amputation · aggressive debridement + antibiotics before fusion

### Footwear after fusion – rocker sole

**Best gait aid after ankle fusion** — **rocker sole (heel-to-toe / rocker-bottom)** → ↓ heel-strike pressure, ↑ toe-off propulsion, "roll off" in late stance, dissipates force across fusion

| ✗ Option | Actual use |
|---|---|
| **SACH heel** | Mimics shock absorption of heel strike; less beneficial than rocker |
| **Extended steel shank** | Stiffens sole; worse unless combined with rocker |
| **Total contact insert** | Cushion / offload, correct flexible deformity |
| **Lateral sole flare** | Varus deformity / lateral ankle instability |
| **Morton's extension** (± carbon fibre) | Forefoot pain |
| **Medial heel wedge** | Not for well-aligned fusion |
| **AFO, Arizona, double-upright drop-lock** | Immobilise an already fused ankle |
| **Pantalar arthrodesis** | Unnecessary if hindfoot + transverse tarsal motion painless |

### Malunited triple arthrodesis – osteotomy

**Valgus–supination triple malunion** — residual subtalar valgus → **lateral subfibular impingement** + forefoot abduction / midfoot supination via TN joint

**Treatment** — **medial displacement calcaneal osteotomy + transverse tarsal (derotational) osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy + lateral ligament reconstruction · tendon transfer + lateral column lengthening + heel cord lengthening (PTTD flatfoot) · ankle arthrodesis with IM nail · TAA + deltoid reefing (ankle normal, patient young)

## 6 Complications

### Complications – adjacent arthritis, nonunion, stress fracture & nerve injury

| Complication | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | **Commonest complication** (OB) · **10%** (Orthobullets) · **0–47%** across foot and ankle fusions (OB) · **smokers 2.7× risk** |
| **Persistent nonunion after revision** | **Neuropathy = greatest risk factor** (✗ Wrong options: diabetes · prior femoral nonunion · post-traumatic arthritis · initial fixation method) |
| **Subtalar (adjacent hindfoot) arthritis** | **Commonest long-term complication once united**; nonphysiologic subtalar motion · **common complication (TO)** |
| **Lateral plantar nerve** | Hindfoot nail insertion at heel → **numb plantar-lateral foot + 4th / 5th toes** (✗ Wrong options: medial plantar · sural · SPN · DPN) |
| **Superficial peroneal nerve** | **Transfibular approach** |
| **Tibial stress fracture** | **Unique to IM nail** — **proximal interlocking screw = stress riser** |

**Subtalar arthritis (TO)** — **common complication** after ankle arthrodesis (TO)

**Most common complications (Orthobullets)** — **subtalar arthritis + nonunion**

**Not ↑ after fusion** — talar AVN (rare) · ipsilateral knee arthritis · contralateral ankle arthritis

**Adjacent joints** — hindfoot, midfoot, forefoot **compensate with ↑ motion** → accelerated arthrosis · TAA may protect

![Lateral plantar nerve near the plantar incision for hindfoot nail](../../MCQ/attachments/q2904-illa-hindfoot-nail-illustration.jpg)

**Nail stress fracture prevention** — **remove proximal screws after union** · **short rod avoiding diaphysis**\
✗ Wrong options: late rotatory deformity (controlled by distal transfixion screws) · talar osteonecrosis (minimal dissection) · hardware failure (any construct)

![Tibial stress fracture at the proximal interlocking screw](../../MCQ/attachments/q6709-a-011a.jpg)

**Failed TTC (RA, after failed TAA), unstable pseudoarthrosis, no infection** — **revision TTC arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: conversion to TAA (subtalar arthrosis, poor talar stock) · supplemental screws alone · **talar resection** (keep talus for vascularity + local graft; femoral head allograft if insufficient) · total contact casting (neuropathic ulcers)

## X vs Y

| | **Tibiotalar** | **TTC** | **Subtalar** | **Triple** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Joints | Ankle | Ankle + subtalar | Subtalar | TN + CC + ST |
| Indication | Ankle OA, **hindfoot spared** | **Ankle + subtalar** OA, **global talar AVN**, Charcot, bone loss | Isolated subtalar OA (cavus, chronic planus) | **Fixed adult flatfoot** / TN + CC + ST arthritis |
| Fixation | **Screws**, plate, ex-fix | **Retrograde IM nail**, plate, ex-fix | Screws | — |
| Approach | Anterior / transfibular | **Lateral transfibular** | — | — |

| | **Primary fusion failure** | **Long-term after union** |
|---|---|---|
| Event | **Nonunion** | **Subtalar arthritis** |
| Risk factor | **Smoking 2.7×**, **neuropathy** (revision) | Nonphysiologic subtalar motion |

## How it's asked

**"Recommended ankle fusion position?"** → **neutral DF / PF, 10° ER, 5° valgus** (TO)

**"Ankle arthrodesis — ALL true EXCEPT?"** → **slight anterior translation of talus** (TO)

**"Entry point for retrograde TTC nail?"** → **bimalleolar bisector × line from 2nd toe tip to heel centre** (TO)

**"Optimal fusion position?"** → **0 degrees dorsiflexion / plantarflexion, 0–5 degree hindfoot valgus, 5–10 degree external rotation**

**"Optimal fusion position — talus?"** → **plantigrade, 10° ER, 5° valgus, posterior positioning of talus**

**"Varus post-traumatic ankle OA, mild subtalar change — procedure?"** → **isolated tibiotalar fusion with slight posterior translation of talus**

**"Post-ORIF tibiotalar arthritis — highest success?"** → **tibiotalar fusion**

**"Cavus, sinus tarsi pain, rigid hindfoot, ankle painless?"** → **subtalar arthrodesis**

**"Talar neck nonunion with AVN and talar head collapse?"** → **TTC arthrodesis with femoral head allograft**

**"Young construction worker, post-traumatic ankle OA, hindfoot normal on CT?"** → **tibiotalar arthrodesis with screws**

**"Prior septic ankle, sterile aspirate, talar AVN?"** → **ankle arthrodesis**

**"Broad-based talar AVN after subtalar dislocation?"** → **TTC arthrodesis using lateral transfibular approach**

**"Diabetic with neuropathy, end-stage ankle OA?"** → **tibiotalar arthrodesis**

**"Single joint fusion → greatest loss of hindfoot motion?"** → **talonavicular**

**"Fusion causing > 90% ↓ hindfoot motion?"** → **talonavicular joint**

**"Numb lateral sole + 4th / 5th toes after hindfoot nail?"** → **lateral plantar nerve**

**"RA, failed TTC after failed TAA, nonunion — next?"** → **revision TTC arthrodesis**

**"Complication unique to hindfoot IM nail?"** → **tibial stress fracture**

**"Greatest risk for persistent nonunion after revision?"** → **neuropathy**

**"United ankle fusion — long-term risk?"** → **subtalar arthritis**

**"Best footwear to restore gait after ankle fusion?"** → **rocker sole**

**"Pain-free fusion, hard walking, hindfoot motion painless?"** → **heel-to-toe rocker sole**

**"Altered gait after successful fusion — device?"** → **rocker-bottom sole**

**"Infected TAA with talar bone loss — second stage?"** → **ankle and hindfoot arthrodesis with structural allograft**

**"Draining post-traumatic ankle with hardware — plan?"** → **removal of hardware, I&D, staged arthrodesis**

**"Absolute contraindication to TTC nail?"** → **severe peripheral vascular disease**

**"Triple arthrodesis for coalition at 13, now lateral impingement?"** → **calcaneal osteotomy + transverse tarsal osteotomy**
