# Ankle Arthroscopy

*Foot & Ankle · 7 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Indications

**Indications** — **osteochondral lesions of talus** (microfracture of OCD) · post-traumatic synovitis debridement · **ATFL / AITFL anterolateral impingement** · **anterior tibiotalar spur resection (anterior bony impingement)** · **os trigonum excision** · loose bodies · cartilage debridement with ankle fusion

### Set-up

**Position** — **supine**, leg over padded bolster · tourniquet + exsanguinate

**Distension** — **external traction device** distracts tibiotalar joint (↓ cartilage injury, ↑ visualisation) · **20 ml normal saline** injected via marked AM portal site (TO)

**Entry** — **nick and spread** (skin nick, mosquito spreads subcutaneous tissue) → ↓ neurovascular injury

**Pre-tourniquet marking (TO)** — dorsalis pedis, DPN, tibialis anterior, great saphenous vein, **peroneus tertius**, malleoli, joint line

**Workhorse portals (TO)** — **anterolateral, anteromedial, posterolateral**

## 5 Management

### Portals – location & structures at risk

| Portal | Location | Role | At risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Anteromedial (AM)** | **Medial to tibialis anterior**, lateral to medial malleolus, at joint line · between TA + saphenous vein | **Primary viewing portal, established first** · 2.7 mm 30° scope (TO) | **Saphenous nerve + great saphenous vein** (mean safe distance **7.4 mm / 9 mm**) · **tibialis anterior tendon** |
| **Anterolateral (AL)** | **Just lateral to peroneus tertius** (**\~4 mm lateral**, OB) + SPN, medial to lateral malleolus · made under vision with 25G needle | Viewing / **working portal** (TO) · lateral gutter | **SPN — dorsal intermediate cutaneous branch** (**commonest**) |
| **Anterocentral** | **Medial to EDL, lateral to EHL** · through extensor tendon if needed (TO) | Rarely used | **Dorsalis pedis artery + DPN** (anterior tibial NV bundle) |
| **Medial midline** | **Between EHL and tibialis anterior** | — | **Deep + superficial peroneal nn.** (well lateral to saphenous) |
| **Posterolateral (PL)** | **Just lateral to Achilles**, medial to peroneals · **2 cm proximal to LM tip** (Orthobullets) · **at level of line from LM tip parallel to sole** (TO) · prone / semi-decubitus | Posterior viewing (os trigonum) · **vision portal in hindfoot arthroscopy**, 4.0 mm 30° scope (TO) | **Sural nerve + short (small) saphenous vein** |
| **Posteromedial (PM)** | **Just medial to Achilles** | Posterior working (os trigonum) | **Posterior tibial artery** (+ tibial nerve, OB) |
| Trans-Achilles | — | **Abandoned** (TO) | Achilles injury / rupture |

![Anterior ankle arthroscopy portals and structures at risk](../../MCQ/attachments/q5953-illa-anteriorportals.jpg)

![Posterior portals — posterolateral (sural, small saphenous) and posteromedial (posterior tibial bundle)](../../MCQ/attachments/q5953-illb-posteriorportals.jpg)

### Anterior portals – superficial peroneal nerve injury

**SPN** — **most commonly injured nerve** in ankle arthroscopy · **anterolateral portal** · variable anatomy · branch of **common peroneal**, runs in **lateral compartment** → **motor to peroneus longus + brevis** · crosses fibula (lateral → anterior compartment) **\~11 cm (3–15 cm) proximal to LM tip** (OB) · fascial exit **10–12 cm** (OB) · **12–15 cm** (Orthobullets) — read the options · terminal **medial dorsal cutaneous (great toe)** + **intermediate dorsal cutaneous (dorsum of foot)**

**Protecting SPN** — **palpate**, **transilluminate** (shine scope light to see nerve under skin), blunt dissection, trace before incision · **4th toe flexion** used to protect nerve when making AL portal (TO; not 4th toe extension)

**Most commonly injured branch** — **dorsal intermediate cutaneous branch** (TO + Orthobullets)\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal medial cutaneous branch

**Dorsal foot numbness after scope** — SPN via **anterolateral portal**

**✗ SPN distractors** — between EHL + EDL = **DPN** (EDB/EHB motor, 1st web) · terminates as **lateral dorsal cutaneous n.** = **sural** · motor to abductor hallucis + FHB = **medial plantar** · terminal branch of femoral = **saphenous**

![Transillumination to identify the superficial peroneal nerve](../../MCQ/attachments/q8842-a-screen-shot-2017-03-28-at-5-22-32-pm.jpg)

### Anteromedial & posterior portals – saphenous & sural nerves

**Saphenous nerve** — travels along distal medial tibia + ankle · **most at risk with anteromedial portal**\
✗ Wrong options: medial midline (well lateral to it)

**Sural nerve** — **posterolateral portal** (os trigonum resection) · permanent numbness risk

### Posterior ankle impingement (os trigonum, Stieda process) (TO)

**PAIS** — mechanical pinching in **terminal plantar flexion** · chronic > acute · ballet (**en pointe**), football, soccer, downhill running · **acute plantar hyperflexion** or **chronic microtrauma** (TO)

**Most common cause (TO)** — **os trigonum**\
✗ Wrong options: Stieda process · sloping posterior tibial lip · capsular calcification

| Osseous causes (TO) | Soft-tissue causes (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Os trigonum** · **Stieda process** · downward-sloping posterior tibial lip · posterior tibial osteophyte · prominent posterior calcaneal process · OCL, loose bodies, chondromatosis, subtalar coalition | Thickened posterior capsule · post-traumatic scar · **post-traumatic capsular calcification** · loose bodies · **FHL tenosynovitis** (commonest tendon involved) · abnormal muscles |

**Os trigonum** — **non-fusion of posterolateral talar secondary ossification centre** · lies **lateral to FHL groove** · appears **7–13 yr** · forms **11–13 yr boys / 8–10 yr girls** · fuses within **1 yr** (→ trigonal / Stieda process) · often bilateral · pain from abutment or **synchondrosis disruption** (TO)

**Os trigonum prevalence** — **7–14%** remain separate (TO) · **7%** don't fuse (TO) · **present in 10–25%** (OB) — read the options

**Stieda process (TO)** — **elongated posterolateral talar process** → **causes posterior ankle impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: ossification at MCL insertion (Pellegrini-Stieda) · unfused ossicle (os trigonum) · elderly head injury

**Imaging (TO)** — lateral X-ray: os trigonum / Stieda (may be normal) · **MRI best: bone marrow oedema** in talus, calcaneus or os trigonum

**DDx (TO)** — Achilles tendinosis / tear, **FHL tenosynovitis**, posterior talar process fracture, Haglund's, OCL, retrocalcaneal bursitis

**Treatment** — nonop (NSAID, activity modification, PT) → **excision open lateral or posterior arthroscopy** via posterolateral portal (**sural at risk**) · stress fracture ± FHL tenosynovitis / entrapment

![Os trigonum on lateral radiograph](../../MCQ/attachments/q218122-illa-screenshot-2023-07-26-at-8-03-33-am.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Neurological (overall)** | **Commonest reported complication** (OB, Orthobullets, TO) · **neuropraxia** commonest · nerve injury **3.5–10%** · overall complications **6–20%** |
| **SPN** | **Commonest nerve** · AL portal · **commonest complication of anterior arthroscopy** (TO + OB) |
| **Cartilage injury** | Up to **30%** (TO) · **anterolateral (zone 3) + central (zone 5) talus** · ↓ by distraction + correct portals · called **commonest complication of ankle arthroscopy** in one TO key |
| **Synovial cutaneous fistula** | "Somewhat common" · prevent by **immobilisation** until portals heal |
| Vascular | Rare · anterocentral portal · **anterior tibial artery aneurysm** (case reports) |
| Tendon / ligament | **EDC, EHL rupture** · ATFL + deltoid injured by accessory portals |
| **Posterior arthroscopy** | **Tibial + sural** = commonest nerve injuries · **FHL injury** · Achilles tightness |
| Other | DVT, infection (**very infrequent**), CRPS |

**Commonest complication — source conflict** — **SPN / neurological injury** (TO17136845, OB) · **cartilage injury** (TO17136847 explanation) — read the options; if SPN is an option it is the keyed answer

### Painful portal neuroma

**Burning at lateral portal, Tinel → medial foot / ankle, injection helped 2 wk** — **SPN medial branch entrapment** → **neuroplasty** (nerve intact) or **excision + burial** (cut / thinned)\
✗ Wrong options: sural neuroplasty (posterolateral portal) · DPN · repeat scope for OCD · anaesthetic patch

## X vs Y

| | **Os trigonum** | **Stieda process** |
|---|---|---|
| What | **Separate ossicle** (unfused ossification centre) | **Elongated posterolateral talar process** (fused) |
| Impingement | **Most common cause** of PAIS | Also causes PAIS |

| | **Anterior arthroscopy** | **Posterior arthroscopy** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | Supine | Prone / semi-decubitus |
| Commonest nerve | **SPN** | **Tibial + sural** |
| Other | Cartilage, fistula | FHL injury, Achilles |

## How it's asked

**"Most common complication of anterior ankle arthroscopy?"** → **superficial peroneal nerve injury** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: cartilage · EDC · fistula

**"True regarding ankle arthroscopy?"** → **4th toe flexion is used to protect the nerve (CPN/SPN) while making the anterolateral portal** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 4th toe extension · dorsal medial cutaneous branch · "commonest complication = CPN neurapraxia"

**"Most common complication with standard lateral portal?"** → **injury to the superficial peroneal nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: tibial · DPA · RSD · deep infection

**"Portal putting SPN at greatest risk?"** → **anterolateral portal**

**"Dorsal foot numbness after scope — portal?"** → **anterolateral**

**"Tendon closest to AL portal?"** → **peroneus tertius**

**"Transillumination protects?"** → **superficial peroneal nerve**

**"Most commonly injured nerve — characteristic?"** → **motor branches to peroneus longus and brevis** (SPN)

**"Lateral portal neuroma, Tinel to medial foot?"** → **neuroplasty or excision + burial of medial branch of SPN**

**"Saphenous nerve most at risk with?"** → **anteromedial portal**

**"Portal 2 (anteromedial) — at risk?"** → **saphenous nerve**

**"Os trigonum resection — portal riskiest for sural?"** → **posterolateral portal**

**"Most common cause of posterior ankle impingement?"** → **os trigonum** (TO)

**"True about Stieda's process?"** → **causes posterior ankle impingement** (TO)

**"Anterocentral portal at risk?"** → **dorsalis pedis artery (+ DPN)**

**"Posteromedial portal at risk?"** → **posterior tibial artery**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ankle Ligaments

*Foot & Ankle · 8 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Osteology & biomechanics

**Ankle joint** — tibial plafond + medial malleolus + lateral malleolus + talus · main motion **plantar / dorsiflexion** · secondary inversion / eversion, rotation

**Distal tibiofibular joint** — distal fibula in **incisura fibularis** (concave distal lateral tibia) · fibula rotates in incisura during gait · **mortise widens from plantar → dorsiflexion** · **syndesmosis screws limit external rotation**

**Joint reaction force** — **5× body weight** walking on level ground

### Ligament overview

| Group | Ligaments |
|---|---|
| **Medial** | **Deltoid** · **calcaneonavicular (spring)** |
| **Lateral** | **Syndesmosis** (AITFL, PITFL, TTFL, IOL, ITL) · **ATFL** · **PTFL** · **CFL** · **LTCL** |

### Lateral ligaments

| | **ATFL** | **CFL** | **PTFL** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Function | **Primary restraint to inversion in plantar flexion** · resists **anterolateral talar translation** | **Primary restraint to inversion in neutral / dorsiflexion** · restrains subtalar inversion → limits talar tilt | Supplementary when ATFL + CFL intact · max strain in **dorsiflexion** · limits **posterior talar displacement + ER** |
| Strength | **Weakest** lateral | — | **Strongest** lateral |
| Origin | Anteroinferior fibula, **10 mm proximal to tip** | Anterior border fibula, **9 mm proximal to tip** | Posterior border fibula |
| Insertion | **Talar neck**, just distal to cartilage (**18 mm distal to joint line**) | Calcaneus **13 mm distal to subtalar joint**, **deep to peroneal sheaths** | **Posterolateral tubercle of talus** |
| Orientation | **45–90°** to tibial axis (Orthobullets) · **perpendicular in neutral → parallel in plantar flexion** (OB) | **Parallel to tibia in neutral** | **Perpendicular** to tibial axis |
| Injured by | **Inversion in plantar flexion** → commonest, **low ankle sprain** | **Inversion with foot in neutral** | **Complete talar dislocation** / severe sprains with other two torn |
| Exam | **Anterior drawer in 20° plantar flexion** (Orthobullets; **10° PF** = greatest translation, OB) vs other side · **> 8 mm forward shift** on lateral X-ray = ATFL tear | **Inversion (supination) test** in slight dorsiflexion · **talar tilt** (ATFL + CFL) | No specific test |

**PTFL if ATFL + CFL incompetent** — short fibres restrict IR + ER, talar tilt, dorsiflexion · long fibres only ER, talar tilt, dorsiflexion

**LTCL** — **lateral process of talus → lateral calcaneus** · **anterior + medial to CFL** · stabilises talocalcaneal joint · injured **with ATFL** · no specific test

![Lateral ankle ligaments — ATFL, CFL, PTFL](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7005-lateral-ankle-ligaments.jpg)

### Syndesmosis

**Function** — **integrity of ankle mortise**

**Components** — **AITFL** · **PITFL** (deep part = **inferior transverse ligament**) · **TTFL** · **interosseous ligament (IOL)**

**Clinical** — **high ankle sprain / syndesmosis injury**, ankle fracture · AITFL + PITFL injured by **eversion + external rotation**

**Tests** — **external rotation test** · **squeeze test**

### Deltoid ligament

**Function** — **primary restraint to valgus talar tilt** · both layers resist hindfoot eversion · stabilises against **plantar flexion, ER, pronation**

| | **Superficial** | **Deep** |
|---|---|---|
| Crosses | **Ankle + subtalar** | **Ankle only** |
| Origin | **Anterior colliculus** | **Inferior + posterior medial malleolus** |
| Insertion | Navicular, talar neck, **sustentaculum tali**, posteromedial talar tubercle | Medial + posteromedial talus |
| Key | **Tibiocalcaneal (sustentacular) = strongest superficial** · **deep posterior tibiotalar = strongest overall** (TO), resists calcaneal eversion | **Primary medial stabiliser** · prevents **lateral displacement + ER of talus** |
| Test | **Eversion test** in neutral | **External rotation stress test** (+ syndesmosis) |

**Injury** — **pronation (eversion)** → forced ER + abduction · ± medial malleolus fracture · ankle fracture, high ankle sprain

### Spring ligament anatomy & flatfoot

**Spring (calcaneonavicular) ligament** — connects **calcaneus and navicular** · **sustentaculum tali → inferior navicular**\
✗ Wrong options: talus–navicular · calcaneus–cuboid · talus–calcaneus

**Components** — **superomedial** + **inferior** calcaneonavicular · **superomedial = largest + most important stabiliser of medial longitudinal arch**\
✗ Wrong options: inferior

**Superomedial attachments** — **anteromedial sustentaculum tali → broadly along medial navicular**\
✗ Wrong options: to medial cuneiform · from talus

**Function** — **static** stabiliser of **medial longitudinal arch + talar head** · arch = dynamic **PTT** + static ligaments / capsule + tarsal interlock · closely related to **deltoid**

**Untreated tear** — **flatfoot deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: syndesmotic widening · TMT widening · hallux valgus · talar tilt

**PTTD** — spring ligament **most likely attenuated in type II flatfoot** · significant disruption → **reconstruction**

**Acute spring tear** — forceful landing on flat foot · **tibialis posterior usually normal**

![Spring ligament MRI — disrupted (A) vs intact (B)](../../MCQ/attachments/q419-illa-spring-ligament.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Inversion injury – ATFL

| Mechanism | Injury |
|---|---|
| **Inversion in ~20° plantar flexion** | **ATFL** (commonest) |
| Inversion in neutral | **CFL** |
| Severe inversion (others torn) | PTFL |
| **Eversion + external rotation** | **AITFL / PITFL (syndesmosis)** |
| **Eversion** | **Anterior colliculus fracture** (deltoid avulsion) |

### Eversion injury – anterior colliculus

**Eversion** — **anterior colliculus fracture** = bony **deltoid** avulsion from medial malleolus

**✗ Wrong options (all inversion-associated)** — **osteochondral talar dome lesions** · **peroneal tendon splits** · **anterolateral soft-tissue (meniscoid) impingement** · **SPN stretch** (paraesthesia) · ATFL + CFL tears

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **AP + mortise** | **Tibiofibular clear space < 5 mm** · **tibiofibular overlap > 10 mm (AP)** (Orthobullets) · clear space **< 6 mm**, overlap **> 6 mm AP / > 1 mm mortise** (OB) — read the options |
| **Weight-bearing mortise** | **Most accurate radiograph** for syndesmosis |
| **CT** | **Most accurate** syndesmosis assessment; no validated normals → **compare uninjured side** |
| **Anterior drawer stress X-ray** | **> 8 mm** = ATFL tear · stress views more accurate in **chronic** injury |
| **Talar tilt X-ray** | Plafond–talar dome angle **< 5° normal** · combined **ATFL + CFL** |
| **Ankle arthrogram** | **CFL rupture → dye leaks into peroneal tendon sheath** |
| **US** | ATFL — **more accurate than radiographs** |
| **MRI** | ATFL, CFL, spring ligament tear · PTFL rarely indicated |
| **Mortise / gravity stress** | **Medial clear space widening** → deltoid injury |
| Arthroscopy | Confirms ATFL tear |

![Tibiofibular clear space < 5 mm and overlap > 10 mm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7005-tibfib-overlap.jpg)

### Syndesmotic ligament avulsion fragments (AITFL)

**Tillaux fracture** — **AITFL (anterior tibiofibular ligament)** avulses **anterolateral distal tibial epiphysis** · transitional fracture, **anterolateral physis closes last**\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL · CFL · EDB · EHB

**Fibular avulsion into syndesmotic space (CT)** — **AITFL** insertion → **syndesmosis injury** · repair fragment ± **syndesmotic screw / suture button** in most\
✗ Wrong options: excise fragment (incompetent syndesmosis) · Broström / allograft (ATFL / CFL) · deltoid (medial) · SPR (lateral fibular wall avulsion, peroneal subluxation)

## 5 Management

### Partial tear – physical therapy

**Isolated partial ATFL tear (grade I)** — stable · **RICE, early protected WB → physical therapy** (peroneal strengthening + proprioception) · brace for sport

**✗ ATFL partial-tear traps** — **cast + NWB 2–4 / 6–8 wk** (WB recommended) · **early open repair** (only for fracture / intra-articular lesion) · **early arthroscopic repair** (scope only for AITFL impingement / posteromedial impingement)

### Acute ankle sprain – taping (TO)

| Taping | Use (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Closed basket weave** | **Acute ankle injury, maximum support** ✓ (TO) · anchors (below calf + behind MT heads) → **stirrups medial → lateral** + **horseshoes behind heel** woven → **C-strips** → **heel locks** |
| **Open basket weave** | Acute sprain with **massive swelling** (TO) · **no overlap in front** → allows swelling + movement · heel locks with **elastic** tape · compression, ↓ inflammation |
| **Low dye** | **Zinc oxide** tape for **midfoot, plantar fascia, heel pain** (TO) · **not for ankle sprain** |
| Medial patellar | Knee (not ankle) |

![Open vs closed basket weave taping](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356178-2.png)

## X vs Y

| | **ATFL** | **CFL** |
|---|---|---|
| Restrains inversion in | **Plantar flexion** | **Neutral / dorsiflexion** |
| Test | **Anterior drawer** (20° PF) | **Inversion test**, talar tilt |
| Orientation in neutral | Perpendicular (OB) | Parallel to tibia |

| | **Superficial deltoid** | **Deep deltoid** |
|---|---|---|
| Joints crossed | Ankle + subtalar | Ankle only |
| Test | Eversion | External rotation stress |

| | **Superomedial CN** | **Inferior CN** |
|---|---|---|
| Size | **Largest** | Smaller |
| Arch support | **Most important** | Less |

## How it's asked

**"Inversion with foot plantar flexed 20° — ligament?"** → **anterior talofibular**

**"Eversion mechanism associated with?"** → **anterior colliculus fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal splits · OCL · SPN palsy · anterolateral impingement

**"Isolated partial ATFL tear — treatment?"** → **physical therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: cast NWB · open / arthroscopic repair

**"Tillaux fragment — attached structure?"** → **anterior tibiofibular ligament**

**"Fibular avulsion into syndesmosis on CT — structure?"** → **anterior inferior tibiofibular ligament (AITFL)**

**"Spring ligament connects?"** → **calcaneus and navicular**

**"Spring ligament complex — true?"** → **superomedial ligament is the most important stabilizer of the longitudinal arch**

**"Untreated spring ligament tear on MRI leads to?"** → **flatfoot deformity**

**"Taping method in runner with acute ankle injury?"** → **closed basket weave taping** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: open (massive swelling) · low dye (plantar fascia)

**"Most accurate radiograph for syndesmosis?"** → **weight-bearing mortise view**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Ankle Sprain

*Foot & Ankle · 9 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest reason** — **missed athletic participation**

**Dancers** — ankle sprain = **commonest injury** (Orthobullets) · inversion sprain = **commonest acute skeletal injury in dancers** (OB)

**Low vs high** — **low (ATFL ± CFL) > 90%** · **high (syndesmosis) 1–10%**

**Risk factors** — patient: **limited dorsiflexion, ↓ proprioception, poor balance** · environment: **indoor-court sports (basketball, volleyball)** highest

### Anatomy & examination – ATFL vs CFL

| Ligament | Attachments | Tight in / injured by | Test | Frequency · strength |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ATFL** | **Anterior fibula → lateral talar neck** | **Plantar flexion + inversion** (parallel to foot axis in PF) | **Anterior drawer in plantar flexion** | **Commonest injured · weakest** |
| **CFL** | **Fibular tip → lateral calcaneal tubercle** | **Dorsiflexion + inversion** | **Drawer laxity in dorsiflexion · talar tilt (varus stress)** | **2nd commonest** |
| **PTFL** | **Distal fibula → lateral tubercle of posterior talus** | Rarely | Not tested by anterior drawer | **Least injured · strongest + deepest of lateral complex** (OB, TO) |

**Strength order** — **ATFL (weakest) < CFL < PTFL (strongest)**

**CFL crosses 2 joints** — contributes to **subtalar + ankle** stability → subtalar instability hard to tell from posterior ankle instability\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL

![Lateral ankle ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7028-ankle-ligaments.jpg)

### Deltoid ligament (TO)

| Layer | Bands | Crosses | Limits |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Superficial (4)** | **Tibiospring** + **tibionavicular** (major) · superficial posterior tibiotalar + tibiocalcaneal (additional) | **Ankle + subtalar** | **Talar abduction** |
| **Deep (2)** | **Deep posterior tibiotalar (major)** · anterior deep tibiotalar | **Ankle only** | **External rotation** |

**Strongest tibiotalar / deltoid part (TO)** — **deep posterior tibiotalar ligament** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior deep tibiotalar · "posterior tibiotalar" · talonavicular

**Pronation of talus** — superficial + deep **equally** limit it (OB)

**Function** — fan-shaped; resists **valgus + rotatory** forces · isolated deltoid injury **very rare**

### Syndesmosis anatomy (TO)

| Ligament | Course | Restrains |
|---|---|---|
| **AITFL** | **Chaput tubercle (anterolateral tibia) → Wagstaffe tubercle (anterior fibula)** | **Fibular external rotation** |
| **IOL** | Distal continuation of **interosseous membrane** | **Lateral fibular translation** |
| **PITFL** | **Volkmann tubercle (posterior tibia) → posterolateral fibula** | **Posterior fibular translation** · **strongest component** |
| **Inferior transverse ligament** | Distal to PITFL | **Posterior talar displacement** |

**PITFL > AITFL** — **PITFL stronger** (TO) (true statement in "false EXCEPT")

![Syndesmosis – AITFL, PITFL, transverse ligament, IOM](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141970-2.png)

### Peroneal weakness in dancers

**Relative peroneal weakness** → **inversion ankle sprain** · **restore full peroneal strength** to prevent recurrence\
✗ Wrong options: fibular fracture · cuboid subluxation · Achilles rupture · midfoot sprain

## 2 Mechanism

**Low sprain** — **inversion on a plantarflexed foot** · recurrent sprains → functional instability

**Sequence in inversion (TO)** — **anterolateral capsule → ATFL → CFL → PTFL** (TO) · ATFL = first or only ligament; total rupture adds CFL + PTFL (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL first then capsule

**Eversion (TO)** — **deltoid** injury

**Syndesmosis mechanism** — **hyperdorsiflexion** (TO) · **external rotation**, pain on forced **dorsiflexion + ER** (TO) · **pronation–ER > pronation–abduction > infrequently supination–ER** (Weber C / B) (TO) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: eversion + PF · inversion · hyperplantar flexion

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain on WB, swelling, ecchymosis · recurrent instability, **catching / popping** after recurrent sprains · "giving way"

**Anterior drawer** — talus translates anterior to tibia · seated, knee flexed over table edge, stabilise leg, pull heel forward · **10° PF** = greatest translation (OB; 20° PF in another OB stem) · **8–10 mm** = positive · **PF → ATFL**, **DF → CFL** · reflects ATFL ± CFL · limited use acutely

**Talar tilt** — tibia stabilised, ankle neutral, invert · **> 15° vs contralateral = ATFL + CFL** · **isolated ATFL cannot give + talar tilt** → CFL must be torn\
✗ Wrong options: PTFL · AITFL / PITFL

**Syndesmosis tests (TO)** — **squeeze** (compress tibia + fibula mid-calf → syndesmotic pain) · **fibular translation** (fibula forward/back) · **Cotton** (talus side-to-side, neutral) · **ER stress** (ER + DF, hip + knee 90°) · gross swelling **usually absent**; tenderness at anterior syndesmosis ± deltoid

**Cotton / hook test intra-op (TO)** — translation considered positive asked (0–1 / 1–2 / 2–3 / 3–4 mm) — **answer key not provided by the source** (TO)

![Talar tilt (varus stress) view – lateral ligament laxity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7028-lateral-ankle-stress-view-mod.jpg)

### Associated injuries – talar bone bruise & deltoid

| Associated finding (acute lateral sprain MRI) | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Talar bone bruise** | **Commonest** |
| Talar + medial malleolar bruises | 8.1% |
| Peroneus brevis injury | 7.5% |
| Lateral malleolus tip avulsion | 2.7% |

**Other associations (Orthobullets)** — **OCD talus**, **peroneal tendon injuries**, **subtle cavovarus**, **anterior / anterolateral synovitis (commonest intra-articular)**, anterior impingement, deltoid injury, **CRPS**, fractures (**5th MT base, anterior process calcaneus, lateral / posterior process talus**)

**Medial pain, ER hurts medially, squeeze −, ER stress X-ray normal** — **deltoid ligament complex**\
✗ Wrong options: inferior transverse ligament · AITFL (squeeze/X-ray normal) · ATFL (usually recovers nonop) · spring ligament (would give flatfoot)

**Deltoid injury** — nonop first; **reconstruct if instability persists**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – Ottawa ankle rules

| X-ray series | Pain zone | Any one of |
|---|---|---|
| **Ankle** | Malleolar zone | **Bone tenderness posterior edge / tip of lateral malleolus (distal 6 cm)** · **same for medial malleolus** · **inability to bear weight immediately + in ED** |
| **Foot** | Midfoot zone | **Bone tenderness 5th MT base** · **navicular** · inability to bear weight |

**Sensitivity** — **96–99%** for ruling out fracture

**Not Ottawa criteria** — **swelling**, **ecchymosis** (medial or lateral), **antalgic gait / walking 10 ft** (only inability to bear weight counts), pain with hindfoot motion, **1st MT base tenderness** (peroneus longus), **deltoid / soft-tissue tenderness**

![Ottawa ankle rules](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7028-ottawa-ankle-rules.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **WB AP, lateral, mortise** | Lateral: **anterior talar translation = ATFL** |
| **ER stress view** | Syndesmosis: asymmetric mortise widening · **MCS > 4 mm** · **TF clear space 6 mm** |
| **Varus stress (talar tilt) view** | **CFL** · measures talar tilt |
| **WB stress X-ray (TO)** | Suggests instability in obscure syndesmotic cases; **guides management** |
| **MRI** | If pain **> 6–8 wk** · **peroneal tendons, OCD, syndesmosis** · not needed acutely (✗ Wrong options: intra-articular contrast) |
| **MRI syndesmosis (TO)** | **Gold standard** · **lambda sign = > 2 mm diastasis on coronal** (sensitive + specific) (TO) · also **hyperintense signal contiguous from mortise into tibiofibular joint** (OB) |
| Peroneal tendon pathology | **MRI** (✗ Wrong options: CT) |

### Syndesmotic radiographic parameters (TO)

| Parameter | Where | Normal | Injury |
|---|---|---|---|
| **TF clear space** | **1 cm above plafond**; medial fibula → lateral posterior tibial prominence (incisura) | **< 6 mm** (AP + mortise) · **< 5 mm** (Orthobullets ligament note) | **↑ > 6 mm** |
| **TF overlap** | 1 cm above plafond | **> 6 mm on AP** · **> 10 mm AP** (Orthobullets ligament note) | **↓** |
| **Medial clear space** | Mortise | **< 4 mm** | ↑ = lateral talar shift |
| Superior joint space | Mortise | Within 2 mm medially of lateral width | — |
| **Dime (ball) sign** | Fibular tip recess → lateral talar process | **Unbroken curve** | **Broken** |
| Shenton's line of ankle | Mortise | Intact | Broken |

**Best view for syndesmosis (TO)** — **AP** (mortise better for joint congruity) · **"TF overlap < 6 mm on mortise = unstable" is FALSE** (TO)

**Medial clear space** — **> 4 mm** abnormal (Orthobullets; TO normal < 4 mm) · **> 5 mm = deltoid tear → unstable syndesmosis** (TO) — read the options

![TF clear space measured 1 cm above the plafond](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17141970-3.jpg)

### Low ankle sprain grading

| Grade | Ligament | Ecchymosis / swelling | Pain on WB | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **No tear** | Minimal | Normal | **Functional rehab** |
| **II** | **Stretch without tear** | Moderate | Mild | **Functional rehab** |
| **III** | **Complete tear (ATFL + CFL)** | Severe | Severe | **Functional rehab** (± 10 d cast NWB, ± semirigid brace) |

### Syndesmotic injury classification (TO)

| By time | By stability |
|---|---|
| **Acute < 6 wk** | **Stable** — AITFL ± IOL tear, **deltoid intact** → nonop |
| **Subacute > 6 wk** | **Unstable** — **AITFL + IOL + deltoid** (MCS > 5 mm) → surgery |
| **Chronic > 6 months** | — |

## 5 Management

```
Acute low ankle sprain (grade I–III)
├─ Ottawa + → X-ray
├─ All grades → RICE → early functional rehab (brace, proprioception, peroneals)
│      (± ~1 wk WB boot; grade III ± 10 d cast)
└─ Pain / swelling > 6–8 wk → MRI (OCD, peroneals, syndesmosis)
```

```
Chronic lateral instability
├─ Untreated / under-treated → proprioception + peroneal strengthening first
├─ Failed nonop, normal alignment → modified Broström-Gould ± arthroscopy
├─ + cavovarus, Coleman corrects → 1st MT dorsiflexion osteotomy + reconstruction
└─ + fixed hindfoot varus → lateralising calcaneal osteotomy + reconstruction
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Grade I–III, persistent pain + instability despite extensive nonop** | **Anatomic reconstruction (Broström-Gould)** or tendon transfer / tenodesis |
| **Grade I–III with bony avulsion** | Reconstruction |
| **Open ankle dislocation, gross lateral ligament disruption** | **Acute repair** (OB) |
| Recurrent sprains + **impingement lesion** (AITFL, posteromedial) | **Arthroscopic debridement** |
| **Syndesmosis with tibiofibular diastasis** | Fixation |
| **Peroneal tear > 50–60%**, irreparable, severe tendinopathy | **Peroneus brevis tenodesis** |

### Acute management – functional rehabilitation & proprioception

**Protocol** — **RICE + elastic wrap** → short (**≈ 1 wk / 7–10 d**) WB immobilisation (boot, Aircast, walking cast) if needed → **early functional rehab**: ROM → strengthening → **proprioception** (tilt-board) → sport-specific · **functional brace** (controls inversion + eversion) during strengthening + prophylactically in high-risk activity

**Quickest return to activity** — **early functional rehabilitation** · supervised PT helps early, no long-term difference · chronic instability **cannot be predicted** by history, exam or X-ray

**Recurrence reduction** — **functional bracing + early proprioceptive training** (✗ Wrong options: NWB cast · WB boot · splint · **neuromuscular training alone** (higher recurrence)) · pathway to retrain = **proprioception** (✗ Wrong options: tactile · pressure · pain · motor)

**Proprioception roles (TO)** — EXCEPT stem: promotes fracture healing / maintains joint stability / prevents reinjury / improves neuromuscular control + balance — **answer key not provided by the source** (TO)

| | **Functional management** | **Cast immobilisation** |
|---|---|---|
| Return to sport | **Faster, more likely long-term** | **Longer time to RTS** |
| ROM | **Greater** | Less |
| Swelling | **Less** (short term) | More |
| Laxity on stress X-ray | **Less** | **More** |
| Satisfaction | **Higher** | Lower |

**FALSE about functional treatment** — "**greater risk of ankle laxity**" (it has **less**)

**Grade III (ATFL + CFL torn on MRI), no syndesmosis / avulsion** — **RICE, elastic wrap, then therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: 6 wk cast · suture button · Broström-Gould · tenodesis

**Acute ATFL tear on MRI, laxity PF + DF** — **early functional rehab, proprioception**\
✗ Wrong options: Broström · Broström-Gould · Watson-Jones (weaved PB) · Evans (non-weaved PB)

**Basketball rebound, ATFL on MRI** — **early WB, functional bracing, peroneal strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: sinus tarsi steroid + taping · suture-tape repair · peroneal autograft reconstruction · boot 4–6 wk (deconditioning + recurrence)

**After 7–10 d splint, + talar tilt + DF drawer** — **early functional rehabilitation**\
✗ Wrong options: CT / MRI acutely · 2–4 wk more NWB cast · rigid walking boot; semirigid brace OK for grade III

**Lateral sprain, 2 mm drawer, squeeze −, X-ray normal** — **functional bracing + PT**\
✗ Wrong options: short leg cast 4 wk · MRI · Broström-Gould

**Chronic untreated sprain (5 mo), stable drawer** — **proprioception + peroneal strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: NSAIDs alone · observation · boot · surgery before full nonop

**PT success** — **> 80%** (OB)

### Chronic instability – Broström-Gould, reconstruction & rehabilitation

**Chronic instability** — **> 6 wk** · repeated episodes "giving way"

**Modified Broström-Gould** — **anatomic shortening + reinsertion (imbrication) of ATFL + CFL** · reinforced with **inferior extensor retinaculum + distal fibular periosteum** · **90% good–excellent** · commonest procedure; preferred in athletes with normal alignment\
✗ Wrong options: Evans · allograft fibula → 5th MT · added osteotomy if aligned

**Gould modification facts** — **no biomechanical difference in ankle stability**\
✗ Wrong options: superior extensor retinaculum · adds PTFL · ↑ nerve injury · ↓ ROM at 6 wk

**Primary repair works even when chronic** — ligaments stay attached to capsule · **graft / internal brace = for failed primary repair** · no technique proven superior

**Suture-tape augmentation** — ↑ tensile strength, no better clinical outcome · **over-tightening → ↓ dorsiflexion**\
✗ Wrong options: inversion · eversion · PF · pronation

**Tenodesis (Watson-Jones, Chrisman-Snook, Colville, Evans)** — **nonanatomic** tendon transfer · **subtalar stiffness** · alters biomechanics → discouraged · **correct any malalignment** first

**Arthroscopy with Broström** — **high incidence of intra-articular pathology** (OCD talus, anterior impingement, synovitis) → **ankle arthroscopy** is the add-on\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar fusion · peroneal debridement if no eversion pain · 1st ray / Dwyer osteotomy if aligned

**Instability + anterior impingement** — **arthroscopic debridement + ATFL + CFL reinsertion with inferior extensor retinaculum + fibular periosteum**\
✗ Wrong options: PTFL · superior extensor retinaculum

| Post-op mobilisation | **Early (< 3 wk)** | **Delayed (> 3 wk)** |
|---|---|---|
| **Functional scores** | **Better** | Worse |
| Ankle laxity (drawer, tilt) | **↑** | Less |
| **Wound complications** | **↑** | Less |
| Return to sport | Same | Same |

### Failure factors – cavovarus / hindfoot alignment & its correction

**Commonest reason for failure** — **hindfoot malalignment (native hindfoot varus)** · normal = **5–7° valgus**, axis through medial calcaneus · varus loads lateral ligaments + peroneals

**Predispose to failure** — **hindfoot varus + 1st ray plantarflexion + cavovarus** = **all of the above**\
✗ Wrong options: planovalgus (strains medial side) · low demand · anatomic reconstruction · pre-op acute traumatic instability (all ↓ failure)

(✗ Wrong options: **Traps** — baseline laxity (Beighton)) · **excessively pronated** subtalar (varus foot **supinates**) · **fixed dorsiflexion** of 1st ray (it's **plantarflexion**) · planovalgus

**Subtle cavovarus** — **mild heel varus + plantarflexed 1st ray** → lateral column overload, **stress fractures**, **peroneal tendon + lateral ligament injury** · forefoot-driven (plantarflexed 1st MT) or hindfoot-driven (tarsal coalition)

**Orthotic for cavovarus** — **full-length semirigid**, **lateral hindfoot (+ forefoot) wedge / posting**, **1st MT head recess**, **lowered medial arch**\
✗ Wrong options: UCBL · medial hindfoot posting ± rigid arch (planovalgus) · lace-up brace alone

| Coleman block | Driver | Add to ligament reconstruction |
|---|---|---|
| **Corrects (flexible hindfoot)** | **Forefoot (plantarflexed 1st ray)** | **1st MT dorsiflexion osteotomy** (± **plantar fascia release**) |
| **Negative (fixed hindfoot varus)** | Hindfoot | **Lateralising (valgus) calcaneal osteotomy (Dwyer)** |

**Cavovarus + recurrent sprains, Coleman corrects, failed orthotics** — **1st MT osteotomy + lateral ligament reconstruction (peroneus brevis graft)**\
✗ Wrong options: sinus tarsi steroid · ligament repair alone · triple arthrodesis

**Lowest recurrence, flexible cavovarus** — **reconstruction + plantar fascia release + 1st MT dorsiflexion osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: reconstruction alone · syndesmosis repair · FDL transfer + MDCO (planovalgus)

**Cavovarus, Coleman negative, failed nonop** — **lateralising calcaneal osteotomy** with Broström\
✗ Wrong options: syndesmotic fixation · post-op PT / orthosis / IER reinforcement (less important)

**X-ray that predisposes to recurrent sprains** — **hindfoot varus**\
✗ Wrong options: pes planus · syndesmotic widening (result not cause) · talar OCD (result) · tibiotalar osteophytes

### Associated peroneal tendon pathology

**Persistent lateral pain after Broström, drawer −, normal labs + X-ray** — **peroneus brevis (longitudinal split) tear** · linked to **SPR laxity**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · repair failure · PL tear · arthritis (takes > 3 mo)

**Pain with resisted eversion after sprain** — **MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: immobilise 4 wk · more PT · steroid · CT

| PB tear | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Small / ~30% split, repairable** | **Debridement, repair + tubularisation** |
| **> 50–60%, irreparable, severe tendinopathy** (e.g. only **35% intact**) | **Peroneus brevis tenodesis** (✗ Wrong options: nothing · groove deepening · SPR imbrication alone) |
| + instability | Reconstruct SPR, flatten fibular ridge / **deepen groove** |

**After SPR release + repair** — **avoid resisted eversion** early → else peroneal subluxation\
✗ Wrong options: inversion, PF, pronation, supination

### High ankle (syndesmotic) sprain – mechanism, tests & fixation (TO)

**Stable (TO)** — **nonop**: RICE + **NWB immobilisation 2 wk** → brace, ROM, strength → neuromuscular + proprioception

**Unstable (TO)** — **surgery**: **syndesmotic screw or suture button ± AITFL repair** · **deltoid + AITFL tear = indication for surgery** (TO)

**Predicting IOM tear / instability in bimalleolar fracture (TO)** — **intraoperative stress testing** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: fibular fracture level · Lauge-Hansen class · medial clear space (widens with deltoid + fibula without IOM tear)

| Syndesmotic fixation (TO) | |
|---|---|
| **Indicated** | **Proximal fibular fracture not fixed + unstabilisable medial injury** · **disruption > 5 cm above plafond** |
| **Not needed** | **< 3 cm above plafond** · medial malleolus fixed / deltoid repaired · no medial injury (✗ Wrong options: "fix all") |
| Controversial | **3–5 cm**, deltoid not repaired |
| Mechanics altered | **> 4.5 cm** with medial lesion |

**Screw position (TO)** — **2–3 cm above plafond**, **parallel to joint**, **angled 30° anteriorly** (perpendicular to tibiofibular joint) · **2–5 cm** (Orthobullets syndesmosis note) — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: 3 cm + 30° posterior · 10–15° cephalad / caudad

| Screw error (TO) | Result |
|---|---|
| Too proximal | **Deforms fibula → mortise widens** |
| Not parallel | **Fibula shifts proximally** |
| Not perpendicular | **Fibula stays laterally displaced** |

**Screw type (TO)** — AO: **fully threaded, neutralisation** · others: lag more secure · **max dorsiflexion during fixation** traditional, but may induce **ER moment → malreduction**

### Return to play

| Injury | Return |
|---|---|
| **Grade I / II** | **1–2 wk** |
| **Grade III** | **3–4 wk** |
| **High ankle, immobilised** | **5–6 wk** |
| **High ankle, screw fixation** | **Season** |

**Prevention** — **semirigid orthosis** (cavovarus: lateral hindfoot/forefoot wedge + 1st MT recess) · **peroneal (evertor) strengthening** · proprioception · season-long programme

## 6 Complications

**Persistent symptoms** — **up to 30%** (Orthobullets) · **up to 40%** residual symptoms (OB) — read the options · pain at 1 yr **5–33%** · pain falls fast in first 2 wk · ↑ risk of sprain both ankles

**Commonest cause of chronic pain** — **missed injury**: fractures (**anterior process calcaneus, lateral / posterior process talus, 5th MT**), **OCD**, **peroneal tendons**, **syndesmosis**, **tarsal coalition**, impingement, **hindfoot varus**

**Impingement** — **anteroinferior tibiofibular ligament** + posteromedial lesions → arthroscopic debridement

### Complications – SPN neurapraxia & CRPS

**SPN stretch neurapraxia** — 4 wk after grade III sprain: **burning, exquisite light-touch tenderness anterolateral ankle / dorsolateral foot**, worse with **PF + inversion**, ATFL torn · SPN injury **1.6–4.4%** · **> 80% of grade III sprains have EMG evidence of tibial or CPN injury** (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: DPN · deltoid · sural · saphenous

**Neuropathic drug** — **gabapentin / pregabalin**: **↓ hyperexcitability of voltage-dependent presynaptic calcium channels** · TCA (amitriptyline) also

| ✗ Wrong option: drug mechanism | Drug |
|---|---|
| Intracellular **Na channel** block | Lidocaine, bupivacaine |
| **Mu agonist** | Opioids |
| **Direct GABA** | Diazepam, baclofen |
| **COX-2** selective (not COX-1) | Anti-inflammatory |

**Nerve complication of lateral ligament reconstruction** — **SPN (commonest) → paraesthesia proximal dorsum of foot**

| Nerve | Sensory / motor sign |
|---|---|
| **SPN** | **Dorsum of foot, anterolateral ankle / distal leg** ✓ |
| **DPN** | **1st dorsal web space** ± TA / EHL weakness |
| **Sural** | **Posterolateral ankle + lateral foot** |
| **Tibial** | **Can't plantarflex** |
| **Saphenous** | **Anteromedial leg / medial hindfoot** |

**CRPS** — listed association of ankle sprain

## X vs Y

| | **ATFL** | **CFL** |
|---|---|---|
| Attachments | Anterior fibula → **talar neck** | **Fibular tip → calcaneal tubercle** |
| Taut in | **PF + inversion** | **DF + inversion** |
| Drawer laxity | **Plantar flexion** | **Dorsiflexion** |
| Talar tilt | Not alone | **Required for + tilt** |
| Frequency | **1st** | **2nd** |

| | **Low ankle sprain** | **High ankle sprain** |
|---|---|---|
| Ligaments | ATFL ± CFL | **AITFL, IOL, PITFL, ITL** |
| Share | **> 90%** | **1–10%** |
| Mechanism | **Inversion + PF** | **Hyperdorsiflexion / ER** (TO) |
| Test | Drawer, talar tilt | **Squeeze, ER stress, Cotton, fibular translation** |
| RTP | 1–4 wk | **5–6 wk / season** |

| | **Cavovarus orthotic** | **Planovalgus orthotic** |
|---|---|---|
| Posting | **Lateral** hindfoot / forefoot wedge | **Medial** hindfoot post |
| Arch | **Lowered** + **1st MT recess** | Rigid arch support, **UCBL**, Arizona |

| | **Broström-Gould** | **Tenodesis (Evans, Watson-Jones, Chrisman-Snook)** |
|---|---|---|
| Type | **Anatomic** (ATFL + CFL + IER) | **Nonanatomic** (peroneus brevis) |
| Problem | Over-tight → **↓ DF** | **Subtalar stiffness** |

## How it's asked

**"Inversion laxity in dorsiflexion, none in PF — ligament?"** → **calcaneofibular ligament (CFL)**

**"Anterior drawer in plantarflexion tests?"** → **ATFL**

**"Talus translates 10 mm in 10° PF — injured?"** → **ATFL**

**"Talar tilt 20° more than other side?"** → **anterior talofibular and calcaneofibular ligaments**

**"Positive talar tilt X-ray — which ligament must be torn?"** → **CFL**

**"Anterior drawer — true statement?"** → **CFL best tested in dorsiflexion**

**"Drawer laxity in DF, stable in PF — attachments?"** → **fibular tip → lateral calcaneal tubercle**

**"Recurrent sprains, drawer lax in DF only — structure?"** → **CFL**

**"Strongest tibiotalar ligament?"** → **deep posterior tibiotalar** (TO)

**"Sequence of ligament failure in inversion?"** → **anterolateral capsule → ATFL → CFL → PTFL** (TO)

**"Usual mechanism of syndesmotic injury?"** → **hyperdorsiflexion** (TO)

**"Syndesmosis — false EXCEPT?"** → **PITFL stronger than AITFL** (TO)

**"Syndesmosis X-ray — FALSE?"** → **TF overlap < 6 mm on mortise = unstable** (TO)

**"Most reliable predictor of IOM tear in bimalleolar fracture?"** → **intraoperative stress testing** (TO)

**"Syndesmotic screw position?"** → **2 cm above plafond, parallel to joint, 30° anterior** (TO)

**"Cotton test positive translation?"** → answer key not provided by the source (TO)

**"Role of proprioception EXCEPT?"** → answer key not provided by the source (TO)

**"Pathway to rehabilitate to cut recurrence?"** → **proprioception**

**"Nonop modality shown to minimise recurrence?"** → **functional bracing + early proprioceptive training**

**"Functional vs immobilisation — FALSE?"** → **functional ↑ laxity** (it's less)

**"Cast + NWB vs functional rehab?"** → **longer time to return to sport**

**"Grade III sprain on MRI, no syndesmosis — treatment?"** → **RICE, elastic wrap, therapy**

**"Acute sprain, + drawer, squeeze − — next?"** → **early functional rehabilitation**

**"Untreated sprain 5 months, stable drawer?"** → **proprioception + peroneal strengthening**

**"Ottawa — finding that warrants X-ray?"** → **distal fibula / lateral malleolus tip tenderness**

**"Chronic instability in athlete, normal alignment — best surgery?"** → **modified Broström**

**"Gould modification — true?"** → **no biomechanical difference in stability**

**"Add-on to Broström-Gould for commonly associated condition?"** → **ankle arthroscopy**

**"Post Broström-Gould rehab — true?"** → **mobilisation < 3 wk ↑ functional scores**

**"Over-tightened suture-tape Broström limits?"** → **dorsiflexion**

**"Commonest nerve complication of ATFL reconstruction?"** → **SPN — proximal dorsum of foot paraesthesia**

**"Factor predisposing to reconstruction failure?"** → **cavovarus foot**

**"Most likely cause of failure in high-arched varus heel?"** → **native hindfoot alignment**

**"Cavovarus, Coleman corrects, recurrent sprains?"** → **1st MT osteotomy + lateral ligament reconstruction**

**"Cavovarus, Coleman negative — what cuts recurrence most?"** → **lateralising calcaneal osteotomy**

**"Lowest chance of recurrence, flexible cavovarus?"** → **reconstruction + plantar fascia release + 1st MT dorsiflexion osteotomy**

**"Orthotic for subtle cavovarus runner?"** → **lateral hindfoot posting + 1st MT head recess**

**"Semirigid orthosis, lowered arch, lateral wedge — deformity?"** → **subtle cavovarus foot**

**"Pain + swelling 8 months after Broström, drawer −?"** → **peroneus brevis tear**

**"Eversion pain 7 wk after sprain despite PT — next?"** → **MRI**

**"PB 35% intact at Broström?"** → **peroneus brevis tenodesis**

**"After PB tubularisation + SPR repair, avoid?"** → **resisted eversion**

**"Burning dorsolateral pain 4 wk after grade III sprain?"** → **SPN stretch neurapraxia**

**"Drug mechanism for SPN neuritis?"** → **↓ voltage-dependent presynaptic Ca channel hyperexcitability (gabapentin)**

**"Commonest acute MRI finding with lateral sprain?"** → **talar bone bruise**

**"Medial pain, ER pain, squeeze −, needs reconstruction?"** → **deltoid ligament complex**

**"Peroneal weakness in dancers causes?"** → **ankle sprain**

# Anterior Tibialis Tendon Rupture

*Foot & Ankle · 10 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy

**Tibialis anterior** — **primary ankle dorsiflexor (80%)** · inserts **medial cuneiform + base of 1st MT** · also inverts foot with dorsiflexion

**Secondary dorsiflexors** — **EHL + EDL** → mask the rupture → **delayed diagnosis common**

**Site** — rupture **at level of ankle joint**, proximal stump retracts (\~3 cm)

### Epidemiology & risk factors

**Attritional (atraumatic)** — **older patients**, men **> 45 yr** · eccentric load against **plantarflexed ankle** (missed step) · degenerative

**Traumatic** — **laceration** or closed rupture · strong eccentric contraction in **young**

**Risk factors** — **older age** · **diabetes** · **fluoroquinolones** · **local steroid injection** · **inflammatory arthritis** · gout

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – foot drop & attritional rupture

**Acute** — **"pop"** → painful anterior ankle swelling · weak dorsiflexion

**Chronic** — **painless** · difficulty clearing foot · minimal swelling

**Classic triad** — **pseudotumour (painless mass) at anteromedial ankle** = retracted stump · **loss of TA contour** (not palpable on resisted DF) · **weak DF + hyperextension of all toes** (EHL / EDL over-firing)

**Eversion on dorsiflexion** — unopposed EHL + EDL (normal foot **inverts** with DF via TA)

**Gait** — **steppage gait** (↑ hip flexion in swing) · **foot slap** after heel strike · toe dragging

![Loss of tibialis anterior contour after rupture (arrow)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7055-tib-ant-rupture.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / role |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (3 views foot + ankle) | Exclude osseous injury · usually unremarkable |
| **CT** | **Not indicated** |
| **MRI** | **Complete vs partial tear** · sagittal **"empty sheath"** · does not decide need for interposition graft |

![Sagittal MRI — empty tibialis anterior sheath](../../MCQ/attachments/q3821-a-tib-ant-rupture.jpg)

### Differential diagnosis

| Condition | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| **Attritional TA rupture** | **EHL + EDL normal**, painless anterior mass, steppage gait, full passive ROM |
| **L4 radiculopathy / lumbar stenosis** | **Tendon palpable, no ankle mass**, dermatomal sensory loss (medial leg), weakness of several groups incl. quadriceps, +ve lumbar MRI |
| **Common peroneal nerve palsy** | **EHL + EDL also weak**, sensory loss, compression history |
| **Deep peroneal nerve palsy** | Toe extensors weak (not in isolated TA rupture) |
| Acute TA laceration | Clear sharp injury, **abrupt** onset (not progressive over months) |
| Anterior ankle impingement | **No** DF weakness, steppage gait or mass |

## 5 Management

```
Tibialis anterior rupture
├─ Low-demand / elderly → AFO
├─ Partial rupture → CAST
├─ Acute < 6 wk, active → DIRECT REPAIR
│      (attempt up to 3 months · gastroc recession if < 5° DF knee extended)
└─ Chronic > 6 wk → RECONSTRUCTION
       └─ gap → INTERPOSITION GRAFT (plantaris, hamstring) / EHL transfer
```

### Operative criteria

| Option | Indication |
|---|---|
| **AFO** | **Low-demand** patient |
| **Casting** | **Partial rupture** |
| **Direct (primary) repair** | **Acute < 6 wk**, active high-demand; may be attempted **up to 3 months** |
| **Gastrocnemius recession** (before tensioning) | **< 5° passive DF with knee extended** |
| **Reconstruction** | **Chronic > 6 wk** |
| **PTT transfer** | **Permanent foot drop** only |

**Surgery even late** — functional DF + gait improved in **18/19** (early 3 d–6 wk and late 7 wk–5 yr repairs) · traumatic ruptures do better with surgery regardless of timing · atraumatic presenting late → bracing acceptable

### Treatment – repair or reconstruction & expected outcome

**Direct repair** — open: through laceration · closed: longitudinal incision over defect · hemostat under **extensor retinaculum** to retrieve proximal stump · **non-absorbable Krackow / Bunnell / Kessler** · oversew frayed ends · avulsion → **suture anchors / bone tunnels**

**Sliding graft** — **half-width of TA turned down** · secured to medial cuneiform / dorsal navicular distal to retinaculum

**Free graft** — **autograft (hamstring, plantaris)** or allograft · also Achilles, EDL, EHB interposition

**EHL transfer / tenodesis** — **distal EHL → EHB** · **proximal EHL stump** used as graft to TA insertion · TA tensioned then sutured to proximal EHL

**Augment with** — **extensor tendons (EHL, EDL)**\
✗ Wrong options: **FHL** (out of phase, too short; FHL for chronic Achilles)

**Expected outcome** — **restores active DF with some residual weakness + improved activity level** · ↑ AOFAS, high satisfaction · peak torque **72–84%** of other side

**Commonest long-term residual** — **decreased dorsiflexion strength** (+ ↓ inversion strength)\
✗ Wrong options: persistent foot drop / AFO (chronic missed or nonop) · numbness (missed superficial nerve laceration) · persistent pain

(✗ Wrong options: **Wrong treatments** — **VY advancement / turndown flap** (Achilles rupture with gap)) · **SPR repair + groove deepening** (peroneal instability) · AFO in active patient (permanent gait disturbance) · chest CT + skeletal survey + oncology biopsy of the "mass" · MRI of proximal tibiofibular joint for ganglion + peroneal EMG · PTT transfer

## 6 Complications

**List** — failure of repair / reconstruction · **weakness of dorsiflexion** · adhesions · neuroma

**Prognosis** — good with treatment

## X vs Y

| | **TA rupture** | **Peroneal nerve palsy** | **L4 radiculopathy** |
|---|---|---|---|
| EHL / EDL | **Normal** | **Weak** | Variable |
| Anterior mass | **Yes (pseudotumour)** | No | No |
| TA tendon palpable | **No** | Yes | **Yes** |
| Sensation | Normal | Sensory abnormality | Dermatomal |

| | **Acute (< 6 wk)** | **Chronic (> 6 wk)** |
|---|---|---|
| Treatment | **Primary repair ± gastroc recession** | **Reconstruction** — interposition graft, sliding graft, EHL transfer |

## How it's asked

**"Elderly soccer injury, video of left anteromedial mass + toe hyperextension — next?"** → **left tendon repair and possible graft augmentation** (check the side)

**"67-yo diabetic, steroid injections, painless anterior mass, EHL / EDL normal?"** → **attritional rupture of the tibialis anterior tendon**

**"Chronic rupture 7 months, 15° DF knee extended — next?"** → **surgical reconstruction with plantaris tendon interposition augmentation**

**"Surgery after acute laceration will most likely?"** → **restore active dorsiflexion with some residual weakness and improve activity level**

**"Most common long-term residual after repair?"** → **decreased dorsiflexion strength**

**"70-yo active, acute rupture — associated procedure?"** → **primary repair with possible gastrocnemius recession**

**"When add gastroc recession?"** → **< 5° passive DF with knee extended**

**"Graft never used for TA?"** → **FHL**
