# Blood Supply to the Foot

*Foot & Ankle · 11 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Source arteries

**Three source arteries** — **peroneal (fibular)** · **posterior tibial (PTA)** · **anterior tibial (ATA)**

| Artery | Origin | Course in leg | Ankle branches | Foot branches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Peroneal** | **PTA, \~2.5 cm from its origin** | Pierces interosseous membrane **\~5 cm above lateral malleolus** (perforating branch) → joins **anterior lateral malleolar a.** → anterior to syndesmosis → **lateral tarsal a.** | **Posterior lateral malleolar** · communicating | **Lateral calcaneal branch** (terminal) |
| **Posterior tibial** | **Larger** terminal branch of popliteal; proximal part = **tibioperoneal trunk** | Between superficial + deep posterior compartment → medial, **palpable behind medial malleolus** | **Posterior medial malleolar** · communicating · **artery of tarsal canal** | **Bifurcates beneath sustentaculum** → medial + lateral plantar |
| **Anterior tibial** | **Smaller** terminal branch of popliteal | **Anterior to interosseous membrane**, supplies anterior compartment · **superficial at ankle midway between malleoli** | **Anterior medial + anterior lateral malleolar** | **Dorsalis pedis** |

**Peroneal supplies (leg)** — soleus, tibialis posterior, FHL, peroneals

![Arterial supply of the foot and ankle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12114-fig-3.jpg)

### Hindfoot & talus

**Artery of tarsal canal** — branch of **PTA** · **dominant blood supply to talar body**

**Artery of sinus tarsi** — branch of **perforating peroneal a.** · with tarsal canal a. supplies **talus**\
✗ Wrong options: heel pad

**Lateral calcaneal branch of peroneal** — **terminal** branch · perfuses **lateral flap of extensile lateral approach to calcaneus** · one of three flap arteries with **lateral malleolar + lateral tarsal** · **minor** contribution to heel pad

![Extensile lateral calcaneal incision — flap on lateral calcaneal artery](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12114-fig10.jpg)

### Heel pad blood supply

**Heel pad** — **medial calcaneal branch of the posterior tibial artery** = major supply\
✗ Wrong options: lateral calcaneal a. (minor) · medial plantar a. · artery of sinus tarsi

**Medial calcaneal branch** — **first branch of lateral plantar a.** (Orthobullets) · "branch of posterior tibial artery" (OB) — same vessel, read the options

**Heel pad avulsion** — high-energy (industrial machine) · fibrous honeycomb septa + specialised skin → **irreplaceable** · risk **heel pad necrosis** → poor prognosis, salvage often fails · replantation with **ipsilateral saphenous vein graft** described

### Plantar branches (PTA)

| Lateral plantar a. branches (in order) | Medial plantar a. |
|---|---|
| 1st **medial calcaneal** · 2nd **abductor digiti minimi** · 3rd **digital branch to 5th toe** · terminal **plantar branch** | Given off **under flexor retinaculum** · supplies **medial side of foot** · terminal → anastomoses with **1st dorsal metatarsal a. (DPA)** · superficial digital branches → plantar metatarsal aa. of **first three** IM spaces |

### Dorsal supply (ATA)

**Dorsalis pedis** — continuation of ATA · palpable **just lateral to EHL** · branches **arcuate, lateral tarsal, medial tarsal** · ends at **1st IM space** → **1st dorsal metatarsal a.** + **deep plantar arch**

**Plantar arch** — **lateral plantar a. + dorsalis pedis** · plantar digital + plantar metatarsal aa.

**Arcuate artery** — dorsal midfoot, **deep to extensor tendons** · dorsal metatarsal aa. to **2nd, 3rd, 4th** IM spaces

![Dorsalis pedis and arcuate artery](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12114-dorsalis-pedis-foot.jpg)

### Veins

**Dorsal venous arch** — drains into **greater + lesser saphenous** systems

## X vs Y

| | **Medial calcaneal branch (PTA)** | **Lateral calcaneal branch (peroneal)** |
|---|---|---|
| Supplies | **Heel pad (major)** | **Extensile lateral calcaneal flap** · heel pad (minor) |
| Injury context | Heel pad avulsion | Lateral wound necrosis after calcaneus ORIF |

| | **Artery of tarsal canal** | **Artery of sinus tarsi** |
|---|---|---|
| Parent | **PTA** | **Perforating peroneal** |
| Supplies | **Talar body (dominant)** | Talus |

## How it's asked

**"Heel pad avulsion — baseline blood supply of the heel pad?"** → **medial calcaneal branch of the posterior tibial artery**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral calcaneal branch of peroneal (minor) · medial plantar · sinus tarsi a.

**"Extensile lateral calcaneal flap perfused by?"** → **lateral calcaneal branch of peroneal artery**

**"Dominant blood supply to talar body?"** → **artery of tarsal canal (PTA)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Bunionette Deformity

*Foot & Ankle · 12 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Tailor's bunion** — **lateral prominence of 5th MT head** · adolescents + adults · **women 2–4×** · often **bilateral**

**Extrinsic cause** — **forefoot compression (tight shoes)** = commonest · abnormal lateral loading

**Intrinsic causes** — congenital (**splayfoot**, **brachymetatarsia**) · inflammatory arthropathy · residual surgical malalignment

**Associated** — **varus 5th MTP** · **pes planus** · forefoot widening

### Pathoanatomy & normal values

| Parameter | Normal | Bunionette |
|---|---|---|
| **4–5 IMA** | **6.5–8°** (Orthobullets, OB) · **6–8°** (OB) | **↑** |
| **Lateral deviation angle** | **0–7°** | **↑** |
| **5th MT head width** | **< 13 mm** | **↑** (> 13 mm) |
| 5th MT shaft | Straight | **Lateral bowing** |

**Lesion** — bony prominence **± bursitis** over lateral 5th MT head

![4–5 intermetatarsal angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7017-4-5ima.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — lateral bunion pain · **plantar callus** · **worse with constrictive shoes** · cosmetic: **medial deviation of 5th toe**, prominent 5th MT head · effect on work

**Exam** — **plantar or lateral hyperkeratosis** · widened forefoot · erythema, swollen 5th bunion · **painless passive 5th MTP ROM** usually · check shoe wear

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**: lateral 5th MT head prominence ± pain + callus

### Key radiology findings

**Radiographs** — **weight-bearing dorsoplantar, lateral, oblique** · ↑ **4–5 IMA** · ↑ **lateral deviation angle** · ↑ **MT head width**

**CT** — rarely needed · associated **trauma or malignancy**

### Coughlin classification

| Type | Deformity | Surgery |
|---|---|---|
| **I** ![Type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7017-type-1.jpg) | **Enlarged 5th MT head / lateral exostosis** (head > 13 mm) | **Lateral condylectomy** |
| **II** ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7017-type-2.jpg) | **Congenital lateral bow of 5th MT** (distal half) · **normal 4–5 IMA** | **Distal chevron osteotomy** (IMA < 12°) |
| **III** ![Type III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7017-type-3.jpg) | **↑ 4–5 IMA** · **most common** | Distal osteotomy if < 12° · **oblique diaphyseal osteotomy if > 12°** |
| IV (OB) | **Combination** of the above | — |

**Type definitions differ** — one OB uses **1 = enlarged lateral condyle · 2 = 5th MT varus · 3 = combined** — read the stem

## 5 Management

```
Bunionette (any type)
├─ First line, all types → NSAIDs, wide shoes, orthotics, padding, callus shaving
└─ Failed nonop
       ├─ Type I (normal IMA) → lateral condylectomy
       ├─ Type II / III, IMA < 12° → distal chevron osteotomy
       └─ Type II / III, IMA > 12° → oblique diaphyseal osteotomy (+ condylectomy if head wide)
```

### Nonoperative first-line care

**First line for all types** — **NSAIDs + shoe wear modification** · orthotics (**semi-rigid inserts**, **custom orthotics** — OB) · **keratosis padding** · **callus shaving** · wide-based shoes · stretch existing shoes · **75–90% success**\
✗ Wrong options: condylectomy · osteotomy · MT head resection as first step

**Asymptomatic / only shoe rubbing** — nonop\
✗ Wrong options: lateral condylectomy · condylectomy + distal osteotomy · diaphyseal osteotomy

**✗ Wrong option: 1st TMT arthrodesis ± Akin** — that's **hallux valgus**, not bunionette

### Operative criteria

| Finding | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Type I**, normal 4–5 IMA | **Lateral condylectomy (lateral eminence resection)** |
| **Type II** (lateral bow, normal IMA) | **Distal chevron osteotomy** |
| Type II / III, **4–5 IMA < 12°** (Orthobullets) · **≤ 12°** · **8–12°** · **9–12°** (OB) | **Distal MT osteotomy** (chevron) ± condylectomy + lateral capsule tightening |
| Type II / III, **4–5 IMA > 12°** | **Oblique diaphyseal rotational osteotomy** |
| **↑ IMA + MT head > 13 mm** | **Diaphyseal osteotomy + lateral condylectomy** |
| Plantar callosity | **Shave plantar 5th MT head** |
| Symptomatic after distal osteotomy | Proximal osteotomy (OB) |
| **Salvage only** | **MT head resection** |

### Procedure by type – lateral condylectomy, distal chevron, diaphyseal osteotomy (4–5 IMA)

**Lateral condylectomy** — resect **lateral ⅓** of 5th MT head (Orthobullets) · **lateral ¼–⅓** (OB) · or up to lateral articular margin · + **tighten lateral MTP capsule** · **no WB restrictions** / no prolonged immobilisation\
✗ Wrong options: for IMA > 10° · lateral half · lowest recurrence · high metatarsalgia

**Condylectomy downsides** — **highest recurrence** · over-resection · **MTP subluxation**, persistent pain

**MT head osteotomy for type I** — larger correction than condylectomy, but WB restrictions

**Distal chevron** — **commonest distal osteotomy** · **Type II: lateral bow, no keratotic lesion, normal IMA** · **biomechanically strongest** vs other osteotomies · fix with **K-wire or screw**\
✗ Wrong options: type I who can't tolerate WB restriction · failed prior condylar resection · type III at 13°

**Other distal osteotomies** — distal transverse · peg-and-slot · step-cut

**Oblique diaphyseal rotational osteotomy** — **Type II + III, IMA > 12°** · **screw** fixation · ± lateral condylectomy · **causes 5th MT shortening + metatarsalgia** · **rotational deformity** + implant problems (OB)

**Proximal osteotomy** — **avoid: poor blood supply** (Orthobullets)

**Sliding distal MIS osteotomy (S-DMMO)** — **type 3, IMA 10°, prominent condyle** → treats deformity, avoids implant complications\
✗ Wrong options: contact casting · lateral eminence resection (type 1) · open diaphyseal osteotomy · MT head resection

**Large IMA (radiograph)** — **MT osteotomy + 5th MT head ostectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: head excision (flail joint + transfer lesion) · plantar condylectomy (↓ plantar pressure only) · Silastic implant

**Rheumatoid, type 1, IMA 7°, congruent MTP, nodule under head** — **simple exostectomy + soft-tissue mass excision**\
✗ Wrong options: MT head excision (no metatarsalgia, normal joint) · osteotomy

![Coughlin bunionette types I–III](../../MCQ/attachments/q3557-illa-bunionette-deformity.jpg)

![Oblique diaphyseal osteotomy of the 5th metatarsal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7017-oblique-diaphyseal-osteotomy-of-the-5th-metatarsal.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Recurrence** | **Condylectomy alone** — commonest / highest |
| **Transfer metatarsalgia** | **Isolated MT head resection** |
| **MTP instability / flail joint** | **MT head resection** (salvage only, poor outcomes) |
| **5th MT shortening** + metatarsalgia | **Diaphyseal osteotomy** |
| Rotational deformity | Diaphyseal osteotomy |
| **Claw toe** | — |

## X vs Y

| | **Type I** | **Type II** | **Type III** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Problem | Wide head / exostosis | **Lateral bow** | **↑ 4–5 IMA** |
| 4–5 IMA | Normal | **Normal** | **↑** |
| Surgery | **Condylectomy** | **Distal chevron** | **Diaphyseal (> 12°)** / distal (< 12°) |

| | **Lateral condylectomy** | **Distal chevron** | **Oblique diaphyseal** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Indication | Type I | IMA < 12° | **IMA > 12°** |
| WB after | **Unrestricted** | Restricted | Restricted |
| Problem | **Recurrence** | — | **Shortening** |

## How it's asked

**"Oblique diaphyseal rotational osteotomy — which radiographs?"** → **type II and type III (B and C)** with IMA > 12°

**"Planned distal chevron — which radiograph?"** → **type II, lateral bow (B)**

**"Diaphyseal 5th MT osteotomy is optimal for?"** → **widened 4–5 IMA + lateral prominence**

**"Distal chevron most indicated for?"** → **laterally bowed 5th MT, normal IMA, no keratotic lesion**

**"Distal chevron — which figure?"** → **type 2 bunionette (Figure C)**

**"Enlarged 5th MT head, failed nonop?"** → **lateral condylar resection**

**"Lateral condylectomy — true?"** → **no weight-bearing restrictions postoperatively**

**"Mid-diaphyseal osteotomy planned — pathology?"** → **type III with 4–5 IMA of 14°**

**"Large 4–5 IMA on radiograph — optimal surgery?"** → **metatarsal osteotomy + 5th MT head ostectomy**

**"IMA 13°, head 16 mm, congruent MTP?"** → **diaphyseal osteotomy + 5th MT condylectomy**

**"IMA 10°, prominent condyle, failed pads?"** → **sliding distal metatarsal MIS osteotomy**

**"RA, nodule under 5th MT head, IMA 7°?"** → **simple exostectomy + soft-tissue mass excision**

**"Painless bump, rubs on shoes — initial?"** → **NSAIDs + shoe wear modification**

**"First visit, IMA 9°, callus?"** → **shoewear modification, custom orthotics, NSAIDs**

**"Commonest complication of condylectomy?"** → **recurrence**

**"MT head resection causes?"** → **transfer metatarsalgia, MTP instability**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Claw Toe

*Foot & Ankle · 13 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Distribution** — typically **multiple toes** · often **bilateral**

### Pathophysiology – EDL vs intrinsic imbalance, neurological causes

**Primary pathology** — **MTP hyperextension** → chronic MTP hyperextension → **unopposed DIP + PIP flexion by FDL** · analogous to **intrinsic minus hand**

**MTP joint** — **EDL overpowering relatively weak intrinsics** · hyperextended MTP makes intrinsics less efficient plantarflexors → deformity progresses\
✗ Wrong options: intrinsics overpowering EDL · plantar plate overpowering EDB · lumbricals overpowering intrinsics · EDL overpowering EDB

**IP joints** — **stronger flexors overpower weaker intrinsics** (intrinsics act as IP extensors)

**Plantar plate** — becomes **insufficient / attenuated** → **base of proximal phalanx translates dorsally**

**Interossei + lumbricals** — **move dorsally** → flexion moment arm shifts to wrong side of MTP centre of rotation

**Most common cause** — **synovitis** (Orthobullets, OB) · "occurs most commonly in **diabetes, alcoholism, neuromuscular** conditions" (another OB) — read the options

**Other causes** — trauma · **delayed / missed compartment syndrome** (deep posterior leg compartment or foot) · **cavus** · **inflammatory arthropathy** (soft-tissue attenuation → MTP instability)

**Neurological causes** — **Charcot-Marie-Tooth**, **Friedreich ataxia**, myelodysplasia, **cerebral palsy**, **multiple sclerosis**, stroke, **diabetic neuropathy** · **clawing of all 4 lesser toes → neurological abnormality**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain at **unstable MTP joint** · **metatarsalgia**

**Plantar** — **depressed metatarsal head with callus** + tenderness

**Dorsal / tip** — flexed IP joints with dorsal callosities · **tip-of-toe ulceration** (neuropathic / diabetic)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — clinical: **MTP hyperextension + PIP flexion + DIP flexion** of a lesser toe

### Lesser toe deformities

| Deformity | MTP | PIP | DIP |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Claw toe** ![Claw toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7013-clawe-toe-moved.jpg) | **Hyperextension** | **Flexion** | **Flexion** |
| **Hammer toe** ![Hammer toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7013-illustration-moved.jpg) | Slight extension (Orthobullets) · neutral (OB) | **Flexion** | **Extension** |
| **Mallet toe** ![Mallet toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7013-mallet-toe-illustration-moved.jpg) | Normal / neutral | Normal / neutral | **Flexion** |
| **Curly toe** | **Flexed** | Flexed | Flexed |
| **Crossover toe** | **Laterally deviated** | Flexed | Neutral |

## 5 Management

```
Claw toe
├─ First line → taping + shoe modification
├─ Flexible, no contracture → Girdlestone-Taylor: EDB tenotomy, EDL lengthening, FDL flexor-to-extensor transfer
│      (diabetic, tip-of-toe ulcer, no infection → isolated percutaneous FDL tenotomy)
├─ Fixed contracture → Girdlestone + MTP capsulectomy + proximal phalanx head/neck resection
└─ All four lesser toes → Girdlestone + Weil (distal MT shortening) osteotomy
```

```
Claw toe (TO)
├─ Flexible → flexor tendon transfer
└─ Fixed → MTP soft-tissue release (dorsal + medial/lateral capsule, extensor lengthening)
       ├─ Reduced → realigned toe
       └─ Not reduced → Weil osteotomy + fixed hammer-toe repair
```

**Nonoperative** — **first line** · plantar padding (**metatarsal / crest pads**, orthotics) to offload plantarly subluxed MT heads · **high toe box** · **sling** holding proximal phalanx parallel to ground

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Painful flexible deformity** without contracture · shoe-wear ulcers · otherwise healthy | **Girdlestone(-Taylor)**: EDB tenotomy + EDL lengthening + **FDL flexor-to-extensor transfer** |
| **Fixed contracture** | Girdlestone + **MTP capsulectomy** + **proximal phalanx head + neck resection** (tendon transfer + bony resection) |
| **Claw toe of all four lesser toes** | Girdlestone + **Weil lesser MT osteotomy** |
| **Flexible deformity, diabetic, tip-of-toe ulcer, no infection** | **Isolated percutaneous FDL tenotomy** |
| Rigid diabetic claw toe with ulcer | Arthrodesis |
| **Rigid mallet toe** (rigid DIP flexion) | Bony: **DIP fusion or resection** |
| **Hammer toe** (PIP flexion, DIP extension) | **FDL-to-EDL transfer** |

### Flexible deformity – flexor tenotomy or flexor-to-extensor transfer

**Flexible claw toe (TO)** — **flexor-to-extensor tendon transfer with extensor lengthening** · flexor split into **two slings**, transferred dorsally as for hammer toe · extensor lengthening may or may not be needed (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: extensor lengthening + PIP arthroplasty · flexor tenotomy + MTP pinning · distal metatarsal osteotomy

**Girdlestone-Taylor** — FDL to extensor surface → long flexor acts like **intrinsics**: **active MTP plantarflexion + IP extension** · **only for flexible deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: PIP arthrodesis · MTP capsulotomy + PP resection arthroplasty (fixed only) · isolated Weil

**Percutaneous FDL tenotomy** — diabetic + **flexible** claw toe + **tip ulcer**, no osteomyelitis (bone not probed) → ↓ tip pressure; minimal exposure (infection, poor healing)

| Percutaneous flexor tenotomy outcome | Value |
|---|---|
| **Ulcer healing** | **98% at 4 weeks** |
| Reoperation | **\~8%** (✗ Wrong options: 50%) |
| Ulcer recurrence | **0–18%** (✗ Wrong options: 30%) |
| **Transfer ulceration** | Reported complication (✗ Wrong options: "no risk") |

**✗ Diabetic tip-ulcer traps** — amputation through 2nd MTP (only with infection / osteomyelitis) · repeat ulcer debridement (doesn't offload) · **DIP fusion** (rigid / arthritic) · **Weil** (all four lesser toes, non-neuropathic; floating toe + transfer metatarsalgia)

### Plantar plate repair

**Plantar plate repair** — **improves the extension deformity at the MTP joint** → toe plantigrade\
✗ Wrong options: MTP flexion (claw has MTP hyperextension) · PIP / DIP (addressed indirectly)

**Recurrence** — **persistent plantar plate dysfunction**

### Weil osteotomy – floating toe complication

**Technique** — **oblique distal MT shortening osteotomy** · translates head **proximal + plantar** · rigid internal fixation · head moved proximal to callus → axial decompression · **average shortening 4 mm**

**Indications** — metatarsalgia (2nd MT, high heels) · MTP subluxation · claw toe of all four lesser toes

**Floating toe** — **most common complication of Weil** = **dorsiflexion contracture at the MTP joint**

**Mechanism** — plantar depression of head + altered centre of rotation → **interossei move dorsal to MTP axis** → **converted from plantarflexors to dorsiflexors** (intrinsics act as MTP extensors)\
✗ Wrong options: articular damage · disruption of dorsal capsular artery · lengthening (it shortens) · ligament instability

**Plantar translation in distal MT osteotomy (TO)** — **floating toe**: intrinsic force shifts volar → dorsal + weak toe flexors → **extension at MTP + IP joints** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: claw · hammer · mallet

**Cause of 2nd toe "floating" post-op** — **technique of surgical osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: immobilisation · CVA · extensor tethering · infection

**Avoid** — osteotomy **parallel to plantar surface of foot** or **resect a wafer of bone** so head isn't depressed

**Rare after Weil** — **osteonecrosis / MTP arthritis** (constant **plantar artery** to MT head + capsule) · nonunion · significant transfer lesions

**Weil vs Helal (shaft) osteotomy** — Weil: higher satisfaction, less recurrent metatarsalgia, fewer transfer lesions

![Weil osteotomy – plantar depression of the head (B–D) vs parallel cut / wafer resection (E–I)](../../MCQ/attachments/q682-illa-weil-osteotomy.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Cause |
|---|---|
| **Floating toe** (most common after Weil) | **Intrinsics migrate dorsal to joint → act as MTP extensors** |
| **Recurrence** | **Persistent plantar plate dysfunction** |
| Transfer ulceration | After percutaneous flexor tenotomy |
| Transfer metatarsalgia | Weil (not the commonest) |

## X vs Y

| | **Isolated FDL tenotomy** | **Girdlestone-Taylor transfer** | **Resection / arthrodesis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Deformity | **Flexible** | **Flexible** | **Fixed / rigid** |
| Patient | **Diabetic, tip ulcer, no infection** | Otherwise healthy | Any |
| Exposure | Percutaneous | Open | Open |

## How it's asked

**"Claw toe — muscular imbalance from which relatively strong structure?"** → **EDL overpowering relatively weak intrinsics**

**"Multiple sclerosis — associated toe deformity?"** → **extended MTP, flexed PIP + DIP**\
✗ Wrong options: curly · mallet · hammer · crossover

**"Plantar translation of MT head in distal MT osteotomy causes?"** → **floating toe** (TO)

**"2nd toe floats after Weil — cause?"** → **technique of surgical osteotomy**

**"Mechanism of commonest Weil complication?"** → **conversion of interossei from plantarflexors to dorsiflexors**

**"Most common complication of Weil osteotomy?"** → **dorsiflexion contracture at the MTP joint**\
✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · nonunion · transfer lesions · MTP arthritis

**"Flexible claw toe — best treatment?"** → **flexor-to-extensor tendon transfer with extensor lengthening** (TO)

**"Diabetic, flexible PIP + MTP, failed nonop — surgery?"** → **Girdlestone-Taylor flexor-to-extensor tendon transfer**

**"Who benefits most from percutaneous flexor tenotomy?"** → **diabetic (A1c 8.5), flexible PIP + DIP flexion, ulcer at tip of toe**

**"Diabetic tip ulcer, flexible 2nd claw toe, bone not probed — treatment?"** → **percutaneous flexor digitorum longus tenotomy**

**"Percutaneous flexor tenotomy — true?"** → **ulcer healing rates 98% at 4 weeks**

**"Claw toe with lateral deviation of 2nd PP — direct result of plantar plate repair?"** → **improves the extension deformity at the MTP joint**

**"Claw toes of all four lesser toes?"** → **Girdlestone + Weil osteotomy** · think **neurological cause**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Deep Peroneal Nerve Entrapment

*Foot & Ankle · 14 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Synonym** — **anterior tarsal tunnel syndrome** · rare compression neuropathy of the **deep peroneal nerve (DPN)**

**Commonest site** — fibro-osseous tunnel under the **inferior extensor retinaculum**

**Zone of compression** — **1 cm proximal to ankle joint → talonavicular joint**

**Risk factors** — **high heels**, compressive shoe wear (tight laces, ski boots), previous fracture

### Anterior tarsal tunnel anatomy

| Border | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Roof (superficial)** | **Inferior extensor retinaculum** |
| **Floor (deep)** | **Talonavicular joint capsule** |
| Lateral | Lateral malleolus |
| Medial | Medial malleolus |

**Contents** — **EDL, EHL, tibialis anterior, peroneus tertius, DPN, dorsalis pedis artery + vein**\
✗ Wrong options: superficial peroneal nerve · posterior tibial artery · intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve · medial calcaneal nerve

**At risk at release** — **dorsalis pedis artery** (vascular bundle runs with the DPN)

**DPN in tunnel** — divides into **lateral (mixed motor + sensory → EDB)** and **medial (sensory only → 1st web space)** branches · nerve lies **lateral to EHL**

**EHB** — crosses superficially over the inferior extensor retinaculum → contributor to DPN compression (OB)

![Anterior tarsal tunnel – DPN compression sites under the extensor retinacula](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7043-anatomy.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Intrinsic** — **dorsal osteophytes over tibiotalar or talonavicular joints** (commonest) · pes cavus, post-fracture deformity (**navicular nonunion**) · ganglion · tumour · hypertrophic EHL / EDL / TA belly · peripheral oedema

**Extrinsic** — **tight laces / ski boots** · **high heels** (plantar flexion) · trauma incl. recurrent ankle instability

**Stretch position** — **ankle plantar flexion + inversion**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **vague dorsal foot pain** · paraesthesia **lateral hallux, medial 2nd toe, 1st web space**

**Motor** — **EDB weakness / atrophy**

**Sensory** — ↓ two-point discrimination

**Provocative** — **Tinel over DPN radiating to 1st web space** · worse with **plantar flexion + inversion** · relief with **lidocaine DPN block**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **Lateral foot + ankle X-ray** | **Dorsal osteophytes**, fracture sequelae |
| CT | Bony anatomy of canal |
| **MRI** | **Mass lesions** (ganglion, tumour) |

## 5 Management

```
DPN entrapment
├─ First line → shoe modification + NSAIDs (≥3 mo)
└─ Failed nonop → release inferior extensor retinaculum + osteophyte / ganglion resection
       └─ RSD symptoms → contraindication to release
```

**Nonoperative** — **padded shoe tongue**, **alternative lacing** (skip the tender area), full-length rocker-sole steel shank, **night splint** (ankle drifts into plantar flexion), PT if instability, injection, diuretic for chronic oedema · **≥ 3 months** (OB)

**Surgery** — **S-shaped dorsal incision** ankle → base of 1st/2nd MT · start distal, find nerve, release **both branches** proximally · resect osteophytes, debulk muscle bellies · no compressive shoes after

**Outcome** — **80% good–excellent** · recovery prolonged; **persistent symptoms** = main complication

### Foot drop – tendon transfer (TO)

**Complete CPN palsy (anterior + lateral groups) (TO)** — motor of choice = **tibialis posterior** to dorsum of foot → removes unopposed inversion + restores dorsiflexion · ± **FDL with tib post** (double transfer to EDL + EHL) · or **anterior transposition of lateral gastrocnemius + peroneal neurotization** (TO)

**Isolated DPN palsy (peronei spared) (TO)** — **rerouting the peroneus longus** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: tib post transfer (removes inversion balance against working evertors) · FDL + tib post · gastrocnemius transposition · foot drop splint

## X vs Y

| | **Deep peroneal nerve** | **Superficial peroneal nerve** |
|---|---|---|
| Tunnel | **Anterior tarsal tunnel** (inferior extensor retinaculum) | Not in tunnel |
| Sensation | **1st web space** | Dorsum of foot |
| Motor in foot | **EDB** (+ **EHB**, Orthobullets nerves note) | — |

## How it's asked

**"Dorsal foot pain, medial 2nd-toe tingling, worse PF + inversion — structure in same tunnel to protect?"** → **dorsalis pedis artery**\
✗ Wrong options: SPN · PTA

**"Commonest intrinsic cause?"** → **dorsal talonavicular / tibiotalar osteophytes**

**"Muscle weak or atrophied?"** → **EDB**

**"Isolated deep peroneal palsy, foot drop — ideal procedure?"** → **reroute peroneus longus** (TO)

**"Complete CPN palsy — tendon transfer motor?"** → **tibialis posterior** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Diabetic Charcot Neuropathy

*Foot & Ankle · 15 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — chronic, progressive, **non-infectious destructive arthropathy** from **loss of protective sensation** (pain + proprioception) · first described by **Charcot in tabes dorsalis, 1868** (TO)

**Incidence** — **0.1–1.4%** of diabetics (Orthobullets) · acute Charcot **0.15–2.5%** (OB) · **1% of diabetics after 12 yr of disease** (TO) · **7.5%** of diabetics **with neuropathy** — read the options

**Age** — **type 1 DM → 5th decade** (20–25 yr after diagnosis) · **type 2 DM → 6th decade** (5–10 yr after diagnosis)

**Bilateral** — **9–35%**

**Site** — **foot and ankle commonest** · also shoulder, elbow · knee → ligamentous instability + bone loss

**Commonest cause** — **diabetic neuropathy** (now) · **tabes dorsalis** = former commonest lower-limb cause, now **2nd** (TO)

### Pathophysiology, classification & upper-limb Charcot

**Neurotraumatic theory** — insensate joint + **repetitive microtrauma** → no protective response · overuse injury (TO)

**Neurovascular theory** — **autonomic dysfunction → ↑ blood flow (AV shunting, hyperaemia)** → bone resorption, periarticular osteopenia, demineralisation (TO)

**Combined (TO)** — both neurotraumatic + neurovascular · often initiated by trauma, frequently unrecalled · **osteoclast overactivity**, vascular shunting · twisting / minor injury may trigger (OB)

| Cause | Typical joints (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Diabetes (commonest)** | **Tarsus + tarsometatarsal (commonest)** · MTP · **ankle > knee + spine** |
| **Tabes dorsalis (2nd commonest)** | **Knee (commonest)** · hip · ankle · lumbar spine |
| **Leprosy (Hansen's)** | IP joints (hand) · MTP (feet) · lower limbs |
| **Syringomyelia** | **Shoulder (commonest)** · elbow · wrist · cervical spine |
| **Myelomeningocele** | Ankle · intertarsal |

**Upper-limb Charcot (TO)** — **syringomyelia = commonest cause** (rare overall) (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Hansen's · diabetes · tabes

**Other causes** — alcoholism / alcoholic neuropathy · leprosy · myelomeningocele / spina bifida · tertiary syphilis · syringomyelia · post-traumatic sensory deficit · **stroke with residual sensory deficit** · HMSN / **Charcot-Marie-Tooth** · congenital insensitivity to pain · amyloidosis · MS · **intra-articular steroids in weight-bearing joints** · RA · methotrexate toxicity · idiopathic

**NOT a cause** — **Reiter's syndrome**

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Role |
|---|---|
| **CGRP** (TO) | Normally **antagonises RANKL synthesis** at nerve terminal · **non-functioning in neuropathic foot** → RANKL unchecked · maintains **joint capsule integrity** → loss = destruction + dislocation |
| **RANKL** (TO) | Drives **osteoclastogenesis** → excess bone turnover |
| **OPG** (TO) | **Decoy receptor** for RANKL · RANKL–OPG balance **disrupted** |
| **IL-1, TNF-α** | ↑ **NF-κB** + **RANK / RANKL / OPG** pathway |
| **✗ Wrong option: transforming growth factor-beta (TGF-β)** | **Not** a key marker (TO) |
| **ESR, CRP, WBC** | **↑ in both** Charcot + infection → don't discriminate |
| **Wound healing** | **Absolute lymphocyte count > 1500/mm³** · **albumin > 3.0 g/dL** |
| **HbA1c > 8.0%** | Poor control → **poor surgical outcomes** |
| **Histology** | **Synovial hypertrophy** · **detritic synovitis** (cartilage + bone fragments in synovium) |

**Bisphosphonates (pamidronate)** — theoretical role, ↑ osteoclast activity (TO)

## 3 Presentation

### Clinical features

**Charcot's triad (TO)** — **gross joint swelling** · **exaggerated movements (instability)** · **painless** presentation (TO)

**Pain** — **painless classically**; absence of pain diagnostic (TO) · usually **some pain**, less than expected (TO) · **50% painful / 50% painless** (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Single joint first** · swollen, deformed, subluxed, unstable ("floppy") · **crepitus** from loose bodies (TO)

**Acute** — **swollen, warm, erythematous**; **3.3 °C warmer** (Orthobullets) · **3–7° warmer** (OB) · mimics infection · **intact skin + loss of protective sensation** ≈ pathognomonic

**Chronic** — structurally deformed · bony prominences · **rocker-bottom deformity** · **medial arch collapse** · ligamentous instability

**Skin rule (TO)** — **skin intact → think Charcot** · **skin broken → most likely infection** (TO)

**Undiagnosed patient with Charcot foot** — **evaluate for diabetes mellitus**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding / fact |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP + lateral foot, ankle series) | **Early** — mimics OA · **Late** — joint space obliteration, **fragmentation of both articular surfaces → subluxation / dislocation**, bone "chunks" in fibrous tissue, soft-tissue oedema, distension, HO |
| **X-ray (TO)** | Marked destruction, **periarticular erosions**, JSN, **osteophytes**, **subchondral sclerosis**, **loose bodies** — simulates OA, **absence of pain diagnostic** |
| **Technetium bone scan** | **Positive in both** neuropathic joint + osteomyelitis (high turnover, non-specific) |
| **Indium WBC scan** | **Cold (negative) in neuropathic joint** · **hot in osteomyelitis** |
| **Sulfur colloid marrow / indium-111** | Added to Tc for specificity |
| **MRI (with + without contrast)** | **Most sensitive for soft tissue infection + osteomyelitis** · **best for abscess vs soft-tissue swelling** · **cannot reliably separate Charcot from infection** (T2 / STIR oedema in both) |
| Ultrasound | Fluid collections; less specific |
| **Biopsy** | **Guides antibiotics** in osteomyelitis / abscess |

![Charcot ankle — destruction and fragmentation of the hindfoot (TO)](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17142017-1.png)

### Diagnosis – elevation test, neuropathy & Charcot vs infection imaging

**Elevation test** — **erythema ↓ / resolves with elevation** (15–30 min, pillows, overnight) = **Charcot** · **unchanged in infection** (cellulitis, osteomyelitis, abscess) = **key differentiator**

**Dependent rubor** — redness only when leg dependent → Charcot

**Semmes-Weinstein monofilament (5.07)** — **confirms neuropathy** · **most sensitive test** · sensitivity **40–95%** · neuropathy has greatest effect on diabetic foot pathology\
✗ Wrong options: Thompson · Cotton · syndesmosis squeeze · Babinski

**Favours Charcot over infection** — no ulcer / intact skin · **normal WBC + normal serum glucose** · no fever / sepsis · fracture without ulceration

**Low-grade fever + normal CRP + erythema settles** — still **neuropathic arthropathy**\
✗ Wrong options: navicular stress fracture · osteomyelitis · embolic ischaemia · RA

**Not useful as next step** — inflammatory markers (↑ in both) · MRI (low specificity) · Tc scan alone

### Diagnostic criteria – Charcot vs infection

| Feature | **Charcot** | **Infection / osteomyelitis** |
|---|---|---|
| **Elevation** | **Erythema decreases** | **Unchanged** |
| Skin | **Intact** | **Broken / ulcer** |
| WBC, glucose | Normal | **Leukocytosis, ↑ glucose**, fever |
| ESR / CRP | ↑ | ↑ |
| Tc bone scan | **+** | **+** |
| **Indium WBC** | **Cold** | **Hot** |
| MRI | Oedema | Oedema; **abscess** |

| ✗ Mimic | Distinguishing point |
|---|---|
| **Cellulitis** | Erythema persists on elevation · no radiographic destruction |
| **CRPS** | Aberrant inflammation, **no** Charcot X-ray changes |
| **DVT** | Swelling + **posterior calf tenderness**; erythema (if any) persists |
| **Occult fracture** | Typically **spinal cord injury** patients |
| Gout | **Normal uric acid** · no rapid bony destruction |
| Charcot-Marie-Tooth, Lisfranc injury, Freiberg | Different history / X-ray |

### Brodsky classification (anatomical)

| Type | Joints | Feature | Frequency |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** (midfoot) | **TMT + naviculocuneiform** | **Fixed rocker-bottom with valgus** · **commonest** | **60%** |
| **2** (hindfoot) | **Subtalar, TN, CC** | **Unstable**, immobilise **up to 2 yr** · 2nd commonest (TO) | **10%** (Orthobullets) · **30–35%** (TO) — read the options |
| **3A** | **Tibiotalar (ankle)** | Late varus / valgus → **malleolar ulcer + osteomyelitis**; significant deformity + disability | **20%** |
| **3B** | **Calcaneal tuberosity fracture** | Distal foot changes / **proximal migration of tuberosity** | < 10% |
| 4 | Combination | — | < 10% |
| 5 | **Forefoot only** | — | < 10% |

**Brodsky trap (TO)** — **"Type 3A = forefoot" is FALSE** → 3A = **ankle** · forefoot = type 5 (TO)

![Brodsky classification — types 1, 2, 3A, 3B](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7047-brodsky.jpg)

### Eichenholtz classification (staging)

| Stage | Name | Clinical | X-ray | Treatment (TO) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0** | — | Oedema, erythema, **loss of protective sensation**, instability | **Negative** (bone scan + in all stages) | **Frequent follow-up + serial X-rays** |
| **1** | **Fragmentation** (dissolution, acute) | **Warm, oedema, ↑ ligamentous laxity** | **Osteopenia, periarticular fragmentation, subluxation / dislocation** | **TCC** |
| **2** | **Coalescence** (subacute, reparative) | **↓ oedema + warmth** | **Coalescence**, absorption of fine debris, early fusion, sclerosis | **TCC → CROW** |
| **3** | **Reconstruction** (Orthobullets) · **consolidation** (TO) — chronic | **No inflammation**, stable, often deformed | **Consolidation + remodelling**, arthrosis, osteophytes | Plantigrade → **custom inlay shoes / CROW** · non-plantigrade / recurrent ulcer → **debridement ± arthrodesis** |

**Time to consolidation (TO)** — **forefoot 6 mo** · **midfoot 12 mo** · **hindfoot 18 mo** (TO)

## 5 Management

```
Charcot foot — goal: stable plantigrade foot in shoewear
├─ Acute (Eichenholtz 0–2), skin intact → TOTAL CONTACT CAST
│      └─ then → CROW → depth shoes + double rocker
├─ Braceable, equinus + focal prominence / ulcer → EXOSTECTOMY + TAL
├─ Unbraceable / unstable deformity → REDUCTION + ARTHRODESIS
│      (ring fixator if ulcer / poor bone · TTC nail for ankle + subtalar)
└─ Failed surgery / recurrent infection → AMPUTATION
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **TCC (nonop)** | **First line** — acute Charcot **without skin breakdown** · **75% success** |
| **Exostectomy + TAL** | **"Braceable" foot** with **equinus** + **focal bony prominence** causing skin breakdown / ulcer failing TCC |
| **Deformity correction, arthrodesis ± osteotomy** | **Severe deformity not "braceable"** · unstable deformity · **persistent plantar ulcer** · **unstable Charcot ankle** · failed orthotics |
| **External (ring) fixation** | **Poor bone quality** or **compromised soft tissues / ulcer** |
| **Amputation** | **Failed previous surgery (unstable arthrodesis)** · **recurrent infection / osteomyelitis** · troublesome ulcers |
| **Avoid surgery in acute stage** | Not enough evidence for stage 1 surgery; avoid during inflammation (some authors propose early fixation) |

**Operative indications (Orthobullets)** — recurrent infection · deformity · severe skin breakdown

**Joint replacement** — **relatively contraindicated** (TO)

### Acute stage – total contact casting

**First line / gold standard** — **total contact cast (TCC)** + offloading for **acute (Eichenholtz 1–2) Charcot without ulcer**

**Duration** — cast changed **every 2–4 wk for 2–4 months** (Orthobullets) · **up to 4 months** (OB) · **weekly cast changes until erythema + swelling decrease** (OB) · **2–4 months** (TO) — read the options

**Weight bearing** — **NWB or limited WB = gold standard** until swelling resolves + consolidation on X-ray (✓ "TCC, no weight bearing, weekly cast changes") · **WB TCC effective** in early stages, no harm (De Souza, OB) · NWB **not conclusively necessary** (OB) — read the options

**Alternative** — pneumatic bracing

**Medications** — bisphosphonates · neuropathic pain drugs · antidepressants · topical anaesthetics

**Stage 1 on cast, still warm at 2 months** — **continue TCC**\
✗ Wrong options: gout treatment (uric acid normal) · PT (aggravates stage 1) · ORIF of navicular · biopsy + ID referral

**Goal in fragmentation** — **prevent further collapse + deformity**, avoid high-contact-stress ulceration

| ✗ Wrong first step | Why |
|---|---|
| **IV antibiotics / admission** | No infection |
| **I&D / emergent debridement** | No ulcer, **negative indium scan**, erythema settles |
| **MRI, inflammatory markers, Tc scan alone** | Don't change management (low specificity) |
| **Midfoot / TN + TMT / subtalar / triple arthrodesis, ORIF, ring fixator** | Only after failed nonop, gross deformity or ulceration |
| **Plantar exostectomy** | Not in acute phase |
| **BKA / transtibial amputation** | Salvage only |
| **Shoe modification, custom orthotics** (Jones bar, carbon shank, AFO leaf spring, plastizote insole) | Not for acute stage |

### Footwear & bracing after casting or partial amputation

**CROW (TO)** — **Charcot restraint orthotic walker** · used **in Charcot joint** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar arthritis · ankle arthritis · gout of great toe

**CROW design** — custom **bivalved total-contact AFO** · full foot enclosure · **rigid rocker sole** · custom insole · rigid polypropylene anterior + posterior shell · **DF stop**, heel lift · rocker sole counters **"nutcracker effect"** · Velcro (TO)

**CROW use** — **after TCC** (oedema reduced first) · controls oedema · enables ambulation · **prevents deformity** during coalescence · **removable → hygiene, wound care, comfort**

**CROW drawbacks** — **high cost** · bulky → poor compliance · **knee + lumbar pain** (TO)

![Charcot restraint orthotic walker (CROW)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7047-crow-walking-boot.jpg)

**Sequence** — **TCC → CROW → custom shoe / depth-inlay shoe with accommodative orthoses**

**Eichenholtz 3 shoe** — **double rocker sole** → ↓ ulcer risk at **plantar apex / midfoot prominence**

![Double rocker sole](../../MCQ/attachments/q4467-d-double-rocker.jpg)

| Rocker sole | Use |
|---|---|
| **Double rocker** | **Plantar midfoot prominence (Charcot rocker-bottom)** |
| Severe-angle rocker | Pressure **distal to ball of foot** (toe-tip ulcers) |
| Mild rocker | Mild **MT head relief**, propulsion |
| Negative heel rocker | **Fixed dorsiflexion**, forefoot pressure → shifts load to midfoot + heel |
| **Heel-to-toe rocker** | **After ankle / subtalar fusion** · fixed lesser toe deformities |

**Partial 1st ray amputation (TA intact, 10° DF)** — **steel shank** (flat 1-inch steel strip) → **extends foot lever, prevents deformity at toe break** ± rocker sole\
✗ Wrong options: snug shoe (volume fluctuation) · custom shoe (unnecessary expense) · leather sole (no catching with 10° DF + intact TA) · lateral wedge

### Chronic deformity & ulceration – exostectomy & reconstructive arthrodesis

**Ulcer cause** — malpositioned tarsal bones after joint collapse

**Recurrent midfoot ulcer, failed TCC, stable deformity, equinus** — **exostectomy + Achilles tendon lengthening + protective brace** · limb salvage **up to 90%**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix · BKA (viable tissue) · observation · exostectomy alone (equinus needs lengthening)

**Silfverskiöld test** — **positive → tight gastrocnemius** (gastroc recession) vs whole Achilles complex (TAL)

**Talar head ulcer (Chopart dislocation), failed 2 TCC cycles** — **reduction + arthrodesis of Chopart joint with ring fixator** (no hardware near ulcer, soft tissue monitored)

| ✗ Option for talar head ulcer | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| CROW / repeat offloading | Failed already → contraindicated |
| Achilles lengthening alone | Doesn't reduce talar head |
| **In-situ TTC with IM nail** | TTC suits **Brodsky 2–3**, but **in-situ** fusion contraindicated — must **reduce** |
| Midfoot osteotomy + Lisfranc plates | For **Brodsky 1**; **plates contraindicated** with poor bone + ulcer |

**Charcot ankle + subtalar, failed bracing, no wounds, active** — **TTC fusion with retrograde nail** (small incisions)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior plate tibiotalar fusion (wound complications + misses subtalar) · BKA · TCC (for ulcers) · CAM orthosis

**Fixation** — internal: screws, pins, plates, **tibiocalcaneal nail** (internal may need **staging**) · **external fixation** (poor bone / soft tissue; usually **single stage**)

**Post-op** — **minimal WB for 3 months**

### Amputation level (Chopart)

**Forefoot gangrene with necrosis up to navicular (smoker, hypertensive) (TO)** — **Chopart amputation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Pirogoff · Syme · Lisfranc

**Chopart advantages (TO)** — **conserves heel support** · technically easier · **shoe + AFO** instead of knee-high prosthesis · little shortening · **heel skin = weight-bearing end** · easier muscle balancing (TO)

**Equinus (TO)** — common cause of foot amputation failure · Chopart → **tibialis anterior transfer proximally + tendo-Achilles lengthening** · Syme → only TA lengthening, slight equinus ruins ambulation (TO)

**Goal** — partial / limited amputation if vascularity allows · BKA for severe deformity with soft-tissue involvement or failed reconstruction

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Arthrodesis complications** | **Up to 70%** — hardware failure, infection, malposition, **recurrent ulceration**, fracture |
| **Overall complication rate** | **50–56%** (Sämann) |
| **Recurrence (secondary episodes)** | **Type 1 DM 41.6% vs type 2 18.3%** |
| **Amputation rate** | **3–9%** — depends on avoiding ulceration (TO) |
| Late complications | Chronic ulceration → infection → **osteomyelitis** |
| Ankle (Brodsky 3A) | **Malleolar ulcer + osteomyelitis** |

## X vs Y

| | **Technetium bone scan** | **Indium WBC scan** | **MRI** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Charcot | **Hot** | **Cold** | Oedema |
| Osteomyelitis | **Hot** | **Hot** | Oedema / abscess |
| Key fact | Sensitive, non-specific | **Differentiates** | **Most sensitive**, can't separate |

| | **TCC** | **CROW** |
|---|---|---|
| Stage | **Acute, first line** | **After TCC** |
| Removable | No | **Yes** (hygiene, wounds) |
| Downside | Cast changes | **Cost**, bulk |

| | **Exostectomy + TAL** | **Reconstructive arthrodesis** |
|---|---|---|
| Foot | **Braceable**, stable, focal prominence | **Unbraceable / unstable** |
| Fixation | — | Ring fixator (ulcer) · TTC nail (ankle) |

## How it's asked

**"Diabetic, painless ankle swelling, prior ray amputation, X-ray destruction?"** → **Charcot arthropathy** (TO)

**"Charcot foot in undiagnosed patient — also evaluate?"** → **diabetes mellitus**

**"Floppy ankle, erythema resolves with elevation — exam?"** → **Semmes-Weinstein monofilament testing**

**"Charcot knee — possible causes EXCEPT?"** → **Reiter's syndrome**

**"Midfoot erythema diminishes with 15-min elevation?"** → **neuropathic arthropathy**

**"Type 1 diabetic, crepitus after twist, hyperaemia ↓ with elevation?"** → **Charcot arthropathy**

**"Diagnosis + associated finding?"** → **Charcot arthropathy; erythema decreases after elevation**

**"Most sensitive imaging for soft tissue infection?"** → **MRI with and without contrast**

**"Redness gone after 30 min elevation?"** → **Charcot arthropathy**

**"Infection vs neuropathic — true?"** → **indium WBC scan negative (cold) for neuropathic joints**

**"Erythema resolves on pillows, type 1 DM?"** → **Charcot arthropathy**

**"Charcot foot, 6-mo gait disturbance — initial management?"** → **total contact casting**

**"Swelling settles overnight, fragmentation on X-ray?"** → **total contact cast and non-weight bearing**

**"Normal indium WBC scan — next?"** → **total contact casting**

**"Red foot, ESR 40, normal WBC + glucose, fracture on MRI?"** → **TCC, no weight bearing, weekly cast changes until erythema and swelling decrease**

**"Stage 1, still warm after 2 months in cast?"** → **continued total contact cast immobilization**

**"Diabetic + prior stroke, red foot settles with elevation?"** → **total contact casting**

**"HbA1c 8.5%, dependent rubor — initial?"** → **total contact casting**

**"Recurrent midfoot ulcer, failed TCC, equinus?"** → **exostectomy & Achilles tendon lengthening with protective brace**

**"Talar head ulcer after 2 TCC cycles?"** → **reduction and arthrodesis of the Chopart joint using a ring fixator**

**"Charcot ankle + subtalar, failed bracing, active golfer?"** → **tibiotalar and subtalar fusion with retrograde TTC nail**

**"CROW orthosis used in?"** → **Charcot joint** (TO)

**"Eichenholtz 3 after TCC — shoe to prevent breakdown?"** → **double rocker sole** (Figure D)

**"Partial 1st ray amputation — shoe?"** → **steel shank to extend the foot lever**

**"Forefoot gangrene to navicular — level?"** → **Chopart** (TO)

**"Brodsky — NOT true?"** → **Type 3A represents forefoot changes** (TO)

**"Commonest cause of upper-limb Charcot?"** → **syringomyelia** (TO)

**"Key Charcot markers EXCEPT?"** → **transforming growth factor-beta (TGF-β)** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Diabetic Foot Ulcers

*Foot & Ankle · 16 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **\~12% of diabetics** have foot ulcers · **commonest medical complication** making diabetics seek treatment

**Amputation** — ulcers cause **\~85% of lower-extremity amputations** · diabetes = **15–40× ↑ amputation risk** · **diabetic foot ulceration = most likely predictor of eventual amputation**

**PVD** — **60–70%** of patients with diabetes > 10 yr · **> 60% of diabetic ulcers** have ↓ blood flow

**Diabetic foot infection** — cause of amputation in **25–50%** · osteomyelitis ↑ amputation risk **up to 4×**

**Charcot** — **7.5% of neuropathic diabetics** · neuropathy in 30–50% of diabetics, neuropathic fracture only 1–2.5%

### Pathophysiology

**Neuropathy** — **largest effect** · **sensory → loss of protective sensation = primary risk factor** for ulcer · **autonomic → dry skin** (no glandular function) · motor → intrinsic dysfunction → dynamic toe deformity

**Angiopathy** — **lesser effect** than neuropathy

**Mechanical** — **equinus → forefoot (MT head) overload** · ↓ MTPJ motion → ↑ IP pressure on hallux · **peroneus longus overpull → plantarflexed 1st MT** → 1st MT head ulcer

**Three pillars of treatment** — **adequate blood supply** · **treat infection** · **pressure relief**

### Risk factors for ulceration & amputation (neuropathy, smoking)

**Most likely to ulcerate** — **cannot feel 5.07 Semmes-Weinstein monofilament** — vascular + nutrition tests predict **healing**, not ulcer formation\
✗ Wrong options: TcpO2 30 · ABI < 0.6 · albumin 3.1 · autonomic dry skin · Silfverskiöld result

**Concurrent variables in amputees** — **ulceration 84%** · neuropathy 64% · infection 59% · gangrene 55% · ischaemia 46% · + ↑ HbA1c, ↓ oxygen tension, ↓ ABI

**Least likely to predict amputation** — **ABI 1.07** (normal)\
✗ Wrong options: ulcer · monofilament loss · infection · HbA1c 10.7

**Only consistent modifiable risk factors** — **smoking + glycaemic control** (Rossboth)

**Single best programme to cut amputations** — **smoking cessation with counselling + pharmacotherapy**\
✗ Wrong options: annual plantar-pressure screening + custom footwear · mandatory TCC for all · routine arterial US · GLP-1 weight-loss clinic

**Infection risk factors** — ulcer **> 30 days** · recurrent ulcer · trauma · **probe to bone** · PAD

### Microbiology

**Usually polymicrobial** — **aerobic gram-positive cocci (S. aureus) commonest** · **gram-negatives ↑ in chronic wounds / recent antibiotics** · **obligate anaerobes with ischaemia / gangrene**

**Gold standard** — **deep cultures + bone biopsy** guide management

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — often **painless**

**Exam** — **depth (probe for bone)** · infection (cellulitis, pus, gangrene) · **Silfverskiöld** · **DP + PT pulses** · hair on toes (absent = poor flow)

**Signs of infection blunted** — ↓ WBC function, PAD, poor metabolic control, neuropathy · commonest signs = **↑ exudate + worsening glycaemic control** · ↑ WBC / ESR / CRP / fever **absent in 50%**

| Ulcer location | Driver | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Plantar MT heads (1st / 2nd)** | **Equinus** | **TCC + TAL / gastrocnemius recession** |
| **1st MT head** | Equinus **+ PL overpull** | **Gastrocsoleus recession + PL → PB transfer** |
| **Plantar hallux IP joint** | **Stiff / rigid MTPJ** | **Keller resection arthroplasty** |
| **Toe tip (apical)** | **Flexible claw / hammer toe** | **Percutaneous flexor tenotomy** |
| **Plantar midfoot** | **Charcot rocker-bottom** | **Exostectomy + TAL** |
| **Heel** | Bed-bound pressure · calcaneal osteomyelitis | Offloading / padding boots · **partial calcanectomy + TAL** |
| Medial malleolus | **Venous stasis** (rarely diabetic) | — |
| Dry toe gangrene | **Arterial insufficiency** | Toe disarticulation |

### Silfverskiöld test

**Technique** — **forefoot inverted, hindfoot subtalar neutral** (locks transverse tarsal joints) · compare passive DF **knee flexed vs extended**

| Finding | Contracture | Procedure |
|---|---|---|
| **DF improves with knee flexion** | **Isolated gastrocnemius** | **Gastrocnemius recession (Strayer)** |
| **DF equal with knee flexed + extended** | **Gastrocsoleus / Achilles** | **Achilles tendon lengthening (Hoke)** |

![Silfverskiöld test — ankle dorsiflexion with knee extended vs flexed](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7046-silverskiold-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

| Condition | Criterion |
|---|---|
| **Neuropathy (TO)** | **10 g (5.07 Semmes-Weinstein) monofilament** not perceived **all 3 times** (TO) |
| **Osteomyelitis** | **Bone biopsy with cultures + pathology = gold standard** · **exposed bone → presume osteomyelitis** |
| **Infected ulcer** | ↑ exudate · ↑ WBC / ESR / CRP · worsening glucose · erythema **persisting despite elevation** |
| **Healing potential** | **TcpO2 = gold standard** (see labs) |

**Monofilament technique (TO)** — **10 g monofilament** · eyes closed · **90° to skin**, pressed **until it buckles 1 cm** · patient says "yes" · **repeat 3 times** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 1 g · 5 g · 1 g polyfilament

**Monofilament accuracy** — sensitivity **41–93% / 57–93%**, specificity **68–100% / 75–100%** · **3-site test: plantar great toe, 3rd MT, 5th MT** (Feng)

### Genes & lab markers – healing predictors (vascular studies & nutritional labs)

| Marker | Favours healing | Poor healing / risk |
|---|---|---|
| **TcpO2** (transcutaneous oxygen) | **> 30 mmHg** (or **40**, by source) — **gold standard** for healing potential | **< 30–40** · **< 20** · **15**, 24, 25 mmHg = poor |
| **ABI** | **> 0.45** (Orthobullets, most OB) · **> 0.60** to heal an ulcer (one OB) — read the options | **< 0.45** · **falsely ↑ by arterial calcification** (↓ compliance) |
| **Toe pressure** | **> 40 mmHg** (OB) · **> 45 amputation, > 60 ulcer** (Orthobullets) | **< 30 mmHg** (amputation wound risk) |
| **Albumin** | **> 3.0 g/dL** | **< 3.0** (most) · **< 3.5** (one OB) — read the options |
| **Total lymphocyte count** | **> 1,500 /mm³** | **< 1,500** |
| **Haemoglobin** (amputation) | **> 11.0 g/dL** | < 11 |
| **Total protein** (amputation) | **> 6.2 g/dL** | < 6.2 |
| **HbA1c** | — | **> 8.0** |
| Doppler / exam | **Triphasic waveforms**, hair on toes | Absent pulses, no hair |
| **Systolic BP > 140** | **Not predictive** | — |

**Next test with absent pulses** — ABI + TcpO2 (noninvasive vascular) · with **palpable pulses → Silfverskiöld**

**Pre-TMA labs — which is the risk?** — **albumin 2.9 g/dL**\
✗ Wrong options: Hb 12.5 · total protein 6.9 · TLC 1,700 · toe pressure 55

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / sign |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP, lateral, oblique foot + ankle) | Demineralisation · **periarticular erosions** · **cortical erosions = late, nonspecific** (confused with Charcot) · abnormal film LR+ 2.3 |
| **MRI** | **Best for abscess vs soft-tissue swelling** · **most sensitive for osteomyelitis** (↑ T2 marrow signal) · LR+ 3.8 / LR– 0.14, accuracy 89% · **hard to separate infection vs Charcot** · less accurate in **ischaemic** ulcers |
| **Bone scan** (Tc-99m, Ga-67, In-111) | Separates **soft-tissue infection vs osteomyelitis vs Charcot** · **↑ indium-labelled WBC = osteomyelitis** · **leukocyte scintigraphy** best for infection vs Charcot |
| **Charcot X-ray** | **Midfoot destruction, fragmentation, osteopenia, subluxation** · rocker-bottom |

**Midfoot plantar ulcer, metformin + gabapentin — X-ray?** — **Charcot midfoot**\
✗ Wrong options: Lisfranc · hallux valgus · CMT cavus · flatfoot without fragmentation

### Osteomyelitis – bone biopsy diagnosis

| Finding | Value |
|---|---|
| **Probe to bone** | **67%** osteomyelitis (Orthobullets) · **\~70%** / nearly ⅔ (OB) · **most specific clinical finding** · sens **66%**, spec **85%**, PPV 89%, NPV 56% · LR+ 6.4 |
| **Exposed bone** | Highly predictive — presume osteomyelitis |
| **Ulcer > 2 cm²** | LR+ 7.2 |
| **ESR > 70 mm/h** | LR+ **11** |
| **Bone biopsy (culture + histology)** | **Most accurate / most specific / gold standard** |
| **Wound / swab culture** | **Positive at all stages → not predictive** of osteomyelitis |
| CBC | Unhelpful (immunocompromise, vascular insufficiency) |
| CRP / ESR | Helpful, **not diagnostic** |

**No single history / exam finding excludes osteomyelitis**

**✗ Biopsy traps** — HbA1c > 15% · ESR + CRP · probe-to-bone · cortical erosions · CBC · technetium scan · WB radiographs

### Wagner classification & monofilament screening (TO)

| Grade | Orthobullets | TO table | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0** | Skin intact, bony deformity — "**foot at risk**" | No open lesion; may have deformity or cellulitis | **Shoe modification + serial exams** |
| **1** | **Superficial** ulcer | Superficial, **partial or full thickness** (not exposing ligament, deep fascia, bone) | **Office debridement + TCC** |
| **2** | **Deeper, full-thickness** extension | To **ligament, tendon, joint capsule, deep fascia**; **no abscess / osteomyelitis** | **Operative debridement + TCC** |
| **3** | **Deep abscess or osteomyelitis** | + **joint sepsis** | **Operative debridement + TCC** (I&D + antibiotics before casting) |
| **4** | **Partial forefoot gangrene** | Gangrene of portion of forefoot **or heel** | **Local vs larger amputation** |
| **5** | **Extensive gangrene** | Entire foot | **Amputation** |

**Exposed tendons, no bone / abscess (TO)** — **Wagner grade 2** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 1 · 3 · 4

**TO wording conflict** — TO text: ulcers "breaking through superficial fascia… with **exposed bone or tendon** = grade 2" · TO table + Orthobullets: **abscess / osteomyelitis = grade 3** — read the options

### Brodsky depth-ischaemia classification

| Depth | Definition | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | At-risk foot, no ulcer | Education, accommodative footwear, regular exams (**shoe modification**) |
| **1** | Superficial, not infected | **Offload — TCC**, walking brace, special footwear |
| **2** | **Deep, exposing tendons / joints** | Surgical debridement, wound care, offload, culture-specific antibiotics |
| **3** | **Extensive ulcer or abscess** (exposed bone, osteomyelitis) | Debridement / partial amputation, offload, culture-specific antibiotics |

| Ischaemia | Definition | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Not ischaemic | — |
| **B** | Ischaemia, no gangrene | Noninvasive vascular testing → **angioplasty / bypass** |
| **C** | **Partial forefoot gangrene** | Vascular reconstruction + **partial foot amputation** |
| **D** | **Complete gangrene** | Full vascular work-up + **major amputation** |

![Brodsky depth-ischaemia classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7046-brodsky.jpg)

### Mycetoma (Madura foot) (TO)

**Definition (TO)** — **chronic granulomatous infection of skin + subcutaneous tissue** · fungi (**eumycetoma**) or bacteria (**actinomycetoma**) · **bacteria \~60%** (TO)

**Commonest organism (TO)** — **Actinomadura madurae** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Pseudallescheria boydii · A. pelletieri · Madurella mycetomatis

| Actinomycetes (bacteria) (TO) | Eumycetes (fungi) (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Actinomadura madurae** · Actinomadura pelletieri · Streptomyces somaliensis · Nocardia | Pseudallescheria boydii · Madurella mycetomatis |

![Madura foot — swollen deformed foot with sinuses](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17129494-1.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Diabetic foot ulcer
├─ Infected / abscess / osteomyelitis → I&D + bone culture → culture-specific abx
│      └─ infection cleared → TCC ± TAL
├─ Clean plantar ulcer, perfused → wound care + TCC
│      └─ forefoot + equinus → add TAL / gastrocnemius recession
├─ Ischaemic → vascular studies → revascularise
└─ Gangrene → amputation (level by PT pulse + labs)
```

```
Forefoot ulcer with limited dorsiflexion
├─ Pulses absent → ABI / TcpO2 first
├─ DF improves with knee flexion → gastrocnemius recession (Strayer)
└─ No improvement with knee flexion → Achilles tendon lengthening (Hoke)
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **I&D + antibiotics before casting** | **Wagner ≥ 3** · deep full-thickness ulcer failing TCC |
| **Flexor tendon tenotomy** | **Flexible toe deformity + toe-tip ulcer** (high healing **if no osteomyelitis**) |
| **TAL / gastrocnemius recession** | **Plantar forefoot ulcer + equinus** (DF ≤ 5°) |
| **Ostectomy ± TAL** | **Bony prominence** causing internal pressure (TAL if tight Achilles) |
| **Partial calcanectomy ± TAL** | **Large heel ulcer + calcaneal osteomyelitis** |
| **Keller resection arthroplasty** | **Plantar hallux IPJ ulcer + stiff MTPJ, failed TCC** |
| **Deformity correction + TAL** | **Nonplantigrade, rigid Charcot deformity** with recurrent ulcers |
| **Syme amputation** | **Forefoot gangrene + palpable posterior tibial pulse** |

### Total contact casting, offloading & NPWT

**Wound care** — **first line** · moist environment · absorb exudate · barrier · offload

**TCC** — **gold standard for mechanical relief of plantar ulcers** · snug below-knee · protects **insensate** limb, keeps patient ambulatory · lower infection, admission, lost income

**TCC contraindications** — **absolute: infection** · relative: marginal arterial supply, poor compliance with cast care, cast claustrophobia

**TCC duration** — changed every **3–4 wk**, prevents daily wound care · total **several months (up to 4 months)** · 83% heal, 17% complications (skin irritation)\
✗ Wrong options: ≤ 4 weeks

**Recurrence after TCC** — typically **3–4 wk after removal** = **greatest risk within 1 month** (newly healed tissue weak to vertical + shear stress) · most within first 6 months

**Sequence** — **TCC (non-removable) → CROW (removable) → custom shoe / orthotic**

**Clean 3-month plantar ulcer, no bone, normal labs** — **total contact casting**\
✗ Wrong options: TCC + empiric IV antibiotics · debridement + antibiotics · CROW

**Infected heel ulcer — NOT indicated** — **total contact casting** · indicated: surgical debridement, tighter glycaemic control, imaging (MRI), culture-directed antibiotics

**✗ TCC traps** — replace weekly with active infection · cast ≤ 4 wk · avoid in insensate feet · custom insoles equally effective (orthoses **prevent**, TCC **treats**) · orthotics > TCC (TCC heals faster, compliance)

**Non-removable > removable** — non-removable casts heal better than removable casts / dressings · **pneumatic walker** = alternative, better wound surveillance, but **removable → compliance**; no evidence of superiority

**NPWT** — **↓ ulcer size + time to healing** · for perfused, uninfected, offloaded ulcers failing standard care / deep after debridement · granulation, ↓ oedema, removes exudate, ↑ local flow\
✗ Wrong options: custom shoes · electrophysical therapy · hyperbaric oxygen (mixed) · topical PDGF

**Without offloading** — debridement or NPWT alone **won't** heal · VAC does **not** reduce recurrence

### Achilles / gastrocnemius lengthening for forefoot ulcers (Silfverskiöld)

**Rationale** — equinus → cyclic forefoot overload → MT head ulcers · percutaneous TAL **↓ forefoot peak pressure \~25%**

**Mueller RCT (TCC ± TAL, DF ≤ 5°)** — healing **100% vs 88%** (no difference) · recurrence **↓ 75% at 7 mo, ↓ 52% at 2 yr** · **81% vs 38%** recurrence at 2 yr

**Lengthening effect** — **↓ recurrence**, not faster healing of active ulcers vs TCC alone · Colen **25% vs 2%** recurrence (94% RRR) — avoid over-lengthening

**TAL + TCC vs TCC alone** — **lower forefoot ulcer recurrence at 2 years** (✗ Wrong options: higher / same · ↑ BKA) · **↑ heel peak pressure → more heel-pad breakdown** (✗ Wrong options: lower)

**Static calf stretching** — **no effect** on ROM or plantar pressure

**Gastrocnemius recession (Strayer) benefits most** — **chronic plantar forefoot ulcer**\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal tendinitis · Freiberg · retrocalcaneal bursitis · calcaneal osteomyelitis

| Scenario | Answer | ✗ Traps |
|---|---|---|
| Forefoot ulcer, no bone, no infection, **palpable pulses** — what next? | **Silfverskiöld test** | MRI, ABI, TcpO2, HbA1c |
| Wagner 1, **failed TCC 8 mo**, gastrocnemius contracture | **Strayer procedure** | Integra + STSG, STSG, more TCC, Weil osteotomy |
| Bilateral **5th MT head** ulcers 2 yr, minimal DF | **Gastrocnemius release + wound care** | Plastazote cut-out, HBO + NWB, healing shoe, **TMA (salvage)** |
| **1st MT head** grade II, failed orthosis + TCC, no deformity | **Gastrocsoleus recession + PL → PB transfer** | Plastazote, 1st MT head resection, ray / TMA |
| DF 10° short, **no change with knee flexion**, failed 3 mo TCC | **Achilles lengthening** | 3 more months TCC, debridement, NPWT, MT head excision |
| MT head ulcer — heals + ↓ recurrence | **Achilles lengthening procedure** | Keep HbA1c 9.2, TcpO2 24, **narrow toe box**, flexor tenotomy |
| Progressed to toe gangrene, no Silfverskiöld change — early step? | **Achilles tendon lengthening** | FDL tenotomy, **isolated gastrocnemius recession**, Keller, pneumatic walker first |
| Healed recurrent forefoot ulcer, rigid equinus | **Ostectomy + Achilles tendon lengthening** | Ostectomy + gastrocnemius recession, revascularisation, TCC in neutral |

**Midfoot / forefoot gastrocsoleus recession** — 10/11 midfoot + 19/20 forefoot healed · add **PL lengthening (1st MT head)** or **tib post lengthening (5th MT head)** (Laborde)

### Osteomyelitis – treatment

**Highest success** — **IV antibiotics based on percutaneous bone biopsy culture sensitivity** · only factor affecting remission · remission only **64% at 1 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: empiric IV vancomycin · oral levofloxacin · ulcer-swab sensitivities · elevation + NWB

**Deep full-thickness ulcer, failed 2 mo TCC, erythema persists despite elevation** — **intra-operative debridement + irrigation with culture** → then antibiotics + casting\
✗ Wrong options: continue TCC · 6 wk empiric IV · office wound culture (nonspecific) · skin graft

**Surgery** — **remove infected + necrotic bone** · pathogens **polymicrobial or S. aureus**

**Greatest predictor of failure → amputation** — **presence of osteomyelitis** (e.g. sesamoid) · nonop sesamoid osteomyelitis → **15/18 needed surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: first ulcer (first ulcers heal better) · HbA1c 7.2 · TcpO2 48 · TAL

### Surgical offloading – exostectomy, deformity correction, phalangeal resection, partial calcanectomy

**Wagner 3 heel ulcer + calcaneal osteomyelitis, failed debridement + TCC** — **partial calcanectomy** (+ bone biopsy, debridement, antibiotics) · preserves limb length, ↓ morbidity vs Syme / BKA\
✗ Wrong options: ankle disarticulation · BKA · fasciocutaneous / free flap (only for uninfected ulcers)

**Partial calcanectomy technique** — remove posterior process; cut **1 cm posterior to subtalar + CC joints** · **Achilles released, retracts** · custom-moulded orthosis · 10/12 healed

![Partial calcanectomy — line of resection](../../MCQ/attachments/q751-illa-partial-calacanectomy.jpg)

**Charcot plantar midfoot ulcer, recurrent 2 yr, no osteomyelitis** — **midfoot exostectomy + Achilles tendon lengthening** (+ debridement, TCC)\
✗ Wrong options: CROW · VAC · in-situ midfoot / subtalar fusion (none ↓ recurrence)

**Nonplantigrade rigid Charcot, ulcer under talar head** — **surgical correction of deformity + TAL + therapeutic footwear** · recurrence likely unless corrected\
✗ Wrong options: depth-inlay shoes + orthosis · CROW · TAL + TCC + CROW · Syme

**Charcot surgery goal** — **stable, plantigrade, ulcer-free foot** · ulcer + deep infection → **15% major amputation**

**Hallux IP plantar ulcer, rigid MTPJ, failed 3× TCC** — **Keller: resect base of proximal phalanx** → restores MTPJ motion, ↓ IP pressure · K-wire keeps plantigrade toe, **5 mm MTP gap**, prevents cock-up\
✗ Wrong options: CROW · distal phalanx base · proximal phalanx head · MT head

![Keller resection arthroplasty with K-wire](../../MCQ/attachments/q5739-illa-illus-a.jpg)

### Toe-tip ulcers – flexor tenotomy

**Indication** — **apical toe ulcer + passively correctable claw / hammer toe** · dynamic **flexor stabilisation** (toes curl at heel lift) linked to apical ulcers · prophylactic with callus

**Outcome** — healing **> 95%** · complications **< 15%** · incision wound complications **< 5%** · **office / outpatient**, percutaneous needle

**Known complication** — **similar ulcer on adjacent toe (transfer lesion)** **8%** → adjacent tenotomy

**✗ Tenotomy traps** — > 50% wound complications · **MT head ulcers** (persist → gastrocnemius recession) · osteomyelitis irrelevant (failures mostly had osteomyelitis) · needs inpatient stay

### Footwear & orthoses (therapeutic shoe bill, rocker sole, depth-inlay, Plastazote)

**Shoe modification** — **prevention** (Brodsky 0 / Wagner 0) · deep / wide shoes, custom insoles, rocker soles · wide toe box + rocker → better healing, ↓ recurrence

**Best forefoot offloading shoe** — **rocker sole**\
✗ Wrong options: polyethylene foam insole · open-toe sandals · custom in-depth shoes · hard post-op shoe

**Healed first Wagner II ulcer + hammertoe, insensate** — moderate risk → **depth-inlay shoes + custom accommodative foot orthosis**\
✗ Wrong options: nothing until second ulcer · Oxford rubber-sole shoes · DF MT osteotomy · TAL

**Plastazote** — **cross-linked polyethylene** · **surface area distribution → ↓ peak pressure** · **heating + moulding ↓ properties** (stiffer)\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ stiffness · no cross-linking · ↑ peak pressure

| Therapeutic Shoe Bill (Medicare, per calendar year) | |
|---|---|
| **Option A** | **1 pair custom-moulded shoes** (+ its inserts) **+ 2 extra pairs of inserts** |
| **Option B** | **1 pair extra-depth shoes** (≥ 3/16 inch) **+ 3 pairs of inserts** |
| Modifications covered | Rocker soles, MT bars, **wedges**, flared heels, **Velcro closures**, **inserts for missing toes** |
| **Not covered** | **Two pairs of custom-moulded shoes** |

### Amputation level selection

**Forefoot gangrene + palpable PT pulse, not septic** — **Syme amputation** → highest function\
✗ Wrong options: TMA (too near infected tissue, only to clear infection first) · BKA · AKA · debridement + antibiotics

**Syme** — **ankle disarticulation, malleoli removed, heel pad anchored** · heel pad fed by **PT artery** branches · needs **patent PT artery + stable heel pad** · success **85%** with palpable PT pulse vs 1/4 without · **90%** with triphasic / normal PT vs **57%**

**Sepsis resolved, albumin 1.9, ABI 0.70** — **local wound care, parenteral antibiotics, metabolic support, re-evaluate in 1 week** · albumin **≥ 2.5 g/dL** → good Syme healing\
✗ Wrong options: bypass · Syme now · guillotine / closed transtibial

**Most likely to fail + need revision** — **transmetatarsal amputation** in uncontrolled diabetic (**HbA1c > 8**) with forefoot ulcer · **HbA1c = main predictor of TMA success**, then need for debridement\
✗ Wrong options: long / short transtibial · transfemoral · Syme

**Surgical site infection** — complicated diabetics **7.25×** vs non-diabetics

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Ulcer recurrence after TCC** | **3–4 wk** after cast removal |
| **Heel-pad breakdown** | After TAL (↑ heel pressure) |
| **Transfer lesion** | Adjacent toe after flexor tenotomy (8%) |
| **TMA failure** | HbA1c > 8, persistent forefoot ulcer |
| **Amputation** | Infection 25–50% · osteomyelitis ↑ 4× · Charcot ulcer + infection 15% |
| **Partial calcanectomy** | Needs custom orthosis to cushion heel |

## X vs Y

| | **Osteomyelitis / infection** | **Charcot arthropathy** |
|---|---|---|
| Elevation | Erythema **persists** | Erythema **abates** |
| Inflammatory markers | ↑ | Usually normal |
| MRI | Excellent for osteomyelitis | Hard to distinguish |
| Best separator | **Leukocyte (indium WBC) scintigraphy** | — |
| Definitive | **Bone biopsy** | Bone biopsy if doubt |

| | **Gastrocnemius recession (Strayer)** | **Achilles lengthening (Hoke)** |
|---|---|---|
| Silfverskiöld | **DF ↑ with knee flexion** | **No change** |
| Contracture | Isolated gastrocnemius | Gastrocsoleus complex |

| | **TCC** | **CROW / pneumatic walker** | **Custom orthoses / shoes** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Role | **Treat** active ulcer (gold standard) | After TCC / alternative | **Prevent** ulcer |
| Removable | No | **Yes** (compliance ↓) | Yes |
| Recurrence | ↓ only with TAL | doesn't ↓ | Moderate-risk prevention |

## How it's asked

**"Diabetic most likely to develop a foot ulcer?"** → **unable to feel the 5.07 Semmes-Weinstein monofilament on the plantar foot**

**"Highest risk of developing the wound shown?"** → **absent plantar sensation with 5.07-gauge monofilament testing**

**"Least likely to predict amputation?"** → **ankle-brachial index of 1.07**

**"One programme to reduce amputations?"** → **smoking cessation with counselling and pharmacotherapy**

**"Recommended neuropathy screen for touch?"** → **10 gm monofilament** (TO)

**"Exposed tendons, no bone or abscess — Wagner grade?"** → **grade 2** (TO)

**"Commonest organism of Madura foot?"** → **Actinomadura madurae** (TO)

**"Good healing predictor EXCEPT?"** → **TcpO2 of 15 mm Hg**

**"NOT predictive of poor healing?"** → **systolic blood pressure > 140 mmHg**

**"Pre-TMA lab that raises wound-healing risk?"** → **albumin of 2.9 g/dL**

**"Septic patient stabilised, albumin 1.9 — best next?"** → **local wound care, parenteral antibiotics, metabolic support, reevaluation in 1 week**

**"Clean 3-month plantar ulcer, normal labs — treatment?"** → **total contact casting**

**"Midfoot ulcer treated in TCC — radiograph?"** → **Figure C, Charcot midfoot**

**"Infected heel ulcer — NOT indicated?"** → **total contact casting**

**"True of TCC?"** → **ulcers at greatest risk of recurring within 1 month of total contact cast discontinuation**

**"Offloaded, uninfected ulcer not shrinking — proven adjunct?"** → **negative pressure wound therapy**

**"Silfverskiöld positive — which condition benefits most from Strayer?"** → **chronic plantar forefoot ulcer**

**"Forefoot ulcer, palpable pulses — next information?"** → **results of Silfverskiöld test**

**"Failed TCC, gastrocnemius contracture — next?"** → **Strayer procedure (gastrocnemius lengthening)**

**"5th MT head ulcers for 2 years, minimal DF — best?"** → **a gastrocnemius release and supportive wound care**

**"Recurrent 1st MT head ulcer after orthosis + TCC?"** → **gastrocsoleus recession and peroneus longus to brevis tendon transfer**

**"TCC + percutaneous TAL vs TCC alone?"** → **lower rate of neuropathic plantar forefoot ulcer recurrence at two years**

**"Failed TCC, DF short of neutral not improving with knee flexion?"** → **Achilles lengthening**

**"Heals ulcer + ↓ recurrence (MT head ulcer)?"** → **performing an Achilles lengthening procedure**

**"Could have prevented progression to gangrene?"** → **Achilles tendon lengthening**

**"Healed recurrent forefoot ulcer, Silfverskiöld negative — keep plantigrade?"** → **ostectomy and achilles tendon lengthening**

**"2nd MT head osteomyelitis — highest success?"** → **IV antibiotics based on percutaneous bone biopsy culture sensitivity**

**"Strong osteomyelitis indicator EXCEPT?"** → **positive wound culture**

**"Deep ulcer failing TCC, erythema despite elevation — next?"** → **intra-operative debridement and irrigation with culture**

**"Metatarsal head visible on probing — best test for bone involvement?"** → **bone biopsy**

**"Most specific for osteomyelitis?"** → **bone biopsy with cultures and pathology**

**"Sesamoid osteomyelitis treated nonop — predictor of failure?"** → **presence of osteomyelitis in his sesamoids**

**"Wagner 3 heel ulcer + calcaneal osteomyelitis — surgery?"** → **partial calcanectomy**

**"Charcot midfoot ulcer, no osteomyelitis — prevent recurrence?"** → **midfoot exostectomy and Achilles tendon lengthening**

**"Hallux IP ulcer, stiff MTPJ, failed TCC?"** → **resect the base of the proximal phalanx**

**"Rigid nonplantigrade Charcot, healed talar-head ulcer — prevent recurrence?"** → **surgical correction of the deformity, Achilles tendon lengthening, and therapeutic footwear**

**"Forefoot gangrene, palpable PT pulse — definitive, best function?"** → **Syme amputation**

**"Level most likely to fail, HbA1c 8.5 smoker?"** → **transmetatarsal amputation**

**"Not covered yearly by the Therapeutic Shoe Bill?"** → **two pairs of custom-molded shoes**

**"Shoe modification to cut forefoot pressure?"** → **rocker sole shoes**

**"Healed first Wagner II ulcer + hammertoe — next?"** → **depth-inlay shoes with a custom accommodative foot orthosis**

**"Why Plastazote?"** → **surface area distribution**

**"True of flexor tenotomy for toe ulcers?"** → **patients can develop a similar ulceration to an adjacent toe after tenotomy**

**"Which ulcer suits percutaneous flexor tenotomy?"** → **Figure A, apical toe ulcer**
