# Foot Anatomy and Biomechanics

*Foot & Ankle · 18 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Plantar fascia

**Origin** — **medial calcaneal tuberosity**

**Insertion** — **plantar plate + bases of all 5 proximal phalanges** · lateral band → **5th MT base**

**Function** — **windlass mechanism**: toes dorsiflex at toe-off → fascia tightens → **↑ arch height, foot shortens** · **2nd most important medial arch support**

**Load transfer** — **plantar aponeurosis = primary structure of force transfer hindfoot → forefoot in stance** · tension peaks in **late stance**, up to **96% body weight** (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: Achilles · Lisfranc ligament · tibialis posterior · tibialis anterior

![Windlass mechanism – toe dorsiflexion tightens the plantar fascia and raises the arch](../../MCQ/attachments/q17-illa-windlass.jpg)

### Hindfoot – subtalar joint

**Facets** — **posterior = largest** · **middle = medial, on sustentaculum** · **anterior = continuous with talonavicular joint**

**Motion** — **inversion / eversion** only · **no role in plantar / dorsiflexion**

### Transverse tarsal (Chopart) joint

**Components** — **talonavicular + calcaneocuboid**

**Talonavicular support** — **spring ligament** = **superomedial + inferior calcaneonavicular ligaments**

**Calcaneocuboid** — **saddle-shaped** · plantar support = **superficial + deep inferior calcaneocuboid ligaments** · superior support = **lateral limb of bifurcate ligament**

### Midfoot – Lisfranc (TMT) joint

**Articulations** — 1st–3rd metatarsocuneiform + 4th–5th metatarsocuboid · naviculocuneiform + intercuneiform joints = dense ligaments, **little motion**

| Column | Bones | Mobility / role |
|---|---|---|
| **Medial** | **1st MT, medial cuneiform, navicular** | **Carries most load in standing** |
| **Middle** | **2nd + 3rd MT, middle + lateral cuneiforms** | **Least mobile** → rigidity for push-off |
| **Lateral** | **4th + 5th MT, cuboid** | **Most mobile** → uneven ground |

| TMT ligament layer | Strength |
|---|---|
| **Interosseous** (contains **Lisfranc ligament**: **plantar medial cuneiform → base of 2nd MT**) | **Strongest** |
| Plantar | Next strongest |
| **Dorsal** | **Weakest** |

**Transverse Roman arch** — dorsal surface wider than plantar · **2nd MT base = keystone**

![Medial column of the foot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7006-medial-column-of-foot-moved.jpg)

### Forefoot

**1st MT** — **shortest + widest** · takes **50% of weight during gait** (Orthobullets)

**2nd MT** — **longest** · **most stress during gait** → **commonest MT stress fracture**

**Which MT bears most weight walking?** — **1st MT 50% in gait** (Orthobullets) · **standing = 1st MT, walking = 2nd MT** (TO) — read the options

**Transverse metatarsal ligament** — **holds hallucal sesamoids in place** as 1st MT head drifts medially → **false image of sesamoid subluxation** (head moves, not sesamoids)

**Adductor hallucis conjoined tendon** — lateral aspect of **lateral sesamoid** + lateral base of proximal phalanx

**2nd MTP plantar plate** — dense phalangeosesamoidal complex · must become **lax before dorsal translation** of proximal phalanx · once attenuated, main deforming force = **EDL** · **weakest at origin (metatarsal neck)**

**Seymour fracture (hallux distal phalanx)** — remove nail + interposed physeal tissue, repair nail bed, splint in extension, **antibiotics**

### Intrinsic muscle function

**Foot intrinsics** — **flex MTP joints + extend IP joints**\
✗ Wrong options: extend MTP + flex IP · flex both · extend both

## 2 Mechanism

### Subtalar & transverse tarsal joint axes in gait

| Phase | Subtalar / calcaneus | Chopart axes | Foot |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Heel strike** | **Eversion** | **Parallel → unlocked** | **Supple** → shock absorption, accommodates ground |
| **Push-off / toe-off** | **Inversion** | **Divergent → locked** | **Rigid lever arm** for propulsion |

**✗ Gait traps** — eversion **unlocks** (✗ Wrong options: locks) · calcaneus **everted** at heel strike (✗ Wrong options: inverted throughout stance) · **tibialis anterior contracts eccentrically** in stance (✗ Wrong options: concentrically) · push-off foot is **rigid** via inversion (✗ Wrong options: flexible via eversion)

![Subtalar eversion – parallel Chopart axes; inversion – divergent axes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7006-subtalar-joint-chopart-joint-axes-moved.jpg)

### Windlass mechanism & push-off

**Flexible → rigid foot at toe-off** — (1) **tightening of plantar fascia** · (2) **progressive external rotation of lower extremity** (pelvis → ankle → subtalar) · (3) **subtalar inversion locks transverse tarsal joint**

**Does NOT occur at push-off** — **loosening of the spring ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: subtalar inversion · Chopart locking · external rotation · plantar fascia tightening (all do occur)

**Flatfoot causes (OB)** — triceps surae insufficiency, obesity, PTTD, laxity of **spring ligament** or other ligaments

## X vs Y

| | **Heel strike** | **Push-off** |
|---|---|---|
| Subtalar | Eversion | **Inversion** |
| Transverse tarsal | **Unlocked** (parallel axes) | **Locked** |
| Limb rotation | — | **External rotation** |
| Plantar fascia | — | **Tight** (windlass) |

| | **1st MT** | **2nd MT** |
|---|---|---|
| Size | **Shortest, widest** | **Longest** |
| Load | **Standing / 50% in gait** (Orthobullets) | **Walking** (TO) · most stress |
| Classic | Sesamoids | **Stress fracture**, keystone, Lisfranc ligament |

## How it's asked

**"Primary site of force transfer hindfoot → forefoot in stance?"** → **plantar aponeurosis**

**"NOT at normal push-off?"** → **loosening of spring ligament**

**"Subtalar–transverse tarsal relationship in gait?"** → **Chopart axes parallel at heel strike**

**"Eversion of subtalar joint does what to Chopart?"** → **unlocks** (flexible)

**"Tibialis anterior in stance?"** → **eccentric contraction**

**"Intrinsic muscles act on toes by?"** → **flexing MTP + extending IP joints**

**"Metatarsal bearing most weight during walking?"** → **2nd** (TO)

**"Lisfranc ligament runs?"** → **plantar medial cuneiform → base of 2nd MT**

**"Strongest TMT ligament layer?"** → **interosseous**

**"Least mobile column?"** → **middle**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Foot Muscle Forces & Deformities

*Foot & Ankle · 19 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Simple deformities – strong vs weak muscle

| Deformity | Strong (deforming) | Weak |
|---|---|---|
| **Equinus** | **Gastrocnemius–soleus** | Dorsiflexors |
| **Cavus** | **Plantar fascia, intrinsics** | Dorsiflexors |
| **Varus** | **Tibialis posterior, tibialis anterior** | **Peroneus brevis** |
| **Supination** | **Tibialis anterior** | **Peroneus longus** |
| **Flatfoot** | **Peroneus brevis** | **Tibialis posterior** |

### Complex deformities

| Deformity | Strong | Weak |
|---|---|---|
| **Equinovarus + supination** | Gastroc–soleus, tib post, tib anterior | **PB + PL** |
| **Equinovalgus** | Gastroc–soleus, **peroneals** | **Tib post, tib anterior** |
| **Calcaneovalgus** | Dorsiflexors / evertors (**L4, L5**) | Plantarflexors / invertors (**S1, S2**) |

### Muscle antagonists & tendon transfers in cavovarus (PL→PB, EHL transfer)

| Muscle | Antagonist / force couple | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Tibialis posterior** | **Peroneus brevis = primary antagonist** | Inverts, secondarily plantarflexes |
| **Tibialis anterior** | **Peroneus longus** | TA secondarily inverts → only **partial** antagonist of TP · **TA eccentrically contracts after heel strike** |
| **Peroneus longus** | Tibialis anterior | **Plantarflexes the first ray** |
| **Peroneus brevis** | Tibialis posterior | **Primary evertor**; does **not** contribute to cavus |

**Cavus driver** — **weak tibialis anterior + strong peroneus longus** → **plantarflexed first metatarsal**

| Nerve | Motor supply |
|---|---|
| **Tibial** | **Tibialis posterior**, long toe flexors (deep + superficial posterior compartments) |
| **Deep peroneal** | Anterior compartment: **TA, EDL, EHL, peroneus tertius** |
| **Superficial peroneal** | Lateral compartment: **PL, PB** |
| **Sural, saphenous** | **No motor** (sensory only) |

**DPN / SPN / L5 root injury** — anterior + lateral compartment weakness → **unopposed tib post + long toe flexors** → hindfoot + forefoot **varus**

**Tendon transfer requirements** — adequate **strength + excursion** · insertion close to substituted tendon, routed in **direct line** · **intact nerve + blood supply** · **functional (supple) joints** · donor must be **innervated**\
✗ Wrong options: TA / peroneals in CPN palsy

## 3 Presentation

### Foot drop

**Definition** — inability to dorsiflex ankle ± toes · **commonest cause = peroneal nerve palsy**

**Causes** — central (brain, cord, roots) · peripheral (sciatic, peroneal) · **traumatic (knee dislocation, laceration, blunt)** · compressive (mass, deformity correction) · systemic (**diabetic polyneuropathy**, mononeuropathy) · iatrogenic (casting, positioning, surgery) · mechanical (muscle debridement, tumour excision) · **anterior compartment syndrome** (devitalised muscle) · **CMT** · polio

| Nerve | Motor loss | Sensory loss |
|---|---|---|
| **DPN** | **Ankle / toe dorsiflexion** | **First dorsal web space** |
| **SPN** | **Ankle eversion** | **Lateral leg + dorsal foot** |

**Gait** — **foot slaps floor at heel strike** = tibialis anterior weakness · **toes extend in swing** = extensors substituting for weak TA

**Knee-level injury** — lateral leg sensory loss + ↓ eversion + ↓ dorsiflexion

### Equinovarus after stroke

**Commonest ankle–foot deformity after stroke** — **equinovarus**

**Equinus** — overactive **gastrocnemius–soleus** · **Varus** — overactive **tibialis anterior** (+ tib post, FHL, FDL)

**Other features** — clonus, intrinsic-minus foot, supple claw toes, compensatory **knee hyperextension**, premature triceps surae firing

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Silfverskiöld test & gastrocnemius recession indications

**Test** — ankle dorsiflexion with **knee extended**, then **knee flexed** (hindfoot locked)

**Why it works** — **gastrocnemius crosses the knee** (origin posterior femur) → relaxes with knee flexion · **soleus does not**

![Silfverskiöld test: dorsiflexion with knee extended then flexed](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7054-silverskiold-moved.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria

| Silfverskiöld result | Diagnosis | Surgery |
|---|---|---|
| **↑ DF with knee flexed** (e.g. −10° ext → +10° flex; −5° → 10°; 0° → 15°; 3° → 16°) | **Isolated gastrocnemius contracture** (positive test) | **Gastrocnemius recession (Strayer)** |
| **Same DF knee flexed + extended**, can't pass neutral in either | **Achilles (gastroc–soleus) contracture** | **Achilles lengthening** (Hoke triple-cut, percutaneous, open) or gastroc + soleus recession |
| DF past neutral in both positions | **Normal** | — |
| ✗ Wrong option: DF better with knee extended | Opposite of positive test | — |

**Thresholds** — gastroc contracture if **DF < 10° with knee extended**, improving with knee flexion (OB) · **difference > 10° = significant** (OB) · **shy of neutral → past neutral** with knee flexion (OB) — read the options

**Not a Silfverskiöld finding** — calf squeeze ± plantarflexion = **Thompson test** (Achilles rupture) · resting ankle position irrelevant

**Other exams (wrong options)** — **Ober** = IT band · **Dial** = knee posterolateral corner · **Thessaly** = meniscus · **Thomas** = hip flexion contracture · **Coleman** = hindfoot flexibility

**Gastroc contracture causes** — ↑ **forefoot strike** → ↑ plantar tension → **plantar fasciitis**, **arch collapse**, **Achilles tendinopathy**, **metatarsalgia** (plantar callus under MT heads), **neuromas**

**Lateral head of gastrocnemius** — muscle tested by the Silfverskiöld test

### Coleman block test – forefoot-driven cavovarus

**Method** — block under **lateral border (lateral column / hindfoot)**, first ray allowed to drop off

**Heel corrects to neutral / slight valgus** — **flexible subtalar / hindfoot** → varus is **forefoot-driven** = **plantarflexed first ray** (valgus forefoot)\
✗ Wrong options: dorsiflexed first ray · varus forefoot · rigid flatfoot

**Heel stays in varus** — **hindfoot-driven (rigid hindfoot)** → needs calcaneal osteotomy

![Coleman block test: forefoot-driven vs hindfoot-driven varus](../../MCQ/attachments/q3878-illa-coleman-block-1.jpg)

**Subtle cavus associations** — **lateral column overload**, recurrent ankle sprains / functional ankle instability, **5th MT stress fracture**

**5th MT stress fracture (esp. chronic / recurrent)** — look for **hindfoot varus deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral ligament instability · talocalcaneal coalition (causes flatfoot, protects 5th MT) · PB rupture · anterior process fracture

## 5 Management

```
Equinus
├─ Silfverskiöld positive (gastroc only) → gastrocnemius recession (Strayer)
└─ Fixed in both knee positions → Achilles lengthening (TAL)
```

```
Cavovarus
├─ Supple, Coleman corrects → dorsiflexion 1st MT osteotomy (± PL→PB)
├─ Coleman does not correct → + lateralising calcaneal (Dwyer) osteotomy
└─ Rigid / degenerative → triple arthrodesis
```

```
Chronic flexible foot drop
├─ < 6–12 mo, nerve may recover → AFO + observation + therapy
├─ Acute laceration → nerve repair, graft or transfer
└─ No recovery, supple joints → tib post transfer to dorsum (lateral cuneiform)
       └─ Equinus contracture → + gastroc / Achilles lengthening
```

### Gastrocnemius recession vs Achilles lengthening

**Strayer** — **fascial release of gastrocnemius** (distal GT) for recession + gentle lengthening · after **failed nonop**\
✗ Wrong options: failed PT alone is not an indication (try orthotics / injections)

| Level | Procedure | Eponym |
|---|---|---|
| 5 | Proximal gastrocnemius tenotomy | Silfverskiöld |
| 4 | Deep gastroc–soleus recession | Baumann |
| 3 | **Distal gastrocnemius tenotomy** | **Strayer** |
| 2 | Superficial gastroc–soleus recession | Vulpius, Baker |
| 1 | **TAL** | **Hoke (triple cut)**, White, Paley |

![Levels of gastrocnemius–soleus lengthening](../../MCQ/attachments/q3878-illb-gastroc-recession-levels.jpg)

**Sural nerve** — at risk with gastrocnemius intramuscular aponeurotic recession

**Plantar fasciitis + positive Silfverskiöld** — **gastrocnemius recession**\
✗ Wrong options: unchanged test · 3 mo failed PT · flexible planovalgus · too-many-toes

**Cavovarus + positive Silfverskiöld (0° → 15°)** — **gastrocnemius recession**\
✗ Wrong options: heel cord / percutaneous / open TAL · gastroc + soleus recession (those for combined contracture)

**Runner, callus under 1st MT head, −5° → 10°** — **Strayer**\
✗ Wrong options: FHL → Achilles (chronic Achilles rupture / degeneration) · Hoke TAL · 1st MT extension osteotomy (cavus with flexible hindfoot) · posterior capsule release

**Metatarsalgia 2nd–4th, normal X-ray, 3° → 16°** — **gastrocnemius recession**\
✗ Wrong options: Weil osteotomies · PTT debridement + MDCO · triple-cut TAL · MTP steroid

**Stage IIA AAFD after MDCO + FDL → navicular** — gastroc recession if **can't DF above neutral with knee extended but can past neutral with knee flexed**

**Isolated Achilles lengthening** — **can't DF past neutral with knee extended or flexed**\
✗ Wrong options: CP gastroc-only → gastroc lengthening · runner anterior impingement → arthroscopic debridement · foot drop → tendon transfer · normal Silfverskiöld

### Cavovarus correction

**Flexible forefoot-driven (Coleman corrects)** — **dorsiflexion first metatarsal osteotomy** → hindfoot resumes slight valgus, **avoids calcaneal osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: triple / subtalar arthrodesis (degenerative only) · PB lengthening · medialising calcaneal osteotomy (flexible flatfoot)

**Coleman corrects + Silfverskiöld unchanged (−5° both)** — **first ray dorsiflexion osteotomy + Achilles lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: gastroc recession · calcaneal osteotomy · triple arthrodesis (deformity supple)

**Rearfoot orthotic** — won't correct a forefoot cause · may still need **lateralising calcaneal osteotomy**

**Subtle cavus, lateral column overload, mild instability, no joint-line pain** — **PT + cavus orthotic: lateral forefoot posting + first metatarsal head recessing** (another OB: **lateral hindfoot posting** + 1st MT head recess)\
✗ Wrong options: MRI (no joint-line pain) · boot (stress fracture) · NSAIDs + lace-up (ignores lateral column) · arch support + **medial posting — contraindicated** in cavus

**Decreases plantarflexion force on 1st MT head** — **peroneus longus → peroneus brevis transfer** (also improves eversion when PB weak / torn)

| Transfer | Indication |
|---|---|
| **PL → PB** | **↓ plantarflexed 1st MT** (cavovarus), weak PB / chronic PB tear |
| **Tib post → dorsum (lateral cuneiform)** | **Weak dorsiflexion / foot drop** |
| **SPLATT** | **Equinovarus**, excessive **forefoot inversion in swing**, spastic TA |
| **FDL → tib post / navicular** | **PTT dysfunction** (flexible flatfoot) |
| **FDL → EDL** | **Flexible hammer / claw toes** |
| **EHL → 1st MT neck (Jones)** | **Hallux claw / hyperextended 1st MTP** |
| **FHL → Achilles** | Chronic Achilles rupture / degeneration |
| **TA → talus dorsum** | **Midfoot amputation** with firing TA (prevents equinus) |

**Jones procedure** — **hallux IP fusion + EHL transfer to 1st MT neck** for **hyperextended 1st MTP** in cavus\
✗ Wrong options: FHL, EHB, EDL, TA (wrong length / direction)

**Polio, flexible cavus, claw toes, equinus, foot slap** — soft tissue only: **plantar fascia release + Achilles lengthening + EHL → 1st MT neck + tib post → dorsum + FDL → EDL**\
✗ Wrong options: calcaneal / metatarsal osteotomies (flexible) · triple arthrodesis (young)

### Foot drop – tibialis posterior transfer to dorsum

**Nonop** — **AFO** + observation + therapy (stretch, keep joints supple) · AFO downsides: ↓ ankle ROM, discomfort

**Wait** — if no return in **6–12 mo** → transfer · **no dorsiflexion 1 yr after nerve repair → assume no recovery**

**Operation** — **tibialis posterior (tibial nerve) → through interosseous membrane → lateral cuneiform / dorsum of foot** (or circumtibial) ± **gastroc / Achilles lengthening** · converts deforming invertor into **strongest available dorsiflexor** · ↓ plantarflexion + inversion torque

**CPN laceration, best single-tendon transfer (MRI label B)** — **tibialis posterior**\
✗ Wrong options: A = TA · E = peroneals (non-functional) · C = FDL (adjunct for toe extension only) · D = Achilles (transfer not described; lengthen it instead)

![Axial T1 MRI ankle: B = tibialis posterior, C = FDL, A = tibialis anterior, E = peroneals, D = Achilles](../../MCQ/attachments/q3842-a-t1-ankle-label.jpg)

**Combined PTT + FHL + FDL transfer (Movahedi)** — foot drop correction excellent **60%**, toe extension excellent **47%**

**Equinovarus 13 mo after MVC, flexible** — **tib post → lateral midfoot + Achilles lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: ankle / subtalar / pantalar arthrodesis (fixed deformity or end-stage arthritis only) · SPLATT (TA not working)

**CMT, no TA / PB, 5° equinus, TP + FDL 5/5** — **gastrocnemius lengthening + tib post → dorsum**\
✗ Wrong options: PB → dorsum · FDL → dorsum · ankle fusion · triple (supple)

**Knee dislocation, CPN transection, no recovery at 1 yr** — **tib post → dorsum**\
✗ Wrong options: sural cable graft (less reliable) · PL → TA · EHL → TA (peroneal-innervated) · ankle fusion in young

**Anterior compartment syndrome, DF 0/5, eversion 4/5, Silfverskiöld unchanged at 20° DF** — **tib post through interosseous membrane to dorsum**\
✗ Wrong options: PL → PB (eversion only) · Achilles lengthening + AFO (no fixed equinus) · **FHL → navicular — inadequate excursion + strength** · TA → talus (TA dead)

### Spastic hemiplegia – bracing & SPLATT

**Timing after stroke** — **AFO + PT for ≥ 6 mo** (Orthobullets, Miller) · **18–24 mo** before reconstruction (Younger & Hansen) — read the options

**Early (3 mo after stroke)** — **AFO + physical therapy** (± phenol / Botox, stretching, ROM)\
✗ Wrong options: Strayer · SPLATT · FHL transfer + toe flexor release · tib post fractional lengthening (all reasonable later)

**Surgical options (after recovery plateau)** — **SPLATT** · **FHL → dorsum of foot + release FDL / FDB at each toe base** · gastrocnemius / Achilles lengthening

**Dynamic varus, TA active in swing + stance, weak evertors, clonus, equinus, 2 yr** — **percutaneous Achilles lengthening + split TA transfer to lateral cuneiform**\
✗ Wrong options: TAL alone · **out-of-phase tib post transfer** or **Bridle** (TA still active, risk **planovalgus**) · toe flexor tenotomy

**TAL in spasticity** — restores DF + **↓ clonus** (stretch response) · if DF still inadequate → **tib post lengthening**

### Flatfoot – FDL transfer & orthotic posting

**Supple pes planoabductovalgus, can't single-leg heel raise** — bony surgery + **FDL tendon transfer** (similar dynamic function to PTT) · alternatives FHL, PB\
✗ Wrong options: EHL · PL repair · **spring ligament release — worsens flatfoot**; repair / reconstruction is OK · lateral ligament reconstruction

**Flexible planovalgus + lateral hindfoot (impingement) pain** — **medial hindfoot posting / wedge** = simplest offload\
✗ Wrong options: lateral hindfoot posting ↑ lateral pressure · rigid / semi-rigid / accommodative orthotic alone (foot still pronates)

## X vs Y

| | **Gastrocnemius contracture** | **Achilles contracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Silfverskiöld | **DF improves with knee flexion** | **No change** |
| Treatment | **Gastroc recession (Strayer)** | **TAL (Hoke, percutaneous, open)** |

| | **Tib post transfer to dorsum** | **SPLATT** |
|---|---|---|
| Problem | **Flaccid foot drop** (CPN palsy, CMT, compartment syndrome) | **Spastic** equinovarus (stroke, TA overactive) |
| TA | **Dead** | **Active / overactive** |
| Insertion | Lateral cuneiform / dorsum via interosseous membrane | Lateral cuneiform |

| | **Forefoot-driven cavovarus** | **Hindfoot-driven cavovarus** |
|---|---|---|
| Coleman | **Heel corrects** | Heel stays varus |
| Surgery | **Dorsiflexion 1st MT osteotomy** | + **calcaneal osteotomy** |

## How it's asked

**"Test in figure (knee extended vs flexed)?"** → **Silfverskiöld — gastrocnemius tightness vs Achilles contracture**

**"Indication for gastroc recession in plantar fasciitis?"** → **10 degrees less than neutral with knee in extension versus 10 degrees beyond neutral with knee flexed**

**"Cavovarus, DF 0° → 15° with knee flexion — Achilles management?"** → **gastrocnemius recession**

**"Runner, 1st MT callus, −5° → 10°?"** → **Strayer procedure**

**"Test for structure at white arrow (lateral gastrocnemius)?"** → **Silfverskiöld test**

**"Gastroc recession after stage IIA flatfoot correction when?"** → **can't DF above neutral with knee extended, can with knee flexed**

**"Metatarsalgia, 3° → 16°, failed orthotics?"** → **gastrocnemius recession**

**"Who benefits from isolated Achilles lengthening?"** → **can't DF past neutral with knee extended or flexed**

**"Coleman corrects, Silfverskiöld unchanged — procedures?"** → **first ray dorsiflexion osteotomy + Achilles lengthening**

**"Coleman block correction proves varus due to?"** → **plantar flexed first ray**

**"Heel corrects to valgus on Coleman — procedure?"** → **dorsiflexion first metatarsal osteotomy**

**"Associated pathology with 5th MT stress fracture?"** → **hindfoot varus deformity**

**"Subtle cavus, lateral midfoot pain, recurrent sprains?"** → **PT + orthotic with lateral forefoot posting + first metatarsal head recessing**

**"CPN laceration, best single tendon (MRI label)?"** → **B — tibialis posterior**

**"Transfer for anterior + lateral compartment deficit — true?"** → **antagonist is peroneus brevis**

**"Flexible post-traumatic equinovarus, no DF / eversion?"** → **tib post transfer to lateral midfoot + Achilles lengthening**

**"CMT foot drop, supple, 5° equinus?"** → **gastrocnemius lengthening + tib post to dorsum**

**"No DF 1 yr after CPN repair?"** → **tibialis posterior transfer to dorsum of foot**

**"Nerve to the transferred muscle in foot drop?"** → **tibial nerve**

**"Foot drop after compartment syndrome — restore DF?"** → **posterior tibialis transfer through interosseous membrane to dorsum**

**"Polio, foot slap, claw toes, flexible cavus?"** → **plantar fascia release, TAL, EHL → 1st MT neck, tib post → dorsum, FDL → EDL**

**"Hyperextended 1st MTP after cavus reconstruction — transfer?"** → **extensor hallucis longus (Jones)**

**"Transfer that ↓ plantarflexion force on 1st MT head?"** → **peroneus longus to peroneus brevis**

**"Peroneus brevis is primary antagonist of?"** → **posterior tibialis**

**"Stroke 3 months ago, spastic TA varus?"** → **AFO with physical therapy**

**"Spastic hemiplegia 2 yr, TA active swing + stance, weak evertors?"** → **percutaneous TAL + split TA transfer to lateral cuneiform**

**"Soft-tissue procedure with bony flatfoot surgery?"** → **flexor digitorum longus transfer**

**"Simplest shoe change for lateral hindfoot pain in planovalgus?"** → **medial hindfoot posting**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Foot Puncture Wounds

*Foot & Ankle · 20 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Setting** — common **workplace** injury (construction sites) · stepping on a **nail** or stick through sock / sole

**Commonest object** — **nails** (TO) · also needles, glass, wood, plastic, sharp metal (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: needles commonest

**Infection** — **\~10%** · **osteomyelitis 1–2%** (penetration to cartilage / periosteum) (TO)

**Complications (TO)** — soft-tissue infection · deep abscess · **pyogenic granuloma** · **epidermal inclusion cyst** · osteomyelitis

### Risk factors for infection (TO)

**Wound (TO)** — presentation **> 6 h** · location · depth · contamination of object · **retained foreign body**

**Host (TO)** — elderly · immunocompromised · PVD · **diabetes = most significant risk factor** → often osteomyelitis

### Microbiology – S. aureus vs Pseudomonas (rubber-soled shoes)

| Infection | Commonest organism | Features asked |
|---|---|---|
| **Soft tissue** (cellulitis, abscess) | **Staphylococcus aureus** (Staph + Strep species) | **Coagulase positive, haemolytic gram-positive cocci** · commonest in diabetics + non-diabetics · S. aureus + group A strep in children |
| **Osteomyelitis** (non-diabetic) | **Pseudomonas aeruginosa** | **Gram-negative rod, single polar flagellum** (motile) · **secretes blue-green pigment in vitro** · grape-like odour · biofilm, exotoxin, moist environments |
| Through **rubber sole of shoe** | **Pseudomonas** (TO) | Grows in **moist inner layers of a used athletic shoe** (not new / very old) → inoculated with rubber |
| Diabetic osteomyelitis | S. aureus more common · **polymicrobial** | Diabetics → more osteomyelitis, more organisms |
| Bodies of water (TO) | Pseudomonas, **Aeromonas hydrophila**, **Vibrio**, **Mycobacterium marinum** | — |
| **Toothpick** (TO) | **Eikenella** (oropharyngeal flora) | — |

**Pseudomonas ✗ traps** — no flagellum · spore-forming · ferments lactose · clear colonies (colonies **blue-green** on nutrient agar)

| ✗ Distractor organism | Description | Classic association |
|---|---|---|
| **Staph epidermidis** | Coagulase-negative, non-haemolytic GPC | **Periprosthetic joint infection** |
| **Salmonella** | GN rod, **multiple (peritrichous) flagella** | **Osteomyelitis in sickle cell** |
| **Clostridium tetani** | Spore-forming GP rod, multiple flagella, anaerobe | **Tetanospasmin** blocks glycine + GABA release |
| **Pasteurella multocida** | — | **Animal bites** |
| **Eikenella corrodens** | — | **Human bites** (OB) · toothpick (TO) |
| **Vibrio** | — | Marine environments |

## 3 Presentation

**Timing** — often **weeks to a month** after injury · limp · fever, drainage

**Exam** — swollen, tender foot · obvious wound ± tract · **well-demarcated erythema** · ± lymphadenopathy

![Plantar nail puncture wound with surrounding erythema](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7026-screen-shot-2014-10-20-at-1-33-14-pm.jpg)

**Relapse after initial improvement** — suggests **osteomyelitis or retained foreign body**

**Child, nail 1 wk ago, heel pain, tender calcaneal tuberosity** — **calcaneal osteomyelitis** (Pseudomonas)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP + lateral) | **Normal early** · bone destruction later · **exclude radiopaque foreign body**, fracture · multiple views for **all puncture wounds except knife wounds** (TO) |
| **Ultrasound** | **Most accurate for wood / toothpick** in plantar arch (abrupt density change) · **non-radiopaque organic FB** + abscess · detects wooden FB **≥ 5 mm** (TO) |
| **MRI** | **Rule out osteomyelitis** (1–2%) · obtain **before operative I&D** · osteomyelitis: **low T1 / high T2 marrow** · abscess + retained FB on post-contrast T1 |
| Bone scan | not indicated when imaging shows no osteomyelitis |

**Missed foreign bodies** — **\~40%** missed on initial examination (TO)

![T1 MRI — calcaneal osteomyelitis after nail puncture in a child](../../MCQ/attachments/q212926-b-calc-osteo-mri.jpg)

![Post-contrast MRI — forefoot abscess around a retained rubber foreign body](../../MCQ/attachments/q3879-b-foot-abscess.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Foot puncture wound
├─ Acute (hours), no infection → tetanus + bedside I&D ± antipseudomonal cover
├─ Soft-tissue infection only → oral amoxicillin-clavulanate
├─ Fails oral abx / through shoe sole → add ciprofloxacin
└─ Delayed deep infection, abscess, FB, osteomyelitis → surgical debridement + IV abx
       └─ improving → convert to PO antibiotics
```

### Prophylaxis & tetanus (TO)

**Prophylactic antibiotics** — **controversial · not advised for all** (TO) · no standard regimen; acute injury should cover **Pseudomonas** (Orthobullets)

**Indications (TO)** — **highly contaminated** · presentation **> 6 h** · **at-risk host (diabetes, immunosuppression)**

**Agents (TO)** — 1st-generation oral cephalosporin · antistaphylococcal penicillin · fluoroquinolone · macrolide

**Through the sole of a shoe** — **antibiotic prophylaxis against Pseudomonas**: oral **fluoroquinolone (ciprofloxacin)** ✓ (TO)

**Tetanus booster** — all puncture wounds with **outdated or unknown** immune status

| Previous tetanus toxoid doses (TO) | Clean, minor: TT vaccine | Clean, minor: HTIG | All other wounds: TT vaccine | All other wounds: HTIG |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **< 3 or unknown** | **Yes** | No | **Yes** | **Yes** |
| **> 3** | Yes if last dose **> 10 yr** | No | Yes if last dose **> 5 yr** | No |

**TO "all FALSE EXCEPT" key** — **antipseudomonal prophylaxis through shoe sole** · keyed false: osteomyelitis = S. aureus, needles commonest, **TT for clean minor wound if status unknown** (the TO's own table says Yes) — read the options

### Microbiology & antibiotic choice

| Situation | Antibiotic |
|---|---|
| Soft-tissue only (S. aureus, β-haemolytic strep) | **Amoxicillin-clavulanate** = first line |
| Pseudomonas, adult | **Ciprofloxacin 750 mg PO q12h** · levofloxacin 750 mg PO q24h — **fluoroquinolones = only oral class active vs Pseudomonas** |
| Pseudomonas, **child** | **Ceftazidime or cefepime** (no fluoroquinolones in children) |
| Fluoroquinolone contraindicated | Parenteral **ceftazidime**, piperacillin, ticarcillin-clavulanate |
| Post-debridement | **IV antipseudomonal** → PO once clinically improved |

**✗ Antibiotic traps** — doubling amoxicillin dose · **TMP-SMX** (MRSA; poor Pseudomonas cover) · **rifampin** (TB) · **vancomycin** (gram-positive, MRSA) · **cephalexin** (superficial gram-positive skin) · **ampicillin-sulbactam** (human bite, polymicrobial)

### Deep infection – surgical debridement & IV antibiotics

**Indications** — **late / delayed presentation with deep infection ± osteomyelitis** · **foreign body removal** · **no improvement with oral antibiotics**

**Technique** — **tract + soft-tissue debridement** · deep culture · **bony curettage** (if osteomyelitis) · **pack with wick** → heal by secondary intention

**Established infection 24–36 h after plantar puncture** — **admit for parenteral antibiotics** (Eidelman)

**Rubber-soled shoe, months of pain + swelling** — retained **rubber foreign body → abscess** → **surgical wound exploration, débridement + IV (broad-spectrum) antibiotics**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · IV antibiotics alone · reinstituting oral antibiotics · expectant · percutaneous / CT-guided drainage (too small to retrieve FB) · débridement + hydrotherapy

**Calcaneal osteomyelitis** — **IV antibiotics + debridement**

## X vs Y

| | **Soft-tissue infection** | **Osteomyelitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Rate | \~10% | 1–2% |
| Organism | **S. aureus** (coag +, haemolytic GPC) | **Pseudomonas** (GN rod, polar flagellum, pigment) |
| Oral drug | Amoxicillin-clavulanate | **Ciprofloxacin** (child: ceftazidime) |
| Surgery | If abscess / FB / no response | Debridement + bony curettage + IV abx |

## How it's asked

**"Commonest organism, soft-tissue infection after puncture?"** → **Staphylococcus aureus**

**"Characteristic of that organism?"** → **coagulase positive, haemolytic gram-positive cocci**

**"Commonest organism, osteomyelitis after puncture (non-diabetic)?"** → **Pseudomonas**

**"True of the osteomyelitis organism?"** → **secretes a pigment in vitro**\
✗ Wrong options: no flagellum · spores · lactose fermenter

**"Nail through shoe, failed amoxicillin-clavulanate — add?"** → **ciprofloxacin**

**"Child, calcaneal osteomyelitis after nail — drug?"** → **may be treated with ceftazidime**\
✗ Wrong options: TMP-SMX · cephalexin · amox-clav

**"Puncture wounds — only true statement?"** → **antipseudomonal prophylaxis if through shoe sole** (TO)

**"Commonest object causing puncture wounds?"** → **nails** (TO)

**"Most significant host risk factor?"** → **diabetes mellitus** (TO)

**"Rubber-soled shoe, 2–3 months pain, abscess on MRI — next?"** → **surgical wound exploration, debridement + IV antibiotics**

**"Imaging to locate a toothpick in the plantar arch?"** → **ultrasound**

**"Imaging to rule out osteomyelitis?"** → **MRI**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Freiberg's Disease

*Foot & Ankle · 21 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & diagnosis (2nd metatarsal head, female predominance)

**Definition** — **infarction (infraction) + fracture of the metatarsal head** · **osteochondrosis** · = **AVN / idiopathic osteonecrosis** of MT head · rare

**Sex** — **female > male**: **\~4:1** (Orthobullets, OB) · **5:1** (OB) — read the options

**Age** — **adolescents > adults**: **13–18 yr** (Orthobullets, OB) · **11–17 yr** (OB) · **female adolescent athletes**\
✗ Wrong options: 30s–40s

| MT head involved | Rate |
|---|---|
| **2nd** (most common), **dorsal** aspect | **68%** |
| **3rd** (second commonest) | **27%** |
| **4th** | **3%** |
| 4th + 5th (lateral rays) | **Rare** |
| **1st** | **Rarely / not typically** — concurrent 1st involvement uncommon |

### Pathophysiology

**Cause** — disrupted blood supply from **repetitive microtrauma / stress overloading** or **idiopathic osteonecrosis** → **collapse of 2nd MT head** · multifactorial: vascular compromise, **genetic predisposition**, altered biomechanics

**Risk factors** — **long 2nd metatarsal** · **gastrocnemius contracture** (OB)

**Natural history** — **dorsal articular collapse first**, plantar surface preserved until late → **degenerative arthritis** of 2nd MTP in adulthood · early disease may **resolve spontaneously with MT head remodelling**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — forefoot pain, swelling, stiffness at **2nd MT head / base of 2nd toe**, **worse with WB** · plantar or dorsal forefoot pain · difficulty with push-off · insidious, no trauma

**Exam** — peri-articular swelling · **↓ (passive) ROM of 2nd MTP** · **pain on passive motion of 2nd toe** · pain worse with **distraction (early)** and **compaction (late)**

**Labs** — **ESR + CRP normal** → osteomyelitis unlikely

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray AP, lateral, oblique foot** | **Early: usually normal** → **subchondral sclerosis** → **flattening of MT head** (later, not an early finding) → **joint destruction** (late) |
| X-ray site | Defect in **upper (dorsal) half of articular surface** |
| **MRI** | **Early disease (stage 1)**: subchondral fracture, **patchy oedema** in MT head · confirms **plantar cartilage sparing** |

![Freiberg disease — flattening of the 2nd metatarsal head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7018-freibergflat.jpg)

### Smillie classification

| Stage | Finding | Treatment if nonop fails |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Subchondral fracture visible only on MRI** | Nonop |
| **2** | **Dorsal collapse of articular surface** on X-ray | **Debridement + MT osteotomy / DCWO** |
| **3** | **Collapse of dorsal MT head, plantar articular portion intact** (± medial + lateral bony spurs) | **Debridement + MT osteotomy / DCWO** |
| **4** | **Collapse of entire MT head**, joint-space narrowing | **DuVries partial head resection / interposition arthroplasty** |
| **5** | **Severe arthritis, joint-space obliteration** | **DuVries / interposition arthroplasty** |

![Smillie stages 1–5](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7018-sim.jpg)

### Differential diagnosis\
✗ Wrong options: distractors

| ✗ Option | Why not |
|---|---|
| **Morton neuroma** | **Normal X-ray**; pain at **(3rd) web space**, not MT head |
| **Osteomyelitis** | **Normal ESR / CRP** |
| **Mucous cyst** | **DIP joints of hand**, end-stage arthritis |
| **Post-traumatic arthritis** | Isolated progressive 2nd MTP OA without trauma unlikely at 17 |
| **Synovial sarcoma** | Young adults; **commonest sarcoma of foot** but commonest **around knee**; **soft-tissue calcification** |
| Benign bone tumour / normal X-ray | X-ray abnormal |

## 5 Management

```
Freiberg disease
├─ All stages first → NSAIDs, activity change, boot / cast, MT pad
├─ Failed nonop, early–mid (Smillie I–III, plantar cartilage spared) → DCWO ± debridement
└─ Late (IV–V, no plantar cartilage) → DuVries partial head resection / interposition
       (avoid MTP fusion · polyvinyl implant)
```

### Nonoperative care – metatarsal pad

**Indication** — **early stage** · first line for **all stages**

**Sequence** — **short-leg walking cast or boot 4–6 wk** (severe / orthotics fail) → **stiff-soled shoe with MT bars or pads** (after casting)

**Metatarsal pad** — **offloads 2nd MT head** → relieves pain\
✗ Wrong options: steroid · arthrotomy · shortening osteotomy · neck dorsiflexion osteotomy (before nonop fails)

**12-yo, normal ESR / CRP** — **osteochondrosis → boot immobilisation + observation**\
✗ Wrong options: osteomyelitis imaging · AVN → osteotomy / resection · tumour · "normal X-ray, reassure"

**Steroid injection** — **↑ intra-articular pressure → may worsen osteonecrosis** · may help **adults with secondary DJD** · short-term relief only, doesn't stop collapse

**Early surgery** — not to prevent progression; no aggressive initial osteotomy

### Surgery – debridement, dorsal closing-wedge osteotomy & contraindications

**Indications** — **progressive pain, joint destruction, deformity** after failed nonop

**Stage II after 2 months failed nonop (16-yo)** — **joint debridement + metatarsal osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: interposition · core decompression · MTP arthrodesis · more immobilisation

**MRI plantar cartilage spared, failed 6 wk nonop** — **dorsal closing-wedge (dorsiflexion) osteotomy of 2nd MT head**\
✗ Wrong options: 6–8 wk more nonop · steroid · interposition (late) · **debridement + cancellous grafting** (second-line, less predictable)

**DCWO goals** — **shortens → offloads MT head** · **resects collapsed dorsal bone + cartilage** · **rotates intact plantar cartilage into contact with proximal phalanx** · lesser-toe MTP approach

![Dorsal closing-wedge osteotomy of the 2nd metatarsal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7018-dorsiflexion-osteotomy.jpg)

| DCWO | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Contraindication** | **End-stage OA with severe joint collapse** (no plantar congruity) · end-stage destruction without plantar sparing → poor outcome |
| **Indication ✓** | **AVN of 2nd MT head** (= Freiberg) · **degenerative changes with plantar articular congruity** · **dorsal collapse with moderate arthritis** |
| **Not a contraindication** | **Age** (65-yo) |

**MTP arthrotomy + loose-body removal** — very rarely; after extensive nonop fails · ± **drilling of MT head**, **subchondral bone grafting**, **interposition arthroplasty** · effective in young + adults with DJD

**Interposition tendon** — **EDL** (Orthobullets) · **EDB** (OB, Çevik — ↑ pain, ROM, function in late disease) — read the options

**DuVries arthroplasty (partial MT head resection)** — **severe stage 4–5**, plantar cartilage insufficient · ± capsular interposition

**Full MT head resection** — **avoid → overloads adjacent MT heads**

**Polyvinyl (implant) arthroplasty** — **not approved for lesser toes**\
✗ Wrong options: even for diffuse collapse + arthritis

**Joint-preserving vs sacrificing** — **joint-preserving → higher satisfaction** (✗ Wrong options: MTP fusion superior) · **lesser-toe fusion → unacceptably high nonunion** (✗ Wrong options: reliable union)

## 6 Complications

**Degenerative joint disease** — **2nd MTP arthritis in adulthood** (natural history)

## X vs Y

| | **Early–mid (I–III)** | **Late (IV–V)** |
|---|---|---|
| Plantar cartilage | **Intact** | Lost |
| X-ray | Normal / sclerosis / dorsal collapse | Whole-head collapse, arthritis |
| Surgery | **DCWO ± debridement** | **DuVries / interposition** |

## How it's asked

**"15-yo girl, 2nd MT head flattening — true?"** → **greater prevalence in females**

**"Subchondral sclerosis + flattening of 2nd MT head — true?"** → **more common in females**

**"12-yo, normal ESR / CRP — pathology + next step?"** → **osteochondrosis → immobilisation in boot + observation**

**"Field-hockey player, failed walking cast — diagnosis?"** → **Freiberg's disease**

**"17-yo male, pain on passive 2nd-toe motion — true?"** → **second MTP joint most commonly affected**

**"Plantar pain at 2nd MT head, 3 months — management?"** → **metatarsal pad**

**"Stage II, failed 2 months nonop?"** → **joint debridement + metatarsal osteotomy**

**"Finding that prevents DCWO?"** → **end-stage OA with severe joint collapse**

**"Plantar cartilage spared, failed nonop?"** → **dorsal closing-wedge (dorsiflexion) osteotomy**

**"X-ray supporting polyvinyl arthroplasty?"** → **none — not approved for lesser toes**

**"Early radiographic finding?"** → **normal X-ray**\
✗ Wrong options: flattening (late)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Gait Cycle

*Foot & Ankle · 22 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions

**Gait cycle** — **heel strike → heel strike** of the same limb

**Stance vs swing** — **60% / 40%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **62% / 38%** (another OB) — read the options

**Stride** — consecutive initial contacts of the **same (ipsilateral) foot**

**Step** — initial contacts of **alternating (contralateral) feet**

**Stance phases (5)** — initial contact → loading response → mid-stance → terminal stance → pre-swing

**Swing phases (3)** — initial swing → mid-swing → terminal swing

| Phase (Rancho terms) | Classic term | Definition | % cycle |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Initial contact** | Heel strike | Foot contacts ground | 0–2% |
| **Loading response** (1st double support) | Foot flat | Initial contact → **elevation of opposite limb**; weight transferred to stance limb | 0–10% |
| **Mid-stance** (single support) | Midstance | Opposite limb elevation → **both ankles aligned in coronal plane** | 10–30% |
| **Terminal stance** (single support) | Heel off | **Supporting heel rises** → opposite heel contacts | 30–50% |
| **Pre-swing** (2nd double support) | Toe off | Opposite initial contact → just before ipsilateral elevation | 50–60% |
| **Initial swing** | Acceleration | Limb elevation → **maximal knee flexion** | 60–73% |
| **Mid-swing** | Midswing | Knee flexion → **tibia vertical** | 73–87% |
| **Terminal swing** | Deceleration | Tibia vertical → just before initial contact | 87–100% |

![Gait phases: Rancho vs classic terms and % of cycle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7001-gait-cycle.jpg)

### Prerequisites of gait – Gage (TO)

**Five prerequisites (TO)** — **stability in stance** · **adequate step length** · **clearance in swing** · **foot prepositioning** (in swing) · **conservation of energy**

**NOT prerequisites (TO)** — **foot clearance in stance** (clearance is a swing requirement) · **adequate stride length** (it is **step** length) (TO)

### Tibialis anterior – eccentric at heel strike / loading response

**TA eccentric** — **heel strike (initial contact) + loading response** → lowers foot to ground, controls **plantar-flexion moment**, limb loading + shock absorption

**TA concentric** — **initial swing + mid-swing** (→ terminal swing) → dorsiflexion for **foot clearance**, prepositions foot for contact

**TA inactive** — **mid-stance, terminal stance, pre-swing**\
✗ Wrong options: eccentric at any of these

**Gastrocsoleus** — **dormant at heel strike** → **eccentric at foot flat / mid-stance** (weight moves forward over foot) → **concentric at toe-off (push-off)** → relaxed in swing\
✗ Wrong options: GS eccentric at heel strike · GS concentric at heel strike · GS concentric in swing · TA concentric at toe-off

**TA weakness (peroneal nerve palsy)** — mild–moderate → **foot slap** at heel strike · in swing → **steppage gait** (↑ hip + knee flexion to clear foot)

**Primary antagonist of TA** — **peroneus longus** (PF + everts) → **superficial peroneal nerve** · TA dorsiflexes + inverts

**Other antagonist pair** — **peroneus brevis vs tibialis posterior**

**Strength** — ankle plantarflexors **6×** stronger than dorsiflexors

### Muscle activity by phase

| Phase | Active muscles |
|---|---|
| **Initial contact** | **Hip extensors** stabilise hip · **quadriceps eccentric** · **TA eccentric** |
| **Loading response** | **TA eccentric** (controls PF moment) · **quads** stabilise knee vs flexion moment |
| **Mid-stance** | **Gluteus medius + calf eccentric** |
| **Terminal stance** | **Toe flexors + tibialis posterior** most active |
| **Pre-swing** | **Hip flexors** propel advancing limb |
| **Initial swing** | **Hip flexors concentric** advance the leg · TA concentric |
| **Mid-swing** | **Ankle dorsiflexors** → foot clearance |
| **Terminal swing** | **Hamstrings** decelerate thigh |

![Muscle activity through the gait cycle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7001-illus-a.jpg)

### Swing mechanics

**Swing momentum** — generated by **ankle plantar flexors (gastrocsoleus) + hip flexors at terminal stance**\
✗ Wrong options: hip flexors + dorsiflexors in early swing · knee flexors in early swing · knee extensors

**Knee in swing** — flexion in early swing + extension in terminal swing are **passive**

**Main concentric contraction in swing** — **tibialis anterior**

**Activity** — **more muscle activity in stance** than swing (stabilise plantigrade foot)

**FHL** — plantarflexes hallux at **push-off**; **inactive in swing**

### Tendon function – tibialis posterior

**Origin** — **tibia + fibula + interosseous membrane**\
✗ Wrong options: FDL = **tibia only** · FHL = **fibula only**

**Activity** — **concentric in terminal stance + pre-swing** · EMG biphasic: initial contact + midstance and pre-swing\
✗ Wrong options: eccentric · initial swing

**Function** — **most powerful inverter** · dynamic stabiliser of medial arch · **inverts hindfoot + locks Chopart's joint** → gastrocsoleus acts through MT heads (**TP + GS synergistic**)

**Insertions** — anterior: **navicular tuberosity** + plantar 1st cuneiform · middle: bases **MT 2–4**, 2nd + 3rd cuneiforms, cuboid, FHB · posterior recurrent: **sustentaculum tali**

**TP loss** — GS pulls **talar head down** → stretches **spring ligament** → medial arch collapses

**TP dysfunction EMG** — **↑ activity in 2nd half of stance** (delayed / right shift)

**Rupture site** — distal to medial malleolus: flattened, fibrocartilaginous, **hypovascular** zone

### Determinants & centre of gravity

| Variable | Value / effect |
|---|---|
| **Pelvic rotation** | **4° medially (anteriorly)** on swing side → lengthens limb |
| **Pelvic tilt** | Pelvis **drops 4°** on swing side → lowers COG at midstance (Orthobullets) · contralateral drop **\~5°** minimal, gluteal-controlled (TO) |
| **Knee flexion at heel strike** | **15°** (Orthobullets) · **10°** (TO) — lowers COG, absorbs shock · read the options |
| **Ankle** | **Plantar flexion** at heel strike + first part of stance |
| **Knee at midstance** | **Extends** as ankle PF + foot supinates → restores leg length |
| **Lateral pelvic shift** | Over stance limb (COG over base of support) |
| **COG (standing)** | **5 cm anterior to S2** |
| **COG vertical** | **Highest at midstance** · **lowest at double-limb support** |
| **COG horizontal** | **5 cm** per adult male step |

### Stance & running biomechanics (TO)

**Heel at loading (TO)** — **force acceptance + shock absorption, NOT force generation** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: "maximum force for further movement generated by heel" = FALSE

**Heel strike posture (TO)** — rearfoot in slight **inversion 0–5°** · foot supinates · pelvis level, **anterior tilt 10°**, internally rotated · hip **ER 5–10°, flexed 20–30°** · knee **flexed 10°** (TO)

**Shock-absorption cascade (TO)** — laterally directed GRF → rearfoot **everts** + tibia / femur **IR** + hip adduction + knee flexion (peaks **\~45°**) · all **eccentric** (TO)

**Anterior pelvic tilt up to 10° (TO)** — **acceptable** in early stance (heel strike → foot flat) · excess strains lumbar spine / hamstrings (TO)

**Slow vs fast running (TO)** — slow = **heel strikes first** · fast = **forefoot strikes first** (TRUE) (TO)

**Forefoot strike (TO)** — ↓ vertical GRF, ↓ knee + hip loads · ↑ **Achilles tendinopathy, plantar fasciopathy, MT stress fractures** (TO)

**Tibiotalar pronation (TO)** — present **when foot is on ground** (stance) = **dorsiflexion + eversion + abduction** (TRUE) (TO)

**Midstance (TO)** — ankle → **max dorsiflexion \~20°** · max pronation after max dorsiflexion · peak rearfoot eversion **\~10°**, forefoot abduction **\~5°** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

### Pathological gait – gluteus maximus paralysis (TO)

**Gluteus maximus** — **chief thigh extensor** · erect posture · lateral rotation · abduction · **inferior gluteal nerve (L5, S1, S2)** (TO)

**Gluteus maximus lurch (TO)** — at heel strike **trunk lurches backwards** · line of gravity shifts **posterior to hip** in early stance → compensates weak hip extension (TO)

**NOT seen (TO)** — **Trendelenburg gait** (gluteus medius) (TO)

### Quadriceps weakness

**Quadriceps atrophy** — affects **stance → midstance**\
✗ Wrong options: swing phases (quads largely silent in swing)

**Compensation** — leans forward at hip → COG **anterior to knee** → knee **hyperextension**

**Hamstring / hip-flexor weakness** — affects **swing** (limits limb advancement)

## 5 Management

### Gait after TAA vs arthrodesis

**Greatest difference (level walking)** — **peak plantarflexion power generation** (↑ with TAA)

**TAA** — **\~43% more sagittal ankle ROM** (21.1° vs 14.7°) · ↑ forefoot-tibia + hindfoot-tibia motion · closer to normal but **neither restores normal gait** · differences amplified on **stair ascent**

**Arthrodesis compensations** — ✗ Wrong options: **↑ hip ROM** · ↑ transient force at heel strike · may have **better coronal eversion** · walking speed / cadence difference only modest

**PROMs** — similar improvement after both

## X vs Y

| | **Step** | **Stride** |
|---|---|---|
| Measured between | Initial contacts of **alternating** feet | Initial contacts of the **same** foot |
| Gage prerequisite | **Yes** (adequate step length) | **No** |

| | **Gluteus maximus gait** | **Trendelenburg gait** |
|---|---|---|
| Muscle | Gluteus maximus (hip extensor) | Gluteus medius (abductor) |
| Nerve | **Inferior gluteal** | Superior gluteal |
| Sign | **Trunk lurches backwards** at heel strike | Pelvic drop |

| | **Tibialis anterior** | **Gastrocsoleus** |
|---|---|---|
| Heel strike | **Eccentric** | **Dormant** |
| Foot flat / mid-stance | Inactive | **Eccentric** |
| Toe-off | Inactive | **Concentric** |
| Swing | **Concentric** | Relaxed |

## How it's asked

**"TA contracts eccentrically during?"** → **heel strike**

**"Phase of eccentric TA contraction?"** → **loading response**\
✗ Wrong options: mid-stance · terminal stance · pre-swing · initial swing

**"Correct muscle activity in normal gait?"** → **TA contracts eccentrically at heel strike**

**"Primary antagonist of TA innervated by?"** → **superficial peroneal nerve** (peroneus longus)

**"% of gait cycle in stance?"** → **60%**

**"Swing momentum generated by?"** → **ankle plantar flexors + hip flexors at terminal stance**

**"Quadriceps atrophy affects which phase?"** → **midstance**

**"NOT a prerequisite of gait?"** → **foot clearance in stance** (TO)

**"NOT a prerequisite of gait (Gage)?"** → **adequate stride length** (TO)

**"False statement about gait?"** → **heel generates maximum force for further movement** (TO)

**"Gluteus maximus paralysis — all true EXCEPT?"** → **Trendelenburg gait** (TO)

**"Ruptured tendon on axial MRI behind medial malleolus — true?"** → **originates tibia + fibula, concentric in terminal stance**

**"TAA vs ankle arthrodesis — greatest gait difference?"** → **peak plantarflexion power generation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hallux Rigidus (MTP joint arthritis)

*Foot & Ankle · 23 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest site of OA in the foot** — 1st MTP (Orthobullets) · **2nd commonest great-toe pathology after hallux valgus** (OB)

**Incidence** — **2.5% of > 50-yr-olds** · **F : M = 2 : 1** · peak **5th–6th decade**

**Risk factors** — **trauma to 1st MTP** (**\~80%** of unilateral cases; e.g. old **turf toe**) · high-impact sport

**Causes of 1st MTP arthritis (OB)** — **trauma**, **hallux valgus**, **elevated 1st metatarsal**

### Etiology & associations

**Primary cause** — **unknown** · acute trauma + repetitive microtrauma predispose

**Pathoanatomy** — osteophytes + cartilage loss **start dorsally** → whole joint later

**Anatomic variants** — metatarsus adductus · MT head morphology · **long 1st MT** · hallux valgus / varus

**Associated** — **sesamoid arthritis** · gout · RA · seronegative arthropathies

### Anatomy & biomechanics

**1st MT** — **shortest and widest**

**Medial branch of medial dorsal cutaneous nerve** — **overlies 1st MTP** → irritated by **dorsal osteophytes** · plantar-medial hallucal nerve also supplies joint

**Load** — 1st MTP carries **\~120% body weight** each step

**Running** — needs **60° dorsiflexion**

## 2 Mechanism

### Checkrein deformity (TO)

**Cause (TO)** — **fracture of distal third of tibia** → **FHL adheres to fracture callus** → bowstring to hallux insertion (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: FHL tear · IP joint fracture · **FHL tethering at 1st MTP**

**Sign (TO)** — **ankle dorsiflexion → great toe flexes** · **ankle plantarflexion → hallux IP extends** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — 1st ray / 1st MTP · **worse at push-off (toe-off / heel-rise)** · swelling · gait abnormality · difficulty with shoes

**Inspection** — **dorsal prominence** (osteophyte) · shoe irritation + redness · severe → **hyperextension deformity**

**Motion** — **↓ dorsiflexion** · **pain at terminal dorsiflexion** early → **pain through whole arc** as it progresses

**Neuro** — **dorsomedial paraesthesia** / ↓ sensation distal medial dorsal foot (**medial dorsal cutaneous nerve**, ± Tinel)

**Grind test +** — **severe disease, central chondral wear**

### Associations & sequelae (transfer metatarsalgia)

**Hallux rigidus leads to** — **restricted dorsiflexion** → forefoot **internal rotation** → **↓ push-off strength** → **inverted gait** → **transfer metatarsalgia**\
✗ Wrong options: **flexor hallucis longus (FHL) rupture** · everted gait · ↑ push-off · Haglund deformity

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing AP, lateral, sesamoid, oblique** | **Dorsal osteophytes** · **joint space narrowing** · **subchondral sclerosis + cysts** |
| **MRI** | Rarely needed · **osteochondral lesion with normal X-ray** · characterises mild OA |

**Radiographic progression** — may **not correlate** with symptoms

### Coughlin & Shurnas classification

| Grade | Exam | Dorsiflexion | Radiograph | X-ray |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0** | **Stiffness**, no pain | **40–60°** (10–20% lost) | **Normal** | — |
| **1** | **Mild pain at extremes** | **30–40°** (20–50% lost) | **Mild dorsal osteophyte**, normal joint space | ![Grade 1](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-grade-1-ap.jpg) |
| **2** | **Moderate pain at extremes**, more constant | **10–30°** (50–75% lost) | **Moderate osteophyte**, **< 50% JSN** | ![Grade 2](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-grade-2-ap.jpg) |
| **3** | Significant stiffness, near-constant pain at extremes, **no mid-range pain** | **≤ 10°** (75–100% lost) | **Severe osteophyte**, **> 50% JSN** | ![Grade 3](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-grade-3-ap.jpg) |
| **4** | As grade 3 **+ pain at mid-range** | As grade 3 | As grade 3 | ![Grade 4](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-grade-4-ap.jpg) |

**Scenario → grade (OB)** — stiffness + ↓ push-off, **no pain** = **0** · dorsal osteophyte, no stiffness, **pain at terminal DF** = **1** · moderate pain at extremes + mild osteophyte + **< 50% JSN** = **2** · JSN + stiffness + pain at end-range = **3** · large osteophyte + stiffness + **mid-range pain** = **3** (OB) · severe JSN + **pain throughout** = **4**

**Other staging (OB)** — "**Stage III** = end-stage, cartilage lost from **phalanx + metatarsal**" · "**Stage 4** = severe dorsal osteophyte + **> 50% JSN**" — read the stem

## 5 Management

```
Hallux rigidus
├─ Grade 0–1 → NSAIDs, activity mod, injection, Morton's extension
├─ Grade 1–2, pain only at terminal DF → dorsal cheilectomy
│      └─ < 30–40° DF after cheilectomy / runner → + Moberg
├─ Grade 3–4, mid-range pain, + grind → MTP arthrodesis
└─ Elderly, low demand → Keller resection arthroplasty
```

```
Failed 1st MTP arthroplasty
├─ Silastic synovitis, great-toe pain only → implant removal + synovectomy
├─ Infection → antibiotic spacer → staged arthrodesis
└─ Loosening / bone loss / shortening + lesser metatarsalgia → removal + structural graft + arthrodesis
```

### Operative criteria

| Grade / situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Grade 0** (motion loss, no X-ray change) | Nonop · **plantar release** (OB) |
| **Grade 0–1** | **Nonop** (NSAIDs, injections, **Morton's extension**) · **up to 55%** succeed |
| **Grade 1–2** · select grade 3 with pain only at terminal DF | **Dorsal cheilectomy** (± synovectomy, debridement) |
| Cheilectomy gives **< 30–40° DF** · **runner** | **+ Moberg** dorsal closing wedge osteotomy |
| **Grade 3–4** · **mid-range pain** · **+ grind** · failed cheilectomy · grade 3 with **< 50% MT head cartilage** left | **MTP arthrodesis** (gold standard) |
| Grade 3–4, wishes to keep motion | Prosthetic / **interpositional arthroplasty** |
| **Elderly, low demand**, fast rehab | **Keller resection arthroplasty** |
| Mild disease + **intra-articular loose bodies** | Dorsal synovectomy + debridement |

### Nonoperative care – Morton's extension & carbon plate

**Morton's extension** — stiff insert with rigid extension **under 1st ray, past the 1st MTP** → **limits 1st MTP dorsiflexion** at push-off · ↓ plantar loading across MTP · **mainstay / first line for hallux rigidus**\
✗ Wrong options: hallux valgus · hallux varus · Morton's neuroma · spring ligament · 2nd MTP synovitis · Jones · hammer / mallet toe (high toe box) · midfoot arthritis · Freiberg

**Limits** — **hallux MTP dorsiflexion**\
✗ Wrong options: midfoot collapse · hindfoot varus · MTP plantarflexion · adduction

**Poor surgical candidate, + grind** — **Morton's carbon fiber extension**\
✗ Wrong options: **UCBL** + **Arizona brace** (flexible pes planovalgus) · **medial flare** (ankle instability) · **negative heel rocker** (↑ PF in terminal stance)

**Other nonop** — NSAIDs · avoid excessive great-toe DF · **stiff-sole shoe** / carbon fiber shank · toe-box stretching · rocker-bottom shoes · molded inserts

**Injections** — **corticosteroid + sodium hyaluronate** → ↓ pain at **4 + 8 wk** in low-grade disease

**Not first line (OB)** — CAM boot, AFO, hard-soled shoes · **PT aggravates** · extending Morton's to 2nd MT adds nothing

![Morton's extension orthosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-morton-extension.jpg)

### Cheilectomy ± Moberg osteotomy – indications

**Ideal candidate** — **pain at terminal DF only** + **shoe-wear irritation** from dorsal prominence · **grade 1–2** · **no mid-range pain**

**Contraindications** — **mid-range pain** on passive motion · **+ grind test** · **sesamoid arthritis** · comorbidities (poor DM control, PVD)

**Technique** — dorsal approach · **complete synovectomy** · resect dorsal osteophyte + **up to 30% dorsal MT head** (Orthobullets) · **25%** (OB) · **> 30% → subluxation / instability**

**Goal** — **70–90% of dorsiflexion** intra-op (Orthobullets) · **70–90° DF** (OB)

**Outcomes** — grade 1–2: **97% excellent**, **92% pain relief at 10 yr** · higher grades: **25–56% → arthrodesis** · progression needing fusion **7–9% within 10 yr**

**Grade 2, 35° arc, failed Morton's** — **cheilectomy + joint debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · sesamoidectomy · Keller · fusion

**Carpenter, terminal-DF pain, mild OA** — **cheilectomy** · cheilectomy ≠ Helal / Weil / chevron osteotomy\
✗ Wrong options: Lapidus · fusion · resurfacing · chevron

**Moberg** — **dorsal closing wedge osteotomy of proximal phalanx** → ↑ DF by sacrificing plantarflexion arc · with cheilectomy: **88% satisfaction at 2 yr**

**Moberg indications** — **runners** (need 60°) · cheilectomy gives **< 30–40° DF** (Orthobullets) · **< 30°** (OB) · failed cheilectomy

**Runner, 30% resected, only 20° DF** — **Moberg procedure**\
✗ Wrong options: close + WBAT · FDL → EDL · Keller (high demand) · fusion

**Ballet dancer, stage 2** — **cheilectomy, débridement ± Moberg**\
✗ Wrong options: **fusion = career-ending** · Keller · uni- / bipolar replacement

![Dorsal cheilectomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-cheilectomy.jpg)

![Moberg dorsal closing wedge osteotomy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-moberg-osteotomy.jpg)

### MTP arthrodesis – indications & optimal position

**Indications** — **standard of care / gold standard** for **grade 3–4** · **commonest procedure** · **pain throughout MTP motion** · failed cheilectomy · failed arthroplasty

**Best long-term function + pain relief** — **arthrodesis** · restores **WB + propulsion of 1st ray**\
✗ Wrong options: synovectomy · cheilectomy · hemiarthroplasty · interposition (worse long-term)

**Failed cheilectomy at 1 yr** — **1st MTP arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: Moberg (won't relieve pain)

**Grade 4, young active golfer** — **great-toe MTP fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: Keller (not for young / active) · bipolar replacement · cheilectomy · fascia lata interposition

**Stage 3, + grind, 10° DF** — **arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: cheilectomy · Moberg · Keller (↓ propulsion + transfer metatarsalgia) · resurfacing hemiarthroplasty (not with phalangeal cartilage loss)

**Fixation** — wires, pins, lag screws, crossed screws, plates · **dorsal plate + compression screw = strongest** · medial→lateral compression screw common

| Position | Orthobullets | OB |
|---|---|---|
| **Valgus** (vs MT shaft) | **5°** | **10–15°** · **5–10°** · **15°** — read the options |
| **Dorsiflexion** (vs floor) | **15°** | **10–15°** · **10°** |
| Rotation | — | **Neutral** |
| **Footplate clearance** | **4–8 mm** toe tip off plate | **\~5 mm** |

**Footplate test** — flat sterile tray, ankle neutral, simulates WB · **5 mm clearance → irrigate and close**\
✗ Wrong options: Moberg · superior capsulotomy · add screw · revise plate

![Intra-op footplate test – toe tip clears plate](../../MCQ/attachments/q212994-b-screen-shot-2019-08-07-at-7-20-18-am.jpg)

**Outcomes** — fusion **90–100%** (Orthobullets) · union **\~94%** (OB) · **95–96% satisfaction** · return: **hiking 92%, golf 80%, jogging 75%, tennis 75%**

**Tennis player, stage 4, failed orthosis** — **arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: cheilectomy + Moberg (stage 1–2) · interpositional (not for athletes) · hemi / total arthroplasty (subsidence, radiolucency)

**Mid-range pain, 10° DF, eroded joint intra-op** — **arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: cheilectomy · bunionectomy + cheilectomy · resection · interposition

### Alternatives – synthetic cartilage implant & Keller resection arthroplasty

**Keller** — **resect base of proximal phalanx** ± capsular interposition · **elderly, low demand → fastest rehab** · interposition: **90% pain improvement, 4% reoperation at 4.5 yr**

**80-yo minimally ambulatory, plantar-flexed toe** — **Keller + osteophyte removal**\
✗ Wrong options: silicone implant · Moberg · cheilectomy · arthrodesis

**Keller contraindications** — **rigid hyperextension deformity** · **sesamoid arthritis** · young active (instability)

**Keller risks** — **cock-up (hyperextension)** · **weak push-off** · **transfer metatarsalgia** (↓ with capsular interposition) · gross MTP instability

**Synthetic cartilage interposition** — keeps motion · **equivalent pain reduction + function vs arthrodesis** · **longer time to max pain relief** · **no prolonged WB restriction** · **not contraindicated in grade 2** · not if **HV / varus > 20°**

**Interpositional arthroplasty (capsule / EHB)** — younger **< 60 yr** late-stage, **strongly opposed to fusion** · limited toe function → not for athletes

### Prosthetic arthroplasty

**Indication** — **grade 3–4** wishing to **preserve motion** · indications unclear

**Contraindications** — **active infection** · **insufficient bone stock** · **sesamoid arthritis**

**Hemiarthroplasty** — unipolar MT head or phalanx base · **keeps 1st ray length** · **easier conversion to fusion** · hemi = total for function, complications, satisfaction

**Generations** — 1st–2nd **silastic** (silicone) · 3rd **press-fit metal** · 4th **threaded-stem metal**

**Silastic** — good short-term, **\~60% failure** · **silicone wear, synovitis, osteolysis** · **abandoned**

**Current implants** — **80–90% survival at \~5 yr** · later **loosening + complex revision with bone loss** · **worse than primary fusion**; fusion after failed arthroplasty worse than primary fusion

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Arthritis progression after cheilectomy** | **7–9% → fusion within 10 yr** |
| **Instability** | **Excessive cheilectomy resection** (> 30%) |
| **Malunion (fusion)** | **16%** → transfer metatarsalgia, **hallux IP arthritis**, poor push-off |
| **Nonunion (fusion)** | **5–10%** · **< 40% symptomatic** → revision fusion + bone graft |
| **Hallux IP OA** | **15%** after MTP fusion · usually asymptomatic · ↑ with **excess valgus** |
| **Cock-up deformity** | **Keller** → treat with **1st MTP arthrodesis** |
| **Failed arthroplasty** | Silicone / early implants; loosening, osteolysis, technical error |
| Infection | **< 2%** |
| Symptomatic hardware | — |

### Arthrodesis malposition complications

| Fused position | Result |
|---|---|
| **Excess dorsiflexion** | Pain at **toe tip, IP joint, under 1st MT head** · **sesamoid / plantar pain** · **dorsal shoe irritation** |
| **Excess plantarflexion** (too straight) | **↑ pressure at toe tip** · can't roll over → **walks on lateral foot**, **lateral calluses**, lateral sole wear (supinates) |
| **Excess valgus** | **Pressure on 2nd toe** → **soft corn / ulcer** · **IP joint degeneration** |
| **Varus** | **Shoe-wear problems** (narrow toe box) · **medial hallux irritation** (✗ Wrong options: sesamoid pain · rollover problem) |
| **Shortened** | **Transfer metatarsalgia** (2nd MT) + **varus of 2nd toe** (lost medial support) · IP degeneration |

**2nd MTP plantar pain + new 2nd-toe deformity after fusion** — **fused shortened**\
✗ Wrong options: varus · valgus · plantarflexion · dorsiflexion

**Lateral foot pain, lateral calluses, stands on outside of foot** — **fused in plantarflexion**

**Sesamoid / plantar 1st MT head pain, fixed MTP** — **first MTPJ arthrodesis in 5 degrees of valgus** (relation to metatarsal shaft), **30 degrees of dorsiflexion** (relation to floor) → ↑ plantar pressure under 1st MT head\
✗ Wrong options: cheilectomy 10% or 30% · 10° valgus / 15° DF (ideal) · 5° varus

### Dorsiflexion malunion after prior surgery

**Dorsal great-toe irritation in closed shoes despite hardware removal** — **dorsiflexion malunion** · functional, but shoe problems\
✗ Wrong options: neuropathy · **nonunion** (activity pain + swelling, relieved by stiff shoes) · **transfer metatarsalgia** (plantar 2nd MT head pain) · **plantarflexion malunion** (gait difficulty, walks on lateral foot)

### Failed implant/hemiarthroplasty – removal & bone-block arthrodesis

**Commonest failure modes** — **aseptic loosening**, **subsidence**, **infection**

**Silastic, 15 yr, synovitis, normal ESR / CRP / WBC, erythema settles on elevation** — **implant removal + synovectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection · orthosis · I&D + IV antibiotics · revision silastic

**Silastic long-term complication complicating revision fusion** — **osteolysis** · also loss of fixation, pathologic fracture\
✗ Wrong options: recurrent deformity · infection (not ↑) · chronic synovitis (less impact) · **floating toe** (Weil osteotomy)

**Lucency around phalangeal component, pain since return to labour, normal labs** — **mechanical failure**\
✗ Wrong options: septic arthritis · periprosthetic fracture · **aseptic loosening from PE debris** (needs prior stable period) · transfer metatarsalgia

**Failed arthroplasty + lesser-toe metatarsalgia + callosities, negative aspirate** — **implant removal, synovectomy, structural bone graft, arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: long-stem revision · silastic conversion · spacer + reimplant (no infection) · removal + synovectomy alone (weak push-off)

**Failed silastic, osteolysis, metatarsalgia** — **implant removal + structural bone graft + MTP fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: Morton's extension · in situ fusion · Keller (worsens metatarsalgia) · titanium total toe

**Failed hemiarthroplasty with bone loss** — **bone-block arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: **in situ arthrodesis** (short 1st ray → transfer metatarsalgia) · Keller · Moberg · silicone

**Failed hemiarthroplasty + shortening + cock-up** — **arthrodesis with structural bone graft**\
✗ Wrong options: tenotomy (treats consequence not cause) · osteotomy · resection · **particulate graft** (minimal bone loss only)

**Severe 1st MT shortening after arthroplasty** — **distraction bone block arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: antibiotic spacer · shortening arthrodesis · **ex-fix lengthening** (brachymetatarsia) · revision arthroplasty

| Structural bone graft indication |
|---|
| **1st MT shortening not rebalanced by lesser MT osteotomy** (usually **> 5 mm**) — commonest after **failed arthroplasty** |
| **Proximal phalanx bone loss** — not enough bone for fixation without violating IP joint |
| **Shortening with loss of medial support of 2nd toe** → **2nd MTP varus** |

**Graft** — **tricortical iliac crest** (allograft commonest, Orthobullets) · autograft or allograft (OB)

![Bone-block arthrodesis of the 1st MTP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7009-bone-block-artrhrodesis.jpg)

## X vs Y

| Orthosis | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Morton's extension** (carbon fiber) | **Hallux rigidus** |
| **UCBL** | Flexible pes planovalgus |
| **Arizona brace** (UCBL + lace-up) | Flexible pes planovalgus |
| **Medial flare** | Ankle instability |
| **Negative heel rocker** | ↑ PF in terminal stance |
| High toe box | Hammer / mallet toe |

| | **Cheilectomy** | **Moberg** | **Arthrodesis** | **Keller** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Grade | **1–2** | Adjunct (1–2) | **3–4** | Any, elderly |
| Pain | **Terminal DF** | Insufficient DF | **Mid-range**, + grind | — |
| Patient | Active | **Runner, dancer** | Young labourer, athlete | **Low demand** |
| Pitfall | > 30% → instability | Loses PF | Malposition | **Cock-up**, metatarsalgia |

| | **Weil osteotomy** | **Helal osteotomy** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Intra-articular**, parallel to sole | **More proximal** |
| Fixation | **Screw perpendicular** to cut | **None** |
| Use | Shortening for **MTP dislocation** · floating toe risk | Shortening |

## How it's asked

**"Morton's extension is used for?"** → **hallux rigidus**

**"Morton's extension limits which motion?"** → **hallux MTP dorsiflexion**

**"Poor surgical candidate, + grind — orthotic?"** → **Morton's carbon fiber extension**\
✗ Wrong options: UCBL · Arizona

**"Which radiograph benefits from this orthosis?"** → **hallux rigidus (Figure F)**

**"Hallux rigidus associations EXCEPT?"** → **FHL rupture**

**"Hallux rigidus can lead to?"** → **transfer metatarsalgia**

**"Grade 2, failed Morton's, 35° arc?"** → **cheilectomy + joint debridement**

**"Terminal-DF pain, mild OA, dorsal osteophyte?"** → **cheilectomy**

**"Carpenter, shoe irritation, failed carbon shank?"** → **resect dorsal osteophyte + 25% of dorsal MT head**

**"Pain at extremes, < 50% JSN, cheilectomy planned — grade?"** → **grade 2**

**"Best cheilectomy candidate?"** → **dorsal osteophyte, no stiffness, pain at terminal DF**

**"Pain only at terminal DF, failed rigid orthotics?"** → **dorsal cheilectomy**

**"Persistent pain at extreme DF despite Morton's?"** → **dorsal cheilectomy**

**"Runner, only 20° DF after cheilectomy?"** → **Moberg procedure**

**"Ballet dancer, stage 2?"** → **cheilectomy, débridement ± Moberg**

**"+ grind, 20° painful arc — best long-term?"** → **fusion in neutral rotation, 15° valgus, 10° dorsiflexion**

**"Pain at 2nd MTP + 2nd-toe deformity after fusion?"** → **fused in shortened position**

**"Lateral foot pain + calluses after fusion?"** → **fused in plantarflexion**

**"Sesamoid pain under 1st MT head after surgery?"** → **5° valgus, 30° dorsiflexion arthrodesis**

**"Tennis player, stage 4?"** → **arthrodesis**

**"Mid-range pain, 10° DF, eroded joint?"** → **arthrodesis**

**"Failed cheilectomy — best pain relief + function?"** → **1st MTP arthrodesis**

**"Positive grind, 10° DF, stage III?"** → **1st MTP arthrodesis**

**"Grade 4 golfer, wants no further surgery?"** → **great-toe MTP fusion**

**"Pain through arc, 5° DF, + grind — most reliable?"** → **MTP arthrodesis**

**"Toe tip clears footplate by 5 mm?"** → **irrigate and close**

**"Best arthrodesis candidate?"** → **pain throughout MTP motion**

**"Silastic implant, synovitis, normal labs?"** → **removal of implant + synovectomy**

**"Failed arthroplasty + lesser metatarsalgia?"** → **implant removal, synovectomy, structural bone grafting, arthrodesis**

**"Failed hemiarthroplasty with shortening and cock-up?"** → **arthrodesis with structural bone graft**

**"Failed silastic, metatarsalgia + great toe pain?"** → **implant removal + structural bone grafting + MTP fusion**

**"Lucency around phalangeal component in labourer?"** → **mechanical failure**

**"Severe 1st MT shortening after prior arthroplasty?"** → **distraction bone block arthrodesis**

**"Failed hemiarthroplasty with bone loss — most reliable?"** → **bone-block arthrodesis**

**"Silastic complication that complicates revision?"** → **osteolysis**

**"80-yo, low demand — fastest rehab?"** → **Keller resection arthroplasty + osteophyte removal**

**"Synthetic cartilage implant — true?"** → **equivalent pain reduction vs arthrodesis**

**"Dorsal shoe irritation after fusion despite hardware removal?"** → **dorsiflexion malunion**

**"Checkrein deformity of great toe follows?"** → **fracture of distal third of tibia** (TO)
