# Hallux Varus

*Foot & Ankle · 25 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **medial deviation of the hallux relative to 1st metatarsal** · most often **iatrogenic overcorrection after bunion surgery**

**Incidence** — **2–14% after hallux valgus surgery** · **more common in women**

**Three components** — **medial deviation of hallux** at 1st MTP · **supination of phalanx** · **claw-toe deformity**

### Causes – excessive lateral release & over-correction

| Cause | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Congenital** | **Metatarsal physeal bracket** |
| **Iatrogenic (commonest)** | Overcorrection at bunion surgery |
| Trauma | — |
| Inflammatory | **RA**, **ankylosing spondylitis** |
| Neurological | **CMT**, **post-polio** |

**Iatrogenic pathoanatomy** — **excessive lateral soft-tissue / capsular release** · **fibular (lateral) sesamoid excision** · **excessive medial eminence resection** · **overplication of medial capsule** · **overcorrection of IMA** (e.g. **excessively large proximal medial opening-wedge osteotomy**) or of hallux valgus interphalangeus · loss of osseous support · malaligned osteotomy, soft-tissue laxity

**Medial release** — listed as a contributor (OB7610) · not **excessive medial capsular release** (OB220893 — lateral release + medial overplication cause it) — read the options

**Not causes** — **tibial sesamoidectomy** · **inadequate** medial eminence resection · **1st TMT hypermobility** · **inadequate IMA correction** · failed pin fixation · choosing soft-tissue procedure over fusion

**After fibular sesamoid resection (TO)** — **unopposed pull of abductor hallucis** → **medial dislocation of tibial sesamoid** → medial hallux translation · also **MTP joint hyperextension** + **buttonholing of metatarsal head** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: **adductor hallucis**

## 3 Presentation

**Complaint** — **appearance** (toe "too straight" → excessive medial deviation) · **difficulty wearing shoes** · often **asymptomatic**

**Pain** — suggests **joint arthritis** or trauma · ↓ ROM, instability, weak push-off

**Exam** — varus angulation · **dorsal contracture of MTP** ± IP contracture · **EHL displaced medially → "bowstring"** · **tibial sesamoid displaced medially** · medial blister · **fixed vs flexible (passively correctable)** decides treatment

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View / finding | Detail |
|---|---|
| **WB AP + lateral foot** | Standard · + NWB oblique + **sesamoid axial** |
| **Hallux valgus angle** | **< 0°** (normal **5–15°**) · e.g. HVA −19° |
| **IMA** | **Reduced** (overcorrected; e.g. 6°) |
| Medial eminence | **Excessively resected** |
| Sesamoids | **Tibial sesamoid subluxed medially** · **lateral (fibular) sesamoid absent** |
| Osteotomy | Overcorrected |
| Joints | **Degenerative change at MTP / IP** → fusion |
| **CT / MRI** | Rarely needed · **osteonecrosis of 1st metatarsal** |

![Hallux varus after bunion surgery](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7012-graphic-2-large-2.jpg)

![Iatrogenic hallux varus after bunion correction](../../MCQ/attachments/q7610-a-22.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Hallux varus
├─ Early post-op (first weeks) → taping / splinting up to 3 months
├─ Flexible, asymptomatic / mild → wide toe box, padding
├─ Flexible (passively correctable), young → tendon transfer + medial release
│      └─ bony overcorrection → lateral closing-wedge osteotomy
└─ Fixed, arthritic, painful, or elderly → 1st MTP arthrodesis
```

### Treatment – reducible vs fixed deformity (tendon transfer vs MTP fusion)

**Taping / splinting** — **early post-op varus**; may correct if started **within first weeks**; continue **up to 3 months** / until soft tissues heal · not for fixed deformity

**Shoe modification** — **wider, flexible toe box** + padding · **flexible, longstanding, asymptomatic** · not for chronic fixed + arthritic · established varus → nonop role limited

**Lateral closing-wedge osteotomy** — **overcorrected proximal / distal MT or proximal phalangeal osteotomy** · ± scar release + lateral ligament repair · not when MTP degenerative

| Tendon procedure (flexible deformity) | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Adductor hallucis reattachment + medial release** | **Difficult after McBride** |
| **Abductor hallucis transfer → lateral base of proximal phalanx** | + reattach / reef **conjoined tendon** in web space |
| **EHL or EHB transfer + medial release ± IP fusion** | Under **transverse intermetatarsal ligament** → **distal MT neck** (lateral → medial) · **split EHL** + MT osteotomy + medial capsule release for **young, passively correctable** |

### Operative criteria

| 1st MTP arthrodesis | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Absolute** | **Fixed (not passively correctable) MTP** with significant deformity + non-functioning hallux · **painful MTP arthritis** |
| **Relative** | **Medial eminence resected beyond tibial sesamoid sulcus** |
| **Age** | **Elderly** — **72-yo, moderately reducible, painful** → **fusion** (soft tissue not indicated for age) |

**Fusion gives** — **most predictable pain relief, alignment, activity, shoe fit, no recurrence**

| ✗ Alternative | Why not |
|---|---|
| **MTP replacement / hemiarthroplasty** | Unpredictable; **radiolucency, subsidence**, high failure (hallux rigidus) |
| **Keller resection arthroplasty** | Unpredictable pain relief, activity, recurrence |
| **Interposition arthroplasty** | **No realignment** |
| **Lateral MT opening-wedge osteotomy** | Doesn't correct **arthritic, incongruent MTP** |
| **1st MT–cuneiform (Lapidus) fusion** | **Salvage for failed hallux valgus**, not varus |
| **Cheilectomy + medial release** / soft tissue alone | Won't correct fixed deformity |
| **Great-toe amputation** | Unreasonable in healthy patient |

## X vs Y

| | **Flexible (passively correctable)** | **Fixed / arthritic** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | **Young** | **Elderly** or degenerative MTP |
| Surgery | **Tendon transfer + medial release** ± osteotomy | **1st MTP arthrodesis** |
| Splint / tape | Useful early | No |

## How it's asked

**"Hallux varus after fibular sesamoid resection — all EXCEPT?"** → **unopposed pull of adductor hallucis** (it's the abductor) (TO)

**"Progressive varus 2 yr after bunion surgery — factor?"** → **excessive lateral soft-tissue release**

**"HVA −19°, IMA 6° after bunionectomy — error?"** → **excessively large proximal metatarsal medial opening-wedge osteotomy**

**"Rigid varus, pain mid-range, 70-yo?"** → **hallux metatarsophalangeal joint fusion**

**"Not completely reducible, failed taping — predictable alignment + pain relief?"** → **MTP joint arthrodesis**

**"72-yo, moderately reducible, painful — most predictable?"** → **first MTP fusion**

**"Fixed varus + DJD after fibular sesamoidectomy?"** → **first MTP arthrodesis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Hammer Toe

*Foot & Ankle · 26 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest lesser-toe deformity** — hammer toe · **older / middle-aged women** · **2nd toe** usually

**Associated** — **painful corn on dorsal PIP** · secondary to **hallux valgus** or curly toe · neurogenic, degenerative, metabolic causes

### Plantar plate attenuation – MTP deformity

**Pathoanatomy** — **plantar plate injury** · **overpull of EDL** · **intrinsic imbalance / weakness**

**Plantar plate rupture → hammer toe** — MT head no longer suspended → **translates plantarly** → **MTP extension** → **EDL overpull** → **PIP flexion** to keep toe plantigrade\
✗ Wrong options: mallet toe · TMT arthritis · Freiberg · hallux valgus (not the direct result)

**Plantar plate attenuation causes** — **MTP joint extension**\
✗ Wrong options: DIP flexion · PIP extension · MTP / DIP malrotation

**Plantar 2nd MTP pain** — "**stepping on a pebble**" · worse in high heels · MRI = degenerative plantar plate tear

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **dorsal PIP pain with shoe wear** · deformity · plantar 2nd MTP pain

**Exam** — **PIP flexion + DIP extension** (+ slight MTP extension)

**Ankle plantarflexion test** — **flexible deformity corrects** · **fixed / rigid does not correct**

**Push-up test** — dorsal pressure under the MT head → **flexible deformity reduces** (removes extrinsic overpull)

![Hammer toe – clinical appearance](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7014-clinical-image-moved.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**: rigid or flexible lesser toe with PIP flexion, DIP extension, slight MTP extension

### Key radiology findings

**Imaging** — **not required** for diagnosis or treatment

### Claw toe vs hammer toe vs mallet toe

| Joint | **Claw toe** ![Claw toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7014-clawe-toe-moved.jpg) | **Hammer toe** ![Hammer toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7014-illustration-moved.jpg) | **Mallet toe** ![Mallet toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7014-mallet-toe-illustration-moved.jpg) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **DIP** | Flexion | **Extension** | **Flexion** |
| **PIP** | Flexion | **Flexion** | Normal |
| **MTP** | **Hyperextension** | **Slight extension** | Normal |
| Cause | — | **EDL overpull** + intrinsic weakness | **FDL overpull / contracture** or EDL rupture |
| Callus | — | **Dorsal PIP** | **Toe pulp** (ulcers / calluses) |

## 5 Management

```
Hammer toe
├─ Pain / dorsal PIP corn → high toe box, foam / silicone sleeves, dorsal PIP padding
├─ Flexible, failed nonop → FDL → EDL transfer
├─ Rigid, failed nonop → PIP resection arthroplasty (± arthrodesis)
└─ MTP involved → Girdlestone (FDL → EDL)
       ├─ Mild MTP hyperextension → EDL Z-lengthening / tenotomy
       └─ Moderate–severe → EDL Z-lengthening + dorsal capsular release
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Flexible**, failed nonop | **FDL → EDL tendon transfer** |
| **Rigid / fixed**, failed nonop | **PIP resection arthroplasty** ± tenotomy / transfers · or **PIP arthrodesis** |
| **MTP involvement** | **Girdlestone** (FDL → EDL) — as for claw toe |
| Mild MTP hyperextension | **EDL Z-lengthening or tenotomy** |
| Moderate–severe MTP hyperextension | **EDL Z-lengthening + dorsal capsular release** |
| 2nd hammer toe + hallux valgus | **Correct hallux valgus** (arthrodesis if severe) · **amputation** if HV touches 3rd toe in elderly / poor health / declines reconstruction |

**Surgery indicated for** — progressive deformity · **fixed contractures** · **dorsal toe ulceration**

### Flexible vs rigid deformity – padding & PIP resection arthroplasty

**Fixed deformity** — **precludes success with soft-tissue procedures alone** → indication for **PIP resection arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: pain with shoes · all lesser toes involved · no MTP deformity · no hallux valgus

**Rigid 2nd hammer toe sequence** — **dorsal PIP padding → shoe modification → PIP resection arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: **plantar** padding (pain is dorsal) · MTP capsular release · MT head excision

**Extensor tenotomy / lengthening** — **with** (not before) resection arthroplasty in severe cases → for **MTP dorsiflexion contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: tenotomy preceding arthroplasty

**Rigid hammer toe, failed nonop** — **PIP resection arthroplasty with K-wire fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: **Weil osteotomy** (irreducible MTP subluxation / transfer metatarsalgia) · **FDL transfer** (flexible) · **EDL lengthening** (claw toe) · **DIP fusion** (rigid mallet toe)

**Resection arthroplasty technique** — resect **head + neck (distal condyle) of proximal phalanx** (± middle phalanx articular surface) → **fibrous joint** or arthrodesis (**true fusion not necessary**) · **K-wire 2–3 wk** · then **tape PIP in extension 3 wk**

**PIP arthrodesis** — option for rigid deformity · **high nonunion rate** · **K-wire = most cost-effective implant**

**Girdlestone** — **EDL Z-plasty lengthening** · **MTP capsule release** · ± oblique **MT shortening osteotomy** · **FDL → EDL transfer**

![Rigid hammer toe – PIP flexion, DIP extension](../../MCQ/attachments/q211176-illa-hammertoe.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Flexible hammer toe** | **Rigid hammer toe** |
|---|---|---|
| Ankle PF / push-up test | **Corrects** | **Does not correct** |
| Surgery | **FDL → EDL transfer** | **PIP resection arthroplasty** / arthrodesis |

| Procedure | Treats |
|---|---|
| **FDL transfer** | Flexible hammer toe |
| **PIP resection arthroplasty** | Rigid hammer toe |
| **EDL lengthening** | Claw toe / MTP hyperextension |
| **DIP fusion** | Rigid mallet toe |
| **Weil osteotomy** | Irreducible MTP subluxation · transfer metatarsalgia |

## How it's asked

**"Indication for PIP resection arthroplasty over soft-tissue balancing?"** → **fixed deformity**

**"Rigid 2nd hammer toe — sequence?"** → **dorsal PIP padding, shoe modification, PIP resection arthroplasty**

**"Rigid hammer toe, failed high toe box and sleeves?"** → **PIP resection arthroplasty with K-wire fixation**

**"Hammer toe — plantar plate attenuation causes?"** → **MTP joint extension**

**"Degenerative plantar plate tear — likely to develop?"** → **hammer toe**

**"Commonest lesser-toe deformity?"** → **hammer toe** (2nd toe)

**"Flexible hammer toe, failed nonop?"** → **FDL → EDL transfer**

**"PIP arthrodesis — cheapest implant?"** → **K-wire** (high nonunion rate)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# High Ankle Sprain & Syndesmosis Injury

*Foot & Ankle · 27 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **0.5% of ankle sprains** without fracture · **13% of all ankle fractures**

**Associated injuries** — OCD **15–25%** · peroneal tendon injury **up to 25%** · **deltoid ligament** · loose bodies · fractures: **Weber C**, Weber B, 5th MT base, anterior process calcaneus, lateral / posterior process talus

### Anatomy – distal tibiofibular syndesmosis

| Ligament | Origin → insertion | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **AITFL** | **Chaput tubercle** (anterolateral tibia) → **Wagstaffe tubercle** (anterior fibula) | Injured in isolated high ankle sprain |
| **PITFL** | **Volkmann tubercle** (posterior tibia) → posterior lateral malleolus | **Strongest component** |
| **Interosseous ligament (IOL)** | Distal continuation of IOM | **Main restraint to proximal migration of talus** |
| **Interosseous membrane (IOM)** | — | Disrupted → synostosis risk |
| **Inferior transverse ligament (ITL)** | — | — |

![Syndesmosis anatomy — IOM, IOL, AITFL, PITFL](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7029-syndesmosis-anatomy-ap.jpg)

**Not syndesmosis** — **ATFL** (anteroinferior fibula → **talar neck**) · CFL, PTFL (lateral complex) · **bifurcate (Chopart) ligament** (subtalar + midtarsal) · IPR (peroneal tendons)

**Fibular incisura morphology** — **crescentic commonest (61.3%)** · trapezoid · chevron · widow's peak · **flat least (3.1%)**

### Biomechanics

**Function** — maintains tibia–fibula integrity · resists **axial, rotational, translational** forces · widens **1 mm** in normal gait

| Ankle position | Talus in mortise | Distal fibula |
|---|---|---|
| **Dorsiflexion** | **Wider anterior talus** engages | **External rotation** + proximal, posterolateral translation |
| **Plantarflexion** | Narrow posterior talus | **Internal rotation** + distal, anteromedial translation |

**Inversion / eversion** — mainly subtalar → little effect on fibular rotation · **supination** (PF + inversion + adduction) → fibular IR

**Deltoid** — **indirectly stabilises** medial mortise · **superficial = restrains eversion** · **deep = prevents excessive ER**

| Sectioned (Massri-Pugin, cadaver) | Result |
|---|---|
| Isolated AITFL or PITFL | **No significant instability** |
| **AITFL + PITFL + IOL** | Diastasis **posterior incisura only** |
| **All syndesmotic + deltoid** | Diastasis **anterior + posterior** incisura |

**Instability** — needs rupture of **≥ 2 ligaments** (Rammelt) · instability ↑ **immensely** once **deltoid** also cut (Zalavras)

## 2 Mechanism

**Commonest** — **external rotation** of foot (or **foot planted, body internally rotates**) → talus rotates laterally, pushes fibula away from tibia

**Consequences** — ↑ tibial compressive stress · lateral fibular subluxation · mortise incongruence

**Eversion injury** — medial + lateral pain, positive calf squeeze

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **anterolateral ankle pain proximal to AITFL** · ± medial tenderness / swelling · **difficulty bearing weight** (lateral sprains usually can WB)

**Clue** — nagging pain with dynamic moves, **relieved by tight circumferential wrap above ankle** → **AITFL** (wrap holds fibula in incisura)

**Syndesmosis tenderness** — **single best predictor of return to play**

| Test | Technique | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Squeeze (Hopkin)** | Compress tibia + fibula at **midcalf** | Pain at syndesmosis |
| **External rotation stress** | **Knee + hip 90°**, ankle neutral; ER / dorsiflex foot | Pain / widening |
| **Cotton (lateral stress)** | **Lateral pull on fibula** | Syndesmosis widening |
| **Fibular translation** | AP drawer on fibula, tibia fixed | ↑ translation + pain · **most false positives** (Beumer) |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – examination, stress radiographs & MRI; Maisonneuve

**Views** — AP, lateral, mortise ankle **+ AP, lateral entire tibia / fibula** (**proximal fibula fracture = Maisonneuve**)

**Optional** — **external rotation stress view** · **gravity stress view** (deltoid competence) · **contralateral ankle** (widening vs normal variant)

**Persisting WB pain 2 wk after "sprain"** — **ankle stress radiographs + ipsilateral tibia-fibula radiographs** (bilateral comparison)\
✗ Wrong options: boot WBAT · NWB boot without further films · MRI first · tib-fib films alone

**Fibular fracture + syndesmotic injury** — **ORIF fibula, then assess syndesmosis** (repair may stabilise; most still need fixation)

### Key radiology findings

| Measurement | Normal | Injury |
|---|---|---|
| **Tibiofibular overlap** | **> 6 mm AP** · **> 1 mm mortise** · **> 10 mm AP** (Orthobullets ligament note) | ↓ |
| **Tibiofibular clear space** | **< 6 mm AP + mortise** · **< 5 mm** (Orthobullets ligament note) | ↑ |
| **Medial clear space** | **≤ 4 mm** (best on mortise) | ↑ · good specificity, **very poor sensitivity** |
| **Lateral tibiofibular ratio** | APTF ratio avg **0.94**; anterior fibular cortex crosses **middle of tibial shadow** | **Most reliable plain-film measure of reduction** |

![AP measurements — 1 tibiofibular overlap, 2 tibiofibular clear space, 3 medial clear space](../../MCQ/attachments/q217419-a-syndesmosis-ap.jpg)

![Lateral tibiofibular ratio — segment 4 (anterior tibia → anterior fibula) over 5 (anterior fibula → posterior tibia)](../../MCQ/attachments/q217419-b-x-ray-of-normal-ankle-lateral.jpg)

**AP measurements** — limited sensitivity, poor reliability · **cannot reliably predict** injury (Krähenbühl)

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **CT** | Suspicion with normal X-rays · **more sensitive than X-ray for minor injury** · **post-op check of reduction** · Wagstaffe tubercle (AITFL insertion) on axial |
| **MRI** | Suspicion with normal X-rays · **> 90% sensitivity / specificity** (Han) · **\~100%** (Krähenbühl) — diagnostic tool of choice |
| **Lambda sign (coronal MRI)** | **Hyperintense signal contiguous between mortise and tibiofibular articulation** (OB) · **> 2 mm diastasis on coronal** (TO) · "**highly sensitive + specific**" (Orthobullets) · not an independent predictor, sensitive + specific **with positive exam** (Ryan) |
| Deltoid discontinuity on MRI | Poor indicator — no deltoid ruptures in Han's 8 syndesmotic injuries |

![Coronal T2 MRI lambda sign — fluid tracking from mortise into syndesmosis](../../MCQ/attachments/q212100-illa-lambda-sign-illustration.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Syndesmotic injury
├─ No diastasis, stable ankle → NWB CAM boot / cast 2–3 wk
├─ Unstable on stress views / refractory sprain → syndesmotic fixation
└─ Fracture + syndesmosis → fix fracture, then stress test
       ├─ stable → no syndesmotic fixation
       └─ unstable → screw or suture button
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative | Operative (screw or suture button) |
|---|---|
| **Sprain without diastasis or ankle instability** | **Sprain (no fracture) unstable on stress radiographs** |
| — | **Sprain refractory to conservative treatment** |
| — | **Fracture still unstable after fracture fixation** |

**Nonop** — delayed WB until pain-free · PT in **brace limiting ER** · recovery **notoriously prolonged + variable**, up to **2× standard sprain**

### Intraoperative assessment & reduction

**Best intra-op test** — **external rotation stress test**: **fewest false positives, smallest inter-observer variance**\
✗ Wrong options: squeeze · fibular translation · Cotton · anterior drawer

**Cotton test** — previously reported least false positives · produces **greater tibiofibular clear space** widening than ER test in Weber C (Stoffel) · ER stress gives **35% more medial clear space** widening (Matuszewski) — read the options

**Dorsiflexion** — **externally rotates fibula** (wide anterior talus) · aids rotational reduction · **maximal dorsiflexion before screw not required** (Tornetta: PF fixation did not limit DF)

**Clamp** — **lateral malleolar ridge of fibula → centre of AP width of tibia** = most accurate reduction (Gardner)

**Goal** — restore **fibular length, rotation, position in incisura** · **outcome depends on reduction, not fixation type**

**Persistent talar tilt after suture button** — **deltoid** incompetent\
✗ Wrong options: interosseous (addressed by button) · bifurcate · CFL · PTFL

**Reconstruction order** — proximal → distal: **IOL + AITFL first** → **repair deltoid** if medial talar tilt persists (Wake)

**AITFL repair** — restores mortise stability · syndesmotic fixation unnecessary once repaired · reconstruct with **4th EDL autograft** or suture (Nelson)

### Fixation – screw vs suture button

**Screw technique** — **two 3.5 or 4.5 mm** screws · **3 or 4 cortices** · **2–5 cm above plafond** (Orthobullets) · **2–3 cm, parallel to joint, angled 30° anterior** (TO) — read the options · NWB **6–12 wk**

**No difference** — stainless steel vs **titanium** · **3 vs 4 cortices** (pullout) · **bioabsorbable** similar · two screws preferable

| | **Syndesmotic screw** | **Suture button (TightRope)** |
|---|---|---|
| **Malreduction** | **Higher — up to 40%** (**39%** vs 15%, Sanders RCT) | **Lower** |
| **Reoperation / implant removal** | **30%** — often requires removal | **4%** — **no removal needed** |
| Function | Same | Same · early results: **earlier return to activity** |
| Mechanics | Medial + anterior talar translation | Closer to native |
| Zone 2 fibular screws | ↑ malreduction | — |

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Malreduction** | Commoner than believed · **up to 40% with screws** |
| **Missed injury** | **End-stage ankle arthritis** |
| **Chronic instability** | Inadequate reduction → instability + post-traumatic arthritis |
| **Tibiofibular synostosis** | **\~10% after Weber C** (Orthobullets) · 15 / 230 operative ankle fractures, mostly Weber C (Albers) |

### Post-injury synostosis / heterotopic ossification

**Cause** — high ankle sprain with **IOM disruption** · prior **tibial stress fracture** (Henry) · Weber C fixation

**Timing** — HO develops within **6–12 months**

**Presentation** — **activity-related leg / anterior shin pain** for 1–2 yr · **loss of ankle dorsiflexion** + tib-fib motion · often **asymptomatic**; most return to sport

**Treatment** — symptomatic first · **surgical excision** (anterolateral approach) only for **persistent pain despite nonop + no uptake ("cold") on bone scan**

**Next step, synostosis with ongoing uptake** — **observation + repeat bone scan → resect when no uptake**

**✗ Synostosis traps** — **indomethacin 25 mg TID 6 wk** + **700 cGy** = HO prophylaxis after acetabular surgery · **60 Gy** (sarcoma / myeloma; wounds won't heal) · chemotherapy (only with biopsy-proven neoplasm) · excise when "hot" · instability or ↓ plantarflexion as indication

![Post-traumatic distal tibiofibular synostosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7029-radiograph-synostosis.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **High ankle sprain** | **Low (lateral) ankle sprain** |
|---|---|---|
| Ligaments | **AITFL**, PITFL, IOL, IOM, ITL | **ATFL, CFL, PTFL** |
| Mechanism | **External rotation** | **Inversion** |
| Weight bearing | **Difficult** | Usually possible |
| Test | Squeeze, ER stress | Anterior drawer |
| Recovery | **≈ 2× longer** | Shorter |

| | **ER stress test** | **Cotton test** |
|---|---|---|
| Manoeuvre | ER foot, tibia stabilised | Lateral pull on fibula |
| Intra-op | **Fewest false positives, least variance** (recent) | Least false positives (older) · more clear-space widening in Weber C |

## How it's asked

**"CT arrow at Wagstaffe tubercle — structure + next step?"** → **anterior inferior tibiofibular ligament (AITFL); ORIF of the fibula with subsequent syndesmotic assessment**

**"Foot planted, body turned, better with a tight wrap above ankle — injured?"** → **AITFL**

**"Sprain, WB pain at 2 wk, ?MCS widening — next?"** → **ankle stress radiographs and ipsilateral tibia-fibula radiographs**

**"Most sensitive + specific imaging finding?"** → **coronal MRI hyperintense signal contiguous between mortise and tibiofibular articulation** (lambda sign)

**"Intra-op test with fewest false positives?"** → **external rotation stress test**

**"Manoeuvre that externally rotates the fibula?"** → **ankle dorsiflexion**

**"Most reliable X-ray measure of syndesmotic reduction?"** → **lateral tibiofibular ratio (measurements 4/5)**

**"Persistent talar tilt after suture button?"** → **deltoid**

**"True of syndesmotic screws?"** → **higher rate of malreduction with screw fixation versus flexible fixation**

**"Syndesmotic synostosis, pain, uptake on bone scan — next?"** → **observation and repeat bone scan, then surgical resection if no increased uptake**

**"Indication + timing for synostosis excision?"** → **persistent pain despite non-operative treatment and no uptake on bone scan**

**"Single best predictor of return to play?"** → **syndesmosis tenderness**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Interdigital (Morton's) Neuroma

*Foot & Ankle · 28 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Demographics** — **middle-aged women** · **F:M 9:1** (Orthobullets) · peak **6th decade** (OB)

**Commonest site** — **3rd web space** (3rd + 4th interdigital nerves, between 3rd + 4th MT heads) · **2nd web space = second commonest**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st web space · 1st MTPJ (hallux rigidus / valgus) · 5th MT head

**Other OB wording** — "2nd or 3rd web space" / "2nd and 3rd interdigital nerves" — read the options

### Anatomy & pathophysiology

**Interdigital nerve** — **plantar to the transverse (deep) intermetatarsal ligament** between MT heads\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal to it

**3rd web space** — **confluence of medial + lateral plantar nerves** (lateral branch MPN + medial branch LPN share a common perineurium)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior tarsal tunnel (deep peroneal nerve)

**Nature** — **compressive (entrapment) neuropathy** → **perineural fibrosis + entrapment** of the interdigital nerve

**Mechanism** — compression / tension around **transverse intermetatarsal ligament** at toe-off · **repetitive microtrauma** · traction from tethering between fixed medial + mobile lateral MTs

**Aggravators** — **high heels**, **narrow toe box**, wide feet

![Third interspace receives branches of both medial and lateral plantar nerves](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7041-medial-branch-of-lpn-and-lateral-branch-of-mpn-merge-and-sha.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis & location (third web space, Mulder's click)

**Pain** — **plantar forefoot**, between MT heads · worse with **weight-bearing + narrow toe-box shoes** · relieved by **removing shoes + massaging foot** · "**walking on a marble**"

**Paraesthesia** — burning / tingling into adjacent toes (3rd + 4th toes for 3rd web space) · plantar aspect of web space

**Most consistent sign** — **tenderness of involved web space** · **positive web-space compression test** · neuroma may be palpable

**Mulder's click** — squeeze forefoot (MTs together) + dorsal pressure between MT heads → painful / bursal click

**Drawer test at MTPJ** — assesses **MTPJ instability** (differential)

![Mulder's click – squeezing the forefoot](../../MCQ/attachments/q213946-illa-screen-shot-2019-12-19-at-8-30-31-am.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (history + exam); advanced imaging generally not required

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **WB AP / lateral / oblique X-ray** | **Usually normal** · may show bony deformity · excludes Freiberg (MT head flattening), MTP arthritis |
| **Ultrasound** | **Non-palpable neuroma** with clear clinical picture · **oval / well-circumscribed hypoechoic mass parallel to MTs** · **operator dependent** |
| **MRI** | **Not required** (✗ Wrong options: "needed to confirm") · rules out other pathology / stress fracture when exam non-diagnostic |

![MRI: interdigital neuroma (arrow) between MT heads](../../MCQ/attachments/q211649-illa-untitled2.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria

| Test | Positive result |
|---|---|
| **Common digital nerve block** (diagnostic lidocaine) | **Confirms diagnosis** → numbness of lateral toe surface + **relief of pain** |
| **No relief after well-placed block** | Think **MTP synovitis** |
| **Histology** | **Perineural fibrosis + nerve entrapment, no fibrinoid necrosis** |

### Differential – rheumatoid nodule & histology

| Lesion | Histology |
|---|---|
| **Morton's neuroma** | **Perineural fibrosis**, entrapped interdigital nerve, **no fibrinoid necrosis** |
| **Rheumatoid nodule** | Subcutaneous · **central fibrinoid necrosis + palisading fibrous cells** |
| **Neurofibroma** | Hypocellular fibroblastic network, **wire-like collagen**, myxoid areas |
| **Schwannoma (benign PNST)** | Encapsulated · **Antoni A** (palisading spindle cells) + **Antoni B** (hypocellular mucinous) |
| **Plantar fibromatosis** | From **plantar fascia** · dense mature collagen + fibrocytes · not provoked by squeeze |

**Other differentials** — **MTP synovitis**, metatarsalgia, **stress fracture**, MTPJ arthritis, MT head osteonecrosis (Freiberg), neoplasm, lumbar radiculopathy \
✗ Wrong options: **anterior tarsal tunnel** (deep peroneal nerve) · **tarsal tunnel**

## 5 Management

```
Morton's neuroma
├─ First line → wide toe-box shoe + firm sole + metatarsal pad
│      (± short-term steroid injection, dorsal)
├─ Failed nonop → dorsal neurectomy + DTML release, bury stump
└─ Recurrent / stump neuroma → revision neurectomy (plantar or dorsal) + burial
```

### Nonoperative

**First line** — **flat shoe with wide toe box + firm sole + metatarsal pad**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · PT (limited role) · serial steroid injections · custom orthotics before shoe change

**Metatarsal pad** — offloads MT heads by **↑ intermetatarsal space** → ↓ nerve pressure

**Results** — unpredictable · **\~20% complete resolution** (Orthobullets) · **roughly half** eventually need surgery (OB) · "most" need surgery (another OB) — read the options · NSAIDs rarely help

**Corticosteroid injection** — **short-term relief only**, **no long-term relief** · usually **dorsal** · steroid + LA **not superior** to LA alone · \~50% still request excision · **serial injections → local tissue (fat-pad) atrophy**

**Alcohol sclerosing injection** — not recommended (no long-term data, risk to surrounding tissue)

(✗ Wrong options: **Wrong orthoses** — **Morton extension** = hallux rigidus) · **longitudinal arch support** = PTT tendinitis · **carbon-fibre insert** = midfoot arthritis · **AFO** = peroneal palsy / foot drop\
✗ Wrong options: "orthotics with ↑ arch height first line"

### Treatment – injection & surgical excision (dorsal vs plantar)

**Failed nonop** — **neurectomy** (most effective, most durable) · **dorsal approach most common**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat steroid · isolated DTML release (less effective) · distal oblique shortening osteotomy / MTP capsular release + extensor lengthening (these are for **MTP synovitis**)

**Percutaneous nerve ablation** — not used

| Dorsal neurectomy step | Detail |
|---|---|
| Incision | **3–4 cm** just proximal to web space |
| Dissection | **Blunt** → protect **superficial peroneal nerve** branches (✗ Wrong options: deep peroneal) |
| Exposure | Spread MTs → tension + **transect transverse intermetatarsal ligament** (✗ Wrong options: "reduces success") |
| Resection | Identify nerve proximal + distal to bifurcation → resect **≥ 3 cm proximal to ligament** |
| Burial | Bury proximal stump in **intrinsic muscles** |
| Closure | **Repair DTML** → prevents intermetatarsal instability |

**Dual neuromas (2nd + 3rd web spaces)** — **resect 2nd web space + neurolysis (DTML release) of 3rd** → equal relief, protects **digital arteries** to 3rd + 4th toes\
✗ Wrong options: resect both via two dorsal incisions (highest arterial risk) · leave 3rd untreated · FDL transfer · single plantar incision

## 6 Complications

### Complications of neurectomy

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Stump neuroma – inadequate resection** | **Commonest** cause of persistent / recurrent pain |
| **Inadequate retraction (traction neuritis)** | Plantar branches tether nerve → no retraction (✗ Wrong options: commonest) |
| **Painful plantar scar** | **\~5%**, with **plantar** incision |
| **Intermetatarsal instability** | DTML not repaired |
| **Wrong structure resected** | Digital artery / neurovascular bundle or **lumbrical** |
| **Toe ischaemia** | Digital artery injury in dual web-space surgery |

### Recurrent neuroma – inadequate proximal resection & revision

**Cause** — **inadequate proximal resection** → residual **plantar-directed nerve branches** tether nerve to plantar skin → no retraction out of WB zone of MT head\
✗ Wrong options: painful scar · infection · Freiberg · MTP arthritis · tarsal tunnel · MTP synovitis

**Presentation** — same symptoms as primary + **Tinel's sign** at prior resection site

**Resection level** — **≥ 3 cm proximal** to DTML (Orthobullets, OB) · plantar branches **absent 4 cm proximal** → cut there (another OB) — read the options · "5 mm proximal" = inadequate

**Revision** — **revision neurectomy through plantar OR dorsal approach + nerve burial**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid + PT · fishmouth web-space incision (poor view, wound problems) · lumbrical transection → MTP extension deformity · dual incisions

**Plantar approach in revision** — preferred by most after a dorsal primary → avoids scar, **direct view of nerve bifurcation**, easier **proximal** dissection, DTML seen directly

**Dorsal approach in revision** — advantage = **lower risk of plantar scar tenderness + wound complications** · **earlier weight-bearing** but **recurrence not lower** · needs DTML release · harder proximal dissection

**Revision outcomes** — function similar · no society recommendation favouring either

## X vs Y

| | **Dorsal approach** | **Plantar approach** |
|---|---|---|
| Use | **Most common primary** | **Preferred for revision** |
| Complete excision | Less likely · revision **8–10%** | **More likely** · revision **< 5%** |
| Sensory loss | **Greater** | Less |
| Scar | No plantar scar · ✓ fewer wound problems | **Painful plantar scar** (\~5%) |
| Weight-bearing | **Earlier** | Longer NWB |
| DTML | Must be **released** | Seen directly |
| Outcomes / satisfaction / ADL limits | Comparable | Comparable |

| | **Morton's neuroma** | **MTP synovitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | Web space, plantar | MTP joint |
| Test | Mulder's click, web-space compression | **Drawer test** (instability) |
| Digital nerve block | **Relief** | **No relief** |
| Surgery | Neurectomy | Shortening osteotomy (long 2nd MT) · capsular release + extensor lengthening |

## How it's asked

**"Commonest location?"** → **third web space**

**"Cyclist, pain between 3rd + 4th toes, worse in shoes — next step?"** → **shoes with a wide toe box**

**"Tingling into 3rd + 4th toes, Mulder's click — true?"** → **confluence of medial + lateral plantar nerves**

**"Burning between 3rd + 4th toes, forefoot click — initial management image?"** → **metatarsal pad** (Figure D)

**"Plantar vs dorsal approach — plantar has?"** → **increased likelihood of complete neuroma excision**

**"Commonest complication of the most effective treatment?"** → **neuroma secondary to inadequate resection**

**"Failed shoe change, brief relief from steroid — next?"** → **dorsal neurectomy**

**"Walking on a marble — true of treatment?"** → **corticosteroid injections do not provide long-term relief**

**"2nd + 3rd web-space neuromas — safest plan?"** → **resect 2nd, neurolysis of 3rd by DTML release**

**"Excised 5 mm proximal to ligament, Tinel's positive — cause?"** → **inadequate proximal resection**

**"Commonest cause of persistent pain after excision?"** → **inadequate resection of an interdigital neuroma**

**"Recurrence after dorsal surgery — best step?"** → **revision neurectomy through plantar or dorsal approach with nerve burial**

**"Primary advantage of dorsal approach for revision?"** → **lower risk of plantar scar tenderness + wound complications**

**"Polyarthropathy, ANA+, perineural fibrosis without fibrinoid necrosis?"** → **Morton's neuroma**\
✗ Wrong options: rheumatoid nodule

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Leg Nerve Entrapment Syndromes

*Foot & Ankle · 29 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Entrapment sites – overview

| Nerve | Site of compression | Name / clue | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ilioinguinal** | Hypertrophied abdominal muscles (intensive training) | Hyperaesthesia · pain ↑ with **hip hyperextension** | Surgical release if nonop fails |
| **Obturator** | Well-developed adductors (**skaters**) | Chronic **medial thigh pain** · **nerve conduction studies** establish diagnosis | Supportive |
| **Lateral femoral cutaneous** | Tight belts, prolonged hip flexion | **Meralgia paresthetica** (lateral thigh pain) | PT, NSAIDs, posture, release compressive devices |
| **Sciatic** | **Ischial tuberosity** · **piriformis** | **Piriformis syndrome** | — |
| **Saphenous** | **Adductor (Hunter's, subsartorial) canal** | **Surfer's neuropathy** · prolonged kneeling · pain **inferomedial to knee** | **Knee pads** first line |
| **Common peroneal** | **Behind fibula / fibular neck** — ganglion cyst, direct blow, pressure | Foot drop | **Proximal tibiofibular joint fusion** to prevent cyst recurrence |
| **Superficial peroneal** | **Fascial exit \~10–12 cm** (OB) · **12–15 cm** (Orthobullets) **proximal to lateral malleolus** | Dorsal foot numbness ↑ with **PF + inversion** | Observation → fascial release |
| **Deep peroneal** | **Inferior extensor retinaculum** | **Anterior tarsal tunnel syndrome** · **1st web space** | — |
| **Posterior tibial** | **Flexor retinaculum** / tarsal tunnel | **Tarsal tunnel syndrome** · medial + plantar foot | — |
| **1st branch lateral plantar (Baxter)** | **Abductor hallucis deep fascia vs medial quadratus plantae** | **Distal tarsal tunnel syndrome** · runners | **Release abductor hallucis fascia** |
| **Medial plantar** | **Knot of Henry** (FDL × FHL) · **foot orthotics** | **Jogger's foot** | **Discontinue orthosis** |
| **Sural** | **12–15 mm distal to fibular tip** (equinus) · gastrocnemius–Achilles musculotendinous junction (calf) | Lateral dorsum + heel | Surgical release |
| **Interdigital** | — | **Morton's neuroma** | — |

### Tarsal tunnel anatomy

**Tarsal tunnel** — fibro-osseous tunnel **directly inferior to medial malleolus** · roof = **flexor retinaculum** · **abductor hallucis = plantar border** · compression by fascial septae, flexor retinaculum, abductor hallucis

**Tibial nerve** — divides into **medial plantar**, **lateral plantar**, **medial calcaneal** branches · plantar nerves = **terminal sensory branches** → most plantar sensation

| Dissection label (medial ankle) | Structure |
|---|---|
| **A** | **1st branch of lateral plantar nerve (Baxter)** — **most commonly entrapped** |
| B | Medial calcaneal nerve branches |
| C | Tibial nerve |
| D | Lateral plantar nerve |
| E | Medial plantar nerve (jogger's foot) |

![Medial ankle dissection — tibial nerve, plantar nerves, medial calcaneal branches, 1st branch of LPN](../../MCQ/attachments/q3349-illb-plantar-foot-anatomy-answer-no-border.jpg)

**Dorsal sensation** — **SPN = dorsum except 1st web space** · **DPN = 1st web space** · **sural = lateral foot**\
✗ Wrong options: plantar surface

### Saphenous nerve entrapment (TO)

**Saphenous nerve (TO)** — **purely sensory** · **longest terminal branch of posterior division of femoral nerve** · **L3–L4**

**Course (TO)** — from below inguinal ligament with femoral artery · **deep to sartorius** throughout thigh · enters **subsartorial / adductor / Hunter's canal** (proximal third of thigh, per TO) — fibrous band **vastus medialis ↔ adductor magnus**

**Exit (TO)** — joins **saphenous branch of descending genicular artery** · both **pierce roof of the canal just proximal to the adductor hiatus**

**Commonest entrapment site (TO)** — **adductor canal**\
✗ Wrong options: below inguinal ligament · at adductor hiatus · below patella

### Common peroneal nerve compression (TO)

**Why CPN (TO)** — **most fixed nerve, minimum tolerance to mobility** · fixed at origin **under biceps femoris** → fixed to **fibular neck** as it winds around

**Unconscious, lying in lateral position, bruised lateral knee (TO)** — **CPN** injury\
✗ Wrong options: tibial · ulnar · trigeminal

![Course of the common peroneal nerve behind biceps femoris and around the fibular neck](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17138089-2.png)

**Branches** — **CPN → DPN + SPN** · SPN in **lateral compartment** → supplies **peroneus longus + brevis** → dorsal foot sensation

## 3 Presentation

### Baxter's nerve (first branch of lateral plantar nerve)

**Who** — **running athlete** · normally arched or **cavus** foot · responsible for **\~⅕ of hindfoot pain**, widely missed

**Symptoms** — **chronic plantar medial heel pain** (often 9–12 months) · start-up pain like plantar fasciitis **plus neuritic burning pain unrelated to loading** · worse at end of day / prolonged activity

**Signs** — **tenderness at abductor hallucis origin** · **Tinel's just proximal to abductor hallucis → radiates to lateral foot / 5th MT base** · **no Tinel's over posterior tibial nerve** · chronic → **abductor digiti minimi atrophy**

**Innervates** — **abductor digiti quinti (minimi)** (OB) · also **FDB + quadratus plantae** (one OB)\
✗ Wrong options: abductor hallucis · FDL · FHL · plantar fascia

**Course** — **deep to abductor hallucis + FDB** · **superficial to quadratus plantae** · **medial to calcaneus** · **just anterior to medial calcaneal tuberosity** / just superior to plantar fascia insertion

**Compression point** — between **deep fascia of abductor hallucis** and **medial caudal margin of medial head of quadratus plantae** (Orthobullets writes "abductor hallucis longus")

**Mechanism** — stretching + tethering in abductor hallucis fascial leashes · hypertrophy of small foot muscles · hindfoot microtrauma in runners

![Plantar nerves — lateral plantar nerve and inferior calcaneal (Baxter) nerve to ADQM](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7044-trifurcation-of-ptn.jpg)

### Superficial peroneal nerve entrapment

**Mechanism** — **inversion injury** · **fascial defect** (± muscle herniation)

**Site** — where it **exits deep crural fascia of anterolateral leg**, **\~12 cm** (Orthobullets) · **10–12 cm** (OB) proximal to lateral malleolus · lateral → anterior compartment

**Symptoms** — **numbness + tingling over dorsum of foot** · ↑ with **passive plantarflexion + inversion** · ± eversion weakness

**Sridhara signs** — ↓ dorsal sensation **sparing 1st web space** · **soft-tissue bulge \~10 cm above lateral malleolus** · **Tinel's over bulge** · bulge ↑ with resisted dorsiflexion / WB · tenderness over bulge or terminal branches

**Variable course** — SPN in **lateral compartment 72%**, anterior 23% (Barrett) · **anterior in almost 50%** (Rosson) → **explore both compartments**

![Site of SPN entrapment at its fascial exit in the distal anterolateral leg](../../MCQ/attachments/q117-illa-spn-entrapment.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key diagnostic findings

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| **EMG** | Lateral plantar nerve entrapment positive in **88%** of neurogenic subcalcaneal heel pain |
| **MRI** | **Abductor digiti minimi atrophy** → Baxter's neuropathy |
| **Nerve conduction studies** | Help establish **obturator** entrapment |
| Electrical studies | Confirm **peroneal compression at fibular neck** |

## 5 Management

```
Baxter's nerve entrapment
├─ First → rest, ice, PT, orthotics, NSAIDs
└─ Persistent > 3 months → release abductor hallucis fascia
       (good–excellent 89%)
```

```
Superficial peroneal nerve entrapment
├─ Most → observation
└─ Refractory / elite athlete → fascial release + SPN neurolysis
       └─ fascial defect → release, never close (worsens)
```

### Operative treatment

| Condition | Operation | ✗ Traps |
|---|---|---|
| **Baxter's** (distal tarsal tunnel) | **Release of abductor hallucis fascia** → decompresses **lateral plantar nerve** branch | partial plantar fasciotomy / gastrocnemius recession (= recalcitrant **plantar fasciitis**) |
| **SPN** | **Fascial release + superficial peroneal neurolysis** (80% satisfied) | **repair herniation + close fascial defect** · 4-compartment fasciotomy · neurectomy · lumbar discectomy |
| **CPN ganglion** | Excision · **proximal tibiofibular fusion** prevents recurrence | — |
| **Medial plantar** (jogger's foot) | **Stop orthosis** | — |
| **Saphenous** | **Knee pads** first line | — |
| **Sural** | Surgical release | — |

**Baxter release complications** — persistent heel pain needing more surgery · neuroma · superficial infection · RSD

## 6 Complications

### Peroneal nerve palsy – order of recovery

**Short leg cast → foot drop** — compression of **peroneal nerve at fibular neck**

**Last muscle to recover** — **extensor hallucis longus** (innervated **most distally**)\
✗ Wrong options: EDL · peroneus longus · tibialis anterior · FDL (tibial nerve)

## X vs Y

| | **Baxter's nerve** | **Plantar fasciitis** | **Tarsal tunnel** | **Jogger's foot** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Nerve | **1st branch LPN** | — | Posterior tibial | **Medial plantar** |
| Site | AbH fascia / quadratus plantae | Calcaneal insertion | Flexor retinaculum, below medial malleolus | **Knot of Henry**, orthotic arch |
| Pain | **Medial plantar heel, burning**, ↑ tapping | **Plantar heel**, first steps, ↑ great-toe dorsiflexion | Medial + plantar foot | Medial arch |
| Tinel's | **At AbH origin → lateral foot** | — | **Over tibial nerve** | — |
| Patient | Runner, cavus | — | — | Runner, **hyperpronation, heel valgus** |
| Surgery | **AbH fascia release** | Partial fasciotomy / gastrocnemius recession | Tarsal tunnel release | Stop orthosis |

| | **SPN** | **DPN** | **Sural** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Site | Fascial exit **10–12 cm** above lateral malleolus | **Inferior extensor retinaculum** | Anywhere; Achilles MTJ; 12–15 mm distal to fibula tip |
| Sensory loss | **Dorsum, spares 1st web** | **1st web space** | **Lateral dorsum + heel** |
| Name | — | **Anterior tarsal tunnel** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Runner, heel pain, Tinel's at abductor hallucis origin radiating laterally?"** → **compression of the first branch of the lateral plantar nerve (Baxter's nerve)**

**"Nerve to abductor digiti quinti compressed under abductor hallucis fascia?"** → **first branch of the lateral plantar nerve**

**"Baxter's nerve — muscle affected?"** → **abductor digiti minimi**

**"Abductor hallucis fascia release relieved heel pain — which nerve?"** → **lateral plantar nerve**

**"Anatomy of the Baxter nerve?"** → **runs deep to the flexor digitorum brevis**\
✗ Wrong options: superficial to AbH · deep to quadratus plantae · branch of medial plantar

**"Medial ankle dissection — nerve most commonly entrapped?"** → **A = 1st branch of lateral plantar nerve**

**"Inversion injury, dorsal foot numbness ↑ PF + inversion — cause?"** → **compression of the superficial peroneal nerve by fascial band proximal to lateral malleolus**

**"Commonest SPN compression site?"** → **proximal to lateral malleolus, exiting fascia of anterolateral leg**

**"Marathoner, Tinel's over distal lateral fibula bulge — surgery?"** → **fascial release and superficial peroneal neurolysis**

**"Foot drop after short leg cast — last to recover?"** → **extensor hallucis longus**

**"Unconscious in lateral position, bruised lateral knee — nerve?"** → **CPN** (TO)

**"Commonest site of saphenous nerve entrapment?"** → **adductor canal** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Lisfranc Injury

*Foot & Ankle · 30 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — TMT fracture-dislocation · disruption between **medial cuneiform and base of 2nd metatarsal** · purely ligamentous or fracture-dislocation (treatment implications)

**Incidence** — **0.2% of all fractures** · **1 per 55,000 / yr** · **males > females** · **3rd decade**

**Missed** — **easily missed**, up to **20%** at initial encounter · missed → **progressive planovalgus**, chronic pain

**Lisfranc equivalents** — **contiguous proximal metatarsal fractures** or **tarsal fractures** · may involve multiple TMT joints

### Anatomy – Lisfranc & plantar ligaments

**Lisfranc joint complex** — **tarsometatarsal** + **intermetatarsal** + **intertarsal (intercuneiform)** articulations · 5 MTs with 3 cuneiforms + cuboid

| Column | Joints | Mobility |
|---|---|---|
| **Medial** | **1st TMT** | Little / none |
| **Middle** | **2nd + 3rd TMT** | Little / none |
| **Lateral** | **4th + 5th TMT** | **Most mobile** — accommodates uneven ground |

**✗ Mobility trap (TO)** — 1st–3rd cuneiform–MT joints have **greater** mobility than 4th–5th (false — they are **stable, rigid lever for push-off**)

**Bony stability** — **trapezoidal bases of 1st–3rd MTs** → **transverse "Roman arch"** · **2nd TMT = keystone**, 2nd MT recessed in mortise between **medial and lateral (recessed middle) cuneiforms** · **shallow 2nd TMT mortise = risk factor** (TO)

| Ligament | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Lisfranc ligament** | **Plantar interosseous** · **lateral surface of medial cuneiform → medial base of 2nd MT** (near plantar surface) · **largest + strongest** interosseous TMT ligament · **8–10 mm wide, 5–6 mm thick** · **tightens with pronation + abduction** of forefoot · stabilises 1st/2nd TMT + midfoot arch (✗ Wrong options: "dorsal interosseous" (TO trap)) |
| **Plantar TMT ligaments** | **Plantar ligament medial cuneiform → 2nd + 3rd MTs** + Lisfranc ligament both needed for **transverse instability** |
| **Dorsal TMT ligaments** | **Weaker than plantar** (TO ✓) → **displacement usually dorsal** |
| **Intermetatarsal ligaments** | **2nd–5th MT bases only** · **no IM ligament between 1st and 2nd MTs** (✗ Wrong options: "1st–2nd IM ligament is strongest" (TO trap)) |
| **Lisfranc ligament complex (TO)** | **TMT ligaments of 1st + 2nd rays + Lisfranc ligament** |
| **1st MT base ↔ medial cuneiform** | **Joint capsule** (not Lisfranc ligament) |

**Point on X-ray** — Lisfranc ligament runs **base of 2nd MT → medial cuneiform**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st MT → 2nd MT · 2nd MT → middle cuneiform · medial → middle cuneiform

**✗ Ligament distractors** — **spring** (calcaneus → navicular, supports talar head) · **plantar fascia** (plantar calcaneus → MT heads) · **AITFL** (tibia → fibula) · bifurcate, long plantar, Chopart, CFL

![Lisfranc ligament — plantar, medial cuneiform to 2nd MT base](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7030-illustration-anatomy-moved.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Indirect (commonest)** — **rotational force + axial load through hyper-plantarflexed forefoot** → hyperflexion / compression / **abduction** moment → MTs displaced **dorsal + lateral** · usually involves at least **2nd TMT**

**Settings** — MVA, fall from height, **athletes** (football player fallen on, stirrup in equestrian, soccer pivot, twist under motorcycle, dancer)

**Direct** — crush (workplace) · **hockey puck to medial foot ≠ Lisfranc** → occult trabecular fractures (below)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — severe midfoot pain · **inability to bear weight** · pain at push-off

**Plantar ecchymosis** — **medial plantar** bruising = **highly suggestive of Lisfranc** (TO) · also seen in **calcaneal fracture** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: mycetoma · hydatid cyst of talus · vertical talus

**Exam** — midfoot swelling, TMT tenderness · pain with **pronation + abduction** of forefoot · midfoot squeeze · **piano key test** (hypermobility)

**Instability test** — grasp MT heads, **dorsal force** while palpating TMTs → **dorsal subluxation = instability** · 1st + 2nd MTs displace medially + laterally → **global instability → surgery** · **plantar ligaments intact → no dorsal subluxation → nonop possible**

![Plantar ecchymosis — Lisfranc injury](../../MCQ/attachments/q211275-b-plantar-ecchymosis.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Sign | View |
|---|---|
| **Medial base 2nd MT ↔ medial middle cuneiform discontinuity** | **AP** — diagnostic |
| **Widening 1st–2nd ray interval** ± **fleck sign** (avulsion of Lisfranc ligament from 2nd MT base, in **1st IM space**) | **AP** — diagnostic |
| **Dorsal displacement of proximal base of 1st or 2nd MT** | **Lateral** |
| **Medial 4th MT base ≠ medial cuboid** | **Oblique** |
| **Disrupted medial column line** (tangent to medial navicular + medial cuneiform) | **Oblique** |

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP, lateral, oblique** | Initial study of choice |
| **Weight-bearing (bilateral, comparison)** | **Non-WB films normal + suspicion → next step** · reveals subtle diastasis · **contralateral** for low-energy injuries |
| **Physician-assisted stress exam / stress fluoroscopy** | Dynamic alternative · **EUA reserved for equivocal cases** |
| **CT** | Comminution, **pre-op planning**, subtle fractures, fleck sign |
| **MRI** | **Confirms purely ligamentous injury** · plantar ligament integrity · doesn't assess **dynamic instability** as well as WB films |

**Mulcahy alignment check** — AP: 2nd MT–middle cuneiform, 3rd MT–lateral cuneiform, 1st MT–medial cuneiform · oblique: middle cuneiform–2nd MT, lateral cuneiform–3rd MT · lateral: talus–2nd MT axis, medial cuneiform–5th MT, dorsal TMT surfaces

![Fleck sign in the 1st intermetatarsal space](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7030-ap-moved.jpg)

### Diagnosis – weight-bearing/stress radiographs, MRI & plantar ecchymosis

**Midfoot pain, plantar ecchymosis, non-WB X-ray normal** — **standing / weight-bearing AP radiographs** (✗ Wrong options: CT · MRI · bone scan (expensive, not warranted) · compartment pressures) · **"of both feet"** when CT shows no fracture (✗ Wrong options: MRI first · RTP stiff-soled shoe · fix · cast 6 wk before stability known)

**Football player, plantar flexed foot, nonstanding films normal** — **weight-bearing AP radiograph**\
✗ Wrong options: RTP · PT · orthosis · NWB cast

**WB radiograph most likely shows** — **dorsal displacement of 1st MT proximal base on lateral**\
✗ Wrong options: fleck lateral to 5th MT base (5th MT avulsion) · calcaneal sclerotic line (stress fracture) · proximal medial sesamoid (**turf toe**) · talar head directly over navicular (**medial subtalar dislocation**)

**MRI: dorsal capsule torn, plantar ligament intact, WB films normal** — **stable → protected WB in CAM boot**\
✗ Wrong options: bridge plate · strict NWB cast 6 wk · stress fluoro EUA · primary arthrodesis

**Hockey puck direct blow to medial foot, X-rays normal** — **MRI** → occult fractures **medial malleolus, medial talar neck, medial cuneiform, 1st MT** (trabecular stress fractures + marrow oedema; medial malleolus can displace)\
✗ Wrong options: bilateral WB X-rays (mechanism inconsistent with Lisfranc) · WBAT · cast without diagnosis · RTP

### Diagnostic criteria – instability

| Finding | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **> 2 mm** displacement / shift between 1st + 2nd MT on WB film | **Unstable → ORIF / anatomic reduction** |
| Diastasis 2nd MT base ↔ medial cuneiform | **Pathognomonic** of Lisfranc ligament rupture |
| **Lisfranc + plantar ligament (medial cuneiform → 2nd/3rd MT) torn** | **Transverse instability** |
| Plantar ligament intact on MRI | **Stable** → nonop |
| Medial gapping on stress view | Injury of that TMT even without frank displacement |
| **> 5 mm** diastasis, neglected pure ligamentous (TO) | **Medial column arthrodesis** |

### Hardcastle & Myerson classification

| Type | Pattern | Diagram |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Complete homolateral** dislocation (all 5 TMTs, same direction) | ![Type A](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7030-type-a-classification.jpg) |
| **B1** | **Partial**, **medial column** dislocation | ![Type B](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7030-type-b-classification.jpg) |
| **B2** | **Partial**, **lateral** dislocation | — |
| **C1** | **Partial divergent** | ![Type C](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7030-type-c-classification.jpg) |
| **C2** | **Complete divergent** | — |

**Primary arthrodesis may give better function + reduction** — **complete** injuries **Type A or C2**

**DDx** — MT base fracture · MT stress fracture · tarsal fracture

## 5 Management

```
Suspected Lisfranc
├─ Non-WB X-ray normal → bilateral WB / stress X-ray (MRI if equivocal)
│      └─ stable, plantar ligament intact → CAM boot / cast
└─ Unstable (> 2 mm) or displaced → operative
       ├─ massive swelling → temporary pinning, definitive at 2–3 wk
       └─ soft tissues OK → ORIF (< 24 h) or primary arthrodesis
```

```
Operative choice
├─ Bony fracture-dislocation, acute → ORIF rigid 1–3 TMT
├─ Purely ligamentous → primary arthrodesis medial ± middle column
├─ Delayed / chronic / arch collapse → arthrodesis
└─ Lateral column (4–5) displaced → temporary K-wires
       (never fuse 4th–5th TMT)
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative | ORIF | Primary arthrodesis (1st–3rd TMT) |
|---|---|---|
| **Non-displaced, stable on WB** · partial Lisfranc rupture, no displacement | **Any instability (> 2 mm shift)** | **Purely ligamentous** (controversial) |
| **Nonambulatory**, serious vascular disease, **severe neuropathy** (insensate diabetic) | **Bony fracture-dislocation**, acute | **Delayed treatment** (e.g. 8 wk, 6 mo) |
| Medically unfit | Young active (soldier) | **Chronic deformity**, missed injury |
| **Cast 8 wk** (Orthobullets) · **NWB cast 6 wk** then gradual return (OB) | — | **Complete A / C2** · severe intra-articular comminution · failed fixation |

**Nonop of displaced / unstable** — significantly **worse** functional + radiographic outcomes · persistent midfoot instability

### ORIF indications & fixation technique (medial vs lateral columns)

**Goal** — **anatomic reduction + stable fixation** = the outcome predictor · **open > closed reduction** (see reduction, **loose osteochondral fragments, soft-tissue interposition** — ligament / bony avulsion may be **incarcerated**)

**Standard construct** — **rigid fixation 1st–3rd TMT (screws or spanning plates)** + **K-wires for 4th + 5th TMT** (flexible, removed later) · stiff fixation of all 5 TMTs → poor function

**Plate vs screw vs wire** — **cortical screws > K-wires** (more rigid) · **pin fixation inferior** (ligament needs prolonged fixation) · plates span TMTs when **MT base comminuted** · more screws = more rigid

**Fix only what's injured** — 1st + 2nd TMT ligamentous injury → **ORIF of 1st and 2nd TMT** (✗ Wrong options: CR + K-wires · 1–3 + K-wire 4–5 (only when all five injured, e.g. **homolateral**)) · no lateral instability → **ORIF medial two columns alone** (✗ Wrong options: add lateral column stabilisation)

**Closed manipulation + percutaneous screw** — acceptable **only if anatomic alignment achieved closed**

**Bridge vs transarticular** — acute displaced → open reduction with **spanning-bridge plate or transarticular screws** · acute reducible → CRPP option

**Timing** — **within 24 h** or wait for swelling (up to **2–3 wk**) · massive swelling → **temporary percutaneous pinning** of medial + lateral columns → delayed ORIF / arthrodesis (↓ wound infection, low-level evidence)

**Approach** — single or dual **longitudinal** incisions · **1st–2nd web space** · 1st TMT exposed **between EHL and EHB** · **protect deep NV bundle (DPN + dorsalis pedis)**

**Sequence** — **reduce intercuneiform instability first** → fix 1st–3rd TMT transarticular screws · check intercuneiform + naviculocuneiform joints

**Post-op** — early midfoot ROM, protected WB · **K-wires out 6–8 wk** · **screws out 3–6 mo** · **full WB 8–10 wk** (Orthobullets) · **protected WB 3–5 mo** + passive midfoot ROM (OB) — read the options · asymptomatic screws across only 1st–3rd TMT may stay · **no vigorous sport 9–12 mo**

![Temporary lateral column pinning](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7030-lateral-column-pinning.jpg)

### ORIF vs primary arthrodesis for ligamentous injuries

**Purely ligamentous — preferred treatment (TO)** — **medial tarsometatarsal joint arthrodesis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: NWB cast · anatomical screw fixation · WBAT cast

**Why fuse ligamentous injuries** — **ligament healing less reliable than bone** → deformity recurs after ORIF · ligamentous injuries do **worse** than bony · nonop → uniformly poor (redislocation)

**Ly & Coetzee RCT (purely ligamentous)** — primary arthrodesis 1st–3rd rays → **higher AOFAS at 2 yr** · **25% of ORIF** → early arthritis → revised to fusion

**Best result, acute purely ligamentous** — **open reduction + primary arthrodesis with screw fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: CRPP · open + pins · metallic or bioabsorbable screws alone

**Alternative to ORIF (ligamentous)** — **open reduction + arthrodesis of medial and middle columns, reduction + stabilisation of lateral column**\
✗ Wrong options: fuse all three columns · CRPP all · closed reduction + cast · boot

| Outcome | **ORIF** | **Primary arthrodesis (PA)** |
|---|---|---|
| **Hardware removal** | **Higher** (\~**75%**, often planned) | **Lower** (\~**20%**) ✓ |
| **Overall reoperation** | **Higher** (driven by HW removal) | **Lower** ✓ |
| Reoperation **excluding HW removal** | Equal (Buda: 163 ORIF vs 54 PA) | Equal |
| **Functional outcome** | **Equivalent** overall (level 1, meta-analyses, delayed cases) | **Better for purely ligamentous** (Ly & Coetzee) · **improved return to activity** |
| **Patient-reported outcomes** | **No difference** (OB) | **Higher with PA** (prospective, OB) — read the options |
| **Cost** | **Higher overall** (OB) · **ORIF less expensive** (another OB) — read the options | — |
| Infection, malreduction, hardware failure, alignment, satisfaction, NWB time | **Equal** | **Equal** |
| PTOA | **Higher** | Lower |
| Adjacent joint stress | — | **Greater concern** |

**✗ PA traps** — "higher implant removal with PA" · "lower PTOA with ORIF" · "higher malreduction with ORIF" · "worse function with PA" · "higher wound infection with PA" · "decreased reduction quality with PA" · "**medial + lateral column arthrodesis better**"\
✗ Wrong options: — inferior

**Lateral column** — **never fuse 4th + 5th TMT** → **inferior functional outcomes** (Mulier: total arthrodesis worse than medial arthrodesis + lateral ORIF) · use **temporary K-wires** · **medial column fusion superior to combined medial + lateral**

**All 5 TMT fused (B2 injury)** — **inferior functional outcomes** vs medial ray arthrodesis + temporary lateral K-wires

**Homolateral fracture-dislocation (medial + lateral columns)** — **arthrodesis of medial column + K-wire fixation of lateral column**\
✗ Wrong options: cast · percutaneous screw / ORIF medial column alone · fuse medial + lateral

**MVC, medial pain, plantar ecchymosis** — **open reduction + arthrodesis of medial two TMT joints**\
✗ Wrong options: ex-fix + staged screws · percutaneous wires 1–3 · Morel-Lavallée debridement · screws + ligament reconstruction

**Equestrian, stirrup, 1 wk** — **arthrodesis of medial TMT joints** · operative reduction mandatory whether fusion or ORIF\
✗ Wrong options: aircast NWB · walking cast · Broström · delayed osteotomy

**Crush at work** — **ligamentous patterns better with primary arthrodesis than ORIF**\
✗ Wrong options: workplace = no difference · fusion only as salvage (post-ORIF salvage fusion does worse than primary) · rigid 4th–5th TMT fixation · suture fixation alone for complete dislocations

**Suture-button** — possible role in **isolated Lisfranc ligament** injury · complete dislocations need rigid fixation

**Fusion technique** — denude TMT cartilage → **cortical screws or square plate** · cast / splint 6 wk → WB in boot 6–12 wk → **full WB in shoes by 12 wk**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Post-traumatic midfoot arthritis** | **Commonest complication** · ↑ with delay + ORIF · **up to 80% non-anatomic ORIF** · **54% symptomatic OA at \~10 yr after ORIF** (Orthobullets) · **> 25% even with anatomic reduction** (OB) → **midfoot arthrodesis** |
| **Malunion** | Non-anatomic ORIF → **shoe modification** (nonsurgical) or **corrective arthrodesis** |
| **Nonunion** | **Smoking** → revision arthrodesis + graft (unless elderly low-demand) |
| **Hardware removal** | **\~75% ORIF** · **\~20% arthrodesis** |
| **Deep infection** | **3–4%** · swelling at definitive surgery → I&D ± HW removal |
| **Planovalgus deformity** | **Nonop** or non-anatomic ORIF · missed injury → arch collapse + **forefoot abduction** |
| Dorsal foot numbness | **Dorsal osteophytes impinging SPN** (tight shoes) |

**Prognosis** — military: **\~70%** resume occupational running at \~3 yr after ORIF or PA · **missed diagnosis = poor prognosis**

### Chronic/missed injuries – midfoot arthritis & arthrodesis

**Most common complication (10 yr after boot NWB 6 wk)** — **midfoot arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · planovalgus · 1st TMT hypermobility ("not associated") · dorsal numbness

**Untreated trauma → chronic pain + progressive deformity** — **Lisfranc injury → pes planovalgus**\
✗ Wrong options: navicular (no planovalgus) · **calcaneus / talar neck → varus** · ATFL (ankle instability)

**Chronic PTOA nonop** — **shoe inserts / modifications + NSAIDs** · fail → **arthrodesis of affected joints**

**Malunion, pain at push-off / stairs** — **shoe modification with cushioned heel + rocker sole** (unloads TMT in terminal stance)\
✗ Wrong options: neutral-posted rigid orthosis · UCBL · AFO locked 90° · Arizona

**2 yr after cast, push-off pain, arch loss** — **TMT joint arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: Evans lateral column lengthening · TN fusion · Dwyer · triple

**6 mo old (ICU head injury), 1st–3rd TMT** — **arthrodesis of 1st through 3rd TMT**\
✗ Wrong options: orthotics · CR + screws · ORIF · add triple

**> 6 mo, instability + arch collapse** — **open reduction + primary arthrodesis** ± **tricortical ipsilateral iliac crest autograft** for bone loss\
✗ Wrong options: RTW · PT / orthotics (only if unfit) · cast WBAT (insensate diabetic / unfit) · ORIF (late presenters fuse)

**Factors favouring primary arthrodesis** — **primarily ligamentous injury + delay in treatment**\
✗ Wrong options: significant bony injury → **ORIF**

**Midfoot arthrodesis best indicated** — **chronic ligamentous Lisfranc injury** (ligament won't heal functionally)\
✗ Wrong options: acute **nutcracker cuboid** (restore lateral column length) · diabetic **equinus** (stretching / TAL / gastroc recession) · **TN nonunion** (revise that joint) · **ulcers under 2nd–3rd MT heads** (relative plantarflexion / 1st ray dorsiflexion)

**Chronic Lisfranc + progressive flatfoot (alcoholic, supple hindfoot)** — **midfoot arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: PT / AFO (PTTI first line) · MCO + transfers + LCL (flexible flatfoot) · triple (rigid arthritic hindfoot)

**Midfoot arthrodesis technique** — denude 1st–3rd TMT + bone graft · **reduce with windlass mechanism** · cast 6 wk → boot 6–12 wk → WBAT at 12 wk if healed

## X vs Y

| | **ORIF** | **Primary arthrodesis** |
|---|---|---|
| Best for | **Bony fracture-dislocation**, acute | **Purely ligamentous**, delayed, chronic, A / C2 |
| Hardware removal | **\~75%** | **\~20%** |
| Function | Equivalent overall | Better in ligamentous (Ly & Coetzee) |

| | **Medial + middle columns (1st–3rd TMT)** | **Lateral column (4th–5th TMT)** |
|---|---|---|
| Motion | Rigid lever for push-off | **Mobile** — uneven ground |
| Fixation | **Rigid screws / plate** or fuse | **Temporary K-wires** |
| Fusion | Acceptable | **Avoid — inferior function** |

| | **Lisfranc planovalgus** | **PTTI planovalgus** |
|---|---|---|
| History | Trauma, plantar ecchymosis | Insidious |
| Treatment | **Midfoot (TMT) arthrodesis** | PT / AFO → MCO + transfer + LCL → triple |

## How it's asked

**"True about Lisfranc joint anatomy?"** → **dorsal TMT ligaments are weaker than plantar TMT ligaments** (TO)

**"Ligament from medial cuneiform to base of 2nd MT?"** → **Lisfranc ligament**

**"Lisfranc's ligament connects?"** → **base of the second metatarsal to the medial cuneiform**

**"Origin / insertion of the injured ligament on labelled X-ray?"** → **point D (2nd MT base) to point A (medial cuneiform)**

**"Ligaments needed for transverse instability?"** → **interosseous first cuneiform–second metatarsal + plantar ligament from first cuneiform to second and third metatarsals**

**"Plantar ecchymosis after fall on stairs — suspect?"** → **ligament connecting the second metatarsal base to the medial cuneiform**

**"Clinical photo of plantar bruising — diagnosis?"** → **Lisfranc fracture dislocation** (TO)

**"Midfoot pain, nonstanding films normal — next?"** → **weight-bearing AP radiograph**

**"Plantar ecchymosis, X-ray negative — next?"** → **standing radiographs**

**"Negative CT, high suspicion — next?"** → **weightbearing radiographs of both feet**

**"Most likely WB finding?"** → **dorsal displacement of the 1st metatarsal proximal base on lateral radiograph**

**"MRI dorsal capsule torn, plantar ligament intact?"** → **protected weight-bearing in a CAM boot**

**"Hockey puck to medial foot, normal X-rays?"** → **MRI of the foot**

**"Preferred treatment of purely ligamentous Lisfranc?"** → **medial tarsometatarsal joint arthrodesis** (TO)

**"Neglected ligamentous Lisfranc in runner, > 5 mm diastasis?"** → **Lisfranc injury and arthrodesis of medial column** (TO)

**"Equestrian, stirrup injury 1 wk ago?"** → **arthrodesis of the medial tarsometatarsal joints**

**"Alternative to ORIF for ligamentous pattern?"** → **arthrodesis of medial and middle columns, reduction + stabilisation of lateral column**

**"MVC, medial pain with abduction, plantar bruising?"** → **open reduction and arthrodesis of the medial two TMT joints**

**"Primary arthrodesis vs ORIF of 1st–3rd TMT — true?"** → **improved return to activity with primary arthrodesis**

**"Crush injury — true statement?"** → **primarily ligamentous patterns do better with primary arthrodesis**

**"Best outcome for acute purely ligamentous dislocation?"** → **open reduction and primary arthrodesis with screw fixation**

**"ORIF vs PA for ligamentous injury — ORIF has?"** → **higher overall cost of treatment**

**"Arthrodesis vs ORIF (bony) — true?"** → **decreased rate of hardware removal at 1 year**

**"ORIF vs PA outcomes — true?"** → **higher hardware removal rates with ORIF**

**"Prognosis with primary arthrodesis?"** → **decreased overall reoperation rate**

**"Delayed 8 wk, medial + middle columns — ORIF vs fusion?"** → **functional outcomes equivalent**

**"ORIF with rigid implants vs fusion — higher rate of?"** → **hardware removal**

**"All five TMTs fused vs medial fusion + lateral K-wires?"** → **inferior functional outcomes**

**"Twisting injury, displaced Lisfranc (no fusion option)?"** → **open reduction and internal fixation**

**"Standard fixation of TMT fracture-dislocation?"** → **plating of joints 1, 2 and 3; K-wire fixation of joints 4 and 5**

**"Soccer Lisfranc — best technique?"** → **open reduction and internal fixation of medial column** (cortical screws)

**"Ligamentous 1st + 2nd TMT gapping on stress view?"** → **open reduction and rigid internal fixation of the first and second TMT joints**

**"WB X-ray > 2 mm, no lateral instability?"** → **ORIF of the injury**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral column stabilisation

**"Rugby player, 2nd MT–medial cuneiform diastasis?"** → **open reduction and internal fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: pins

**"Dancer with Lisfranc dislocation?"** → **open reduction and internal fixation**

**"Homolateral dislocation after temporising reduction?"** → **arthrodesis of the medial column and K-wire fixation of the lateral column**

**"Soldier, plantar ecchymosis, displaced Lisfranc?"** → **Lisfranc open reduction and internal fixation**

**"Chronic injury, push-off pain, arch loss?"** → **tarsometatarsal joint arthrodesis**

**"6-month-old 1st–3rd TMT dislocation?"** → **arthrodesis of 1st through 3rd TMT joints**

**"6 months, instability + arch collapse?"** → **open reduction and primary arthrodesis**

**"Malunion, pain at push-off — nonsurgical?"** → **shoe modification with a cushioned heel and rocker sole**

**"Factors favouring primary midfoot arthrodesis?"** → **primarily ligamentous injury + delay in treatment (1 and 3)**

**"Midfoot arthrodesis best indicated for?"** → **55-year-old with chronic ligamentous Lisfranc injury**

**"Untreated injury → progressive planovalgus?"** → **Lisfranc injury**

**"Chronic Lisfranc with progressive flatfoot?"** → **midfoot arthrodesis**

**"Most common complication of Lisfranc injury?"** → **midfoot arthritis**

# Lower Extremity Os

*Foot & Ankle · 31 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Definitions & epidemiology

**Accessory ossicle** — **secondary ossification centre that stays separate** from parent bone · may mimic fracture

**Sesamoid** — **bone incorporated into a tendon**, moves with it

**Number** — up to **40 accessory ossicles** + multiple sesamoids in foot and ankle

**Commonest ossicles** — **os trigonum**, **accessory navicular (os tibiale externum)**, os intermetatarseum · os trigonum = **second commonest accessory bone** (OB)

**Commonest sesamoids** — **os peroneum** (in **peroneus longus**) · **hallux sesamoids** (in **FHB**, under 1st MT head)

### Os trigonum – anatomy

**Origin** — separated **posterolateral (lateral) tubercle of talus** · ossifies **8–13 yr**, normally **fuses within 1 yr** · unfused → **synchondrosis** with talus

**Stieda process** — elongated lateral tubercle (unfused) that may fracture with repetitive trauma

**Relations** — os lies **lateral to FHL, tibial nerve, PTT, posterior tibial artery** · FHL runs in fibro-osseous tunnel between **posterolateral (os) + posteromedial talar tubercles**

![FHL runs just medial to the os trigonum (OT)](../../MCQ/attachments/q210173-illa-illustration-of-fhl-near-ot.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Os trigonum syndrome** — **repetitive forced plantarflexion** (microtrauma → stress fracture) or **acute forced plantarflexion** (acute fracture) · traumatic disruption of synchondrosis → pain

**Impingement** — os squeezed between **posterior tibial malleolus + calcaneal tuberosity / superior calcaneus**

**At risk** — **ballet dancers (en pointe)**, basketball, soccer, football, kicking, downhill running, push-off

**Os peroneum injury** — direct trauma · **sudden inversion + supination** → associated **PL rupture**

## 3 Presentation

### Os trigonum & posterior impingement – diagnosis

**Symptom** — chronic / recurrent **posterolateral ankle pain** with **forced plantarflexion**, **en pointe**, push-off, jumping

**Exam** — pain with **passive maximal plantarflexion** · no Achilles insertion tenderness · ± FHL swelling / tenderness if FHL tenosynovitis

| Feature | **Os trigonum syndrome** | **FHL tendinitis / stenosing tenosynovitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Pain site | **Posterolateral** | **Posteromedial** (behind medial malleolus) |
| Provocation | **Forced plantarflexion** | Hallux motion, push-off, deep squat |
| Sign | Plantarflexion pain | **Triggering**, crepitus, **great toe locking** |
| X-ray | Tibio-calcaneal impingement on plantarflexion lateral | May show incidental os |

**Inflamed structure in os trigonum + posteromedial tenderness** — **FHL** (labelled C on axial MRI)

**✗ Posterolateral look-alikes** — peroneal pathology (instability, pain with passive DF + eversion) · subtalar arthritis / loose body (pain with inversion / eversion) · tarsal tunnel (medial heel, plantar paraesthesia) · PTT tendinitis (posteromedial; **does not abut os trigonum**)

### FHL stenosing tenosynovitis – examination

**Functional hallux rigidus** — **low-lying distal FHL muscle belly** jams in retrotalar fibro-osseous tunnel ("**cork in a bottle**")

**Test** — **maximal knee extension + ankle dorsiflexion → passive hallux dorsiflexion limited + painful**\
✗ Wrong options: forced plantarflexion (os trigonum) · posterolateral tenderness behind peroneals (os trigonum) · DF + eversion (anterior impingement) · calf squeeze (Achilles)

**FHL origin** — inferior fibula + interosseous membrane

**Hamilton** — good results after **FHL release** · **presence of os trigonum on X-ray does not mandate excision**

### Os subfibulare & os peroneum – presentation

**Os subfibulare** — often asymptomatic · focal tenderness + swelling · **chronic ankle instability, recurrent sprains** · laxity on anterior drawer + inversion / eversion stress

**Os peroneum** — painful os peroneum syndrome · fracture · diastasis · PL rupture

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Accessory ossicles and sesamoids

| Accessory bone | Prevalence | Clinical significance | Differential |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Os trigonum** ![Os trigonum on lateral X-ray](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7049-lateral-xr-os-trigonum.jpg) | **10–25%** | **Posterior ankle impingement, FHL entrapment** | **Shepherd's fracture** (posterolateral process) |
| **Type II accessory navicular** | **2–12%** | **PTT dysfunction** | Navicular tuberosity avulsion, type I accessory navicular |
| **Os subfibulare** | **2%** (table) · **1–2%** (text) | Painful os subfibulare, instability | **Lateral malleolus (ATFL) avulsion fracture** |
| **Os peroneum** | **9–20%** · **bilateral 60%** · **bipartite 30%** | Painful os peroneum, fracture, diastasis, **PL rupture** | Painful os vesalianum, bipartite os peroneum |
| **Os vesalianum** | **2%** | Painful os vesalianum | **Avulsion fracture of 5th MT base** |
| **Hallux sesamoids** | **\~100%** | Fracture, stress fracture | **Bipartite tibial sesamoid** |

### Key radiology findings

| Ossicle | Study | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| **Os trigonum** | **Lateral X-ray in plantarflexion** | Round / oval / triangular ossicle **impinged between posterior tibia + calcaneal tuberosity** |
| Os trigonum | **Axial CT** | Ossicle **off posterior talus** (✗ Wrong options: posterior malleolus (tibia) · accessory navicular (medial + distal) · coalition (distal) · anterior loose body) |
| Os trigonum | **MRI** | **Fluid around os + marrow oedema in os**, **↑ T2 signal**, FHL oedema / inflammation · **no talar marrow oedema** (vs acute posterior process fracture) |
| **Os subfibulare** | **Weight-bearing AP, lateral, mortise + varus stress view** | Ossicle · **talar tilt** on varus stress → instability · **↑ separation of os from fibular tip** |
| **Os peroneum** | X-ray + MRI | Normal os · acute fracture · **proximal migration = PL rupture** |

### Os subfibulare vs type VII transepiphyseal fracture (child)

| | **Os subfibulare** | **Type VII transepiphyseal fracture** |
|---|---|---|
