| X-ray | **Smooth-edged, short ossicle** at **inferior pole** of epiphysis | **Long, irregular line in middle third** of distal fibular epiphysis |
| Nature | Anatomic variant **or ossified ATFL avulsion** | **Sleeve avulsion** through secondary ossification centre into joint (ATFL, CFL, PTFL stronger than physis) |
| Initial treatment | **Protected weight-bearing (CAM boot)** | **Protected weight-bearing (CAM boot)** |

**Differentiates them** — **radiographs**, not clinical picture

**Paediatric ankle injuries** — **\~17% of all physeal injuries**

![Os subfibulare in a child after inversion injury](../../MCQ/attachments/q218670-a-screenshot-2023-09-16-at-2-41-19-pm.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic os trigonum
├─ First → NSAIDs, rest, immobilisation (CAM), restricted WB, PT
└─ Failed ≥ 3 mo → excision (open lateral or posterior arthroscopy)
       └─ FHL involved → ± FHL tenolysis, debridement or repair
```

### Os trigonum excision (arthroscopic vs open, sural nerve)

**Nonop** — NSAIDs, rest, immobilisation, restricted weight-bearing, activity modification, PT · PT = short-term relief only · **steroid injection not recommended** here

**Surgery** — after **≥ 3 months failed nonop** → **excision**, open **or** arthroscopic · **similar return-to-play** both (Orthobullets) · **open excision = high RTP, low complications** (OB) · **arthroscopic = 80% good / excellent at 2–5 yr** (Van Dijk) · **RTP 2–3 months**

**✗ Os trigonum traps** — **fixation / ORIF** (not indicated) · FHL debridement alone (doesn't remove culprit) · PTT debridement · peroneal debridement + SPR repair (no snapping / instability) · retrial of failed rest · Haglund excision · os naviculare excision · biopsy

**Posterolateral approach** — **sural nerve at greatest risk**\
✗ Wrong options: SPN (more anterior)

**Ballet dancer, failed 1 mo rest + NSAIDs + PT** — **arthroscopic os trigonum excision**

**Basketball player, chronic posterior pain** — **open excision** = highest success\
✗ Wrong options: PT · steroid · arthroscopic fixation · ORIF

**Soccer player, posterolateral pain with plantarflexion, ↑ T2 around os** — **arthroscopic excision**\
✗ Wrong options: PB tubularisation · FHL debridement · EMG · subtalar loose body removal

### Os subfibulare & os peroneum treatment

**Os subfibulare / type VII** — **protected weight-bearing first** · **failed nonop → excision** ± ligament re-attachment\
✗ Wrong options: initial MRI (only for chronic pain / instability) · acute surgery · mandatory ATFL re-attachment

**Os subfibulare excision for functional instability without laxity (children)** — better AOFAS than rehab alone

| Os peroneum situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Painful os peroneum syndrome, minimally displaced fracture | **NSAIDs, rest, immobilisation, restricted WB** |
| Refractory syndrome (minimal tendon involvement), **displaced fracture** | **Excision** |
| **With PL tendon rupture** | **Excision + PL repair or tenodesis to peroneus brevis** |

## X vs Y

| | **Os trigonum** | **Shepherd's fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Edges | Smooth, corticated, synchondrosis | Acute fracture of posterolateral process |
| MRI | Fluid + oedema around os, **no talar body oedema** | **Talar marrow oedema** |

| | **Os vesalianum** | **5th MT base avulsion** |
|---|---|---|
| Edges | Smooth ossicle | Acute fracture line |

## How it's asked

**"Ballet dancer, posterior pain en pointe, failed 1 mo nonop?"** → **arthroscopic os trigonum excision**

**"Basketball player, painful max plantarflexion — highest success?"** → **open excision**

**"CT arrow posterior to talus?"** → **os trigonum fragment**

**"Posterolateral approach for os trigonum — diagnosis + nerve at risk?"** → **symptomatic os trigonum, sural nerve**

**"Dancer with os trigonum, posteromedial tenderness — inflamed structure?"** → **C — FHL**

**"Posterolateral pain with plantarflexion, no pain DF + eversion, ↑ T2?"** → **arthroscopic excision of os trigonum**

**"Elite dancer — high RTP, low complications?"** → **open surgical excision of the os trigonum**

**"Child, os subfibulare vs type VII fracture — true?"** → **both treated initially with protected weightbearing**

**"Functional hallux rigidus from low FHL belly — exam?"** → **maximal knee extension + ankle dorsiflexion, assess passive hallux dorsiflexion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Metatarsal Fractures

*Foot & Ankle · 32 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Adults** — **5th MT** most commonly fractured

**3rd MT** — rarely isolated · **68%** with 2nd or 4th MT fracture

**Peak age** — 2nd–5th decade

### Paediatric metatarsal fractures – most common location

**Young child** — **1st MT (proximal)** most common · **< 4 yr** (Orthobullets) · **< 5 yr** (OB) — read the options

**Older child (> 5 yr)** — **5th MT**

**Course** — usually minimally displaced, rarely operative

**Ossification (age clue)** — lateral cuneiform **1 yr** · middle **3 yr** · medial **4 yr**

### Anatomy

**1st MT** — **widest + shortest** · plantar crista articulates with sesamoids · **bears 30–50% of weight** in gait · **50%** (Orthobullets anatomy) · TO: standing = 1st MT, walking = **2nd MT** — read the options · **no intermetatarsal ligament** support

**2nd MT** — **longest** · **commonest stress fracture site**

**Distal intermetatarsal ligaments (2nd–5th)** — maintain length + alignment in **isolated** fractures · implicated in Morton's neuroma · **multiple fractures lose this stability → ↑ displacement**

**Muscle balance** — extrinsic (EDL, FDL) vs intrinsic (interossei, lumbricals)

**Blood supply** — dorsal + plantar metatarsal arteries

## 2 Mechanism

**Indirect (commonest)** — forefoot fixed, hindfoot / leg rotates

**Direct crush** — significant soft-tissue injury

**Multiple proximal MT fractures** — **Lisfranc-equivalent** injury

**Stress** — repetitive microstress; MTs loaded **perpendicular to long axis** · relatively fixed proximal 2nd + 3rd MTs → diaphyseal stress fractures

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain, inability to bear weight · **antecedent pain** suggests stress fracture

**Exam** — foot alignment (neutral / **cavovarus** / planovalgus) · tenderness · soft tissues in crush · **overlap / malrotation** of toes · Semmes–Weinstein monofilament if neuropathy

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **AP, lateral, oblique foot** | **Required** · ± contralateral, stress / WB views |
| **Repeat X-ray at 2 wk** | X-rays **lag symptoms by weeks** → callus / periosteal reaction appears |
| **CT** | Not routine · periarticular injury, **rule out Lisfranc** · better shows bone when fracture visible on X-ray |
| **MRI** | **Occult / stress fracture** · fluid sequences show **endosteal marrow + periosteal oedema** (earliest) · useful screening tool |
| **Bone scan (Tc-99)** | Occult stress fracture when X-rays unclear · **most useful** (OB1095 reference) · finds **noncontiguous simultaneous fractures** (e.g. 2nd MT + tibia) |

![March fracture: periosteal reaction, no fracture line yet](../../MCQ/attachments/q1095-illa-fx-mt-ans.jpg)

### Classification

| Descriptor | Record |
|---|---|
| Location | Base / shaft / neck / head |
| Pattern | Transverse, spiral, comminuted |
| Displacement | mm translation |
| Angulation | Sagittal plane degrees |
| Articular involvement | Yes / no |

## 5 Management

```
Acute metatarsal fracture
├─ 1st MT → any displacement → ORIF / pinning
├─ Central (2–4) → isolated, < 10° sagittal, < 4 mm → stiff shoe / boot, WBAT
│      └─ > 10° sagittal, > 4 mm, multiple → fixation
├─ 5th MT diaphyseal spiral (dancer's) → hard-soled shoe, WB
└─ Open fracture → fixation
```

### Operative criteria

| Nonoperative (stiff-soled shoe / walking boot, WBAT) | Operative (percutaneous or open) |
|---|---|
| **1st MT non-displaced** | **1st MT any displacement** (✗ Wrong options: 3 mm displaced 1st MT nonop) |
| **Central MT isolated, non / minimally displaced** | **Central MT sagittal deformity > 10°** (✗ Wrong options: 12°) |
| — | **> 4 mm translation** (✗ Wrong options: 6 mm) |
| — | **Multiple MT fractures** |
| **5th MT spiral diaphyseal (dancer's fracture)** | **Open fractures** |
| **Stress fractures** | Failed nonop stress fracture / nonunion |

### Shaft fractures – nonoperative indications

**5th MT spiral diaphyseal (dancer's) fracture** — **high union, low morbidity** → **hard-soled shoe + WB**

**Fixation goals** — restore alignment for normal force transmission across MT heads · **maintain length** → avoid **transfer metatarsalgia**

**Techniques** — antegrade / retrograde pinning · lag screws or mini-fragment plates for length-unstable patterns

**Prognosis** — majority of isolated MT fractures heal nonop

### Stress fractures – second MT, dancers, management & metabolic work-up

**March fracture** — **2nd, 3rd, 4th MTs**, **2nd commonest** · diaphyseal, callus + good alignment · runners, **military recruits**

**Ballet dancers** — **base of 2nd MT** · **amenorrhoea / delayed menarche** · pain with push-off + en pointe\
✗ Wrong options: Lisfranc injury · cuneiform · 1st MT base · plantar fascia strain

**Commonest stress site** — **distal 2nd MT shaft** (length + immobility) · "2nd + 3rd most frequent", also 5th (OB)

**Risk factors** — **long 2nd MT**, **hypermobile 1st ray**, **female**, recent ↑ training, **cavovarus** (recurrent), metabolic bone disease

**Female athlete triad** — repeated stress + **low BMD** + **dietary restraint** → ↑ fracture risk

**Metabolic work-up** — **serum cortisol, TSH, vitamin D, calcium** · menstrual history · **DEXA** when needed · (hypothyroidism, amenorrhoea) · older patient (55 yr) → **fragility fracture**

**Treatment** — **protected WB / short walking cast / fracture boot WBAT / hard-soled shoe** + activity modification · rest **4–6 wk** (OB1095) · return over **6–8 wk** (OB218223) — read the options · base fractures sometimes limited WB

**✗ Stress-fracture traps** — ORIF, K-wires, IM screw, calcium phosphate / BMA (only after failed nonop) · **biopsy / oncology referral** (no red flags) · continue running with orthotic · observation alone · NWB cast · **bone stimulator** (no evidence) · NSAIDs + immediate RTP · bone density in a teenager (normal, no help) · MRI / bone scan once X-ray diagnostic

**MRI already shows oedema, X-ray normal** — **fracture boot WBAT** (limit miles + repeat X-ray at 2 wk only if no MRI)

**Nonunion (dancer, 2nd MT base)** — fixation

![Second metatarsal base stress fracture in a ballet dancer](../../MCQ/attachments/q1112-a-stress-fracture-base-of-second.jpg)

**Differential** — metatarsalgia, MTP synovitis, **Freiberg's infarction**, Morton's neuroma

## 6 Complications

**Malunion** — **transfer metatarsalgia** or plantar keratosis → **corrective osteotomy**

**Multiple fractures** — loss of intermetatarsal ligament stability → displacement

![Multiple metatarsal shaft fractures – loss of intermetatarsal ligament support](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7032-metatarsal-fracture-222x300.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **1st MT** | **Central MTs (2–4)** | **5th MT diaphysis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ligament support | **None** | Distal intermetatarsal ligaments | — |
| Load | **30–50%** of weight | — | — |
| Fix if | **Any displacement** | **> 10° sagittal, > 4 mm, multiple** | Rarely (spiral → nonop) |

| | **Bone scan** | **MRI** |
|---|---|---|
| Strength | Occult fracture · **noncontiguous fractures** | Earliest oedema · occult fracture · screening |
| When not needed | X-ray already diagnostic | X-ray already diagnostic |

## How it's asked

**"Runner, 3rd MT callus — next step?"** → **protected weightbearing with crutches, slow return to running**

**"Ballet dancer, delayed menarche, midfoot pain?"** → **second metatarsal base stress fracture**

**"55-yo ballet, 2nd MT stress fracture on X-ray / scan / MRI?"** → **walker boot + evaluation for metabolic bone disease**

**"Ballerina, 2nd MT shaft, callus on 2-wk X-ray — next?"** → **protected weight-bearing**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · bone scan · biopsy · observation

**"Runner, normal X-ray, MRI oedema of 3rd MT?"** → **fracture boot, WBAT**

**"Recruit, amenorrhoea, distal 2nd MT on MRI — treatment?"** → **immobilisation in a short walking cast**

**"Field-hockey player, 3rd MT stress reaction — next?"** → **serum cortisol, TSH, vitamin D + calcium levels**

**"Young child fell — commonest MT fracture site?"** → **1st MT** (label 1)

**"Best managed nonoperatively?"** → **5th MT spiral fracture**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Midfoot Arthritis

*Foot & Ankle · 33 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & etiology

**Joints** — **naviculocuneiform**, **intercuneiform**, **tarsometatarsal (metatarsal–cuneiform)** · 2nd + 3rd TMT commonly narrowed

**Commonest cause** — **idiopathic / primary OA** (Orthobullets, OB) · **post-traumatic** articular damage (another OB) — read the options

**Post-traumatic** — missed / untreated **Lisfranc injury** (plantar bruising, "ankle sprain") → progressive flatfoot + midfoot arthrosis

**Inflammatory** — RA, lupus · **rule out inflammatory + neuropathic causes** before treating

**Pathoanatomy** — large forces through joints with **limited motion** · supporting soft tissues overloaded → **midfoot collapse** · over time Achilles shortens → **equinus**

**RA foot** — **forefoot commonest** (dislocations, hallux valgus) · midfoot: symmetric degeneration across **talonavicular + intertarsal** joints

## 3 Presentation

### Post-traumatic vs inflammatory, abduction & gait

**Symptom** — **midfoot / arch pain with toe-off (push-off)** · dull, achy, worse with weight-bearing · walking distance limited

**Gait phase** — **toe-off** (load passes hindfoot → midfoot → forefoot)\
✗ Wrong options: heel strike · midstance · swing

![Gait cycle: midfoot arthritis pain peaks at toe-off (E)](../../MCQ/attachments/q218894-a-gait-cycle.jpg)

| Deformity on WB | Present? |
|---|---|
| **Longitudinal arch collapse** | **Yes** (✗ Wrong options: arch maintained) |
| **Midfoot collapse** — looks like **PTTI** | **Yes** |
| **Forefoot abduction** | **Yes** |
| **Hindfoot valgus** | **Yes** (✗ Wrong options: hindfoot varus) |
| **Equinus (Achilles) contracture** | **Yes** (✗ Wrong options: peroneus brevis contracture) |
| **Hallux valgus** | Yes |
| Dorsal midfoot prominence | Large **dorsal osteophyte** |

**Palpation** — arch / midfoot tender

**Midfoot vs PTTI** — **single-limb heel rise possible**, **inversion strength 5+**, supple hindfoot → PTT working → deformity **isolated to midfoot**

**Differential** — **PTTI** · post-traumatic **Lisfranc** · lateral ankle instability

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Lateral (WB)** | **Break in Meary's line (talus–1st MT)** with **apex at midfoot** · longitudinal arch collapse · **dorsal osteophytes** · normal calcaneal pitch (vs PTTI) |
| **AP (WB)** | Midfoot arthritic change · **forefoot abduction** · normal talar head coverage (vs PTTI) |
| Inflammatory | **Symmetric joint degeneration**, minimal extra bone |
| Primary OA | **Subchondral cysts, osteophytes**, asymmetric narrowing |
| Image-guided steroid injection | **Diagnostic + therapeutic** |

![Lateral radiograph: midfoot collapse with Meary's line break at the midfoot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7036-43a-moved.jpg)

![AP radiograph: midfoot arthritis with forefoot abduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7036-43b-moved.jpg)

| X-ray pattern | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **Symmetric degeneration** | **Inflammatory (lupus, RA)** |
| Subchondral cysts + osteophytes | **Primary OA** |
| Isolated dorsal osteophyte at 1st MTP | **Hallux rigidus** |
| Navicular sclerosis + collapse | **Köhler disease** |
| 1st–2nd base widening ↑ on stress | **Lisfranc injury** |

## 5 Management

```
Midfoot arthritis
├─ First line → NSAIDs, activity change, stiff rocker-bottom shoe, arch support, image-guided steroid
└─ Failed nonop → midfoot (medial column) arthrodesis
       ├─ Equinus → + Achilles lengthening
       └─ Hindfoot valgus → + hindfoot realignment
```

### Nonoperative care – orthoses & bracing

**Best orthosis** — **stiff-soled rocker-bottom shoe** → transfers load **hindfoot → forefoot** with minimal midfoot stress

**Others** — **cushioned heel**, **longitudinal arch supports**

![Rocker-bottom shoe for midfoot arthritis](../../MCQ/attachments/q218571-e-rocker-bottom-shoe.jpg)

| Orthosis | Use |
|---|---|
| **Rocker-bottom shoe** | **Midfoot (1st–2nd TMT) arthritis** |
| **Arizona brace** | **Flexible hindfoot planovalgus**; ankle + hindfoot stability |
| **Lateral heel wedge** | **Cavovarus with fixed forefoot pronation** |
| Lateral wedge + **1st ray cutout** | Flexible **cavovarus** |
| **Morton's carbon-fibre extension** | **1st MTP arthritis / hallux rigidus** |
| **Posterior leaf-spring AFO** | **Foot drop** |
| Medial wedge insole, **UCBL** | Flexible **planovalgus** |
| **Metatarsal pad** | **Freiberg disease** (2nd MT head AVN) |

### Midfoot / medial column arthrodesis

**Indication** — **failed nonoperative management** · midfoot joints are **non-essential** → fusion gives **near-normal foot function**, esp. push-off

**Isolated midfoot flatfoot (heel rise OK, flexible hindfoot, mild heel cord contracture)** — **medial column arthrodesis + heel cord lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT debridement · tendon transfer + spring ligament / LCL · triple arthrodesis (PTTI / hindfoot procedures)

**TMT collapse, inversion 5+, supple hindfoot, normal heel cord** — **medial Lisfranc arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: LCL + FDL · TN · subtalar · triple (hindfoot procedures)

**Atraumatic midfoot OA, push-off pain, failed nonop** — **midfoot arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: PRP (not studied) · 1st MT opening wedge (hallux valgus) · **dorsal exostectomy — limited relief in advanced OA** · double calcaneal osteotomy (planovalgus abduction)

**Iliac crest autograft** — **improved fusion rate / ↓ nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: cost · fewer implants · long-term alignment (depends on technique + fixation) · donor morbidity (significant donor pain)

**Realignment arthrodesis** — **1st ray → 1st TMT** · **2nd / 3rd rays → NC + intercuneiform joints** · **do NOT fuse 4th / 5th TMT** (lateral ray mobility accommodates stance) · select cases: **interposition arthroplasty of 4th / 5th TMT** (keeps lateral column length)

**Technique** — TMT joints **2–3 cm deep** → prepare fully · screws, staples, midfoot plates · ± **Achilles lengthening**, ± **hindfoot realignment**

**Outcome** — union linked to satisfactory outcome in **up to 90%** (Kurup) · **union 92%**, few complications at 4.5 yr (Nemec) · inflammatory midfoot: **primary fusion** most reliable

## 6 Complications

### Triple arthrodesis – talonavicular nonunion (TO)

| Complication of triple arthrodesis | Fact (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion** | **Commonest complication overall** + **commonest early / midterm** (TO) |
| Site of nonunion | **Talonavicular** (most difficult joint to fuse) **> calcaneocuboid** (TO) |
| **Ankle arthritis** | **40–100%** with long follow-up = **commonest long-term** complication (TO) |
| **Midfoot arthritis** | **\~50%** (TO) |
| Hardware irritation, infection, osteonecrosis, malunion, DVT, nerve injury | **Rare** (TO) |

## X vs Y

| | **Midfoot arthritis** | **PTTI flatfoot** |
|---|---|---|
| Single-limb heel rise | **Possible** | Unable |
| Apex of collapse | **Midfoot** (Meary break at TMT / NC) | Talonavicular / hindfoot |
| Calcaneal pitch, talar coverage | **Normal** | Abnormal |
| Surgery | **Medial column / Lisfranc arthrodesis** | Tendon transfer, osteotomies, triple |

| | **Inflammatory** | **Primary OA** |
|---|---|---|
| X-ray | **Symmetric degeneration**, little bone formation | **Cysts, osteophytes**, asymmetric |

## How it's asked

**"Lupus, midfoot pain — X-ray finding?"** → **symmetric joint degeneration**

**"Clinical finding with post-traumatic midfoot arthritis?"** → **forefoot abduction**

**"Gait phase that worsens midfoot arthritis?"** → **E — toe-off**

**"1st–3rd TMT OA, arch collapse — initial orthosis?"** → **Figure E — rocker-bottom shoe**

**"Rocker-bottom shoe prescribed — diagnosis?"** → **first and second tarsometatarsal arthritis**

**"Active 70-yo, push-off pain, failed nonop?"** → **midfoot arthrodesis**

**"Flat foot, heel rise OK, flexible hindfoot, tight heel cord?"** → **medial column arthrodesis and heel cord lengthening**

**"TMT collapse, inversion 5+, supple hindfoot?"** → **medial Lisfranc arthrodesis**

**"Why iliac crest autograft in midfoot fusion?"** → **improved fusion rate**

**"Commonest complication of triple arthrodesis?"** → **talo-navicular nonunion** (TO)

**"Commonest pseudoarthrosis after triple arthrodesis?"** → **talonavicular** (TO)

**"Commonest long-term complication of triple arthrodesis?"** → **ankle arthritis** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# MTP Dislocations

*Foot & Ankle · 34 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **multiplanar instability of a lesser MTP joint** → **crossover toe** (dorsomedial deviation of 2nd toe)

**Commonest joint** — **2nd MTP**

### Plantar plate anatomy

**Structure** — broad, thick ligamentous plate on plantar MTP joint

**Origin** — **metatarsal head** via thin synovial attachment, just proximal to articular surface (**weakest point = origin at metatarsal neck**)

**Insertion** — **plantar base of proximal phalanx** (ruptures here and retracts proximally)

**Function** — **resists sagittal tensile load, esp. MTP dorsiflexion** · cushions joint, supports weight-bearing

![Plantar plate of a lesser MTP joint](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7053-plantar-plate.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

### Pathoanatomic stages

| Stage | Event | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Plantar plate disrupted** — **chronic inflammation (commoner)** or traumatic rupture | Dorsal laxity |
| **2** | **Lateral collateral ligament fails** (OB: **LCL fails first**, plantar plate second — read the options) | **Medial deviation of 2nd toe**; plantar plate + flexors displace medially |
| **3** | **Medial structures contract** — lumbrical, interosseous, MCL, medial capsule | Adduction force |
| **4** | **Plantar plate fails completely** | **Hyperextension → dorsal instability / dislocation** |

**Trauma** — **hyperextension injury** of toe (push-off, spin) → plantar plate tear

**Chronic** — **repeated MTP dorsiflexion** attenuates plantar aponeurosis, plantar plate, capsule

**Accelerated by** — **cortisone injection** for misdiagnosed **Morton's neuroma** or MTP synovitis

**Once plate fails** — intrinsic + extrinsic forces dorsiflex the proximal phalanx · main deforming force = **EDL**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain at **toe-off / push-off** · **"walking on a marble / pebble / rock in shoe"** · better in padded shoes than barefoot

**Point tenderness** — **plantar aspect of 2nd MTP**; pain on hyperextension

**Calluses** — **plantar under MT head** (fat pad migrates **dorsally**, uncovering head) + **dorsal over PIP** (abuts toe box)

**Deformity** — **dorsomedial deviation (crossover toe)** · **hammertoe**: MTP extension + PIP flexion · crossover develops when **collateral ligament(s)** also injured

**Early instability** — before deformity, **mimics Morton's neuroma**

![Crossover second toe](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7053-crossover-toe.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Plantar plate injury & instability – drawer test

**MTP drawer / modified Lachman** — grip proximal phalanx (neutral) + MT head → **dorsally directed force** → **↑ sagittal (dorsal–plantar) translation / dorsal subluxation** · **> 50% subluxation** = marked instability

![MTP drawer test](../../MCQ/attachments/q2874-illa-mtp-drawer-test.jpg)

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **WB AP, oblique, lateral** | AP: proximal phalanx dislocated — **medial > lateral** · lateral: **hyperextension + dorsal dislocation** of proximal phalanx |
| X-ray early | Often **normal** in plantar plate tear |
| **MRI** | **Early MTP synovitis / plantar plate tear**; rules out other pathology · not useful once dislocated |

### Clinical grading of plantar plate rupture (OB)

| Grade | Injury | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Sprain** | **Rest, ice, NSAIDs, stiff-soled shoe, MT pad** |
| **II** | **Partial tear** | **Nonop first** (± cast) |
| **III** | **Complete tear** | Repair if nonop fails |

### Surgical grading of plantar plate tears

| Grade | Tear pattern |
|---|---|
| **0** | Plate / capsular **attenuation** ± discolouration |
| **1** | **Transverse distal tear < 50%** (next to phalangeal insertion) ± midsubstance < 50% |
| **2** | **Transverse distal tear > 50%** ± midsubstance < 50% |
| **3** | **Extensive transverse ± longitudinal tear** (may involve collaterals) |
| **4** | **Extensive tear with button-hole (dislocation)**; little plate left to repair |

### Differentials by exam

| Finding | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| **↑ sagittal translation, + drawer / Lachman, MTP hyperextension pain** | **Plantar plate tear** ✓ |
| **Squeeze test + / palpable click (Mulder)**, numbness, **3rd web space** | **Morton's neuroma** |
| Point tenderness **2nd MT shaft** | **2nd MT stress fracture** |
| **Flexion contracture + dorsal ulceration** | **Hammer toe** |
| **AVN, flattening / collapse of 2nd MT head** from repetitive microfractures | **Freiberg's infraction** |
| Intrinsic weakness | **Claw toe** |
| TMT pain, X-ray arthritis | Midfoot arthritis |

## 5 Management

```
Lesser MTP instability / plantar plate tear
├─ Sprain, partial tear, first presentation → nonop (does not correct deformity)
├─ Failed nonop, painful instability → plantar plate repair + Weil osteotomy
└─ Severe subluxation / dislocation, fixed → Weil shortening osteotomy + soft-tissue balancing
       └─ Release + tendon lengthening fail → add MT shortening osteotomy
```

**Nonop (first line)** — **taping**, **shoe modification / extra-depth shoes**, **metatarsal pads**, well-padded liners, **Budin splint**, **stiff-soled shoe**, NSAIDs, Achilles stretching, ± cast · **won't correct deformity**

**Crossover toe 1 yr after hallux valgus, 1 month symptoms, > 50% drawer** — **orthotics with MT pads + shoe modification + taping + NSAIDs**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection (attenuates plate/capsule further) · revision HV correction · Weil + plate repair before nonop fails · MTP fusion

**Grade II plantar plate tear in athlete** — **rest, NSAIDs, stiff-soled shoe**\
✗ Wrong options: direct repair · repair + osteotomy · reconstruction (plate usually repaired) · arthrodesis

**Plantar plate repair indications** — failed nonop · (hallux) **sesamoid retraction, loose fragments** · lesser toe repair is done **with Weil osteotomy** (protects repair) · dorsal or plantar approach, both good

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Significant pain + loss of function, fixed deformity** | **Weil (distal oblique shortening) osteotomy** |
| Mild–moderate deformity | **Capsular release, collateral tightening, tendon lengthening, flexor transfer** |
| **Severe subluxation / dislocation, persists after release** | **MT shortening osteotomy** (decompresses joint, lengthens soft tissues) |
| Incompetent plantar plate | **Plantar plate repair** (sutures distal plate → drill holes in proximal phalanx) + osteotomy |
| Incompetent lateral structures | **EDB transfer under transverse intermetatarsal ligament** (dynamic stabiliser) |
| Dynamic flexion support | **FDL → EDL transfer (Girdlestone-Taylor)** — split FDL over proximal phalanx |
| Salvage | **DuVries metatarsal head resection arthroplasty** |

### Metatarsal shortening osteotomy (Weil/Helal) & floating toe

**Weil** — **intra-articular capital (metatarsal neck / head) osteotomy** · cut **parallel to plantar foot** · **longitudinal decompression by shortening** → joint reduces · **screw perpendicular to osteotomy** · with dorsal + medial capsular release, lateral reefing

**Helal** — **more proximal (shaft)** osteotomy · **no fixation**

| | **Weil** | **Helal** |
|---|---|---|
| Level | **MT neck / head (distal)** | **Shaft (proximal)** |
| Fixation | **Screw** | **None** |
| Satisfaction, AOFAS | **Higher** | Lower |
| Recurrent metatarsalgia, transfer lesions | **Fewer** | More |
| MTP reduction maintained | **Better** | Worse |
| **Malunion / pseudoarthrosis** | **Lower** | **Higher** |

**Weil vs Helal EXCEPT** — Weil does **NOT ↑ malunion / pseudoarthrosis**

**✗ Next-step traps (subluxation after release)** — **flexor tendon resection** (floppy toe, no dorsal correction) · **proximal phalangeal osteotomy** (distal to problem) · **lesser MTP arthrodesis** (poorly tolerated) · **distraction osteogenesis of MT** (lengthens → more instability)

![Weil osteotomy](../../MCQ/attachments/q3447-illa-weil-osteotomy.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Floating toe** — **commonest complication of Weil osteotomy** · toe elevated in sagittal plane, **doesn't touch floor** standing · inability to flex MTP → **2nd toe dorsiflexion deformity** · from surgery, injury, or shortened MT\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal release + pin · silicone implant · excisional arthroplasty · Helal

**Toe vascular compromise** — correcting **chronic dislocation** stretches contracted vessels → **may need to reverse the procedure** to save the digit

## X vs Y

| | **Plantar plate rupture** | **Freiberg's infraction** | **Morton's neuroma** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pathology | Ligament failure → **dorsal instability** | **AVN of MT head subchondral bone** (repetitive microfracture) | Interdigital nerve |
| Exam | **+ drawer / Lachman**, ↑ sagittal translation | MT head pain | **Mulder click**, squeeze, numbness |
| Site | **2nd MTP** | **2nd MT head** | **3rd web space** |
| X-ray | Normal early | **Flattening / collapse** | Normal |

## How it's asked

**"Golfer, 2nd MTP hyperextension, calluses dorsal PIP + plantar head — all true EXCEPT?"** → **repetitive microfractures compromising MT subchondral blood supply** (that's Freiberg)

**"Plantar callus mechanism?"** → **dorsal displacement of fatty cushion under MT head**

**"MRI 2nd MTP plantar plate tear — exam finding?"** → **↑ sagittal translation (+ drawer)**\
✗ Wrong options: hyperflexion · squeeze pain · shaft tenderness

**"Grade II plantar plate tear, footballer — next step?"** → **rest, NSAIDs, stiff-soled shoe**

**"Rock in shoe, + modified Lachman, normal X-ray — diagnosis?"** → **plantar plate rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: Freiberg · Morton's · claw toe

**"Joints still subluxate after extensor lengthening + capsular release?"** → **metatarsal shortening osteotomy**

**"Weil vs Helal — all true EXCEPT?"** → **↑ malunion / pseudoarthrosis**

**"Floating toe — commonest complication of?"** → **Weil (metatarsal neck) osteotomy**

**"Crossover toe after HV surgery, 1 month symptoms?"** → **MT pads, shoe modification, taping, NSAIDs**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid injection

**"Commonest complication of MTP dislocation correction?"** → **floating toe**

**"Pale toe after correcting chronic dislocation?"** → **vascular stretch → reverse correction**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Nerves of the Foot

*Foot & Ankle · 35 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Tibial nerve branches

**Tibial nerve** — ankle **plantarflexion**, **toe flexion**, **plantar sensation** · 2 primary branches **medial + lateral plantar** → **all intrinsic foot muscles except EHB + EDB**

| Branch | Motor | Sensory |
|---|---|---|
| **Medial calcaneal n.** | — | **Plantar medial heel** (lesser role) |
| **Medial plantar n.** | **Abductor hallucis**, **FHB**, **FDB**, **lumbricals to 2nd + 3rd toes** (TO: **1st lumbrical**) | Medial plantar foot |
| **Lateral plantar n. (LPN)** | **Adductor hallucis**, **quadratus plantae**, **all interossei**, **lateral two lumbricals**, **abductor digiti minimi** (via Baxter's) | **Plantar lateral foot**, lateral 4th toe, 5th toe (TO: **lateral ⅓ plantar foot + 4th + 5th toes**) |
| **Baxter's n.** | **1st branch of LPN** = nerve to **abductor digiti quinti (ADM)** | Courses **anterior to medial tuberosity between QP and FDB** |

**Lateral / medial plantar injury** — **weak or absent MTP flexion** (**intrinsic minus**)

**Medial plantar proper branch** — at risk in **medial-plantar approach to tibial sesamoid**

![Plantar nerves of the foot](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7004-scan-nerves-pl-foot-1-moved.jpg)

### Dorsal & cutaneous nerves

| Nerve | Supplies | Most vulnerable |
|---|---|---|
| **Sural** | Dorsal sensation **4th web space (70–80%)** · lateral foot | **Extensile lateral approach to calcaneus** · **screw for proximal 5th MT stress fracture** · **Achilles repair (esp. percutaneous)** · **gastroc recession** · **peroneal tendon repair (posterolateral incision)** |
| **Deep peroneal (DPN)** | Motor **EDB + EHB** · sensation **1st dorsal web space** | Compressed **beneath inferior extensor retinaculum** (anterior tarsal tunnel) · **lateral branch**: **calcaneonavicular bar excision**, **Lisfranc reduction approach** · anterior ankle approach |
| **Superficial peroneal (SPN)** | **Most of dorsum of foot** | **Medial branch (dorsomedial cutaneous)**: **bunion surgery** · **lateral cutaneous branch**: **anterolateral arthroscopy portal**, **distal fibula ORIF (esp. Weber C)** · **ankle inversion injury** · anterior ankle approach (medial branch) |
| **Saphenous** | **Medial side of foot** | — |
| **Tibial** | Plantar foot | **Tarsal tunnel release (posteromedial)** |

**DPN injury** — **weak / absent EDB + EHB** · trauma or **CMT**

**SPN exit from deep fascia** — **12–15 cm** proximal to lateral malleolus tip (Orthobullets, lateral cutaneous branch) · **10–12 cm** (OB) — read the options · wraps **fibular head**, branch of **common peroneal**

![Plantar sensory distribution — tibial, sural, saphenous, medial and lateral plantar](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7004-sensory-plantar-foot-moved.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Tarsal tunnel – denervation pattern

**Tarsal tunnel syndrome** — **commonest tibial nerve compression** · plantar numbness, Tinel **posterior to medial malleolus** · EMG **prolonged sensory + distal motor latencies** · untreated → intrinsic **denervation atrophy on MRI**

**Spared muscle** — **peroneus brevis** (**SPN**) — least likely to atrophy

| Coronal T1 MRI label | Muscle | Nerve |
|---|---|---|
| A | **Abductor hallucis** | Medial plantar |
| B | **Quadratus plantae** (flexes 2nd–5th toes) | Lateral plantar |
| C | **Flexor digitorum brevis** | Medial plantar |
| D | **Abductor digiti minimi** | Lateral plantar (Baxter's) |
| **E** | **Peroneus brevis** | **SPN — spared** |

![Coronal T1 MRI hindfoot muscles A–E](../../MCQ/attachments/q215618-a-foot-mri.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Iatrogenic nerve injury by incision

| Incision | Nerve at risk |
|---|---|
| **Gastroc recession (posterior calf)** | **Sural** ✓ |
| Anterior total ankle approach | **Medial branch of SPN + DPN** (✗ Wrong options: tibial) |
| Tarsal tunnel release (posteromedial) | **Tibial nerve + branches** (✗ Wrong options: DPN) |
| Peroneal tendon repair (posterolateral) | **Sural** (✗ Wrong options: SPN) |
| Calcaneus ORIF | **Sural** (✗ Wrong options: lateral plantar) |
| Bunion (medial MTP incision) | **Dorsomedial cutaneous n.** |

**Dorsomedial cutaneous nerve** — **terminal branch of SPN** · **5–15 mm medial / plantar to EHL** · injury → **reduced sensation over medial aspect of great toe** ± painful neuroma\
✗ Wrong options: saphenous · DPN · motor weakness

**✗ Motor traps** — 1st MTP **abduction** weakness = **abductor hallucis (medial plantar)** · IP **extension** weakness = **EHL / EHB (DPN)** · IP **flexion** weakness = **tibial n.**

**Dorsomedial cutaneous nerve syndrome** — severe neuritic pain over medial eminence + 1st toe after bunion surgery · nerve transected / scarred → **resection + burial** if nonop fails · **positive nerve block** before surgery

![Dorsomedial cutaneous nerve isolated in a bunion incision](../../MCQ/attachments/q3835-a-dmcn.jpg)

### Painful neuroma – neurectomy & burial

**Chronic SPN injury after inversion sprain** — paraesthesia dorsum, **Tinel anterolateral ankle**, no instability · failed 1 yr nonop (steroid, brace, PT) → **neurotomy + transposition to bone** (bone burial > muscle burial for pain)\
✗ Wrong options: Semmes-Weinstein (diabetic screening) · PRP (no evidence) · neuroma excision + nerve grafting · Broström-Gould (no instability)

**Glass laceration, plantar lateral forefoot numbness** — **lateral plantar nerve laceration** (MRI: through abductor hallucis + LPN)\
✗ Wrong options: foreign body · Baxter's (Tinel deep to abductor hallucis) · interdigital neuroma (Mulder's click) · digital nerve (more distal)

## X vs Y

| | **SPN** | **DPN** |
|---|---|---|
| Sensation | **Dorsum of foot**, dorsomedial hallux | **1st dorsal web space** |
| Motor (foot) | None | **EDB, EHB** |
| Classic injury | Bunion (medial branch), anterolateral portal, Weber C, inversion sprain | Anterior tarsal tunnel, CN bar excision, Lisfranc approach |

| | **Medial plantar** | **Lateral plantar** |
|---|---|---|
| Motor | AbH, FHB, FDB, lumbricals 2–3 | AdH, QP, all interossei, lateral 2 lumbricals, ADM |
| Sensation | Medial plantar | Plantar lateral, 4th (lateral) + 5th toes |

## How it's asked

**"Nerve looped in medial bunion incision — true?"** → **terminal branch of the superficial peroneal nerve; reduced sensation over medial aspect of great toe**

**"Correct incision–nerve pairing?"** → **gastroc recession – sural nerve**

**"Chronic SPN pain after ankle sprain, failed nonop — next?"** → **neurotomy and transposition to bone**

**"Stepped on glass, plantar lateral numbness, no Mulder's, no Tinel?"** → **lateral plantar nerve laceration**

**"Tarsal tunnel — muscle least likely to atrophy?"** → **peroneus brevis (E)**

**"First branch of LPN?"** → **Baxter's nerve (to abductor digiti minimi)**

**"Sensation to 1st dorsal web space?"** → **deep peroneal nerve**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Orthotics

*Foot & Ankle · 36 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Uses & principles

**Uses** — **off-load** high pressure + ↓ shear · **cushion** vulnerable tissue (diabetics) · **correct flexible / accommodate rigid** deformity (planovalgus) · **eliminate painful motion** (hallux rigidus) · **replace lost motion**, improve gait (**ankle fusion**)

**Stabilisation** — **static** = rigid, holds segment fixed · **dynamic** = mobile, permits motion · or combination

**Naming** — by joints controlled (ankle + foot = **AFO**)

**Patient factors** — soft tissue at risk · functional level (orthosis must match) · **pathology**: soft tissue, flexible vs rigid deformity, painful motion, weakness

**Design** — **three-point pressure** system · aligned at anatomic joint · simple, light, durable, acceptable cosmesis

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Foot posture index (TO)

**FPI (TO)** — **static** clinical assessment of **standing** foot posture · quantifies supinated / neutral / pronated foot · **relaxed double-limb stance \~2 min**\
✗ Wrong options: **dynamic biomechanical assessment** = FALSE

**6 items (TO)** — **talar head palpation** · curves above + below malleoli · **calcaneal frontal-plane position (inversion / eversion)** · **talonavicular congruence** · **medial longitudinal arch height + congruence** · forefoot abduction / adduction

| Item (TO) | −2 (highly supinated) | 0 (neutral) | +2 (highly pronated) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Talar head palpation** | Lateral side only | Equal medial + lateral | Medial side only |
| Curves above / below malleolus | Below straight or convex | Roughly equal | Below markedly more concave |
| **Calcaneal inversion / eversion** | > 5° inverted (varus) | Vertical | > 5° everted (valgus) |
| **TN congruence** | Markedly concave | Flat | Bulging markedly |
| **Medial arch height** | High, acutely angled | Normal | Very low, arch contacts ground |
| Forefoot abd / adduction | No lateral toes visible | Equal | No medial toes visible |

| Total score (TO) | Foot type |
|---|---|
| **−5 to −12** | **Highly supinated** |
| **−1 to −4** | Supinated |
| **0 to +5** | **Neutral** |
| **+6 to +9** | Pronated |
| **≥ +10** | **Highly pronated** |

**Uses (TO)** — diabetic neuropathic ulcer risk · research inclusion / exclusion · sports injury risk · falls in elderly · age-related foot change · **more valid** than goniometric WB / NWB methods

## 5 Management

### Shoes & shoe modifications

| Shoe | Foot |
|---|---|
| **Extra-depth** | Room for orthosis / deformity |
| **Stiff, supportive** | **Flexible** foot (flexible planovalgus) |
| **Soft, shock-absorbing** (running shoe) | **Rigid, bony** foot |
| **Custom** | Severe deformity (**Charcot**) |
| **High-top lace-up** | **Poor distal proprioception** (proximal feedback, balance) |

**Internal vs external modification** — internal = **more mechanically effective**, ↓ shoe space · external = keeps volume, worse cosmesis, **wears faster**

| Modification | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Cushioned heel** | **Heel pad atrophy** |
| **Lateral flare** | Resists **inversion** (ankle instability) |
| **Medial flare** | Resists **eversion** |
| **Lateral sole wedge** | **Cavovarus with fixed forefoot pronation** (forefoot reaches ground without compensatory varus) |
| **Medial wedge** | **Flexible planovalgus (PTTD)** → corrects hindfoot valgus |
| **Lateral heel wedge** | Unloads **medial knee** compartment (fixed knee varus) |
| **Heel lift** | **Equinus**, **LLD** |
| **Extended shank** (carbon / steel) | Splints forefoot / midfoot: **hallux rigidus (Morton's carbon-fibre extension)**, midfoot arthritis |
| Sole excavation, metatarsal pad, toe crest, scaphoid pad | Bony prominences / painful sites |

**Lateral wedge for cavovarus with fixed forefoot pronation** — **lateral sole wedge** (Orthobullets) · **lateral heel wedge** (OB) — read the options

### Rocker soles

**Effect** — transfer body weight forward · **can destabilise the knee** → caution with balance / proprioception problems

| Rocker | Shape | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Mild** | Mild angle toe + heel — **commonest** | ↓ MT head pressure, forward propulsion |
| **Heel-to-toe (single)** | More angled toe + heel | ↓ heel-strike pressure + **need for ankle motion**: **ankle / subtalar arthritis or fusion**, midfoot amputation, **calcaneal ulcers** |
| **Toe only** | Angled at toe | ↑ WB proximal to MT heads |
| **Severe angle** | Steep toe | **MT head / toe-tip ulcers** |
| **Negative heel** | Heel lower than sole | **Fixed dorsiflexion** deformity |
| **Double** | Two rockers over forefoot + hindfoot | ↓ midfoot pressure: **midfoot prominence, Charcot** |

![Heel-to-toe rocker sole](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12278-heel-to-toe-rocker.jpg)

![Double rocker sole for midfoot prominence](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12278-doublerocker.jpg)

### Gait orthosis after ankle arthrodesis

**Ankle fusion gait** — **↑ metabolic energy 9–15%** vs normal

**Rocker-bottom sole** — **mimics ankle DF / PF** · redistributes plantar pressure · improves kinematics + function\
✗ Wrong options: **Arizona brace** (immobilises ankle, ankle arthritis) · non-articulating / articulating **AFO** (immobilise, foot drop) · **Morton's extension** (hallux rigidus)

### Foot orthoses (inserts)

| Insert | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Heel cup** | Rigid plastic; prevents **lateral calcaneal shift** in flexible planovalgus |
| **UCBL** | Rigid plastic over cast in **maximum manual correction**; heel + midfoot, medial / lateral / posterior walls; heel **vertical neutral** · **flexible planovalgus only** — **rigid → painful, skin breakdown** |
| **Longitudinal arch support** | Prevents subtalar depression, corrects pes planus |
| **Metatarsal pad / bar** | **Metatarsalgia** → load moved from MT heads proximally |

![UCBL orthosis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12278-ucbl-lateral.jpg)

### Ankle orthoses & AFO

**Arizona brace** — **UCBL + lace-up ankle support** · **flexible planovalgus** (rigid hindfoot support) (Orthobullets) · **immobilises ankle** → nonop **ankle arthritis** (OB) · **PTTI / AAFD**: limits ankle motion → **articulated AFO** preferred in active walkers (OB)

**AFO construction** — footplate, calf support, calf band · plastic, metal, leather

**Ankle → knee** — **ankle PF = knee extension force** · **ankle DF = knee flexion force**

| | **Non-articulating AFO** | **Articulating AFO** |
|---|---|---|
| Look | More aesthetic | — |
| Knee | **Flexion force at weight acceptance** (neutral ankle) | Adjustable stops / assists |
| Stance | No eccentric PF in early stance · **trim lines set late-stance flexibility** (max / moderate / min DF resistance) | More natural gait; also medial–lateral control |

| AFO design | Key facts | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior leaf spring** | **Commonest AFO** · narrow trim line **behind malleoli** · resists PF · **no medial–lateral control** | **Foot drop** |
| **Solid** | Trim line **anterior to malleoli** · blocks PF + varus / valgus | Rigid control |
| **Hinged** | Adjustable stops | Multiplanar instability, ankle pain, **spina bifida (mid-lumbar)** |
| **Ground reaction** | Pretibial shell, forefoot trim line · **max PF resistance → knee extension** | **CP with over-lengthened triceps surae + mild crouch** |
| **Patellar tendon bearing** | Load on patellar shelf | **↓ WB force through foot** |
| **CAM walker** | Off-the-shelf immobilisation, WB allowed | Ankle sprain, stable ankle fracture, Achilles rupture |
| **SMO (supramalleolar)** | **Shortest AFO**, ends above malleoli; heel neutral vertical | Flexible pes planus, planovalgus, hyperpronation |

![Posterior leaf spring AFO](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12278-plsafo.jpg)

| Ankle joint option | Effect | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Free motion** | Free DF + PF; **medial–lateral stability only** | **Ligamentous ankle instability** |
| **Plantarflexion stop** | Blocks PF, free DF → **knee flexion moment** at weight acceptance (✗ Wrong options: **quadriceps weakness**) | **Swing-phase foot drop**, flexible equinus |
| **Dorsiflexion stop** | Blocks DF, free PF → **knee extension moment** (prevents buckling) | **Weak plantarflexion in stance** (± weak quads) |
| **Limited motion stop** | Blocks both | **Global ankle weakness** |
| **DF assist spring** | Posterior coil spring aids swing DF | **DF weakness with preserved motion** |
| **T-strap** | Medial strap → lateral upright = **valgus correction** · lateral strap → medial upright = **varus correction** | Varus / valgus |

### Knee orthoses

**KAFO** — AFO + uprights + mechanical knee + 2 thigh bands · **quadriceps weakness / paralysis**, flexible genu varum / valgum · harder to don · not with moderate–severe cognitive dysfunction

**Double-upright metal KAFO** — **commonest** KAFO

**Scott-Craig orthosis** — **paraplegia (SCI)** standing + ambulation · T-shaped footplate, adjustable ankle stops, pawl-lock knee + bail · **hip hyperextension** puts COG behind hip, in front of locked knee + ankle · **10° ankle DF** alignment → swing-to / swing-through with crutches

## X vs Y

| | **Plantarflexion stop** | **Dorsiflexion stop** |
|---|---|---|
| Blocks | PF | DF |
| Knee moment | **Flexion** (avoid if quads weak) | **Extension** (prevents buckling) |
| Use | **Foot drop**, flexible equinus | **Weak plantarflexors** in stance |

| | **UCBL / medial wedge / Arizona** | **Lateral wedge / 1st ray cutout** |
|---|---|---|
| Foot | **Flexible planovalgus** | **Cavovarus** |

## How it's asked

**"Gait problems after ankle arthrodesis — device?"** → **Figure C — rocker-bottom sole**

**"FALSE about foot posture index?"** → **it is a dynamic biomechanical assessment** (TO)

**"Toe-tip / MT head ulcer — rocker?"** → **severe angle rocker**

**"Fixed ankle dorsiflexion deformity — rocker?"** → **negative heel rocker**

**"Charcot midfoot prominence — rocker?"** → **double rocker**

**"Commonest AFO, for foot drop?"** → **posterior leaf spring AFO**

**"UCBL in rigid planovalgus?"** → **painful, skin breakdown**

**"CP, over-lengthened triceps surae, mild crouch?"** → **ground reaction AFO**

**"AFO joint to avoid with quadriceps weakness?"** → **plantarflexion stop**

**"Paraplegic standing + swing-through gait?"** → **Scott-Craig orthosis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteochondral Lesions of the Talus

*Foot & Ankle · 37 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **69% of ankle fractures**, **70% of ankle sprains** (Orthobullets, OB) · **2–6% of all ankle injuries** (another OB) — read the options

**Unexplained chronic ankle pain** — OLT in **81%**

**Sprains that fail to settle** — **\~10%** → think **OLT** or persistent **instability**

**Bilateral** — **10%**

**Medial vs lateral frequency** — **medial more common**: \~**68%** (OB) · 50–56% vs lateral 34–44% · paediatric medial **53–73%** · most lesions actually **medial + central** on MRI (classic teaching: posteromedial / anterolateral)

**Chronic lateral ankle instability (CLAI)** — **32–56%** develop OLTs · repetitive talar tilt + translation → **impaction on medial dome**

### Diagnosis & aetiology – medial vs lateral lesions

| | **Medial talar dome** | **Lateral talar dome** |
|---|---|---|
| Trauma history | **Usually none** (overuse / microtrauma / ischaemia) | **Usually traumatic** (inversion ± dorsiflexion) |
| Trauma rate | 80% (Flick) · 60% (McGahan) | 100% (Flick) · \~90% (McGahan) |
| Position | **More posterior** (posteromedial) | **More central or anterior** (anterolateral) |
| Size / depth | **Larger, deeper** | **Smaller, superficial / shallow** |
| Frequency | **More common** | Less common |
| Spontaneous healing | Better | **Lower** → harder with nonop |
| Behaviour | Less symptomatic | **More often displaced + symptomatic** |

### Anatomy

**Talus** — frustum shape · **anterior broader than posterior** · **no muscular attachments**

**Cartilage** — covers **70% of talus** · **among thickest in body** · keeps tensile strength longer than femoral head with age

**Blood supply** — relies on **extra-osseous** supply · **deltoid artery supplies majority of talar body + dome**\
✗ Wrong options: release deltoid in medial malleolar osteotomy

**Mortise** — highly congruent · oriented **15° externally** from midsagittal line

## 2 Mechanism

**Lateral OLT** — **inversion + dorsiflexion + axial load** → shear of lateral dome

**Medial OLT** — **inversion + external rotation + plantarflexion + axial load** → shear of medial dome

**Pathophysiology after inversion injury** — **chondral shearing injury**\
✗ Wrong options: failure of embryonic segmentation = coalition · ATFL / CFL sprain · SPR tear

**Microtrauma** — repetitive microtrauma → ischaemic environment → loss of subchondral integrity → softened, disrupted cartilage

**Talar tilt** — **inverse relationship** between talar tilt on stress X-ray and OLT (bony constraint) · stable ankle + effusion after sprain → OLT

**Associated** — **cavus hindfoot** · **CLAI**

**LIFT lesion** — **lateral inverted osteochondral fracture of talus** after inversion · fragment's joint surface **faces talus, not tibia** · arthroscopy → **open reattachment** (most) or excision · good function at \~9 yr, osteophytes in all

![Coronal CT – LIFT lesion: inverted lateral dome fragment](../../MCQ/attachments/q221007-illa-screen-shot-2026-02-06-at-9-23-21-pm.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **inversion ankle sprain** · persistent pain after sprain that does not heal

**Symptoms** — pain over **ankle joint line** · **effusion** · **mechanical catching / locking**

**Exam** — effusion · **palpation rarely reproduces pain** · cavus · ROM limited by pain / effusion · **stable anterior drawer** (no instability) in classic stem

**Check ligaments** — **untreated lateral ligament insufficiency at time of OLT repair ↑ failure**

| Differential (option) | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| Tarsal coalition (failure of segmentation) | Sprains but ↓ subtalar motion |
| ATFL sprain | **Anterior drawer laxity in plantarflexion** |
| CFL sprain | **Anterior drawer laxity in dorsiflexion** · 2nd commonest sprained |
| SPR tear | Peroneal instability, **no effusion** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing ankle X-ray** | First step · **often normal** · subtle lucency / fragmentation · shows **open physes** |
| **Bone scan** | Equivocal X-ray · **94% sensitive, 96% specific** |
| **CT** | **Subchondral bone + cysts** · pre-op planning · **less reliable for purely cartilaginous / nondisplaced** lesions |
| **MRI** | **Persistent pain after sprain, normal X-ray, stable ankle → next step** · size, oedema (may **overestimate**), stability · **fluid deep to subchondral bone = unstable** · **92% sensitive** for stability |

**Persistent pain 6 mo after sprain, no instability, normal X-ray** — **MRI of ankle**\
✗ Wrong options: continue PT · CAM boot 2 wk · steroid injection · diagnostic arthroscopy

![AP ankle – lateral talar dome osteochondral lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7034-osteochondral-lesion.jpg)

### Juvenile lesions – nonoperative care & prognostic MRI features

**Only factor correlated with stability** — **open physes / skeletal immaturity**\
✗ Wrong options: absence of effusion · subchondral sclerosis · perilesional marrow oedema · T1 homogeneity · cysts · signal rim · intra-articular body

**MRI prognostic factor** — **incomplete cartilage cap with fluid penetration** beneath lesion = **unstable, poor prognosis** · intact cap = stable\
✗ Wrong options: perilesional stress fracture · ATFL / CFL rupture (not prognostic) · physeal closure (seen on X-ray) · synovial hypertrophy

**Open physes vs MRI sign** — open physes = only correlate of stability (OB, Patel) · four MRI stability criteria: **cartilage defect, oedema-like signal, T2 bright rim, subchondral cysts** (Hembree) — read the options (physes are judged on **X-ray**)

**Juvenile OCD counselling** — **nonop frequently relieves pain but often no radiographic healing even at 6 months**\
✗ Wrong options: spontaneous healing common (rare) · healing < 12 wk · hyperbaric O₂ · fusion frequently needed

**Paediatric** — mean age \~13 yr · stable lesions in skeletally immature → **trial of nonop** · conservative treatment best in children

### Berndt and Harty (radiographic)

| Stage | Description | Diagram |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Small area of subchondral compression** | ![Berndt and Harty stages 1–4](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7034-berndt.jpg) |
| **2** | **Partial fragment detachment** | |
| **3** | **Complete detachment, not displaced** | |
| **4** | **Displaced fragment** | |

### Ferkel and Sgaglione (CT)

| Stage | Description |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Cystic lesion within dome, intact roof** on all views |
| **2a** | Cystic lesion **communicating with dome surface** |
| **2b** | **Open articular surface** lesion with overlying nondisplaced fragment |
| **3** | Nondisplaced lesion **with lucency** |
| **4** | **Displaced fragment** |

### Hepple (MRI)

| Stage | Description |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Articular cartilage oedema** |
| **2a** | Cartilage injury + underlying fracture **+ surrounding bone oedema** |
| **2b** | 2a **without** bone oedema |
| **3** | **Detached, nondisplaced** fragment |
| **4** | **Displaced** fragment |
| **5** | **Subchondral cyst** formation |

## 5 Management

```
OLT
├─ Acute, nondisplaced, incomplete → immobilise + NWB 6 wk
├─ Acute displaced, viable cartilage + bone (> 0.5 cm²) → surgical fixation
├─ Chronic, < 1 cm, or small fragment with little bone → arthroscopic debridement + marrow stimulation
├─ > 1 cm, intact cartilage cap → retrograde drilling ± bone graft
└─ > 1 cm displaced, shoulder, cystic, failed BMS / drilling → osteochondral graft (OAT, ACI, allograft)
```

**Treatment depends on** — **age, activity demands, lesion size, stability** · surgery if nonop fails after **6 months**

### Operative criteria

| Lesion | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute injury, nondisplaced fragment, incomplete fracture** | **Immobilisation + NWB** (cast / boot 6 wk → PT) · **45% good–excellent** (Orthobullets) · 45–50% (OB) |
| **Acute displaced, viable cartilage, adequate bone, > 0.5 cm²** | **Surgical fixation** (headless compression screws / absorbable pins) |
| **Chronic, < 1 cm**, displaced small fragment with minimal bone | **Arthroscopic removal of loose fragment, debridement + marrow stimulation** |
| Ideal for BMS | **< 10 mm diameter, < 100 mm² area, < 5 mm depth** · excellent < 1.5 cm², best < 1 cm |
| **> 1 cm with intact cartilage cap** | **Retrograde drilling ± bone grafting** |
| **> 1 cm + displaced, shoulder lesions**, salvage of failed BMS / drilling | **Osteochondral grafting** (OAT, ACI, bulk allograft) |
| **Contraindications to grafting** | **Diffuse ankle arthritis · bipolar kissing lesions · advanced talar dome osteonecrosis** |

**Early surgery favoured by** — **displaced fragment no longer congruent with talar dome**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral location alone · < 1 cm · presenting within 2 wk · subchondral sclerosis (chronic / contained)

**Surgery indicated** — chronic lesions · acute displaced fragments · higher grade · failed nonop · **loose bodies**

**Success rates** — excision + curettage + drilling **85%** · excision + curettage 78% · nonop **45%** · excision alone **38%** · arthroscopic excision + BMS **87%** (OB)

### Small lesions – arthroscopic marrow stimulation

**Chronic medial OLT < 1 cm, failed nonop** — **arthroscopic marrow stimulation** (microfracture)\
✗ Wrong options: total contact cast (diabetes) · arthrodesis (no arthritis) · open ACI (large lesions, not first line) · steroid injection (short-lived)

**Antegrade microfracture** — **subchondral bone violated through tibiotalar joint arthroscopically**\
✗ Wrong options: via sinus tarsi (retrograde) · knee autograft (larger) · posterolateral talus autograft (not described)

**Technique** — debride unstable flaps → stable contained defect · **awl perpendicular**, **2–4 mm deep**, holes **2–3 mm apart** · stop inflow → **fat / blood** from holes = adequate · K-wire via anterior portals or **transmalleolar** for central / posterior lesions

**Repair tissue** — **fibrocartilage (type I collagen)** · fibrocartilage in 60% at second look, no correlation with outcome\
✗ Wrong options: hyaline at 6 months

**Outcomes** — **85% pain improvement** · 65–90% ↑ PROs · larger lesions worse · **age not a predictor**

**Complications** — cartilage delamination, graft failure

### Intact cartilage cap – retrograde drilling ± grafting

**Indication** — **> 1 cm (e.g. 15 mm), contained, intact cartilage cap** → **retrograde drilling and/or bone grafting** (initial surgery)

**Best outcomes** — **1.2 cm defect + intact cartilage cap + no fragment displacement**

**Retrograde drilling not for** — displaced fragment (→ fixation or debridement + antegrade drilling) · 0.8 cm, no oedema (→ immobilise) · 0.5 cm displaced (→ debridement + antegrade drilling) · acute loose body (→ arthroscopic removal + debridement + antegrade drilling)

**Technique** — probe cap (softening, dimpling) · **K-wire from sinus tarsi into defect** (fluoro / guide) · cannulated drill → curette cyst → graft

**Complication** — **violation of intact cartilage cap**

![Retrograde drilling from the sinus tarsi beneath an intact cap](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7034-retrograde-drilling.jpg)

### Displaced fragments – fixation

**Acute displaced 1 × 1 cm anterolateral, viable cartilage + intact subchondral bone** — **surgical fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · excision · excision + microfracture (chronic, < 1 cm², poor healing potential) · OAT (failed BMS or > 1.5 cm², cystic)

### Large / cystic lesions – osteochondral autograft

**1.6 cm² displaced lesion** — **osteochondral autograft transplantation of type II collagen** (hyaline)\
✗ Wrong options: marrow stimulation (type I fibrocartilage) · NWB 6 wk · retrograde drilling (needs intact cap) · excision

**OAT indications** — **> 1.0–1.5 cm²** · cystic / uncontained · **failed BMS** · displaced · failed drilling · CLAI with > 50–60 mm²

**OAT contraindication** — **bipolar kissing lesions** · diffuse arthritis · advanced talar dome osteonecrosis\
✗ Wrong options: displaced · failed drilling · failed BMS · > 1 cm (all indications)

**OAT technique** — donor **ipsilateral knee** · graft depth **12–15 mm** · perpendicular access, **flush** placement · knee cartilage **thinner** than talus → graft looks **prominent on X-ray** though cartilage flush

**OAT results** — 80–87% good / excellent · failure ↑ **> 2.25 cm²** · **donor-site morbidity up to 15%** · no worse after prior BMS · lesion size ∝ worse MOCART

**Fresh osteochondral allograft** — failed prior arthroscopic cartilage procedures · **large cystic component** · contained > 1.5 cm² · uncontained shoulder · **knee OA / prior knee infection** · patient fears knee donor morbidity · comparable to femoral autograft

**ACI** — **two-stage**: arthroscopic chondrocyte harvest (ankle or knee) → culture → implant via osteotomy under **tibial periosteal patch** (fibrin glue) · > 1 cm², ± cyst, shoulder, primary or revision · **MACI** = equivalent, may avoid osteotomy

**Approach** — **medial malleolar osteotomy** for **medial + posterior** lesions (2 parallel K-wires aim at lateral extent of OLT; predrill 2 holes) · lateral lesions → **lateral malleolar osteotomy or ATFL / CFL release**

### Concomitant instability – ligament reconstruction

**7 mm medial OLT + CLAI (> 15° talar tilt, + drawer) for BMS** — add **lateral ligamentous complex reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: peroneal debridement · deltoid reconstruction · OATS · OCA

**Unaddressed CLAI** — **↑ clinical failure** (AOFAS < 80) · larger lesions (**≥ 150 mm²**, > 1.5 cm) · **bipolar lesions** (medial malleolus tip, tibial plafond)

**1.8 cm posteromedial OLT, 12 mm cyst, intact cartilage, CLAI** — **OAT via medial malleolar osteotomy + concomitant lateral ligament repair** (+ subchondral bone graft)\
✗ Wrong options: microfracture + Broström (> 1.5 cm², cystic) · **staged** ligament surgery 6 mo later · retrograde drilling alone · ACI via anterior arthrotomy (two-stage)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Cause / procedure |
|---|---|
| **Graft failure** | All grafting procedures |
| **Persistent pain** | Small % regardless of treatment |
| **Osteotomy delayed / nonunion** | Malleolar osteotomy |
| **Talar body ischaemia** | **Deltoid released** (deltoid artery) |
| **Ankle impingement** | **Graft plug left proud** |
| **Donor-site morbidity** | Knee autograft harvest (up to 15%) |
| **Violation of cartilage cap** | Retrograde drilling |
| Progression | Lesion may involve entire ankle |

## X vs Y

| | **Antegrade BMS / microfracture** | **Retrograde drilling** | **OAT** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Size | **< 1 cm** | **> 1 cm** | **> 1–1.5 cm²**, cystic |
| Cartilage cap | **Absent / unstable** | **Intact** | Displaced / shoulder |
| Route | **Through tibiotalar joint** | **Through sinus tarsi** | Malleolar osteotomy |
| Tissue | **Fibrocartilage (type I)** | Own cap preserved | **Hyaline (type II)** |
| Pitfall | Delamination | **Breach of cap** | Proud plug, donor site |

## How it's asked

**"Dancer, pain 6 mo after sprain, stable ankle, normal X-ray — next step?"** → **MRI of the ankle**

**"Lateral vs medial talar OCD — true?"** → **increased incidence of traumatic aetiology**\
✗ Wrong options: deeper · better spontaneous resolution · more posterior · more common

**"Persistent pain after inversion, stable drawer, effusion, 2° talar tilt — pathophysiology?"** → **chondral shearing injury**

**"12-yo talar OCD — counsel parents?"** → **nonop relieves pain but often no radiographic healing even at 6 months**

**"MRI / lesion feature associated with stability and nonop?"** → **open physes**

**"MRI ordered in a 13-yo to assess which prognostic factor?"** → **incomplete cartilage cap with fluid penetration**

**"Chronic medial OLT < 1 cm, failed 6 mo nonop?"** → **arthroscopic marrow stimulation**

**"0.8 cm anteromedial lesion, no cap — best procedure?"** → **subchondral bone violated through tibiotalar joint arthroscopically**

**"15 mm contained lesion, intact cartilage cap — initial surgery?"** → **retrograde drilling and/or bone grafting**

**"Best outcomes after retrograde drilling?"** → **1.2 cm defect, intact cartilage cap, no displacement**

**"Acute displaced 1 × 1 cm anterolateral fragment, viable cartilage + bone?"** → **surgical fixation**

**"Strongest reason for early surgery over immobilisation?"** → **displaced fragment no longer congruent with talar dome**

**"1.6 cm² displaced talar dome lesion?"** → **osteochondral autograft transplantation (type II collagen)**

**"Contraindication to talar OAT?"** → **bipolar kissing lesions**

**"1.8 cm posteromedial OLT + cyst + CLAI?"** → **OAT via medial malleolar osteotomy + concomitant lateral ligament repair**

**"7 mm medial OLT + CLAI — add to BMS to reduce failure?"** → **lateral ligamentous complex reconstruction**

**"Repair tissue after microfracture?"** → **fibrocartilage (type I collagen)**

**"Graft looks proud on post-op X-ray after knee OAT?"** → **thinner knee cartilage** (surface is flush)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Peroneal Tendon Tears and Instability

*Foot & Ankle · 38 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Spectrum** — tenosynovitis → tendinopathy → tears → instability (subluxation / dislocation)

**Tears in lateral ankle instability** — **23–77%** (Orthobullets) · "up to **77%**" (OB) → often **missed** in lateral ankle pain

**Prevalence** — tears = **4%** of ankle injuries

**Instability** — young, athletic · presents late, mistaken for **ankle sprain**

**PB vs PL** — **PB tears far commoner** (vulnerable between fibula + PL) · PL tears less frequent (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: "PL tear more common" (TO)

### Anatomy – brevis / longus relationship & course

| Level | Peroneus brevis (PB) | Peroneus longus (PL) |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | **Distal ⅔ fibula** + intermuscular septum (TO) | **Proximal ⅔ lateral fibula**, septum, **lateral tibial condyle** (TO) |
| Becomes tendinous | **2–4 cm** above fibular tip (Orthobullets) · **2–3 cm** (TO) | — |
| **Retromalleolar groove** | **Directly posterior to fibula**, flat (TO) · **anterior + medial (deep) to PL** | **Posterior to PB**, rounded (TO) |
| **Peroneal tubercle** | **Superior / dorsal** to tubercle (at sinus tarsi level) | **Inferior / plantar** → cuboid tunnel |
| **Insertion** | **Base of 5th MT** (dorsolateral) | **Plantar base 1st MT + medial cuneiform** (via cuboid tunnel between **long plantar ligament** + cuboid groove — TO) |
| Function | **Primary evertor** · **63% of eversion power** (TO) · assists PF | **Plantarflexes 1st ray** + **everts foot** · **secondary ankle PF** + **stabilises medial column** (TO) · **transverse arch** · antagonises TA |
| Nerve | **Superficial peroneal (S1)** | **Superficial peroneal (S1)** |

![Axial MRI: PB directly behind fibula, PL posterior to PB](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7023-brevis-and-longus.jpg)

**✗ Insertion traps** — navicular + medial cuneiform = **tibialis posterior** · base of hallux distal phalanx = **FHL** · distal phalanges 2–5 = **FDL** · 1st MT base + medial cuneiform = **PL** (not PB)

**✗ Course traps** — 90° turn at medial malleolus → navicular + plantar MT 2–4 = **tib post** · anterior ankle → medial cuneiform + 1st MT base = **tib ant** · between talar tubercles → hallux = **FHL** · inferior to tubercle → medial cuneiform + 1st MT = **PL**

**✗ Ligament traps** — fibula → talar neck = **ATFL** · posterior fibula → posterolateral talar tubercle = **PTFL** · fibula → calcaneus = **CFL**

**Common sheath (TO)** — **\~4 cm above to 1 cm below** lateral malleolus tip · splits into two sheaths at **peroneal tubercle** (TO)

**Retromalleolar groove** — fibro-osseous tunnel · deepened by **fibrocartilaginous ridge** (lateral) · only **\~5 mm** deep · bounded by **SPR, PTFL, CFL, PITFL** · surface ↑ laterally by fibrocartilaginous ridge, medially by osseous groove (TO)

**Superior peroneal retinaculum (SPR)** — **primary restraint** to peroneal subluxation · from **posterolateral ridge / distal 1–2 cm of fibula** (TO: distal 2 cm) → lateral calcaneus / **Achilles sheath** · secondary restraint to anterolateral ankle instability · single fibro-osseous tunnel

**Inferior peroneal retinaculum** — at **peroneal tubercle**, forms 2 separate sheaths · not involved in instability · "sheaths thicken to form the inferior extensor retinaculum" (TO wording)

**Blood supply** — vincula from **anterior + posterior tibial arteries**, **whole tendon vascularised, avascular zones disproven** (Orthobullets) · vincula from **posterior peroneal + medial tarsal arteries** (TO) · "**vascular watershed** just posterior to fibula" (OB) — read the options

**Peroneus tertius (TO)** — separate muscle in **anterior** compartment (TO)

### Variants – peroneus quartus, os peroneum, low-lying PB

| Variant | Facts |
|---|---|
| **Peroneus quartus** | Arises from **PB muscle belly, distal ⅓ leg** (✗ Wrong options: **from PL** (TO EXCEPT)) · runs posteromedial to tendons · inserts **retrotrochlear eminence** of calcaneus · incidence **up to 21%** (OB) · quartus or quintus **10–34%** (Orthobullets) · **mass effect** → pain, tears |
| **Low-lying PB belly** | Below superior margin of SPR · up to **33%** (TO) |
| **Enlarged peroneal tubercle** | **29%** · entraps PL → **PL tenosynovitis / tears** · Harris view |
| **Flat / convex retromalleolar sulcus** | **18%** (Orthobullets) · "flat or concave" (TO wording) |
| **Os peroneum** | Sesamoid in **PL** near **CC joint / lateral plantar cuboid** · radiographically as few as **5%** (TO) · uni / bi / multipartite · symptomatic = "**stepping on a pebble**" |
| **POPS (TO)** | **Painful os peroneum syndrome** → acute fracture … hypertrophied peroneal tubercle entrapping PL / os (TO) |

### Associated conditions & risk factors

**Risk factors** — **lateral ligament (ATFL / CFL) instability** · **cavovarus hindfoot** · **Charcot–Marie–Tooth** · low-lying PB · enlarged tubercle · quartus / quintus · flat sulcus · os peroneum · **calcaneal malunion + subfibular impingement**

**Not risk factors** — **pes planus / PTT insufficiency** (TO, OB) · 5th MT base fracture (↑ with cavovarus but doesn't cause tears) · talocalcaneal coalition · OLT

**PL tears associated with** — **diabetes**, RA, **hindfoot varus**, enlarged peroneal tubercle

## 2 Mechanism

**SPR tear (TO)** — **rapid forced dorsiflexion + inversion** → **reflex peroneal contraction** → SPR torn → subluxation / dislocation = **most common mechanism** (TO) · e.g. **ski tips caught**, foot caught on astroturf

**Mechanism wording** — **dorsiflexion + inversion** (Orthobullets, TO, most OB) · "dorsiflexion + eversion" in some OB explanations — read the options\
✗ Wrong options: plantarflexion + inversion (line of pull direct, little subluxation)

**PB split – two mechanisms (TO)** — **subluxation over posterolateral fibular edge** (SPR lax / torn, chronic instability) → attrition · **compression between fibula + PL** in inversion (TO)

**PB tear pattern (TO)** — **longitudinal split** at **tip of fibula** (tendon changes direction acutely + compression by PL) (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: radial · base of 5th MT

**PL tears** — direct trauma, sports · **cuboid notch / tunnel** = commonest PL site\
✗ Wrong options: musculotendinous junction (TO)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **pop** / snap with **dorsiflexion** injury · lateral instability · pain + clicking returns after cast removal

**Pain** — lateral / **posterolateral** ankle, retrofibular · worse with **active eversion**, PF, **passive dorsiflexion**

**Inspection** — swelling behind lateral malleolus · **cavovarus** · "pseudotumour" · voluntary subluxation ± pop · tendons palpable as ridge over lateral fibula

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Apprehension test** | Subluxation / apprehension with **active DF + eversion against resistance** (most consistent) |
| **Palpable snapping** | **Tendon snapping over fibula during ankle dorsiflexion** (+ eversion) → **SPR injury** |
| **Compression test** | Pain / crepitus with **passive DF + eversion** |
| **Active circumduction** | Recreates instability |
| **Coleman block** | Hindfoot flexibility (cavovarus) |
| **Anterior drawer** | Concomitant ATFL laxity |

**✗ Exam traps** — +anterior drawer = **ATFL** · +ER stress = **syndesmosis** · anterolateral crepitus = arthritis / OCD / loose body · tenderness at **5th MT base with eversion = peroneal (PB) tear**, not SPR

**PL tendinopathy signs** — pain with **resisted eversion** + **resisted plantarflexion of 1st MT**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| **X-ray (WB)** | Often normal · **fleck sign** = cortical avulsion of SPR off **distal / posterior lateral malleolar ridge** (rim fracture) · cavovarus: plantarflexed 1st MT, ↑ Meary angle |
| **Os peroneum migration** | **Proximal migration = pathognomonic of PL rupture** · best on **oblique + lateral** · compare contralateral |
| **Harris view** | Peroneal tubercle morphology |
| **Ultrasound** | **Dynamic subluxation**, intra-sheath subluxation · **90% accurate** for tears · user dependent |
| **CT** | Calcaneal malunion, subfibular impingement, groove / tubercle shape |
| **MRI** | **Axial, slightly plantarflexed ankle** (↓ **magic angle**) · **83–90% sens, 72–75% spec** · circumferential sheath fluid = tenosynovitis · fatty infiltration · **three structures behind fibula = longitudinal split** · **boomerang sign** (PB wraps around PL) · accessory muscles, low PB belly · ATFL / CFL, OCD |

![Fleck sign – SPR avulsion off the lateral malleolus](../../MCQ/attachments/q1144-illa-fleck-sign.jpg)

**✗ PL rupture traps** — avulsion at 5th MT base (PB) · lateral tenderness (sprain) · **inability to plantarflex** (gastrocsoleus still works) · **pes planovalgus** (PTTD)

### Anatomic classification of SPR tears (Orthobullets)

| Grade | Description | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | SPR **partially elevated** off fibula, **fibrocartilaginous ridge intact** → both tendons sublux | ![SPR tear grades](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7023-classification.jpg) |
| **2** | SPR separated from **cartilofibrous ridge** → tendons sublux between SPR and ridge | |
| **3** | **Cortical avulsion** of SPR off fibula (fleck) → tendons under fragment | |
| **4** | SPR torn from **calcaneus**, not fibula | |

### Eckert & Oden classification of peroneal dislocation (TO)

| Type | Pathoanatomy | Frequency | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **SPR detaches from FCR + fibula with the anterior fibular periosteum** → false pouch | **51% — most common** | ![Eckert–Oden types](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733295-1.png) |
| **2** | **FCR + SPR + periosteum avulsed together** from anterior fibula | **33%** | |
| **3** | **Bony avulsion** of anterior fibula with FCR + SPR | **13%** | |
| **4** | **SPR itself ruptured** (added by Oden) | Rare, unknown | |

### Raikin classification – intra-sheath subluxation

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **1** | PL lies **deep** to PB |
| **2** | PL subluxes **through a PB tear** |

### Redfern & Myerson tear classification

| Type | Tendons | Treatment | Algorithm |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Both intact, partial tears** | **Debride + tubularise** | ![Redfern–Myerson algorithm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7023-algorithm.jpg) |
| **II** | **One intact, other majority torn** | **Tenodesis** to intact tendon | |
| **IIIa** | **Both torn (unusable), no muscle excursion** | **FHL / FDL transfer** | |
| **IIIb** | **Both torn, muscle excursion present** | **Allograft** (one-stage) · scarred bed → **staged silicone rod** | |

**Other OB wording** — "Type I = < 50% one or both tendons, Type II = > 50% complex" — read the options

### Brandes–Smith zones (TO)

| Zone | Site | Typical tear |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Under **SPR** | Subluxation, mass effect of **quartus / low-lying PB** |
| **B** | **Inferior peroneal retinaculum** | **Peroneal tubercle hypertrophy** |
| **C** | **Cuboid notch** | **Commonest site of PL pathology** |

## 5 Management

```
Peroneal instability (SPR tear)
├─ Acute, nonprofessional → reduce + SLC 6 wk, slight PF + inversion
├─ Acute, high-level athlete → SPR repair ± groove deepening
├─ Chronic / failed nonop → SPR repair + groove deepening
└─ Recurrent, bony abnormality → bone block / rerouting (salvage)
       └─ hindfoot varus → add calcaneal osteotomy
```

```
Peroneal tendon tear (after failed rest, boot, PT)
├─ < 50% (50–60%) of width → debride + tubularise
├─ > 50% (> 60%), one tendon usable → excise + tenodesis
├─ Both > 50%, muscle excursion → interposition allo / autograft
└─ Both > 50%, no excursion → FHL / FDL transfer
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Tenosynovitis / tendinopathy**, recalcitrant, < 50–60% to resect | **Tenosynovectomy + debridement** (tendoscopic) |
| **Tear < 50%** (Orthobullets 50–60%) | **Debridement + tubularisation** |
| **Complex PB tear > 50%** (multiple splits, tendinosis; OB: **> 60%**) | **Excise + proximal + distal tenodesis PB → PL** · 70–80% success, RTS 12 wk |
| **Complex PL tear > 50%** | **Tenodesis PL → PB** |
| **Both > 50%, excursion preserved (IIIb)** | **Interposition graft** (hamstring auto / allo) |
| **Both > 50%, no excursion (IIIa)** | **FHL / FDL transfer** |
| **Acute dislocation, high-level athlete wanting quick return** · longitudinal tear | **SPR repair + groove deepening** |
| **Chronic / recurrent with bony abnormality / incompetent SPR** | **Groove deepening + soft-tissue transfer / bone block** (salvage) |
| **Rigid hindfoot varus / valgus** | **Add corrective osteotomy** |

### Peroneal subluxation – SPR repair ± groove deepening

**Acute, nonop** — **tendons reduced** + **short-leg cast 6 wk**, protected WB, **slight plantarflexion + inversion** (TO, Orthobullets) · **irreducible acute dislocation → surgery** · some advise acute repair in athletes (high nonop failure) (TO) · **U- or J-shaped pad** · effective in **\~50%** (OB) · **> 50% failure for chronic** · up to **83% failure for tears**

**No PT / rehab for unstable tendons** — may further damage them · no more casting or stirrup splint after failed cast

**Failed nonop / chronic subluxation** — **SPR repair ± fibular groove deepening**\
✗ Wrong options: ATFL repair · CFL repair · Broström–Gould with solid drawer end point · fibular shortening osteotomy · walker boot in PF

**Fleck sign + persistent popping, solid drawer** — **peroneal retinacular reconstruction**

**Dislocated tendons + convex fibular groove** — **débridement + fibular groove deepening + SPR repair**\
✗ Wrong options: tear repair alone (won't stop dislocation) · tenodesis alone

**Commonest procedure for chronic dislocation (TO)** — **SPR repair + groove deepening** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: CFL rerouting · distal fibular osteotomy + CFL rerouting · complete fibular osteotomy / bone block

**SPR repair + groove deepening** — **RTS 83–100%** (higher than SPR repair alone) · excellent PROMs

**Surgical categories (TO)** — SPR repair / replacement · groove deepening · bone block / osteotomy · soft-tissue rerouting · tendon graft (TO)

| Technique (TO) | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Direct SPR repair** | Chronic SPR redundant → **pants-over-vest** (anterior flap over posterior) · direct, bone tunnels or anchors |
| **Groove deepening – direct** | Elevate cartilaginous floor → burr / curette cancellous bone → tamp floor back |
