| **Groove deepening – indirect** | **3.5 mm drill retrograde from fibular tip** parallel to groove → tamp floor into tunnel (preserves floor) |
| **Bone block (Kelly)** | Partial / complete fibular osteotomy, posterior displacement → barrier · firmer, but **fracture, nonunion, tendon irritation / adherence** |
| **CFL rerouting (Platzgummer)** | Tendons placed under CFL · salvage |
| **Tendon graft** | Achilles, PB, **plantaris** reinforce SPR via fibular tunnel |

**Bone block + rerouting (TO)** — nonunion, tendon adherence, **sural neuropathy**, instability → **salvage / revision only** (TO)

**Approach** — longitudinal incision over tendons, **avoid sural nerve** · SPR split longitudinally leaving cuff, or transected from fibula

### Tendon tears – debridement / tubularisation vs tenodesis vs transfer

**First line** — rest, activity modification, **boot then PT** (boot stops when rest pain resolves) · orthosis for varus · NSAIDs, bracing

**Tubularise (TO)** — after excising thin split, **> 50% of PB remains → tubularise** · **< 50% remains → tenodesis to PL** (TO)

**Severe degeneration (TO)** — **muscle excursion adequate → hamstring auto / allograft** · **scarred muscle → FHL / FDL transfer** · **fibrotic sheath → staged silastic rod → pseudosheath → repair** (TO)

**Single longitudinal PB tear, no tendinosis** — **core repair + tubularisation**

**PB: multiple splits, > 70% tendinosis over 3 cm** — **excise + proximal + distal tenodesis to PL**\
✗ Wrong options: core repair · excision without transfer · groove deepening · arthroscopic debridement

**PB tenodesis levels** — proximal **\~3 cm above** fibular tip · distal **\~2 cm below** tip, side-to-side

**30% PB + PL tear** — **debridement + tubularisation only**\
✗ Wrong options: 30% PB + shallow groove → add **groove deepening** · 60% PB → tenodesis · 60% both + excursion → graft · 60% both, no excursion → transfer

**SPR tear + complex PB 65%** — **SPR repair + groove deepening + PB → PL tenodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: tendinitis → tenosynovectomy · 30% → tubularise · both 70% → graft vs transfer

**PL tendinopathy (cuboid tunnel), PB normal** — **debride PL + tenodesis PL → PB** · proximal tenodesis just above fibular groove, distal just proximal to cuboid\
✗ Wrong options: PL / PB repair (chronic, no excursion) · PB allograft

**PL tear (MRI)** — **peroneus longus repair**\
✗ Wrong options: SPR repair + groove deepening (only if intact + dislocated)

**Failed PB repair, both tendons torn** — **FDL transfer to 5th MT** → balance + hindfoot mobility\
✗ Wrong options: **subtalar fusion** (salvage) · **PTT transfer** (loses inversion + arch) · **excise both** → imbalance → **forefoot varus**

**Persistent pain after boot + PT, eversion weakness** — **SPR repair + peroneal tendon repair / tenodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: recast · syndesmotic fixation · OLT excision · biopsy

**Tendoscopy** — viewing portal **2 cm distal**, working portal **3 cm proximal** to fibular tip

### Hindfoot varus – correction to prevent failure

**Hindfoot varus** — **risk factor for failure / recurrence** of peroneal stabilisation + repair → correct it\
✗ Wrong options: bunion · tarsal coalition · accessory navicular · talar OCD

**Correction** — **calcaneal osteotomy (lateral closing wedge, Dwyer)** or **lateral closing-wedge subtalar arthrodesis** (rigid / subtalar arthritis)

**PB tear + cavovarus NOT correcting on Coleman block** — **calcaneal osteotomy + 1st ray dorsiflexion osteotomy + PL → PB transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st ray osteotomy alone (Coleman non-correcting) · PB repair alone · **PRP** · **amniotic augmentation**

**Supple hindfoot, chronic PB tear** — contributor = **hindfoot alignment (cavovarus)**

### Calcaneal malunion – subfibular impingement

**Anterior ankle pain + ↓ dorsiflexion** — horizontal talus impinges on tibia → **subtalar bone-block arthrodesis**

**Lateral pain** — peroneal dislocation, **subfibular impingement**, subtalar arthritis → **lateral wall exostectomy**

**Malunion + subtalar arthrosis + exostosis + height loss** — **subtalar bone-block arthrodesis + lateral wall exostectomy ± Achilles lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT transfer · Achilles lengthening + exostectomy alone · SPR repair ± sural neurolysis (only for isolated incompetent SPR) · TTC nail (needs tibiotalar arthritis)

| Stephens–Sanders type | Treatment (OB) |
|---|---|
| **I** | **Lateral wall exostectomy + peroneal tenolysis** |
| **II** | + **subtalar bone-block arthrodesis** (bone graft) |
| **III** | + **closing-wedge calcaneal osteotomy** for varus, or triple arthrodesis |

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Sural neuroma** | **Most common** complication after surgery |
| **Recurrent instability** | **Unaddressed malalignment (hindfoot varus)** → corrective osteotomy ± soft-tissue reconstruction |
| **Persistent pain** | **Over-tightened SPR** → tendon stenosis |
| **Tibial nerve compression** | After **FHL transfer** → release FHL–NV bundle adhesions |
| **FHL / FDL transfer** | Residual **eversion weakness** |
| **Bone block** | Fracture, nonunion, tendon irritation / adherence |

## X vs Y

| | **Peroneal instability (SPR)** | **Peroneal tear** |
|---|---|---|
| Exam | Snapping / subluxation with **DF + eversion**, circumduction | Retrofibular tenderness, pain on resisted eversion, **no instability** |
| X-ray | **Fleck sign** | Usually normal (PL: os peroneum migration) |
| Surgery | **SPR repair ± groove deepening** | Tubularise / tenodesis / graft / transfer |

| | **SPR** | **Inferior peroneal retinaculum** |
|---|---|---|
| Attachment | Distal posterior fibula → Achilles sheath / lateral calcaneus | **Peroneal tubercle** of calcaneus |
| Role | **Primary restraint** to subluxation | Separates tendons into 2 sheaths |
| In instability | **Torn — repair** | Rarely injured, no repair |

| | **Debride + tubularise** | **Tenodesis** | **Graft** | **FHL / FDL transfer** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tear size | **< 50%** | **> 50%** one tendon | Both > 50% | Both > 50% |
| Muscle excursion | — | — | **Present** | **Absent** |

## How it's asked

**"Peroneal muscles — all true EXCEPT?"** → **peroneus quartus originates from the peroneus longus belly** (it arises from PB) (TO)

**"PB relation to PL in retromalleolar groove?"** → **anterior**

**"MRI longitudinal tear, tendon just behind fibula — insertion?"** → **fifth metatarsal base**

**"MRI shows PL tear — management?"** → **peroneus longus repair**

**"Structure A in peroneal sheath at surgery?"** → **peroneus quartus**

**"Tendon to plantar 1st MT base — name + function?"** → **peroneus longus; eversion of foot**

**"Snap while walking — most indicative of PL rupture?"** → **proximal migration of the os peroneum**

**"Chronic PB tear on MRI — true of the structure?"** → **dorsal to the peroneal tubercle**

**"Torn tendon course to insertion?"** → **superior to peroneal tubercle at sinus tarsi → base of fifth metatarsal**

**"Commonest pathoanatomy of peroneal dislocation?"** → **SPR detached from FCR + fibula with anterior periosteum** (Eckert–Oden 1) (TO)

**"Commonest procedure for chronic dislocation?"** → **SPR repair + groove deepening** (TO)

**"Mechanism causing SPR disruption?"** → **dorsiflexion + inversion**

**"Skier, snapping behind lateral malleolus — mechanism?"** → **forced dorsiflexion + inversion**

**"Popping, tendons sublux on active eversion — injured structure?"** → **superior peroneal retinaculum**

**"Chronic subluxation after failed PT?"** → **fibular groove deepening + SPR repair**

**"Exam finding of SPR injury?"** → **palpable tendon snapping over the fibula during ankle dorsiflexion**

**"Failed boot + physio, posterolateral pain, eversion weakness?"** → **SPR repair + peroneal tendon repair / tenodesis**

**"Popping + weakness after remote injury — deficient structure?"** → **superior peroneal retinaculum**

**"MRI dislocated tendons — repair what to stop popping?"** → **superior peroneal retinaculum**

**"Fleck sign, solid drawer end point — treatment?"** → **peroneal retinacular reconstruction**

**"Skier, clicking after cast, convex fibular groove?"** → **débridement + fibular groove deepening + SPR repair**

**"Axial T2 MRI after rolling ankle — diagnosis?"** → **PL dislocation at the level of the SPR**

**"Football player, labelled figure — injured structure?"** → **SPR (label 4)**

**"Skier, instability on circumduction — repair which structure?"** → **SPR (structure B)**

**"Commonest pattern + site of peroneal tear?"** → **longitudinal tear at tip of fibula** (TO)

**"True about peroneal tendon tear?"** → **rapid forced dorsiflexion + inversion is the commonest mechanism** (TO)

**"Diabetic, +Coleman block, PB normal, PL scarred?"** → **debridement of PL + tenodesis of PL to PB**

**"PB multiple splits, > 70% tendinosis?"** → **excision with proximal + distal transfer to PL**

**"Failed PB repair, PL + PB tears?"** → **FDL transfer to the fifth metatarsal**

**"Indication for debridement + tubularisation only?"** → **30% longitudinal PB tear + PL tear**

**"Soccer player, stable ankle, PB tear on MRI?"** → **symptomatic PB tear, tubularise if < 50%**

**"MRI finding indicating SPR repair + groove deepening + PB→PL tenodesis?"** → **complex PB tear of 65%**

**"PB tear + cavovarus not correcting on Coleman block?"** → **calcaneal osteotomy + 1st ray dorsiflexion osteotomy + PL to PB transfer**

**"Failure to correct what jeopardises peroneal stabilisation?"** → **hindfoot varus**

**"Supple hindfoot, chronic PB tear — contributor?"** → **hindfoot alignment**

**"Nonop calcaneus malunion — anterior + lateral pain, ↓ DF?"** → **subtalar bone-block arthrodesis + lateral wall exostectomy ± Achilles lengthening**

# Plantar Fasciitis

*Foot & Ankle · 39 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Demographics** — **men = women** · typical age **40–70** (OB) · common **bilaterally** · **posteromedial heel**

**Risk factors** — **obesity (↑ BMI)** · **↓ ankle dorsiflexion** (tight calf / foot musculature) in non-athletes · **weight-bearing endurance activity** (running, dancing) · steep ↑ training volume

### Diagnosis & anatomy

**Plantar fascia** — nonelastic multilayered **connective tissue** originating on the **medial calcaneal tubercle** → inserts on **metatarsal heads** · **supports the arch** · **windlass mechanism** in late stance\
✗ Wrong options: abductor hallucis origin · Baxter's nerve · Achilles insertion on calcaneal tuberosity · tibialis posterior on navicular tuberosity

**Shared origin on medial calcaneal tubercle** — **abductor hallucis, FDB, quadratus plantae** (may be inflamed too)

**Pathology** — **degenerative**, chronic overuse → **microtears at fascia origin** · recurrent inflammation + periostitis

**Heel spur** — may coexist · **not the cause**

### Associated conditions

**Heel pain triad** — **plantar fasciitis** + **posterior tibial tendon dysfunction** + **tarsal tunnel syndrome**

**Others** — **calcaneal apophysitis** · **gastrocnemius–soleus contracture** · femoral anteversion · pes cavus · pes planus

## 3 Presentation

**"Start-up" pain** — insidious **inferior heel pain on first steps out of bed** / after rest · may walk on toes first · **eases with ambulation** · worse at **end of day** after prolonged standing

**Exam** — **tender medial calcaneal tuberosity** (plantar medial / anteromedial heel) · **↑ with dorsiflexion of toes + foot** · **↓ ankle DF** (tight Achilles · gastrocnemius contracture on **Silfverskiöld**)

**Abductor hallucis origin tenderness** — small subset → **Baxter's nerve** (first branch of lateral plantar nerve = **inferior calcaneal nerve**) entrapment · **pain persists after activity** rather than improving

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; normal radiographs\
✗ Wrong options: navicular stress fracture · Freiberg · Baxter's entrapment · anterior tarsal tunnel

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **Radiographs** | **Not necessary on first visit** · often normal · may show **plantar heel spur** · optional WB axial + lateral hindfoot |
| **MRI** | **Surgical planning** |
| **Bone scan** | Quantifies inflammation · **rules out stress fracture** |
| **Labs** | Not routine · if **inflammatory arthritis / infection** suspected |
| **EMG** | **Rule out nerve entrapment** |

![Lateral radiograph – plantar calcaneal spur](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7025-calc-bone-spur.jpg)

![Sagittal MRI – plantar fascia at its calcaneal origin](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7025-pf9.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Plantar fasciitis
├─ First line → PF-specific + Achilles stretching, prefabricated insert, NSAIDs
├─ Persists > 6 wk → night splint (± steroid injection, cast)
├─ > 6 mo, failed → ESWT (FDA-approved)
└─ Failed 6–12 mo (9 mo) → partial plantar fasciotomy (medial ⅓)
       └─ + tarsal tunnel compression → open release + distal tarsal tunnel decompression
```

### First-line treatment – stretching, night splints & orthoses

**Best initial** — **Achilles + plantar fascia stretching** with **prefabricated shoe insert** / heel cups\
✗ Wrong options: short leg cast · steroid injection · custom orthotic · surgical release

**Plantar medial heel pain + calcaneal spur** — **Achilles stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: walker boot · steroid · 50% release · **heel spur resection**

**Highest satisfaction at 8 wk** — **plantar fascia–specific stretching** (recreates **windlass**)\
✗ Wrong options: isolated Achilles stretching · steroid · ESWT · tarsal tunnel decompression + release

**NWB plantar fascia–specific stretching > WB Achilles stretching** · all stretching programs **equal satisfaction at 2 yr**

**Prefabricated vs custom** — **prefabricated more effective** (with stretching) + cheaper (Orthobullets, OB) · "Level III evidence supports custom orthotics" (OB) — read the options

**Chronic plantar fasciitis orthosis** — **dorsiflexion night splint** = **only orthosis proven effective**\
✗ Wrong options: gel heel cushion · full-length prefabricated / accommodative insert · **Morton's extension** (1st MTP pathology) · **recessed first ray / cavus orthotic** (cavus foot) · semi-rigid medial post insert

![Dorsiflexion night splint](../../MCQ/attachments/q3834-e-figure-e.jpg)

**Plateau at 6 wk with NSAIDs + PT** — add **night splints**, continue PT\
✗ Wrong options: stop PT for custom orthotics · ESWT · endoscopic fasciotomy · bipolar radiofrequency

**Second tier (> 6 wk)** — **night splints**, repeat **corticosteroid injection**, **cast immobilisation** (short leg cast **8–10 wk**, Orthobullets)

**Symptom duration** — up to **6–12 months** to resolve

### Shock wave therapy

**ESWT** — **second line** · **chronic heel pain > 6 months** failing other treatment · **FDA approved** · painful · effective at 6-month follow-up · alternative to surgery once nonop exhausted

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Plantar fasciotomy** (open or arthroscopic / endoscopic) | **Persistent pain > 9 months** of failed conservative care (Orthobullets, AAOS) · **6–12 months** (OB) · after **6 months of second-tier** modalities (OB) |
| **Release + distal tarsal tunnel decompression** | **Concomitant tibial nerve compression** in tarsal tunnel → **open** procedure · **70–90% success** |
| **Gastrocnemius recession** | **No clear indications** established |
| **Baxter's nerve release** | Release deep fascia of **abductor hallucis** → may improve outcomes |

**Release extent** — **medial ⅓ to ⅔** of fascia only (OB: **medial third**) → protects **medial longitudinal arch**

**Avoid complete release** — arch destabilisation · **lateral column overload** · **dorsolateral foot pain**

**Fasciotomy outcomes** — complications common · protracted recovery

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Lateral plantar nerve injury** | Surgical |
| **Complete release** | **Medial longitudinal arch collapse** · ↑ stress **dorsolateral midfoot** |
| Chronic pain | — |
| **Heel pad (fat pad) atrophy** | **Steroid injection** → shoe padding |
| **Plantar fascia rupture** | **Athletes**, **minimalist runners**, **steroid injection** |

### Plantar fascia rupture – steroid injection & immobilisation

**Steroid mechanism** — **inhibit heel pad fibroblast proliferation** → **fat pad atrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: decrease inflammation (PF is degenerative) · no rupture association · good medium / long-term relief · US guidance prolongs relief (not needed)

**Steroid evidence** — ↓ **short-term** pain only · **no high-quality evidence** · risks: fat pad atrophy, **PF rupture**, local reaction · used rarely / second line

**Rupture presentation** — acute **pop** in runner after steroid shot → can't bear weight · **loss of medial arch → pes planus**\
✗ Wrong options: os trigonum (posterior pain) · pes cavus + peroneal subluxation · PTTI (no heel pain) · lateral talar process (snowboarders)

**Rupture treatment** — **cast immobilisation 3–6 wk** (OB) · **CAM boot** sufficient (OB) · stretching **after** immobilisation\
✗ Wrong options: urgent / delayed primary repair (no evidence) · stretching first · steroid

![Plantar fascia rupture on MRI](../../MCQ/attachments/q212989-illb-plantar-fascia-rputure.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Plantar fasciitis** | **Baxter's nerve entrapment** | **Calcaneal stress fracture** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pain pattern | **Start-up**, eases with walking | **Persists after activity** | Worse with WB |
| Tender | Medial calcaneal tubercle | **Abductor hallucis origin** | **Heel squeeze** |
| Test | Clinical | **EMG** | **Bone scan** / MRI |

| Orthosis | Use |
|---|---|
| **Dorsiflexion night splint** | **Chronic plantar fasciitis** (only proven) |
| **Morton's extension** | 1st MTP pathology |
| **Recessed first ray** | Cavus foot |
| Gel heel cushion / prefabricated insert | Acute PF with stretching; not proven in chronic |

## How it's asked

**"Start-up heel pain, BMI 35, gastrocnemius contracture, normal X-ray?"** → **plantar fasciitis**

**"Structure causing morning plantar heel pain?"** → **connective tissue originating on medial calcaneal tubercle**

**"Runner, anteromedial heel pain — best initial?"** → **Achilles + plantar fascia stretching with prefabricated insert**

**"Plantar heel pain with calcaneal spur — initial?"** → **Achilles stretching exercises**\
✗ Wrong options: spur resection

**"Highest satisfaction at 8 weeks?"** → **plantar fascia–specific stretching program**

**"Chronic PF, failed stretching — which orthosis?"** → **dorsiflexion night splint**

**"Orthosis proven in chronic PF?"** → **night splint**

**"Improved 40% then plateaued at 6 weeks?"** → **night splints**

**"ESWT indicated when?"** → **> 6 months failed nonop** (FDA)

**"Fasciotomy — how much to release?"** → **medial third** (avoid arch collapse)

**"PF + tarsal tunnel compression — surgery?"** → **open plantar fascia release + distal tarsal tunnel decompression**

**"Steroid injection — mechanism / efficacy?"** → **inhibits heel pad fibroblast proliferation**

**"Pop after steroid shot in marathoner — exam / diagnosis?"** → **pes planus, plantar fascia rupture**

**"First line for plantar fascia rupture?"** → **cast immobilisation for 3–6 weeks**

# Poliomyelitis

*Foot & Ankle · 40 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Post-polio syndrome & epidemiology

**Pathology** — viral destruction of **anterior horn cells** (spinal cord) + **brain stem motor nuclei** · **dorsal columns spared** · up to **95% of anterior horn cells** may be infected in acute stage (TO)

**Virus** — **enterovirus (poliovirus)** · **faecal–oral** spread

**Household contacts** — **seroconversion > 90% (90–100%)** · 4–7 secondary infections per case

**Outcome of infection** — **\~75% asymptomatic** / mild flu-like · **paralysis in \~1%** · respiratory failure **5–15%** of paralytic cases

**Age at risk** — **younger children (< 5 yr)**\
✗ Wrong options: older > 5 yr

**Course** — **transient infection** → **lifelong motor dysfunction** in most paralytic cases\
✗ Wrong options: paralysis from active / chronic infection

**Eradicated in the USA** (vaccine) · late sequelae common in **developing countries** (TO)

## 3 Presentation

### Acute polio presentation

**Acute polio (TO)** — **flaccid paralysis, DTR absent, muscle atrophy** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: spastic paralysis · DTR ++

**Probable case definition (TO)** — **acute flaccid paralysis of ≥ 1 limb** + **↓ / absent tendon reflexes** · no other cause · **no sensory or cognitive loss** (TO)

**Acute stage (TO)** — lasts **7–10 days** · malaise → encephalomyelitis · **asymmetrical paralysis** (TO)

**Younger children (TO)** — listlessness, sore throat, low fever → recurrence with hyperaesthesia / paraesthesia, headache, vomiting, **nuchal rigidity**, back pain, **limited SLR** (TO)

**Older children + adults (TO)** — low fever, **flushing**, apprehension, **tender muscles** · **superficial reflexes lost first**, **DTRs disappear when muscle group paralysed** (TO)

**Paralytic polio** — **proximal, asymmetric weakness**, back pain + stiffness, **no sensory deficit** (after diarrhoeal / flu-like illness)

**Hallmark (Orthobullets)** — **muscle weakness with normal sensation**

### Polio foot deformities

**Causes (TO)** — **muscle imbalance** · abnormal posture · gravity · walking on the deformed foot (TO)

**Tibialis anterior (TO)** — **one of the most frequently completely paralysed muscles** → **foot drop → fixed equinus** (TO)

**Commonest deformity** — **equinocavovarus** (OB) · TA weakness (↓ dorsiflexion), **cavus**, **claw toes**, **equinus**

**Range (TO)** — equinus · calcaneus · hindfoot varus / valgus · pes cavus / planus · combinations (TO)

**Neglected deformity effects (TO)** — abnormal gait · pressure areas + **callosities** · ↑ energy expenditure · arthritis (TO)

![Polio equinocavovarus foot — clinical and lateral X-ray](../../MCQ/attachments/q3844-illa-1-s2-0-s126877310800091x-gr1.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – post-polio syndrome (TO)

| Criterion (TO) | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Prior paralytic polio** | Motor neuron loss — history, residual weakness + atrophy, **EMG denervation** |
| **Recovery + stable interval** | Partial / complete recovery → **stable function usually ≥ 15 yr** |
| **New weakness** | **Gradual** (rarely abrupt), progressive, persistent weakness or **↓ endurance** ± fatigue, atrophy, muscle / joint pain · may follow trauma, surgery, inactivity |
| **Duration** | Symptoms **≥ 1 year** |
| **Exclusion** | Other neuromuscular, medical, orthopaedic causes |

## 5 Management

```
Polio foot deformity — goal: painless, stable, plantigrade foot
├─ First → ORTHOTICS, physio, stretching, bracing
├─ Flexible, failed nonop → SOFT TISSUE: release, lengthening, tendon transfer
└─ Fixed / rigid → BONY: osteotomy, wedge resection, arthrodesis
       └─ Adult, flail (TA + TP grade 2), rigid equinus → PANTALAR ARTHRODESIS
```

### Operative criteria

| Factor | Rule (TO unless stated) |
|---|---|
| **Flexible deformity** | **Soft-tissue procedures** — release, lengthening, **tendon transfer** |
| **Fixed / rigid deformity** | **Osteotomy + arthrodesis**; wedge resection **extra-articular** where possible |
| **Arthrodesis timing** | **Reserved until skeletal maturity** |
| **Tendon transfer** | Needs **supple joints** + donor power **grade IV–V (normal / near normal)** · document **manual muscle testing** first |
| **Younger child** | Release / lengthen contracted tendons · **older child → bony surgery** |
| **Orthoses fail to give satisfactory standard of living** (Orthobullets) | **Contracture release, tendon transfer, arthrodesis** |

**Lightweight orthoses** — **first line** for polio foot deformity · keep patient functionally independent

### Equinocavovarus correction

**Rigid equinus, 18 yr, TA + TP grade 2 (TO)** — **pantalar arthrodesis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: tendon transfer (rigid + weak donors) · ankle arthrodesis alone · triple (second best)

**Pantalar vs triple (TO)** — pantalar = **triple + tibiotalar arthrodesis** → better stability · triple without muscle balance → **late secondary ankle deformity** (unbalanced forces on mobile joint proximal to fusion) · **pantalar > triple** (TO)

**Flexible equinocavovarus, failed nonop** — **Achilles tendon lengthening + tibialis posterior tendon transfer (to dorsum) + plantar fascia release** · **FDL → EDL** for claw toes

| Component | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Equinus** | **Achilles lengthening** |
| **↓ Dorsiflexion (TA weak)** | **Tibialis posterior transfer to dorsum** |
| **Cavus** | **Plantar fascia release** |
| **Claw toes** | **FDL → EDL transfer** |

**✗ Flexible-foot traps** — **triple arthrodesis** / subtalar arthrodesis + medial cuneiform osteotomy (for **fixed** deformity) · **medializing calcaneal osteotomy + FDL transfer** (tibialis posterior insufficiency) · **lateralizing calcaneal osteotomy + tibialis anterior transfer** (TA usually weak)

![Polio foot deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7048-page8-1-moved.jpg)

### Post-polio syndrome – management

**Nonop (first line)** — **limited exercise with rest periods** ± lightweight orthosis · **exercise to sub-exhaustion levels** → tone muscle without breakdown · maintain, don't overuse

**Operative** — tendon transfers, contracture releases, arthrodesis to optimise function

**Drugs (TO)** — **no effective treatment** · lamotrigine, **IVIg** some success · analgesics / antidepressants (amitriptyline, duloxetine, codeine) — may worsen fatigue (TO)

**Team (TO)** — physio, OT, speech, respiratory, podiatry, psychology, dietetics, pain, orthotics (TO)

## 6 Complications

### Post-polio syndrome (PPS)

**Nature** — **aging phenomenon**: more nerve cells inactive with time · **NOT viral reactivation** / chronic infection

**Incidence** — **up to 50%** of polio cases (Orthobullets, OB) · **20–85%** depending on criteria (TO) — read the options

**Onset** — **≥ 15 yr after initial infection** (TO) · **20–40 yr after** (OB) · **after middle age** (Orthobullets) · **not 5–10 yr after paralytic polio** (TO)

**Severity (TO)** — **more severe after acute / severe initial disease**; minimal initial symptoms → mild PPS (TO)

**Risk factors (TO)** — **female sex** + **respiratory symptoms** = most important · permanent disability, severe residual weakness, aging, muscle overuse / disuse, immune reaction (TO)

**Motor features** — **new weakness**, ↓ endurance, atrophy, **myalgia**, fasciculations, **fatigue**, cold intolerance, dysarthria, **dysphagia**, respiratory compromise · ↑ difficulty with ADLs

**Sensory symptoms (TO)** — **frank sensory deficits + paraesthesias occur in PPS** (sensory evoked potential changes ↔ cord atrophy on MRI) · contrast: acute polio = **normal sensation** (Orthobullets) (TO)

**Cognitive (TO)** — word-finding difficulty, poor concentration, memory impairment, mood change → social isolation (TO)

**Counselling a recovered child** — **may experience muscle weakness and fatigue as an adult**\
✗ Wrong options: no risk · safe after skeletal maturity · certain paralysis + avoid sport · wheelchair by 25

## X vs Y

| | **Acute polio** | **Post-polio syndrome** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | Active poliovirus infection | **Aging of depleted motor units** (✗ Wrong options: reactivation) |
| Timing | 7–10 day acute stage | **≥ 15 yr** (TO) / **20–40 yr** (OB) later |
| Weakness | **Flaccid, asymmetric, proximal** | New, gradual, previously affected muscles |
| Reflexes | **Absent DTRs** | — |
| Sensation | **Normal** | **Sensory symptoms possible** (TO) |

| | **Flexible deformity** | **Fixed deformity** |
|---|---|---|
| Surgery | **Soft tissue** — TAL, PT transfer, plantar fascia release | **Bony** — osteotomy, **triple / pantalar arthrodesis** |
| Age | Younger | Older child / **after maturity** |

## How it's asked

**"Post-polio syndrome — all true EXCEPT?"** → **seen 5–10 years after the onset of paralytic polio** (TO)

**"Recovered child from Liberia — counselling?"** → **may experience muscle weakness and fatigue as an adult**

**"Enterovirus flaccid paralysis — true?"** → **seroconversion rate > 90% of household contacts**

**"Rigid equinus, 18 yr, TA + TP grade 2 — best treatment?"** → **pantalar arthrodesis** (TO)

**"Flexible equinocavovarus in polio, failed nonop?"** → **Achilles tendon lengthening, tibialis posterior tendon transfer, plantar fascia release**

**"Acute poliomyelitis presentation?"** → **flaccid paralysis, DTR absent, muscle atrophy** (TO)

**"Polio involves which cells?"** → **anterior horn cells + brain stem motor nuclei**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal column

**"Exercise advice in PPS?"** → **sub-exhaustion levels**

# Posterior Tibial Tendon Insufficiency (PTTI)

*Foot & Ankle · 41 / 48*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Commonest cause** — **adult-acquired flatfoot deformity (AAFD)** · renamed **progressive collapsing foot deformity (PCFD)** (Myerson consensus) = hindfoot valgus + forefoot abduction + midfoot varus

**Who** — **women**, **6th decade** (50s–60s), obese, middle-aged

**Risk factors** — **obesity**, **hypertension**, **diabetes**, ↑ age, **corticosteroid use** (incl. repeated injections), **seronegative inflammatory** disorders

**Associated conditions** — **inflammatory arthropathy** · **tarsal coalition** (young, rigid pes planus ± recurrent ankle sprains) · acute onset after **pronation–external rotation** ankle fracture

### Anatomy & biomechanics of the PTT

**Muscle** — origin **posterior fibula, tibia + interosseous membrane** · **tibial nerve (L4–5)**

**Course** — **posterior to medial malleolus** → divides into **3 limbs**

| Limb | Insertion |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** | **Navicular tuberosity + 1st (medial) cuneiform** |
| **Middle** | **2nd + 3rd cuneiforms, cuboid, MT 2–4** |
| **Posterior** | **Sustentaculum tali** (anteriorly) |

**Blood supply** — **posterior tibial artery branches** distally (Orthobullets) · branches of sural, peroneal + posterior tibial arteries (OB)

**Watershed** — **2–6 cm proximal to navicular insertion** (navicular → distal medial malleolus) = main site of **synovitis + rupture**

**Excursion** — **2 cm** (normal)

**Axis** — **posterior to tibiotalar** + **medial to subtalar** joint axis

**Functions** — **primary dynamic arch support** · **hindfoot invertor** · **adducts + supinates forefoot** in stance · **secondary ankle plantarflexor** · with FDL + FHL dynamically restrains arch

**Primary antagonist** — **peroneus brevis** (evertor)\
✗ Wrong options: peroneus longus (antagonist of tibialis anterior) · tibialis anterior (antagonist of PL) · gastroc-soleus (antagonised by ankle dorsiflexors) · EHL (antagonised by FHL)

**Locking mechanism (TO)** — midstance: **TP initiates subtalar inversion → locks the transverse tarsal (TN + CC) joints** → **rigid lever arm for toe-off** · adducts + plantarflexes navicular on talar head → buttresses medial arch (TO)

**Dysfunction (TO)** — **failure of inversion of the hindfoot that locks the transverse tarsal joints** → heel rise difficult, **heel remains in valgus** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: eversion · "unlocks"

**Calf fatigue** — unlocked transverse tarsal joint → no rigid lever → ↓ triceps surae mechanical advantage → **medial + distal calf pain, fatigue** → tendinopathy primarily affects the **transverse tarsal joint**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st TMT (dorsiflexed, not the cause) · subtalar (altered, no extra triceps work) · Lisfranc (unaffected) · tibiotalar (stage IV only)

![PTT plantar insertions with spring, long and short plantar ligaments and peroneus longus](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7020-shows-the-insertions-of-the-posterior-tibial-tendon.jpg)

### Spring ligament & related anatomy

**Spring ligament complex** — **superomedial calcaneonavicular (CNL)** + **inferior calcaneonavicular** ligaments · **anterior sustentaculum tali → plantar-medial navicular** · supports **plantar-medial talar head** · **primary static stabiliser of the TN joint**

**Torn / attenuated in AAFD** — **superomedial calcaneonavicular** (most often; 18 / 22 PTT ruptures had spring injury)\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal cuneonavicular · dorsal intercuneiform · dorsal calcaneocuboid · anterior tibiofibular

**Deltoid link** — distal (superficial) deltoid **blends with superomedial spring ligament** = **tibiocalcaneonavicular ligament (TCNL)** · also attenuated · deltoid-spring reconstruction for advanced medial peritalar instability

**Static arch stabilisers** — **spring ligament complex**, **plantar fascia**, **plantar ligaments** (long + short) · TO: long + short plantar, spring + bifurcate ligaments kept tight by TP tension → lost tension → plantar ligament failure, esp. **calcaneonavicular** (TO)

**Failure** — **plantar + medial migration of the talar head** → arch flattens → **loss of medial peritalar stability**

**Medial labelled figure** — **A = spring (superomedial CN) ligament** · B = joint capsule · C = plantar ligaments · D = plantar fascia · E = tibialis posterior tendon

**Plantar labelled figure** — **structure D = spring (plantar calcaneonavicular) ligament** · A = peroneus longus · B = long plantar ligament · C = short plantar ligament · E = tibialis anterior

![Spring ligament complex, medial view](../../MCQ/attachments/q212155-illa-sping-labeled.jpg)

**✗ Ligament–condition traps** — Lisfranc injury = **Lisfranc ligament** (plantar-lateral medial cuneiform → plantar-medial 2nd MT base) · peroneal dislocation = **SPR** · pes cavus = muscle imbalance · 2nd toe crossover = **plantar plate + collateral ligament**

## 2 Mechanism

**Aetiology** — exact cause **unknown** · acute injury vs **long-standing tendon degeneration**

**Sequence** — **tenosynovitis → tendinosis / elongation → loss of dynamic arch stabilisation → attritional failure of static stabilisers** (spring ligament, plantar fascia, plantar ligaments) → fixed degenerative joint changes

**Structure order** — **PTT → spring + talocalcaneal ligaments → deltoid** (last = stage IV)

**Deformity** — **pes planus** · **hindfoot valgus** · **forefoot abduction** · **forefoot varus / supination** (compensatory → keeps foot plantigrade)

**Equinus cycle** — hindfoot valgus → **Achilles lateral to subtalar axis → acts as evertor** · valgus shortens gastrocnemius → **fixed equinus, Achilles / gastroc contracture** → worse arch collapse → worse equinus

**Stage IV** — **deltoid incompetence → valgus talar tilt in mortise** → tibiotalar arthritis

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis & examination (too-many-toes, single-limb heel rise, equinus)

**Early** — **medial ankle / foot pain + weakness** · tenderness + swelling **just posterior to tip of medial malleolus** / along PTT · ↓ endurance

**Progressive** — **loss of arch** ("lost her arch", needs shoe inserts), foot "looks wider"

**Late** — **lateral (sinus tarsi) pain from subfibular impingement** (calcaneus / talus against fibula tip)

| Present in AAFD | Not a feature |
|---|---|
| **Hindfoot valgus** | Hindfoot varus |
| **Forefoot abduction** ("too many toes") | Forefoot adduction |
| **Forefoot varus / supination** (compensatory) | Forefoot valgus / pronation (cavovarus) |
| **Pes planus** | Cavus, plantarflexed 1st ray (cavovarus compensation) |
| **Equinus / Achilles / gastrocnemius contracture** | Claw toes, hallux varus, plantar fasciitis |
| — | **Hallux valgus** (no ↑ rate in PTTI) |

**Single-limb heel rise** — **hallmark** · patient **unable to raise the heel off the ground and stand on the toes while supported on one leg** · **stages II–IV can't** · stage I can (TO: grossly normal single raise but **fails repeated / successive raises**)

**Heel on double-leg heel rise** — normal / flexible: heel **inverts**, arch **reconstitutes** (stage II) · rigid: heel **stays valgus** (stage III)

**Too-many-toes sign** — viewed from behind · **forefoot abduction** · **> 40% TN uncoverage** (stage IIB)

![Too-many-toes sign: forefoot abduction seen from behind](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7020-forefoot-abduction.jpg)

**PTT power** — foot **plantarflexed + fully inverted** → can't hold against examiner's eversion force · pain with resisted inversion

**Forefoot varus check** — hold flexible heel **neutral** → fixed varus = **lateral border more plantarflexed than medial** · flexible = MT heads perpendicular to long axis of tibia + calcaneus

**Flexible vs rigid** — flexible = passively correctable to plantigrade (**stage II**) · rigid = not correctable (**III, IV**) → **decides joint-sparing vs fusion**

**Equinus (must be evaluated + addressed at surgery)** — **Silfverskiöld** with hindfoot / TN held **neutral**: **increased ankle dorsiflexion with knee flexion = gastrocnemius contracture** · no change = gastroc + soleus (Achilles) · TO: equinus = **< 10° DF with knee extended** (TO)

**✗ Exam traps** — anterior drawer in 20° PF = **ATFL** · **Coleman block** = cavovarus (correction = flexible, forefoot-driven cavovarus) · **piano-key** = Lisfranc · **Thompson** = Achilles rupture · inability to flex hallux = **FHL**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Views** | **WB AP + lateral foot**, **ankle mortise** |
| **AP foot** | **↑ TN uncoverage** (TN coverage angle normal **< 7°**) · **↑ AP talo-1st MT angle (Simmon)** — stages II–IV |
| **WB lateral** | **↑ lateral talo-1st MT angle (Meary) > 4° = pes planus** (II–IV) · **↓ calcaneal pitch** (normal **17–32°**) · **↓ medial cuneiform–floor height** · **dorsal navicular subluxation on talus** |
| Lateral (TO) | **Midfoot break at talo-MT angle**, abnormal talocalcaneal angle, **↓ cuneiform height** (stage 2) (TO) |
| Hindfoot | **Subtalar arthritis** — stages **III–IV** |
| **Mortise** | **Talar tilt (valgus)** = **deltoid insufficiency** — **stage IV** |
| **US** | Stage 1 / 2a **tenosynovitis — target sign** · degenerating tendon **hyper- → hypo-echoic** · stage 2b **empty groove sign** = ruptured tendon gap (TO) · ↑ role |
| **MRI** | Tendinosis, tenosynovitis, tearing · **elongated, thinned tendon** (TO) · TN, subtalar, tibiotalar arthritis |

![Talonavicular coverage angle: normal under 7°, flatfoot 33°](../../MCQ/attachments/q5661-illa-talonavicular-coverage.jpg)

**Meary in stems** — 8°, 12°, 30° = pes planus · collapse sometimes reported as **"negative" Meary angle**

### Diagnostic criteria

| Finding | Threshold / meaning |
|---|---|
| Pes planus | **Meary (lateral talo-1st MT) > 4°** |
| Calcaneal pitch | Normal **17–32°** → ↓ = loss of arch height |
| **Stage IIB / LCL** | **TN uncoverage > 40%** (Orthobullets, OB, Thordarson consensus) · **> 30%** (OB; Myerson IIa < 30% vs IIb > 30%) · "> 40% or > 30°" (OB) — read the options |
| Gastroc contracture | **DF ↑ with knee flexion** (Silfverskiöld +) |
| Equinus (TO) | **< 10° DF with knee extended** |
| Stage I vs II | **Can vs can't single-leg heel rise** |
| Stage II vs III | **Flexible vs rigid** hindfoot · rigid = stage III **irrespective of arthritis on X-ray** |
| Stage III vs IV | **Deltoid compromise / talar tilt / ankle arthritis** |
| 1st TMT instability | **Plantar gapping medial cuneiform–1st MT base** |

### Differential

**Midfoot-driven flatfoot** — midfoot OA / **chronic Lisfranc**; apex at **naviculocuneiform** = **cuneiform subluxes dorsally, TN alignment maintained** (vs PTTI = navicular subluxes dorsally on talus) → **midfoot fusion + realignment**

**Isolated spring ligament incompetence (no PTT pathology)** — standard flatfoot reconstruction **+ adjunctive spring ligament reconstruction**

**Tarsal coalition** — young, rigid pes planus, recurrent sprains

**Adolescent flexible flatfoot** — ligamentous laxity → **conservative**

### Staging (Johnson & Strom, Myerson modification)

**Johnson and Strom classification** — for **tibialis posterior** (TO) · stage IV added later (Myerson)\
✗ Wrong options: tibialis anterior · peroneus longus · FHL

| Stage | Deformity | Exam | X-ray | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Tenosynovitis, no deformity** | **(+) single heel raise** | Normal | **Cast / boot 3–4 mo** → tenosynovectomy |
| **IIA** | **Flexible** hindfoot valgus, **normal forefoot** | **(−) single-leg heel raise**, mild sinus tarsi pain | Arch collapse | **FDL + MDCO** ± TAL / gastroc |
| **IIB** | Flexible + **flexible forefoot abduction** (too many toes, **> 40% TN uncoverage**) | (−) heel raise, mild sinus tarsi pain | Arch collapse | **+ lateral column lengthening** |
| **IIC** | Flexible hindfoot + **rigid forefoot abduction / varus** | (−) heel raise, **moderate** sinus tarsi pain | Arch collapse, **gapping of 1st TMT** | **+ Cotton** or **medial column fusion** |
| **III** | **Rigid hindfoot valgus**, rigid forefoot abduction | (−) heel raise, **severe sinus tarsi pain** | **Subtalar arthritis** | **Triple (hindfoot) arthrodesis** |
| **IV** | III + **deltoid ligament compromise** | + **ankle pain** | **Talar tilt in ankle mortise** | Correctable → **triple + deltoid recon** · rigid + arthritis → **TTC** |

### Johnson & Strom stages (TO)

| Stage | TO description |
|---|---|
| **1** | Tenosynovitis, **no deformity** · tendon pain, normal function, **able to repeat tiptoes** |
| **2** | **Flexible deformity** · tendon rupture, ↓ strength, **unable to repeat tiptoes** |
| **3** | **Rigid flat foot**, valgus heel, **loss of subtalar motion**, forefoot varus, absent heel raise, **anterolateral impingement**, secondary OA |
| **4** | **Ankle joint involvement — tibiotalar degeneration** |

**Stage 1 (TO)** — medial pain, ↓ endurance · erythema, swelling, warmth, tenderness along TP · arch + alignment normal (TO)

**Stage 2 (TO)** — inflammatory signs + **flexible hindfoot valgus** · flattened arch **returns on single-leg raise** · forefoot **abducted + supinated** · yet usually **can't do an unsupported single heel rise** (TO) — Orthobullets: (−) single-leg heel raise — read the options

**Stage 3–4 (TO)** — **subtalar arthropathy**, deformity fixed · stage 4 = **tibiotalar degeneration** · X-rays diagnostic · treatment = **arthrodesis** (TO)

### Four-stage AAFD with surgical treatment (Myerson)

| Stage | Deformity | Surgery |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | No AAFD deformity (may have pre-existing flatfoot) | **Tenosynovectomy**, possible tendon transfer and / or medial slide osteotomy |
| **IIa** | Mild / moderate flexible, **< 30% TN uncoverage** | Tendon transfer + medial slide osteotomy ± **Cotton** |
| **IIb** | Severe flexible, abduction through TN, **> 30% TN uncoverage** | Transfer + medial slide ± **LCL** or hindfoot fusion (subtalar or TN + CC) · Cotton / TMT fusion to lower 1st ray |
| **III** | **Fixed** (triple-joint complex) | **Hindfoot fusion, usually triple** — all three joints |
| **IV** | Foot deformity + **lateral talar tilt** | Correct foot ± **deltoid reconstruction** · severe arthritis → **ankle fusion or TAA** + foot correction |
| IVa / IVb | Flexible / fixed foot | Foot corrected as **IIb** / as **III** |

### Bluman classification (detailed Myerson)

| Stage | Hallmark | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I A / B / C** | Normal anatomy, PTT tenderness (A ± systemic disease · C slight HF valgus) | Immobilisation, NSAIDs, cryotherapy, orthoses, tenosynovectomy |
| **II A1** | Supple HF valgus, **flexible forefoot varus** | Orthoses · MDCO · **TAL / Strayer + FDL** if deformity corrects only with ankle PF |
| **II A2** | Supple HF valgus, **fixed forefoot varus** | MDCO + FDL + **Cotton** |
| **II B** | **Forefoot abduction**, TN uncovering | MDCO + FDL + **LCL** |
| **II C** | **Fixed forefoot varus + medial column instability**, **1st TMT plantar gapping**, 1st ray dorsiflexes with HF correction | MDCO + FDL + **Cotton or medial column fusion** |
| **III A** | Rigid HF valgus, sinus tarsi pain, subtalar space loss, angle of Gissane sclerosis | Custom bracing if not surgical · **triple** |
| **III B** | + forefoot abduction | **Triple ± LCL** |
| **IV A** | **Supple tibiotalar valgus** | Correct HF + **deltoid reconstruction** |
| **IV B** | **Rigid tibiotalar valgus** | **TTC or pantalar fusion** |

### RAM classification (rearfoot · ankle · midfoot)

| Stage | Rearfoot | Ankle | Midfoot |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ia** | PTT tenosynovitis | Neutral | Neutral |
| **Ib** | PTT tendonitis, no deformity | Mild valgus **< 5°** | Mild flexible supination |
| **IIa** | Flexible planovalgus: **< 40% talar uncoverage, < 30° Meary, incongruency 20–45°** | Valgus + deltoid insufficiency, no arthritis | Supination, no instability |
| **IIb** | Flexible: **> 40%, > 30° Meary, incongruency > 45°** | Valgus + deltoid insufficiency + **tibiotalar arthritis** | Supination + **midfoot instability**, no arthritis |
| **IIIa** | Fixed / arthritic, < 40% · < 30° | Valgus from **lateral plafond bone loss**, deltoid normal | Arthritis **isolated to medial column** (NC or 1st TMT) |
| **IIIb** | Fixed, > 40% · > 30° — **not correctable through triple** | Plafond bone loss **+ deltoid insufficiency** | **Medial + middle column** arthritis |

## 5 Management

```
PTTI — nonoperative first at every stage
├─ I tenosynovitis, heel rise OK → walking cast / boot 3–4 mo
│      └─ fails → tenosynovectomy / PTT debridement
├─ II flexible → UCBL / AFO with medial post
│      └─ fails → FDL transfer + MDCO (+ adjuncts)
├─ III rigid hindfoot ± subtalar arthritis → triple arthrodesis
└─ IV deltoid failure, talar tilt → decide by ankle
       ├─ ankle valgus correctable → triple + deltoid reconstruction
       └─ rigid valgus + tibiotalar arthritis → TTC / pantalar
```

```
Stage II reconstruction — base FDL → navicular + MDCO
├─ Forefoot abduction, TN uncoverage > 40% (IIB) → + lateral column lengthening
├─ Residual forefoot varus, stable medial column → + Cotton osteotomy
├─ 1st TMT plantar gapping / hypermobility / arthritis → + 1st TMT fusion (Lapidus)
├─ DF ↑ with knee flexion (gastroc only) → + gastrocnemius recession
└─ No change with knee flexion (gastroc + soleus) → + TAL
```

```
Rigid flatfoot (stage III–IV)
├─ Unfit / declines surgery → custom AFO (articulated if ankle normal + active)
├─ Hindfoot arthritis, CC joint spared → TN + subtalar arthrodesis (medial)
├─ Hindfoot + CC involved → triple arthrodesis
│      └─ forefoot varus unmasked → derotate through transverse tarsal joints
└─ + ankle arthritis / rigid tilt → TTC (hindfoot nail) or pantalar
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Contraindication |
|---|---|---|
| **Tenosynovectomy / PTT debridement** | **Stage I after failed immobilisation** | Stage II+ alone (no deformity correction) |
| **FDL transfer → navicular** | **Stage II** (FDL synergistic with PT) | **Subtalar rigidity (< 15° motion)** · **fixed forefoot varus > 10–12°** |
| **Stage II reconstruction** | Failed nonop, flexible | **Hypermobility** · **neuromuscular** · **severe subtalar arthritis** · obesity (relative) · **age > 60–70** (relative) |
| **MDCO** | **Flexible hindfoot valgus** (IIA; all stage II) | Rigid valgus |
| **Lateral column lengthening (Evans)** | **Forefoot abduction / TN uncoverage > 40%** (IIB) | Stage IIA · normal calcaneal pitch or mild valgus (Chi) |
| **Cotton** | **Residual forefoot varus / supination, stable medial column** | Medial column unstable / arthritic |
| **1st TMT (Lapidus) / NC fusion** | **Stage II with 1st TMT hypermobility, instability or arthritis** · NC sag | — |
| **Isolated subtalar arthrodesis** | **No fixed forefoot deformity** | **Fixed forefoot supination / varus** (→ lateral border overload) · hypermobility |
| **Triple / hindfoot arthrodesis** | **Stage III** · **stage II with severe subtalar arthritis** | Ankle valgus / arthritis not addressed |
| **Triple + TAL + deltoid reconstruction** | **Stage IV, passively correctable ankle valgus** | Ankle arthritis |
| **TTC arthrodesis** | **Stage IV: rigid hindfoot, valgus talus, tibiotalar + subtalar arthritis** | — |

### Nonoperative care – orthoses (UCBL, AFO, medial posting)

**Stage I** — **immobilisation in walking cast / boot 3–4 months = first line** → custom-molded in-shoe orthosis after

**Stage I (TO)** — immobilisation, ice, NSAID, rest · **CAM walker boot → less muscle atrophy than plaster** · orthotics eliminate forefoot pronation, rest TP · PT once inflammation settles · recalcitrant → **arthroscopic or open debridement + synovectomy** (TO)

**AFO indications** — **initial treatment for stage II, III + IV** · non-operative candidates (**sedentary / low demand, age > 60–70**, comorbid)

| Orthosis | Key facts |
|---|---|
| **Custom in-shoe orthosis** | Stage I after immobilisation, stage II · **medial heel lift + longitudinal arch support** · **+ medial forefoot post if fixed forefoot varus / supination** |
| **UCBL with medial posting** | Plastic over cast in **maximal manual correction** · **flexible** deformity · first-line orthotic for stage II · not for rigid → uncomfortable, **skin breakdown** |
| **AFO family** (Arizona, molded, articulating) | **Most effective** · **acquired flexible flatfoot deformity** · medial post supports valgus collapse |
| **Short articulated AFO** | **Rigid flatfoot (stage III), active patient, normal stable ankle** → supports deformity, **keeps ankle motion** |
| **Arizona brace** | **Molded leather lace-up gauntlet** (UCBL + laced ankle support) → stabilises tibiotalar, hindfoot + arch · **significantly limits ankle motion** · TO: custom rigid AFO above malleoli accommodates rigid flatfoot (TO) |
| **Non-articulated AFO** | **Rigid planovalgus** (OB) · **stage IV** — deltoid incompetence + talar tilt (Elftman; stage III → articulated AFO) — read the options |
| **Custom-molded AFO** | Advanced rigid AAFD, **not an operative candidate** (fitted in situ) |
| **Rocker-bottom shoe** | **No role in PTTI** (OB — ankle / subtalar arthritis, calcaneal ulcers) · palliative for rigid flatfoot (TO) — read the options |
| PTB brace (TO) | Offloads foot in severe rigid flatfoot (TO) |

**Medial post** — **promotes hindfoot inversion** · elevates medial heel → ↓ lateral hindfoot impingement pressure · fits any shoe\
✗ Wrong options: medial post for eversion · lateral post for inversion · lateral post for forefoot varus / adduction

**Rigid flatfoot nonop (TO)** — palliative only, accommodates deformity · activity modification, NSAIDs, **steroid injection into subtalar / TN joint** · for elderly, low-demand, medically unfit or surgery-averse (TO)

**Best nonop responders** — **elderly patient with a sedentary lifestyle**\
✗ Wrong options: young active · inflammatory systemic disorder · balance / ambulation problems: **Parkinson's**, severe ipsilateral **hip / knee arthritis**

**Outcomes** — AFO + PT: **83% improved at 4 mo, 89% satisfied** (Alvarez) · molded AFO (rigid) / UCBL (flexible): **67%** good–excellent (Chao) · **~90%** satisfaction nonop stage II (Deland) · eccentric exercise group improved most (Kulig)

**✗ Orthotic traps** — **lateral hindfoot posting + 1st ray cutout** = cavovarus (worsens PTTI) · full-length semi-rigid insole + 1st ray depression + lateral wedge = cavovarus · **total contact cast** = insensate / Charcot · **Morton's extension** / rigid forefoot plate limiting MTP DF = hallux rigidus · wide toe box = hallux valgus · rigid sole + rocker = midfoot arthritis · pressure-dissipating insoles + accommodative laced shoes = diabetic neuropathy · AFO = mild equinovarus · **CROW** = neuropathic severe deformity

**Stage II, fixed forefoot supination, first visit** — **medial heel lift, longitudinal arch support + medial forefoot posting**\
✗ Wrong options: Lapidus + LCL + spring repair (1st TMT hypermobility, after failed orthosis) · short boot + lateral heel wedge (stage I) · triple · FDL + MDCO (after failed nonop)

**Stage II, untreated, no abduction** — **physical therapy and orthotics with medial hindfoot posting**\
✗ Wrong options: FDL + calcaneal osteotomy + Cotton + gastroc (only after failed nonop) · triple (rigid)

**Stage II, improved with 6 wk PT** — custom orthotic with **medial post + arch support** → hindfoot inversion

**Rigid stage III, avid walker, normal ankle, declines surgery** — **short articulated AFO**\
✗ Wrong options: short leg cast NWB (early tendon "rest") · CAM boot + PTT strengthening + Achilles stretch (immobilises normal ankle) · UCBL + medial heel wedge (stage I–II flexible) · Arizona (limits ankle motion, affects gait)

### Flexible deformity – FDL transfer, calcaneal osteotomy, lateral column lengthening, Cotton, gastrocnemius recession

**Classic stage II** — **medial displacement calcaneal osteotomy + posterior tibial augmentation with flexor digitorum longus tendon transfer** to navicular\
✗ Wrong options: PTT debridement alone · triple · ankle fusion · Lapidus

**Why MDCO** — corrects hindfoot valgus (**static**) · **shifts Achilles pull medially** · **offloads / protects the transferred FDL** · moves heel WB axis toward tibial axis (TO) · **transfer alone → early failure**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated FDL transfer

**FDL (dynamic)** — **synergistic with PT** → augments a deficient PT · found at **knot of Henry** · inserted into **navicular** near PT insertion

**Knot of Henry** — at ankle FDL anterior to FHL ("Tom, Dick and Harry") · **in the midfoot FHL crosses dorsal (deep) to FDL** → structure immediately deep to FDL at harvest = **flexor hallucis longus**\
✗ Wrong options: Achilles (not in exposure) · FHB (anterolateral to FHL) · adductor hallucis (too distal) · abductor hallucis (never crosses FDL)

**FDL vs FHL** — **FDL is standard** (Orthobullets) · FHL harder to mobilise, no better results · FHL + MDCO: arch not reconstituted, good satisfaction (OB) · **TO answer = FHL transfer** (FDL not offered) — read the options

**Stage II (TO)** — bilateral planovalgus, heel valgus **passively correctable non-WB**, can't single heel rise → **medial displacement calcaneal osteotomy, tibialis posterior tendon debridement and FHL transfer** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: posterior tibial tenotomy + lengthening · PTT debridement + medial arch support orthotics · subtalar arthrodesis + MDCO + FHL (no arthritis)

**Outcome** — FDL + MDCO: **87% arch reconstitution, 84% normal shoes without orthotics** (Myerson)

**IIA (flexible, no forefoot abduction)** — **medial calcaneus displacement osteotomy + FDL transfer to navicular** alone\
✗ Wrong options: lateral calcaneal displacement (worsens valgus) · peroneus longus → brevis (cavovarus) · **split PTT transfer** (spastic equinovarus)

**IIB (too many toes)** — **+ lateral column lengthening (Evans)**: opening wedge of **anterior calcaneus** or through **CC joint** · **LCL corrects forefoot abduction** · aims: adduct TN joint, plantarflex midfoot, derotate calcaneus out of valgus · may need MDCO too\
✗ Wrong options: hindfoot valgus (MDCO) · hindfoot varus, forefoot adduction, cavus (not PTTI features)

**LCL titration** — overlengthening → correct with 1st TMT fusion or medial cuneiform osteotomy · **overcorrection (> 5–10 mm) → lateral column overload**

**IIB package** — **medial calcaneal displacement osteotomy, lateral column lengthening, FDL tendon transfer and tendoachilles lengthening** (or gastroc recession) ± **Cotton**\
✗ Wrong options: tenosynovectomy + UCBL (stage I) · **Dwyer closing wedge + 1st MT closing wedge + plantar fascia release** (cavovarus) · triple (stage III) · **lateral** calcaneal displacement

**Flexible planovalgus, failed AFO** — **lateral column lengthening, medializing calcaneal osteotomy, FDL transfer to the navicular**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated FDL · 1st ray dorsiflexion osteotomy + PL → PB (cavus) · lateralizing calcaneal osteotomy · triple

**Large talar head uncoverage, painful heel rise that corrects valgus** — **medializing calcaneal osteotomy + lateral column lengthening + flexor digitorum longus transfer to the medial navicular**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT débridement (stage I only) · FDL alone · MCO + FDL without LCL · triple (supple, no arthritis)

**Equinus not correcting with knee flexed, 50% uncoverage, no arthritis** — **stage IIB: transfer of FDL to medial navicular + MCO + lateral column lengthening + tendo-Achilles lengthening (TAL)**\
✗ Wrong options: stage I debridement · IIA (has abduction) · triple / TTC

**Gastroc-only equinus** — DF **−5° knee extended → +10° flexed** → **open gastrocnemius muscle lengthening** (recession / Strayer) · gastroc + soleus both tight → **TAL** (open Achilles lengthening for significant deformity, percutaneous) · recession = less weakness, better cosmesis, less powerful correction\
✗ Wrong options: posterior ankle capsule release (ankle can DF) · PL → PB

**No equinus** — Silfverskiöld **+5° / +5°** → no lengthening needed

**Forefoot varus / supination** — compensatory; **unmasked once hindfoot corrected** intra-op → must be corrected or residual deformity

**Cotton osteotomy** — **plantarflexion (dorsal) opening wedge medial cuneiform osteotomy** = **plantarflexion osteotomy to correct residual forefoot varus** · requires **stable medial column** (navicular colinear with 1st MT)\
✗ Wrong options: medial opening wedge for abduction · for forefoot valgus · for hindfoot valgus

**Cotton corrects** — **forefoot supination / varus**\
✗ Wrong options: forefoot abduction (LCL) · forefoot pronation (cavovarus) · hindfoot equinus (gastroc / Achilles release) · hindfoot valgus (MCO static + FDL dynamic)

**After FDL + MCO + TAL, forefoot still supinated** — **plantarflexion opening wedge medial cuneiform osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsiflexion closing wedge (worsens) · in-situ 1st–3rd TMT fusion (Lisfranc) · lateral column closing-wedge shortening · subtalar fusion (subtalar arthritis)

**Cotton vs 1st MT–cuneiform fusion** — Cotton: **predictable union, preserves 1st-ray mobility, easily varied correction**

![Cotton osteotomy: plantarflexion opening wedge of the medial cuneiform](../../MCQ/attachments/q3645-illa-cotton-osteotomy.jpg)

**Unstable medial column (plantar sag at 1st TMT and / or NC)** — **medial column fusion**: isolated 1st TMT, NC, or both

**1st TMT instability + abduction, flexible hindfoot** — **first tarsal-metatarsal arthrodesis, medializing calcaneal osteotomy, flexor digitorum longus tendon transfer and lateral column lengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: Cotton + MCO + FDL (no 1st TMT instability) · triple + Achilles lengthening (rigid) · TTC + gastroc (stage IV) · 1st TMT + subtalar fusion + PTT debridement ± LCL (IIB with severe subtalar arthritis; subtalar fusion + LCL rarely combined → CC fusion instead; PTT debridement no proven benefit)

**Modified Lapidus** — indication = **plantar gapping between medial cuneiform and first metatarsal base** (1st TMT instability) · = 1st TMT fusion + distal soft tissue (modified McBride) ± medial cuneiform osteotomy · also 1st TMT arthritis, hypermobility, neuromuscular\
✗ Wrong options: flexible hindfoot valgus · Meary 12° · TN uncoverage 50° → LCL · + Silfverskiöld → gastroc recession

**Stage IIB + gastroc tightness, no TMT instability** — **flexor digitorum longus transfer, medial calcaneal slide osteotomy, lateral column lengthening, Cotton osteotomy, gastrocnemius recession**\
✗ Wrong options: PTT synovectomy + FDL (no bony correction) · lateral calcaneal slide (varus feet) · 1st TMT arthrodesis (no instability) · triple

**Contraindication to FDL + osteotomies + LCL + Cotton** — **rigid hindfoot valgus** → arthrodesis\
✗ Wrong options: failed heel raise · forefoot varus · forefoot abduction · pes planus (all expected in stage II)

| Procedure (Chi) | Indication | Contraindication |
|---|---|---|
| **Lateral column lengthening** | TN sag, forefoot abduction, **valgus calcaneus** | **Normal calcaneal pitch or mild valgus** · can overcorrect → forefoot varus |
| **Medial column stabilisation** | NC / cuneiform–1st MT **sag**, forefoot abduction, **neutral calcaneus** | Valgus calcaneus with flat pitch |
| **Combined medial + lateral** | NC / cuneiform–1st MT sag **+ valgus calcaneus** | — |
| **Medial column + MCO** | **Valgus calcaneus with normal pitch** | — |

**MIS vs open calcaneal osteotomy** — **traditional open technique associated with higher wound complications + longer surgical time compared to MIS technique** · MIS: smaller incision, **fewer wound complications**, **↓ nerve injury**, **shorter time**, **same radiographic + patient outcomes** · radiation **↓ with MIS** (Orthobullets) · **no difference** (OB) — read the options

**Exam ↔ procedure** — **flexible forefoot abduction and hindfoot valgus deformities + gastrocnemius contracture** ↔ FDL + MCO + LCL + gastroc lengthening · flexible HF valgus + gastroc contracture ↔ MCO + gastroc lengthening · isolated PTT tenderness ↔ tenosynovectomy after failed immobilisation · rigid forefoot + hindfoot ↔ triple + gastroc · + tibiotalar arthritis ↔ tibiotalar fusion ± triple

**FDL + MCO alone** — female with pain, **inability to perform single leg heel raise + flexible hindfoot valgus deformity**, no abduction = **IIA**\
✗ Wrong options: rigid → triple · + deltoid compromise → triple + deltoid / TTC · retained heel raise → stage I, maximise nonop then debridement · + forefoot abduction → IIB, needs LCL ± spring reconstruction

### Rigid deformity – triple, hindfoot & TTC arthrodesis

**Rigid adult flatfoot causes (TO)** — **stage III / IV PTTD**, post-traumatic deformity, inflammatory arthropathy (TO)

**Surgery indication (TO)** — persistent pain + functional limit despite adequate nonop, fit + willing (TO)

**Stage III gold standard** — **triple arthrodesis (talocalcaneal, talonavicular + calcaneocuboid fusion) with appropriate structural realignments** (TO) (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: LCL (Evans) + Cotton · isolated PTT reconstruction with FDL transfer + spring repair · isolated subtalar fusion without addressing the midtarsal complex

**Triple technique (TO)** — one or two incisions (medial + lateral) · realign valgus, abducted, dorsiflexed hindfoot → **neutral / slight valgus (5°), adducted, plantigrade** · **multiple cannulated screws** · bone graft (resected surfaces / iliac crest) · **NWB 6–8 wk** → boot until union **12–16 wk** (TO) · fusion **98%** at 2 yr (Pell)

**Principles (TO)** — **plantigrade, braceable foot** > perfect angles · **correct all components** (valgus + abduction + arch; subtalar alone leaves TN subluxation) · **equinus → Strayer or percutaneous TAL** concurrently (deforming force into pronation) · **forefoot varus → Cotton** (dorsal opening wedge medial cuneiform, plantarflexes 1st ray) or **lateral border overload** (TO)

**Preferred stage III surgery** — **arthrodesis of calcaneocuboid, talonavicular and subtalar joints**\
✗ Wrong options: FDL + MDCO + TAL (flexible) · pantalar (stage IV) · FDL + LCL through anterior calcaneus · PTT debridement + tenodesis to FDL

**Forefoot derotation in triple** — after subtalar realigned, **forefoot varus unmasked** → **derotation of the forefoot through the transverse tarsal joints** (TN + CC) → plantigrade, no **lateral border foot pain** · alternatives: Cotton, 1st TMT fusion, NC fusion\
✗ Wrong options: derotation through CC alone (can't fix abduction) · dorsiflexion osteotomy medial cuneiform · isolated TN fusion (subtalar degenerate too) · no further step

**Double arthrodesis** — **talonavicular + subtalar** via **single medial incision** when **CC not substantially arthritic** (OB; medial approach: no wound complications) · TO: double = **TN + CC**, spares subtalar when subtalar arthrosis minimal + deformity at TN (TO) — read the options

**TN + subtalar case** — rigid hindfoot + TN not passively correctable, pain on resisted inversion, failed bracing → **talonavicular and subtalar arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: CROW (neuropathic) · MCO + PTT debridement ± FDL ± LCL (joint-sparing fails in rigid: no arthritic pain relief, loss of correction)

**Isolated subtalar fusion** — **stage III** with mainly subtalar symptoms / arthritis (OB) · no fixed forefoot deformity (Orthobullets) · **rarely sufficient** for rigid flatfoot — TN usually degenerate → isolated subtalar coalition or post-traumatic subtalar arthritis (TO) · obese elderly with Chopart arthritis → subtalar alone leaves **TN + CC symptomatic** → triple (OB) — read the options

**Rigid = stage III examples** — 70-year-old, **hindfoot not passively correctable** → **triple arthrodesis** (✗ Wrong options: talocalcaneal arthrodesis · FDL + calcaneal slide ± LCL through cuboid) · obese 75-year-old → **triple** (✗ Wrong options: double calcaneal osteotomy · subtalar alone) · obese 62-year-old, **plantar-medial callus = talar head**, TN dislocation, no hindfoot motion → **triple** (✗ Wrong options: exostectomy (destabilises) · LCL · MCO · TN arthrodesis)

**Heel rise test for rigidity** — **hindfoot valgus persisting with double-leg heel rise** = rigid → **triple** even with only 25% TN uncoverage\
✗ Wrong options: heel inverts to varus on heel rise = flexible IIA / IIB → reconstruction · 14-year-old flexible flatfoot → conservative

**Hindfoot arthrodesis** — **stage III** (usually triple) · **stage II with severe subtalar arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: walking cast / boot 3–4 mo (stage I) · FDL + calcaneal osteotomy + TAL ± forefoot osteotomy (stage II) · 1st TMT fusion + calcaneal osteotomy + TAL ± LCL (stage II + 1st TMT hypermobility) · TTC (no tibiotalar arthritis / tilt)

**Stage IV** — **passively correctable ankle valgus** (cartilage preserved) → **triple arthrodesis + deltoid ligament reconstruction** (+ TAL) · **rigid ankle valgus + tibiotalar arthritis** → **tibiotalocalcaneal (TTC) arthrodesis** with **retrograde hindfoot nail** · end-stage ankle arthritis → **pantalar (triple + ankle) fusion** — eliminates all hindfoot + ankle motion (TO; OB: "stage IV → pantalar")

**Stage IV, Meary 30°, ankle osteophytes, rigid subtalar + tibiotalar valgus** — **tibiotalocalcaneal arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: AFO (functionally limiting deformity) · MCO + FDL ± deltoid repair (rigid) · triple (ignores ankle)

**TTC caveat** — salvage only; **tibiotalar joint provides most DF / PF**

**Unfit for surgery** — stage IV + **severe symptomatic aortic stenosis** → **custom-molded ankle-foot orthosis (AFO)**\
✗ Wrong options: TTC · triple (stage III) · triple + deltoid (stage IV correctable) · in-shoe orthosis with medial forefoot post (stage I–II with forefoot varus)

**Midfoot (NC) flatfoot** — flexible hindfoot, full strength, collapse at NC joint on X-ray → **navicular-cuneiform arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral calcaneal slide (worsens, cavovarus) · MCO / LCL (stage II PTTI) · TN arthrodesis (part of stage III)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Cause / fact |
|---|---|
| **Lateral column overload** → **5th MT stress fracture**, lateral column stiffness | **LCL (anterior calcaneal opening wedge) for stage IIb** · overcorrection > 5–10 mm |
| CC arthritis | After LCL — **14%** radiographic, rarely symptomatic (Mosier-LaClair) |
| **Lateral border foot pain** | **Forefoot varus not corrected** after hindfoot realignment / isolated subtalar fusion with fixed forefoot varus |
| Early transfer failure | FDL transfer **without** hindfoot valgus correction |
| **Lateral plantar nerve** | **Retrograde hindfoot (TTC) nail start point** (mid plantar heel, centred on talus + tibia) → **numbness lateral plantar midfoot** → careful dissection to bone for guidewire + nail |
| **FHL impingement** | **Long lateral → medial calcaneal interlocking screw** by **sustentaculum** → ↓ hallux flexion |
| Loss of correction | Joint-sparing surgery in rigid deformity |
| Progression untreated | Flexible → rigid (peritalar arthritis) → deltoid failure → **tibiotalar valgus**, arthritis, near-total loss of ankle ROM |

### Lateral column overload & hindfoot-nail nerve injury

**5th MT stress fracture after correction of** — **stage IIb PTTI** (LCL)\
✗ Wrong options: ligamentous Lisfranc (planovalgus malreduction, PTOA) · moderate hallux valgus (iatrogenic hallux varus, transfer metatarsalgia) · stage I (tenosynovectomy) · stage IIa (normal TN coverage, no LCL)

**Numbness after retrograde hindfoot nail** — **lateral plantar midfoot** (lateral plantar nerve, branching medially from tibial nerve)\
✗ Wrong options: medial plantar midfoot (medial plantar n.) · lateral hindfoot (sural) · medial hindfoot (saphenous) · plantar hindfoot (medial calcaneal n.)

**Long distal interlocking screw by sustentaculum** — **flexor hallucis longus**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral plantar nerve (nail start point, not screw) · tibialis anterior · saphenous vein (superficial) · tibial nerve (anterior to FHL, not by sustentaculum)

## X vs Y

| | **MDCO** | **LCL (Evans)** | **Cotton** | **1st TMT fusion (Lapidus)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Corrects | **Hindfoot valgus** | **Forefoot abduction / TN uncoverage** | **Forefoot varus / supination** | Forefoot varus + **unstable / arthritic 1st TMT** |
| Typical stage | All stage II (IIA alone with FDL) | **IIB** | IIA2 · IIB · IIC (stable medial column) | IIC (unstable medial column) |
| Pitfall | Lateral slide = wrong (cavovarus) | **Lateral column overload, 5th MT stress fracture** | Dorsiflexion wedge = wrong | — |

| | **Gastrocnemius recession** | **TAL** |
|---|---|---|
| Silfverskiöld | **DF ↑ with knee flexion** | **No change** (gastroc + soleus) |
| Trade-off | Less weakness, better cosmesis, less power | More powerful |

| | **PTTI flatfoot** | **Midfoot (NC / Lisfranc) flatfoot** |
|---|---|---|
| Apex | TN — navicular dorsal on talus | NC / TMT — cuneiform dorsal, TN aligned |
| Treatment | FDL + MCO ± LCL → triple | **NC / midfoot fusion + realignment** |

| | **Flexible planovalgus orthotic** | **Cavovarus orthotic** |
|---|---|---|
| Post | **Medial hindfoot post** (± medial forefoot post) | **Lateral post + 1st ray cutout / depression** |

| | **Articulated AFO** | **Arizona / non-articulated AFO** |
|---|---|---|
| Ankle motion | **Preserved** | **Limited** |
| Use | Active patient, rigid stage III, normal ankle | Stage IV / talar tilt · rigid planovalgus |

| | **Lateral plantar nerve** | **FHL** |
|---|---|---|
| Hindfoot nail injury from | **Start point** in plantar heel | **Long lateral → medial interlocking screw** |
| Sign | Numb lateral plantar midfoot | ↓ hallux flexion |

## How it's asked

**"Heel rise difficult, heel stays in valgus — failure of?"** → **inversion of the hindfoot that locks the transverse tarsal joints** (TO)

**"Advanced PTTI — feature to evaluate + address at surgery?"** → **equinus contracture**

**"Obese 46-year-old, flatfoot X-rays — likely physical finding?"** → **Achilles tendon contracture**

**"Primary antagonist of tibialis posterior (MRI arrow)?"** → **peroneus brevis**

**"Too many toes, calf pain + fatigue — tendinopathy affects which joint?"** → **transverse tarsal joint**

**"Exam finding of disease causing attritional spring ligament failure?"** → **unable to raise the heel off the ground and stand on the toes on one leg**

**"Medial pain, needs arch inserts — exam finding?"** → **increased ankle dorsiflexion with knee flexion**

**"Can't single-limb heel rise + arch loss — other finding?"** → **hindfoot valgus**

**"Johnson and Strom classification is for which tendon?"** → **tibialis posterior** (TO)

**"Rigid valgus hindfoot, subtalar DJD, no talar tilt — stage?"** → **III**

**"Stage II (Meary 8°, too many toes) — procedures EXCEPT?"** → **deltoid ligament reconstruction**

**"Triple arthrodesis performed — stage?"** → **stage III**

**"Supple, 50% uncoverage, equinus unchanged with knee flexed — stage + treatment?"** → **stage IIB: FDL → medial navicular + MCO + LCL + TAL**

**"Isolated subtalar arthrodesis shown — stage?"** → **stage III**

**"Triple arthrodesis shown in figure — likely pre-op state?"** → **radiograph with subtalar arthritis, inability to perform single-leg heel raise, severe sinus tarsi pain**

**"Heel not reducible under tibia, > 40% uncoverage, no tilt — procedure (figure D)?"** → **double (TN + subtalar) or triple arthrodesis**

**"Superomedial calcaneonavicular ligament tear most associated with?"** → **adult-acquired flatfoot deformity**

**"Ligament attenuated in AAFD?"** → **superomedial calcaneonavicular**

**"Incompetent spring ligament on labelled medial figure?"** → **A**

**"Structure causing loss of medial peritalar stability (plantar figure)?"** → **structure D — spring ligament**

**"Stage II, fixed forefoot supination — next step?"** → **medial heel lift, longitudinal arch support + medial forefoot posting**

**"Custom AFO is most effective nonop for?"** → **acquired flexible flatfoot deformity**

**"Medial hindfoot posting treats?"** → **flexible pes planovalgus**

**"AFO / UCBL — highest satisfaction in?"** → **elderly patient with a sedentary lifestyle**

**"Sedentary 65-year-old, flexible, can't heel raise — initial?"** → **orthotic management with the UCBL (figure C)**

**"Rigid flatfoot, normal ankle, avid walker, declines surgery?"** → **short articulated ankle-foot orthosis (AFO)**

**"Custom orthotic after successful PT for stage II — feature?"** → **medial post to promote hindfoot inversion**

**"Flexible, no abduction, untreated — long-term next step?"** → **physical therapy and orthotics with medial hindfoot posting**

**"Talar tilt + subtalar arthritis, severe aortic stenosis?"** → **custom-molded ankle-foot orthosis (AFO)**

**"Correctable bilateral planovalgus, can't heel rise (FNB)?"** → **MDCO + tibialis posterior tendon debridement + FHL transfer** (TO)

**"Structure immediately deep (dorsal) to FDL in the midfoot?"** → **flexor hallucis longus (FHL)**

**"Passively correctable valgus, failed orthotics?"** → **MDCO + posterior tibial augmentation with FDL transfer**

**"Flexible valgus, abducted forefoot, equinus — surgery?"** → **medial calcaneal displacement osteotomy + LCL + FDL transfer + tendoachilles lengthening**

**"Cotton osteotomy is?"** → **plantarflexion osteotomy to correct residual forefoot varus**

**"Flexible planovalgus, failed AFO + PT — surgery?"** → **LCL + medializing calcaneal osteotomy + FDL transfer to the navicular**

**"After FDL + MCO + TAL, forefoot still supinated — next?"** → **plantarflexion opening wedge medial cuneiform osteotomy**

**"Triple for stage 3, forefoot varus after subtalar aligned — prevent lateral border pain?"** → **derotation of the forefoot through the transverse tarsal joints**

**"Flexible hindfoot, abduction, 1st TMT instability?"** → **first TMT arthrodesis + MCO + FDL transfer + LCL**

**"DF −5° knee extended, +10° flexed — add?"** → **open gastrocnemius muscle lengthening**

**"Large talar uncoverage, correctable hindfoot, no arthritis?"** → **MCO + LCL + FDL transfer to the medial navicular**

**"Evans procedure corrects?"** → **forefoot abduction**

**"Full subtalar motion, flexible forefoot supination, no abduction?"** → **medial calcaneus displacement osteotomy + FDL transfer to navicular**

**"Indication for modified Lapidus?"** → **plantar gapping between medial cuneiform and first metatarsal base**

**"Contraindication to FDL + slide + LCL + Cotton?"** → **rigid hindfoot valgus**

**"IIB with gastroc tightness, no TMT instability?"** → **FDL transfer, medial calcaneal slide osteotomy, LCL, Cotton osteotomy, gastrocnemius recession**

**"5th MT stress fracture from lateral column overload after surgery for?"** → **stage IIb PTTI**

**"FDL + MCO alone suits which patient?"** → **57-year-old female, pain, inability to perform a single leg heel raise, flexible hindfoot valgus deformity**

**"MIS vs open calcaneal osteotomy — true?"** → **traditional open technique = higher wound complications + longer surgical time**

**"Opening wedge medial cuneiform osteotomy addresses?"** → **forefoot supination**

**"FDL + MCO + LCL + gastroc lengthening matches which exam?"** → **flexible forefoot abduction and hindfoot valgus deformities, gastrocnemius contracture**

**"Stage III AAFD, rigid eversion, subfibular impingement — reconstruction?"** → **triple arthrodesis with appropriate structural realignments** (TO)

**"Preferred surgery for stage III?"** → **arthrodesis of calcaneocuboid, talonavicular and subtalar joints**

**"70-year-old, uncorrectable hindfoot, orthotics fail?"** → **triple arthrodesis**

**"Obese, no hindfoot motion, plantar-medial callus over talar head?"** → **triple arthrodesis**

**"Obese 75-year-old, rigid acquired flatfoot?"** → **triple arthrodesis**

**"Rigid hindfoot + TN, resisted inversion painful, CC spared?"** → **talonavicular and subtalar arthrodesis**

**"Rigid subtalar + tibiotalar valgus, ankle osteophytes?"** → **tibiotalocalcaneal arthrodesis**

**"Stage IV with passively correctable ankle valgus?"** → **triple arthrodesis + deltoid ligament reconstruction**

**"Numbness after retrograde hindfoot nail — where?"** → **lateral plantar midfoot**

**"Long distal interlocking screw by sustentaculum — structure?"** → **flexor hallucis longus**

**"Flexible hindfoot, collapse at naviculocuneiform joint?"** → **navicular-cuneiform arthrodesis**

**"Most likely to benefit from triple arthrodesis?"** → **57-year-old male, hindfoot valgus persisting with double-leg heel rise, 25% talonavicular uncoverage on AP radiographs**

**"Rigid forefoot abduction + rigid hindfoot valgus, severe sinus tarsi pain?"** → **hindfoot arthrodesis**

# Sesamoid Injuries of the Hallux

*Foot & Ankle · 42 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Spectrum** — fracture (hyperextension + axial load) · dislocation · sprain (**turf toe**) · sesamoiditis (trauma / infection) · chondromalacia · OCD · FHB tendonitis

### Epidemiology

**Tibial (medial) sesamoid** — **more commonly injured**: **greater weight-bearing** + **larger** than lateral

**Stress fracture** — **commonest sesamoid problem** (OB) · **medial (tibial) sesamoid** · **mainly females** (OB)

**Bipartite sesamoid** — **10–25%** · **97% tibial** · **25% bilateral**

### Anatomy – intersesamoid ligament

**Held together by** — **intersesamoid ligament + plantar plate**\
✗ Wrong options: intermetatarsal ligament (bases of 2nd–5th MTs) · spring ligament (supports talar head / arch) · plantar fascia · FHL (inserts distal phalanx)

**Plantar plate** — inserts **base of proximal phalanx** of hallux

**Embedded in** — tendons of **FHB** (short flexor); FHB attaches to **both** sesamoids · both lie **within 1st MTP capsule**, plantar to MT head (TO)

| Sesamoid | Muscle attached | Flexor-tendon complex (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **Tibial (medial)** | **Abductor hallucis** | **Medial FHB head + abductor hallucis** → medial proximal phalanx |
| **Fibular (lateral)** | **Adductor hallucis** | **Lateral FHB head + adductor hallucis** → lateral proximal phalanx |

**Functions** — **absorb weight-bearing** pressure · ↓ **friction** at MT head · **protect FHL** (glides **between** sesamoids) · **fulcrum for FHB → ↑ MTP flexion power**

**Analogy** — **patella**: ↑ mechanical advantage of FHB

![Sesamoid complex: M/L sesamoids, iSL intersesamoid ligament, FHL between, FHB heads, ABD medial, ADD lateral](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17142194-1.png)

### Metatarsal load distribution (TO)

**Most weight during walking (TO)** — **2nd MT** · **standing → 1st MT** (TO) · foot anatomy note (Orthobullets): **1st MT 50% in gait** — read the options

**1st MTP joint (TO)** — carries **\~119% body weight** each step

**Sesamoid complex (TO)** — transmits **50% body weight** · **> 300% during push-off**

## 2 Mechanism

**Commonest** — **forced dorsiflexion (hyperextension) of 1st MTP** → plantar plate avulsion off **base of proximal phalanx** → **proximal migration of sesamoids**

**Fracture** — **hyperextension + axial loading**

**Chronic overuse** — dancers, players: **grade 3 1st MTP sprain → chronic sesamoiditis** (TO)

**Complete plantar plate tear (TO)** — may cause **sesamoid fracture** · **separation of bipartite sesamoid** · rarely **proximal migration**
