**Bilateral sesamoiditis** — red flag → **Reiter's disease** (urethritis, conjunctivitis / iritis, IBD) · **psoriatic arthritis** · **seronegative RA**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — generalised big-toe pain · **worse in terminal stance** / hyperextension

**Exam** — possible **plantar-flexed MTP with cavus foot**

### Sesamoiditis in dancers (TO)

**Dancer, walks on lateral margin, painful great-toe MTP plantar flexion (TO)** — **sesamoiditis of first MTP joint** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: 1st MTP joint arthritis · stress fracture of 1st MT head · AVN of 1st MT head

**Features (TO)** — **transfer of weight-bearing to lateral surface** · pain + restricted MTPJ movement · **painful push-off** of great toe

**DDx (TO)** — sesamoid stress fracture · AVN · osteochondral fracture · **hallux rigidus** (1st MTP arthritis)

**Treatment (TO)** — orthotics + immobilisation → sesamoidectomy if operative

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **AP + lateral foot** · **medial oblique (sesamoid view)** · **axial sesamoid view** · (TO) start with radiographs incl. **axial + lateromedial** sesamoid views |
| X-ray finding | **Proximal migration of sesamoids** → suspect **intrinsic-minus hallux** (plantar plate avulsion) |
| Early X-ray | **Often negative** in stress fracture (OB) |
| **Bone scan** | **Bipartite vs fracture** (↑ uptake = fracture) · **25–30% of asymptomatic** show ↑ uptake → **compare with uninjured side** |
| **Bone scan + CT** | **More reliable** for confirming **stress fracture** (OB) |
| **MRI** | **Confirms** diagnosis · indicated if radiographs **inconclusive** (TO) |

![Axial sesamoid view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7010-sesamoid-axial-view-illustration-moved.gif)

![Axial view showing fractured sesamoid (arrows)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7010-sesamoid-axillary-view-xray-greer-richardson-moved.png)

![Bone scan: ↑ uptake in right tibial sesamoid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7010-bone-scan-courtesy-richardson-moved.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Sesamoid injury
├─ First line → NSAIDs, ↓ WB, activity change, orthoses
│      (acute fracture: SLC with toe extension — controversial)
├─ Keratotic lesion, normal alignment → shave plantar half of sesamoid
├─ Plantar-flexed 1st ray → dorsiflexion osteotomy
└─ Failed nonop 3–12 mo → partial / complete sesamoidectomy
       └─ Nonunion, 2 large fragments, no osteonecrosis → ORIF / autologous bone graft
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Partial / complete sesamoidectomy** | **Failed nonop 3–12 months** (Orthobullets) · **≥ 3 months** (OB) |
| **Autologous bone grafting** | **Nonunion** or fracture |
| **ORIF of sesamoid** | **Two large fragments without osteonecrosis** (OB) |
| **Dorsiflexion osteotomy** | **Plantar-flexed first ray** with sesamoid injury |
| **Sesamoid shaving** | **Keratotic lesion** ↑ pressure on sesamoid · **contraindicated with plantar-flexed 1st MT** |
| **Both sesamoids** | **Never excise both** |

### Stress fracture/osteonecrosis – excision indications & outcomes

**Initial treatment** — almost always **accommodative orthosis** (OB)

**Medial sesamoid excision indicated** — **recalcitrant medial sesamoid stress fracture with fragmentation**\
✗ Wrong options: with Lapidus · with 1st MTP arthrodesis (neither needs sesamoidectomy) · acute fracture (nonop first) · Morton's neuroma (neurectomy, bury in intrinsic muscle)

**Excision technique** — **proximal fragment** in some · **entire sesamoid** in others

**3-yr medial sesamoid pain, fragmentation + ↑ density, failed orthotics** — **medial sesamoid excision**\
✗ Wrong options: 1st MTP arthrodesis (doesn't unload sesamoid) · ORIF (only 2 large fragments without osteonecrosis) · shoe / heel-height change (already tried) · steroid injection (no long-term relief)

**Triathlete, failed nonop tibial sesamoid fracture — fastest reliable RTS** — **tibial sesamoidectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF + autogenous calcaneal bone graft (valid but **slower** RTS) · plantar plate reconstruction (turf toe) · MTP arthroscopy + debridement (mild arthritis) · chevron + Akin (bunion)

### Intractable plantar keratosis – sesamoid shaving

**IPK under 1st MT head, normal standing alignment, adequate passive DF** — **planing / excising superficial (plantar) half of medial sesamoid** → preserves function, ↓ pressure

**✗ IPK traps** — **complete medial sesamoid excision** (→ hallux valgus) · **FHL transfer** (no cock-up toe) · **dorsiflexion osteotomy** (no deformity) · **gastrocnemius recession** (passive DF adequate → more instability) · Keller bunionectomy

### Turf toe (TO)

**Definition (TO)** — **hyperextension injury to plantar plate + sesamoid complex** of hallux MTP · mechanism **hyperextension + axial loading**

**Plantar plate (TO)** — fibrocartilaginous: **thickened plantar capsule + medial and lateral flexor-tendon complexes** · 1st MT head → proximal phalanx · stabilisers allow **\~50° DF + 30° PF**

**Plantar plate insufficiency (TO)** — **primary pathology of MTP instability** → main focus of surgery

**Clinical (TO)** — pain, swelling, tenderness at MTP · **push-off painfully weak** · **negative plantar (palmar) grip** · **positive vertical Lachman / drawer test**

**Treatment (TO)** — **mostly nonop: ice, rest, taping, stiff-sole shoe / rocker-bottom sole** · **plantar plate repair only in a few** → "treatment is plantar plate repair" is the **false** statement (TO)

**Surgery indications (TO)** — failed nonop · **retraction or fracture of sesamoids with diastasis** · **loose fragments** in joint · **hallux deformity**

### Approaches – sesamoidectomy

| Sesamoid | Approach | Nerve at risk |
|---|---|---|
| **Tibial (medial)** | **Medial-plantar**, at junction of **glabrous skin** | **Proper branch of medial plantar nerve** = **plantar-medial cutaneous nerve of hallux** (directly under incision) |
| **Fibular (lateral)** | **Plantar** | **Proper branch to lateral hallux** + **1st common digital branch to 1st web space** |

**Medial sesamoidectomy — most vulnerable** — **plantar-medial cutaneous nerve** → painful neuroma, shoe-wear problems\
✗ Wrong options: abductor hallucis tendon (**dorsal** to incision) · intermetatarsal ligament · dorsomedial cutaneous nerve · crista

## 6 Complications

### Complications of sesamoidectomy – cock-up toe, hallux malalignment & nerve injury

| Excision | Complication |
|---|---|
| **Both sesamoids** | **Cock-up toe deformity** (commonest) |
| **Tibial (medial)** | **Hallux valgus** |
| **Fibular (lateral)** | **Hallux varus** |
| Either | Pain, **nerve injury (neuroma)**, infection, deformity |

**Cock-up toe** — ↓ **FHB moment arm** / FHB weakening → **MTP extension** + relative flexor overpull → **IP flexion** · prevent by **meticulous FHB repair**; **avoid excising both**

**Hallux valgus after tibial sesamoidectomy** — **commonest reported deformity** · from **inadequate medial capsular repair / reefing** (OB)

**HV 1 yr after "removing a painful bone"** — **medial (tibial) sesamoidectomy**\
✗ Wrong options: accessory navicular excision · dorsal cheilectomy · lateral sesamoidectomy → varus · os vesalianum excision

![Hallux valgus after medial sesamoidectomy](../../MCQ/attachments/q218823-d-fig-d.jpg)

## X vs Y

| | **Tibial (medial) sesamoid** | **Fibular (lateral) sesamoid** |
|---|---|---|
| Injury frequency | **More common** (larger, ↑ WB) | Less |
| Bipartite | **97%** | Rare |
| Muscle | Abductor hallucis | Adductor hallucis |
| Approach | Medial-plantar | Plantar |
| Excision → | **Hallux valgus** | **Hallux varus** |

| | **Bipartite sesamoid** | **Sesamoid fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Bone scan | No ↑ uptake vs other side | **↑ uptake** |
| Laterality | 25% bilateral | Usually unilateral |

| | **Sesamoiditis (TO)** | **Turf toe (TO)** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | **Chronic overuse** (dancers) | **Acute hyperextension + axial load** |
| Sign | Walks on **lateral margin**, painful push-off | **+ drawer / vertical Lachman**, negative plantar grip |
| Treatment | Orthotics, immobilise → sesamoidectomy | **Nonop** (taping, stiff sole) · repair in few |

## How it's asked

**"Dancer bears weight on lateral margin, painful hallux MTP plantar flexion?"** → **sesamoiditis of first MTP joint** (TO)

**"Turf toe — all true EXCEPT?"** → **treatment is plantar plate repair** (TO)

**"Turf toe — exam?"** → **positive drawer test, negative plantar grip** (TO)

**"Metatarsal bearing most weight during walking?"** → **2nd** (TO)

**"Hallucal sesamoids held together by?"** → **intersesamoid ligament**

**"Medial sesamoid excision indicated for?"** → **recalcitrant stress fracture with fragmentation**

**"Triathlete, failed nonop tibial sesamoid fracture — fastest RTS?"** → **tibial sesamoidectomy**

**"Fragmented medial sesamoid, failed orthotics?"** → **medial sesamoid excision**

**"Commonest complication of excising both sesamoids?"** → **toe cock-up deformity**

**"Structure most at risk in medial sesamoidectomy?"** → **plantar-medial cutaneous nerve of hallux**

**"HV after removal of a painful bone — which procedure?"** → **medial (tibial) sesamoidectomy**

**"Lateral sesamoidectomy causes?"** → **hallux varus**

**"IPK under 1st MT head, normal alignment, failed nonop?"** → **plane superficial half of medial sesamoid**

**"Bipartite vs fractured sesamoid?"** → **bone scan** (compare with uninjured side)

**"Bilateral sesamoiditis — think of?"** → **Reiter's**, psoriatic arthritis, seronegative RA

**"Proximal migration of sesamoids on X-ray?"** → **plantar plate avulsion / intrinsic-minus hallux**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Synovitis of 2nd MTP

*Foot & Ankle · 43 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **commonest monoarticular synovitis of the MTP joints**

**Risk factors** — **elongated 2nd MT relative to 1st (Morton foot)** · **hallux valgus**

### Plantar plate anatomy

**Role** — weight-bearing pushes proximal phalanx **dorsally** · **plantar plate + intrinsics (interossei, lumbricals)** resist → hold phalanx neutral at MTP

**Stabilisers of 2nd MTP** — **plantar plate + collateral ligaments**

![Sagittal MRI of lesser MTP plantar plate (FT flexor tendon, ET extensor tendon)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7016-mri-plantar-plate.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Cascade** — **synovitis → capsuloligamentous stretch → instability → deformity**

**Plantar plate attenuation** — MTP **extension** · **sagittal-plane deformity** · → **crossover toe**

**MTP instability** — → **dorsal dislocation of MTP** · predisposes to **hammer toe**

### Plantar plate deficiency – crossover toe deformity

**Plantar plate deficiency — commonest association** — **crossover toe deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: Freiberg's infraction (no plantar plate pathology) · Lisfranc injury (acute trauma) · claw toe (weak intrinsics vs stronger EDL on extensor sling) · curly toe (FDL + FDB contracture, unknown cause)

**High-grade 2nd MTP plantar plate rupture — greatest risk** — **second toe crossover**\
✗ Wrong options: hallux varus · hallux valgus (may contribute to or follow crossover but not from rupture) · Freiberg's (microtrauma → blood supply loss → 2nd MT head collapse) · 2nd mallet toe (DIP flexion + lateral deviation, FDL contracture)

**Crossover toe** — 2nd toe crosses **over or under the hallux** · requires **plantar plate + collateral ligament** failure

**Order of failure** — **LCL fails first → medial deviation**, plantar plate **second → dorsal displacement** of proximal phalanx (OB) · (Orthobullets, MTP dislocations): **plantar plate first (stage 1), LCL second (stage 2)** — read the options

![Crossover second toe](../../MCQ/attachments/q4490-a-crossover-toe-deformity.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain · **warm** joint · **fullness** of joint · "walking on marbles" (OB)

**Tenderness** — **plantar over plantar plate** or **over dorsal capsule** · may be in **2nd web space** (inflammation or pressure on interdigital nerve)

**Motion** — pain with ROM, **↓ with traction** · **↓ plantar flexion** · global MTP swelling

**Deformity** — **passively correctable** in predislocation stages · progresses to **crossover** (varus or valgus) when collateral + plantar plate disrupted

**Dorsal drawer test** — demonstrates instability · **dorsal subluxation possible = plantar plate attenuated**

### Diagnosis – pain worse barefoot

**Synovitis finding** — **more pain when barefoot** (✗ Wrong options: less pain barefoot (neuroma)) · **toe ROM painful** (✗ Wrong options: painless ROM (neuroma))

**✗ Wrong options (these are neuroma signs)** — **pain + click with medial–lateral forefoot compression** (Mulder) · **pain with 2nd–3rd web-space compression**

**Site** — Morton's neuroma usually **3rd interspace** · pain in **2nd interspace → think 2nd MTP synovitis / metatarsalgia**

**Why it matters** — **steroid injection** helps neuroma but **weakens capsuloligamentous structures** → instability → **dorsal subluxation, crossover toe**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing AP + lateral foot** | **Widening / medial–lateral joint-space imbalance** of 2nd MTP |
| X-ray | **Dorsal subluxation** — looks like **joint-space narrowing** or **proximal phalanx overlapping MT head** |
| X-ray | Varus or valgus toe deformity |
| **MRI** | If **diagnosis unclear** · **quantifies plantar plate / ligament disruption** |

![Dorsal dislocation of the 2nd MTP joint — phalanx overlaps MT head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7016-2nd-mtp-dislocation-courtesy-richardson-moved.jpg)

### Causes of metatarsalgia (TO)

**Metatarsalgia (TO)** — pain in forefoot **under one or more MT heads** · groups: **primary, secondary, iatrogenic** (after forefoot surgery)

| Primary (TO) | Secondary (TO) |
|---|---|
| Plantar soft-tissue lesions | **Iatrogenic** — 1st ray shortening, MT head resection |
| Abnormal metatarsal parabola | **Neurogenic** — referred pain, tarsal tunnel syndrome |
| **Morton's foot** | OA · RA |
| Neuroma | **Atrophied plantar fat pad** · stress fracture |
| Trauma · brachymetatarsia | Capsulitis / bursitis / tendinitis · **Freiberg's infraction** |
| Bone tumours | Pes cavus / planus · inappropriate shoegear |

| Rocker (TO) | Phase | Metatarsalgia from |
|---|---|---|
| **1st** | Heel contact → foot flat | Congenital deformity, **tight heel cord**, severe cavus |
| **2nd** | Tibia over talus; triceps surae activated at end | **Limited ankle DF**, **plantarflexed metatarsals** |
| **3rd** | Foot turns over dorsiflexed MTP joints | **MTP joint deformities** |

**Work-up (TO)** — exam of plantar foot + **weight-bearing radiographs**

## 5 Management

```
2nd MTP synovitis
├─ First line → shoe / activity change, NSAIDs, taping 10–12 wk
├─ No deformity, failed nonop → synovectomy
└─ Fixed deformity
       ├─ Long 2nd MT → Weil distal oblique shortening osteotomy
       └─ No long 2nd MT, sagittal → Girdlestone-Taylor FDL → EDL / capsular release + EDL lengthening
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **First line** | Activity / shoe change, NSAIDs, external MTP support (**crossover taping, Budin-type splint**) for **10–12 weeks** |
| **No deformity + failed nonop** | **Synovectomy** |
| **Fixed deformity + long 2nd MT** | **Weil (distal oblique shortening MT osteotomy)** |
| **Fixed deformity, NO long 2nd MT**, sagittal deformity | **FDL → EDL transfer (Girdlestone-Taylor)** or **MTP capsular release + extensor lengthening** |
| No | **Steroid injection** — contraindicated (weakens capsule) |

### Weil osteotomy technique

**Weil used for (TO)** — **metatarsalgia** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Perthes · SCFE · genu valgum

**Nonop metatarsalgia first (TO)** — physio, orthotics, shoe modification, injections

**Definition (TO)** — **distal oblique osteotomy of MT head** · corrects **transverse + sagittal** MTP pathology · **decompresses MTP joint → relaxes soft tissues**

**Orthobullets** — preserves joint · **rebalances MT cascade** · relaxes plantar plate

**Indications (TO)** — **long or plantarflexed metatarsal** · **transverse-plane MTP deformity** · **MTP subluxation / dislocation** · **recalcitrant generalised metatarsalgia**

**Technique (TO)** — start at **dorsal articular surface** of MT head · cut **dorsal-distal → proximal-plantar**, **30° to shaft**, **parallel to weight-bearing surface** · **screw** fixation

![Weil osteotomy: dorsal-distal to proximal-plantar cut, head slides proximally, screw](../../MCQ/attachments/TO19356199-2.png)

**Long 2nd MT + fixed dorsiflexion deformity, "walking on marbles" after 4 mo orthotics** — Weil · avoid commonest complication (**floating toe**) by **osteotomy parallel to plantar surface of foot** (✓)

**✗ Weil traps** — protect **collateral ligaments** (prevents **AVN** — much rarer than floating toe) · **inject plantar to intermetatarsal ligament** (steroid ↑ instability) · **shorten ≥ 4–7 mm** (over-shortening → plantar displacement → floating toe; **shorten ≤ 3 mm**) · **strict immobilisation 4 wk** (↑ stiffness; use **protected WB in surgical shoe + aggressive ROM**)

**Other floating-toe prevention** — remove a **wafer of bone** (shorten without plantar migration) · combine soft-tissue procedures

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Floating toe** | **Commonest after Weil** (OB) — **plantar shift of MT head centre of rotation** → intrinsics pass **dorsal** to axis → act as **MTP dorsiflexors** |
| **Weil (TO)** | **Dorsiflexed toe** · pain on MTP DF · **↓ MTP ROM** · **PIP contracture** (TO) |
| AVN | Collateral ligament injury · uncommon |
| Stiffness | Prolonged immobilisation |
| **Vascular compromise** | Correcting **chronic dislocation** → contracted vessels stretched → **may need to reverse procedure** to save digit |

## X vs Y

| | **2nd MTP synovitis** | **Morton's neuroma** |
|---|---|---|
| Interspace | **2nd** | **3rd** (usually) |
| Barefoot | **Worse** | Less pain |
| Toe ROM | **Painful** (↓ with traction) | Painless |
| Compression | — | **Click** (medial–lateral), web-space pain |
| Drawer | **+** if unstable | — |
| Steroid | **worsens instability** | Appropriate |

| Deformity | Cause |
|---|---|
| **Crossover toe** | **Plantar plate + collateral ligament** failure |
| Claw toe | Weak intrinsics, EDL overpull on extensor sling |
| Curly toe | FDL + FDB contracture |
| Mallet toe | FDL contracture — DIP flexion |
| Freiberg's | 2nd MT head collapse (blood supply) |

## How it's asked

**"Finding most likely in 2nd MTP synovitis?"** → **more pain when barefoot**

**"Click on medial–lateral forefoot compression?"** → **Morton's neuroma**\
✗ Wrong options: synovitis

**"Plantar plate deficiency most associated with?"** → **crossover toe deformity**

**"High-grade 2nd MTP plantar plate rupture — progressive deformity?"** → **second toe crossover**

**"Weil's osteotomy is used for?"** → **metatarsalgia** (TO)

**"Long 2nd MT, fixed deformity — avoid commonest Weil complication?"** → **osteotomy parallel to plantar surface of foot**

**"Commonest complication of Weil?"** → **floating toe**

**"Maximum Weil shortening?"** → **≤ 3 mm**\
✗ Wrong options: 4–7 mm

**"Fixed deformity without long 2nd MT?"** → **FDL → EDL transfer / capsular release + extensor lengthening**

**"No deformity, failed nonop?"** → **synovectomy**

**"Why avoid steroid injection?"** → **weakens capsule → instability, crossover**

**"Correcting chronic dislocation — feared complication?"** → **vascular compromise → reverse procedure**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tarsal Navicular Fractures

*Foot & Ankle · 44 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Rare midfoot fracture** — **traumatic** (avulsion, tuberosity, body) or **stress** (repetitive microstress)

**Articulations** — **talus, calcaneus, cuboid, cuneiforms** · key to **inversion–eversion** motion

**Medial column** — navicular supports **medial longitudinal arch** · only **displaced fracture / navicular dislocation** → arch instability (OB)

**PTT** — inserts on navicular (tuberosity)

### Stress fracture epidemiology

**Who** — **athletes running on hard surfaces** · **baseball players** (predilection; e.g. catcher jumping from crouch) · gymnasts, soccer, marathon runners (OB stems)

**High-risk stress fracture** — **risk of AVN** · commonest complications **delayed union + nonunion**

**At presentation** — **\~83% incomplete** · complete ones usually **nondisplaced** (OB)

## 2 Mechanism

| Type | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Avulsion** | **Plantarflexion** or **eversion / inversion** · TN or naviculocuneiform ligaments |
| **Tuberosity** | **Eversion + simultaneous PTT contraction** · may be **acute diastasis of an accessory navicular** |
| **Body** | **Axial loading** |
| **Stress** | **Chronic overuse** · hard surfaces |
| **Mueller-Weiss** | Spontaneous adult **navicular AVN** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **vague midfoot pain** + swelling · **insidious** in stress fracture → high index of suspicion

**Exam** — midfoot (**dorsomedial / medial**) swelling + **tenderness** · **ankle + subtalar ROM usually full** · full DF / PF / inversion strength

**Not** — anterior tibial tendinitis (no evidence → no cortisone injection into tibialis anterior sheath)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / role |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **AP, lateral, oblique 45°** · **often missed / read as normal** · **45° oblique best for tuberosity fractures** |
| **CT** | **More sensitive than X-ray** · shows **nondisplaced fracture line** through navicular · may be **false-negative** without frank cortical disruption |
| **MRI** | **T2 high signal** (oedema / inflammation) · **best test to confirm** when X-ray negative + high suspicion or failed restricted WB (OB) · stress reaction = **oedema, no fracture line** |
| Mueller-Weiss X-ray | **Narrow navicular + lateral sclerosis** · **"double navicular sign"** (split navicular) (OB) |

**Best imaging** — **CT more sensitive than X-ray** (Orthobullets, OB) · **MRI best to confirm** (OB, CT false negatives) — read the options

![Axial CT: navicular stress fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7033-ct-navicular-stress-fx-internet-moved.jpg)

![Sagittal T2 MRI: navicular stress fracture (arrow)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7033-mri-foot-sag-navicular-stress-fx-internet-moved.jpg)

### Sangeorzan classification (navicular body)

| Type | Fracture | Deformity |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Transverse**, **dorsal fragment < 50%** of bone | **None** |
| **II** | **Oblique**, usually **dorsolateral → plantar-medial** | **Forefoot ADduction** |
| **III** | **Central or lateral comminution** | **Forefoot ABduction** |

![Sangeorzan types 1–3](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7033-sangeorzan-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Navicular stress fracture
├─ Any type, first presentation → NWB short-leg cast 6–8 wk
├─ Stress reaction (oedema, no line) → restricted WB short-leg cast
└─ Failed cast / nonunion / recurrence → ORIF + autologous bone graft
       └─ High-level / pro athlete → ORIF (OB, Orthobullets)
```

```
Traumatic navicular fracture
├─ Avulsion, most tuberosity, undisplaced type I–II → NWB cast
│      └─ Failed avulsion / symptomatic tuberosity nonunion → fragment excision
├─ Displaced / intra-articular type I–II → ORIF (medial approach), lag screws
└─ Type III or AVN → ORIF + ex-fix vs primary fusion
```

### Operative criteria

| Injury | Operate when |
|---|---|
| **Avulsion** | **> 25% of articular surface** → ORIF · failed nonop → **excision** |
| **Tuberosity** | **> 5 mm diastasis** or **large intra-articular fragment** → ORIF · **symptomatic nonunion → excision** |
| **Body type I–II** | **Displaced or intra-articular** → ORIF (**medial approach**) |
| **Body type III** | **ORIF + ex-fix vs primary fusion** · maintain **lateral column length** |
| **Stress fracture** | **Fracture line across 2 cortices**, or **1 cortex + displacement or cystic change** (OB) · **failed NWB cast** · **nonunion** · **high-level athletes** |
| **Navicular AVN** | **TN + naviculocuneiform fusion** |

### Stress fractures – non-weight-bearing cast first-line

**First line (any type)** — **short-leg cast + NWB 6–8 weeks** (± walking boot + crutches) → **high success**

**45-yo runner** — **short leg cast + non-weight bearing**\
✗ Wrong options: hard-sole shoe (insufficient immobilisation) · partial WB boot / cast (risks progression)

**21-yo baseball player, normal X-ray, MRI T2 signal** — **NWB cast for planned 6–8 weeks**\
✗ Wrong options: observation · steroid into anterior tibial sheath · partial WB boot · ORIF first line

**Gymnast, CT nondisplaced fracture** — **NWB + cast** · **NWB cast more effective than surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: WBAT hard-soled shoe or Aircast boot · partial WB cast (inferior outcomes) · ORIF first

**15-yo soccer, 4 mo pain, MRI oedema, no fracture line (stress reaction)** — **restricted weight bearing in short-leg cast** (± boot)\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · WBAT boot (may **displace + injure TN joint**) · orthotic + bone stimulator · carbon-fibre orthotic

**High-level athletes** — **NWB cast works even in high-level athletes** · operative first line not supported (OB) · vs **ORIF for pro athletes** who can't tolerate long recovery, to prevent painful nonunion (OB, Orthobullets) — read the stem

### Failed or complete stress fractures – ORIF with bone graft

**Marathoner, recurrent chronic stress fracture with diastasis, failed immobilisation, intra-articular** — **ORIF with autologous calcaneus bone graft**\
✗ Wrong options: WBAT hard-soled shoe · repeat NWB cast · fragment excision + PTT advancement (not described) · CRPP (not described)

**18-yo gymnast, 1 yr pain, failed 6 wk NWB cast** — **ORIF with autologous bone grafting**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF alone · more NWB cast · PWB walking cast · no treatment

### Acute fracture fixation

**Acute displaced body fracture with diastasis (basketball)** — **open reduction + lag screw fixation** → interfragmentary compression, restores **medial column length** + articular congruity

**✗ Acute traps** — **NWB cast 6–8 wk** (nondisplaced body fracture / stress reaction only) · **CRPP** (insufficient for displacement) · **ex-fix** (**highly comminuted** / substantial **medial column shortening**) · **primary TN fusion** (pre-existing **TN arthritis** or non-reconstructable)

**Column length** — Type III: maintain **lateral column length** (Orthobullets) · ex-fix for **medial column shortening** (OB) — read the options

### Navicular arthrosis – talonavicular & naviculocuneiform fusion

**56-yo, longstanding midfoot pain, no trauma, failed semirigid insoles** — Mueller-Weiss → **fuse talonavicular + naviculocuneiform joints**\
✗ Wrong options: TN only (misses NC joint) · any fusion including **subtalar — spared** in this disease

**Pain source** — abnormal motion at **TN + NC joints**

![Mueller-Weiss: collapsed navicular on lateral X-ray](../../MCQ/attachments/q3893-a-navicular-avn.jpg)

### Mueller-Weiss syndrome (adult navicular osteonecrosis)

**Definition (TO)** — **spontaneous adult-onset tarsal navicular osteonecrosis** · aka **Brailsford disease** (TO) · related to **Köhler's** (Köhler = paediatric) (OB)

**Who (TO)** — **40–60 yr** · **women** · **frequently bilateral** (TO) · 4th–5th decade (OB) · middle-aged (Orthobullets)

**Aetiology (TO)** — uneven load → **compression of lateral half of navicular** · preconditions: **subtalar varus**, **1st ray brachymetatarsia**, **mild clubfoot** → lateral shift of force, **lateral incompetence of acetabulum pedis**

**Clinical (TO)** — chronic **mid + hindfoot pain** · navicular collapse → secondary OA → **pes planovarus** · tibia forced into **external rotation → knee OA**

**Exam (TO)** — dorsomedial midfoot swelling + tenderness · **heel varus** · prominent navicular tuberosity → **pseudo hindfoot valgus** · flattened medial arch

**Paradoxical pes planovarus (TO)** — **pseudo hindfoot valgus + pes planus** · "**pseudo hindfoot varus**" = the **false** option (TO)

**Treatment** — **semirigid insoles first**, fusion if failed (OB) · **mostly surgical** (TO) — read the options · fuse symptomatic joints + restore arches by correcting **Meary-Tomeno line** · **talonavicular-cuneiform arthrodesis with bone grafting** = best results (TO)

## 6 Complications

### Nonunion risk

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Nonunion + delayed union** | **Two commonest** after both nonop + surgery |
| **AVN** | Why stress fracture is **high risk** |
| Malunion | **Very rare** (nondisplaced) |
| Infection | Rare unless open |
| Arch instability | Only **displaced fracture / dislocation** |
| Neuroma | Rare (stress fracture, nonunion) |

**Most common complication (stress fracture image)** — **non-union**\
✗ Wrong options: malunion · infection · longitudinal arch instability · neurovascular injury

**Pro baseball catcher, 5 mo dorsomedial pain, failed nonop, MRI stress fracture — most at risk** — **nonunion**\
✗ Wrong options: arch collapse / acquired flatfoot (chronic **PTTI** sequela) · **cavovarus** · PTTI (chronic, not from stress fracture) · transfer metatarsalgia (after 1st ray shortening)

## X vs Y

| | **Navicular stress fracture** | **Mueller-Weiss (adult AVN)** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | Young athletes | **40–60**, women |
| Cause | Overuse, hard surfaces | Spontaneous; lateral compression |
| X-ray | Often **normal** | **Narrow, lateral sclerosis, double navicular** |
| Treatment | **NWB cast 6–8 wk** → ORIF + graft | Insoles → **TN + NC fusion** (subtalar spared) |

| | **Mueller-Weiss** | **Köhler's** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **Adult (4th–5th decade)** | **Paediatric** |

| Sangeorzan | Deformity |
|---|---|
| II (oblique) | **ADduction** |
| III (comminuted) | **ABduction** |

## How it's asked

**"Navicular stress fracture — initial immobilisation + WB?"** → **short leg cast + non-weight bearing**

**"Baseball player, normal X-ray, MRI signal in navicular?"** → **NWB cast for planned 6–8 weeks**

**"Gymnast, CT navicular stress fracture — next step?"** → **non-weightbearing with cast immobilization**

**"Adolescent navicular stress reaction on MRI?"** → **restricted weight bearing in short-leg cast**

**"Surgery for navicular stress fracture when?"** → **line across 2 cortices, or 1 cortex + displacement / cysts**

**"Chronic recurrent stress fracture, failed casting?"** → **ORIF with autologous calcaneus bone graft**

**"Gymnast, failed 6 wk NWB cast?"** → **ORIF with autologous bone grafting**

**"Most common complication of navicular stress fracture?"** → **non-union**

**"Pro catcher with navicular stress fracture — most at risk for?"** → **nonunion**

**"Acute displaced navicular body fracture?"** → **open reduction and lag screw fixation**

**"Best view for tuberosity fracture?"** → **45° oblique**

**"Avulsion ORIF threshold?"** → **> 25% articular surface**

**"Tuberosity ORIF threshold?"** → **> 5 mm diastasis**

**"Sangeorzan III deformity?"** → **forefoot abduction**

**"Mueller-Weiss — fuse which joints?"** → **talonavicular and naviculocuneiform**

**"Mueller-Weiss — all true EXCEPT?"** → **pseudo hindfoot varus** (TO)

**"Paradoxical pes planovarus = ?"** → **pseudo hindfoot valgus + pes planus** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tarsal Tunnel Syndrome

*Foot & Ankle · 45 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **compressive neuropathy of the tibial nerve** at the tarsal tunnel → pain + paraesthesia of the **plantar foot**

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**: burning plantar pain + **Tinel's over tibial nerve** · EMG / NCS can help confirm · **history = most useful diagnostic aid**

### Tarsal tunnel – anatomy, causes (flatfoot, space-occupying lesion) & symptoms

| Posterior tarsal tunnel | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Roof (superficial)** | **Flexor retinaculum (laciniate ligament)** |
| **Medial wall (deep)** | **Calcaneus + talus** |
| **Inferior** | **Abductor hallucis** |
| **Contents** | **Tibial nerve · posterior tibial artery · FHL · FDL · tibialis posterior** |

![Posterior tarsal tunnel — flexor retinaculum over tibial nerve, PTA and flexor tendons](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7042-tarsal-tunnel2.jpg)

**Tibial nerve course** — along fascia of **tibialis posterior in deep posterior compartment** → gives off **medial calcaneal nerve just proximal to ankle** → dives **deep to flexor retinaculum** → most often **bifurcates in the tunnel** into medial + lateral plantar nerves

**3 distal branches** — **medial plantar · lateral plantar · medial calcaneal** · bifurcation **proximal to tunnel in 5%**

**Plantar nerves** — pass through **two separate tunnels within and deep to abductor hallucis** (superficial + deep fascial layers) · can be compressed in their own sheath **distal** to the tarsal tunnel

| Intrinsic causes | Extrinsic causes |
|---|---|
| **Ganglion cyst** (classic) | Shoes |
| Tendinopathy, tenosynovitis | Trauma |
| Lipoma / tumour | Deformity — **tarsal coalition, valgus hindfoot** |
| Perineural fibrosis | Post-surgical scarring |
| Osteophytes | Systemic inflammatory disease, lower-limb oedema |

**Identifiable cause** — **80%** of cases · commonest = **space-occupying lesion in the tunnel proper** (OB)

**Heel pain triad** — **posterior tibial tendon deficiency (adult-acquired flatfoot) + plantar fasciitis + tarsal tunnel syndrome** · loss of **static + dynamic stabilisers of medial arch → traction neuropathy** of tibial nerve · **86% improvement** after surgery (Labib)

**Not associated with TTS** — insertional Achilles tendinitis · hypermobile first ray · metatarsal stress fracture · gastrocnemius contracture

### Baxter's nerve (heel pain) (TO)

**Tibial nerve divisions (TO)** — **medial calcaneal nerve (MCN) · medial plantar nerve (MPN) · lateral plantar nerve (LPN) · first branch of LPN** · tibial = larger of the two sciatic divisions

**Nerve in heel pain syndrome (TO)** — **1st branch of lateral plantar nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: medial plantar nerve · medial calcaneal nerve · 2nd branch of sural nerve

**First branch of LPN (TO)** — = **nerve to abductor digiti minimi** · sensory branches to **calcaneal periosteum** · **supplies no skin**

**Commonest neural cause of plantar heel pain (TO)** — **entrapment of first branch of LPN**

**Entrapment sites of first branch (TO)** — **sharp edge of deep fascia of abductor hallucis** · **just distal to medial edge of calcaneus**

**Second commonest (TO)** — **medial calcaneal nerve**

**LPN (TO)** — supplies **most foot muscles** · skin of **lateral ⅓ of plantar foot + 4th and 5th toes** · entrapped **between abductor hallucis and quadratus plantae**

**MPN (TO)** — motor **abductor hallucis, FHB, FDB, 1st lumbrical** · skin **medial ⅔ of plantar foot** · entrapment **less common** than the others

### Anterior tarsal tunnel (deep peroneal nerve)

| Anterior tarsal tunnel | Orthobullets | OB |
|---|---|---|
| **Roof (most superficial)** | **Inferior extensor retinaculum** | **Inferior extensor retinaculum** |
| Floor (deep) | Fascia over **talus + navicular** | **Talonavicular capsule** |
| Medial / lateral | — | **Medial malleolus / lateral malleolus** |
| **Contents** | **DPN + branches, EHL, EDL, peroneus tertius, dorsalis pedis a.** | Same **+ tibialis anterior + dorsalis pedis vein** |

**EHB** — **not in the tunnel** (originates **distal** to it) · DPN may also be trapped **under the EHB tendon** (OB)

![Anterior tarsal tunnel — DPN under the inferior extensor retinaculum, EHL, EDL, EHB](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7042-anterior-tibial-tunnel.jpg)

**Commonest DPN entrapment site** — **under the inferior extensor retinaculum = anterior tarsal tunnel syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: EHB tendon · TN osteophytes · os intermetatarseum · base of 5th MT

**Other DPN compressors (runners)** — **dorsal talonavicular osteophytes** · **os intermetatarseum** (between 1st + 2nd MT bases)

**Causes** — contusion of dorsum · **tight shoe laces / compressive shoes** (commonest) · TN osteophytes · ganglion · **pes cavus** · scar · synovial cyst · hypertrophic extensor muscles · **dorsalis pedis aneurysm** · **dorsal navicular avulsion fracture**

**Peroneal neuropathy** — **commonest compressive neuropathy of lower limb** · CPN at **fibular prominence** · SPN at **exit from lateral compartment** · DPN **under extensor retinaculum**

**Frequency** — anterior TTS **5% of foot numbness**, often misdiagnosed

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **prolonged standing / walking** · **vague, misleading medial foot pain** · **sharp, burning** · may radiate proximally **up into the calf**

**Numbness** — **intermittent paraesthesia + numbness of plantar foot** · lateral foot burning = **lateral plantar nerve fibres** · history of trauma or surgery

**Exam** — **Tinel's over tibial nerve** · sensory exam equivocal · **pes planus** · **intrinsic wasting: abductor digiti quinti or abductor hallucis**

| Provocative test | Manoeuvre |
|---|---|
| **Compression test** | **Plantar flexion + inversion + digital pressure over tunnel** → **highly sensitive + specific** |
| **Dorsiflexion–eversion test** | **Ankle dorsiflexion + heel eversion + toe dorsiflexion** → reproduces pain / paraesthesia |
| Simple stretch | Pain with **dorsiflexion + eversion** |

**Ganglion in tunnel** — **burning + numbness on the bottom of the foot** · pain medial heel + plantar foot · affects **toe flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: loss of toe extension

**Nerve** — **tibial nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: sural · saphenous · SPN · DPN (not in the triad)

**Anterior TTS** — **dull ache / vague dorsomedial pain, numbness, paraesthesia of dorsum → 1st web space** · ↑ with **plantar flexion**, inactivity, **tight shoes** · **EDB atrophy** · **Tinel's over anterior ankle** radiating between 1st + 2nd toes · ballet dancers, marathon runners, hikers

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / use |
|---|---|
| **Weight-bearing X-ray** | Osseous structure (deformity, coalition, osteophytes) |
| **MRI** | Rules out **accessory muscle** or **soft-tissue tumour** · **ganglion cyst in tunnel** (sagittal: bright fluid lesion) |
| Ultrasound | Complements clinical diagnosis |
| **Lateral foot X-ray (anterior TTS)** | **Dorsal navicular avulsion fragment** / dorsal osteophytes compressing DPN |

![Sagittal MRI — ganglion cyst in the tarsal tunnel](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7042-6a-moved.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria

| EMG / NCS (posterior TTS) | Positive finding |
|---|---|
| **Distal motor latency** | **≥ 7.0 ms** |
| **Sensory latency** | **> 2.3 ms** |
| **Motor amplitude** | ↓ to **abductor hallucis** or **abductor digiti minimi** |
| Which first | **Sensory (SAP) abnormal before / more often than motor** |

**Electrodiagnostics** — **not very sensitive or specific**, **false negatives** in symptomatic patients · still helpful to **localise compression** (nerve root vs tarsal tunnel vs more distal) · **EMG confirms anterior TTS**

## 5 Management

```
Tarsal tunnel syndrome
├─ Initial → NSAIDs, SSRIs, orthosis / footwear, short-leg cast
└─ Failed 3–6 months nonop
       ├─ Compressive mass (ganglion) → TARSAL TUNNEL RELEASE (best results)
       ├─ Positive EMG + reproducible exam → TARSAL TUNNEL RELEASE
       └─ Traction neuritis → release, poorer response
```

**Nonoperative** — anti-inflammatories · **SSRIs** · orthosis / footwear to correct **hindfoot alignment** · period of **short-leg cast** · usually **ineffective**

### Operative criteria

| Release indicated after **3–6 months** failed nonop **and** | Outcome |
|---|---|
| **Compressive mass (ganglion cyst)** identified | **Best results** — compressing anatomic structure removed |
| **Positive EMG** | — |
| **Reproducible physical findings** | **Positive Tinel's pre-op** → good results |
| Traction neuritis | Responds less well |

**Success** — **50–90%** (Orthobullets) · **44–96%** (OB, Ahmad) — read the options · **early** operation to prevent fibrosis

### Release sites & causes of recurrence

**Technique** — **identify the nerve proximally** → release all layers

| Must release | Not a TTS site |
|---|---|
| **Flexor retinaculum** | **Medial band of the plantar fascia** |
| **Deep investing fascia of the leg** | — |
| **Superficial fascia of abductor hallucis** | — |
| **Deep fascia of abductor hallucis** | — |

![Compression sites of the tibial nerve — high tarsal tunnel, tarsal tunnel, distal sites, jogger's foot](../../MCQ/attachments/q3850-illa-compression-sites.jpg)

**Anterior TTS** — nonop: **activity modification, remove compressive shoes** → surgical release · **endoscopic** for **osteophytes / isolated ganglion** · **open** for **synovitis / unknown cause**

## 6 Complications

### Recurrence

**Commonest cause** — **incomplete / inadequate release**\
✗ Wrong options: < 5% recurrence · revision = primary outcome · motor latencies more sensitive

**Other causes** — adhesive neuritis · **neuroma** · incorrect initial diagnosis · infection · **double crush** · bleeding + scarring · anatomic variation · pre-existing nerve damage

**Recurrence rate** — **5–40%**

**Revision** — **less successful than index operation** · **repeat release not recommended** (Orthobullets; some authors) · barrier materials ↓ adherence · **excise neuromas** · then nerve stimulators, systemic medication

**Worse prognosis** — **double crush** · post-operative scarring · revision surgery

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior TTS** | **Anterior TTS** | **Baxter's nerve** (TO) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Nerve | **Tibial** | **Deep peroneal** | **1st branch LPN** |
| Roof | **Flexor retinaculum** | **Inferior extensor retinaculum** | **Deep fascia of abductor hallucis** |
| Symptoms | **Burning plantar foot** | **Dorsum → 1st web space** | Plantar heel pain |
| Motor | AbH / ADM wasting | **EDB atrophy** | **Abductor digiti minimi** |

| Heel / foot pain | Clue |
|---|---|
| **Plantar fasciitis** | **Worst first thing in the morning** · commonest misdiagnosis of TTS |
| **Tarsal tunnel** | Burning plantar numbness, Tinel's, ganglion on MRI |
| Malignant tumour (e.g. synovial sarcoma) | **Night pain** · aggressive imaging |
| Os trigonum | Impingement, **no burning / tingling** |
| **Missed foot compartment syndrome** | **Intrinsic-minus (claw) toes** from interosseous + lumbrical fibrosis · patchy paresis |
| **L5/S1 radiculopathy** | **Dermatomal** numbness, radiating pain |
| **Morton's neuroma** | **2nd / 3rd interdigital nerve** at transverse intermetatarsal ligament |
| **CRPS** | Diffuse burning pain, touch / movement intolerable |

## How it's asked

**"Flatfoot, burning lateral foot, medial heel tenderness — nerve?"** → **tibial nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: sural · saphenous · SPN · DPN

**"Associated with tarsal tunnel syndrome?"** → **adult-acquired flatfoot** (heel pain triad)

**"Plantar burning after failed stretching + night splints, sagittal MRI cyst?"** → **tarsal tunnel syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: recalcitrant plantar fasciitis

**"Ganglion in tarsal tunnel — symptom?"** → **burning and numbness on the bottom of the foot**

**"Release sites EXCEPT?"** → **medial band of the plantar fascia**

**"Recurrent TTS — true?"** → **commonest cause is incomplete release**

**"Revision release outcome?"** → **worse than primary**

**"Sensory vs motor latencies?"** → **sensory prolonged first**

**"Best surgical results when?"** → **compressing structure (ganglion) identified + removed**

**"Ballet dancer, dorsomedial pain to 1st web space, EDB atrophy, navicular avulsion?"** → **anterior tarsal tunnel syndrome**

**"Commonest DPN entrapment site?"** → **inferior extensor retinaculum**

**"Most superficial boundary of the space (dorsal pain to 1st–2nd toes)?"** → **inferior extensor retinaculum**\
✗ Wrong options: EHL · EDL · peroneus tertius (inside) · EHB (distal)

**"Nerve involved in heel pain syndrome?"** → **1st branch of lateral plantar nerve** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Tibiotalar Impingement

*Foot & Ankle · 46 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — anterior ankle pain, **most often osteophyte impingement** in anterior tibiotalar joint · **"footballer's ankle"** (high incidence in soccer players)

**Who** — athletes on **turf / grass** or needing **maximum dorsiflexion**: **rugby, football, soccer, dancers (ballet), wrestlers**

**Associated (OB)** — **lateral ankle instability**, **osteochondral lesions**, **gastrocnemius contracture** → look for + treat concomitantly

## 2 Mechanism

**Causes** — **repetitive overuse** · trauma · degenerative sequelae · excess **anterolateral soft tissue** · posterior soft-tissue / osseous abnormalities

**Pain generator (OB)** — spurs of **talar neck + anterior tibia rarely touch** (cadaver) → **anterior synovial + capsular tissue impingement** is the source

| Subtype (OB) | Cause |
|---|---|
| **Anteromedial** | **Intra-articular exostoses of anterior tibia + talar neck** pinch interposed synovium / capsule → chronic inflammation |
| **Anterolateral** | **AITFL catching on talus** · or **after ATFL sprain** → **fibrotic tissue in anterolateral gutter** |

**Sources of anterior impingement pain** — **AITFL** · **tibial osteophyte** · **inflamed synovium + capsule** · **talar neck exostosis**\
✗ Wrong options: **os trigonum** = accessory ossification near **posterolateral talar process** → **posterior** impingement

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **anterior ankle pain** · worse **crouching** / squatting

**Exam** — **pain with forced dorsiflexion** · **limited dorsiflexion** (e.g. passive DF to **10°** reproduces pain) · **anterior joint-line point tenderness** · soft-tissue swelling, effusion · **subtalar joint pain-free**

**Anterolateral soft-tissue impingement** — old **ankle sprain** · pain **anterolaterally at end-arc passive dorsiflexion** · **normal talar drawer** · **no** tenderness at fibula, ATFL, CFL · no posterior pain with passive PF

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray: AP, lateral, oblique** | **Spurs on anterior distal tibia** + **dorsal talus (talar neck)** · dorsiflexed lateral shows contact |
| **Oblique view** | Reveals **anteromedial talar spurs** · better than standard series (Tol & van Dijk) |
| **CT** | **Extent of bony osteophytes** |
| **MRI** | **Spurring + joint fluid** · fluid-sensitive: **anterior capsular thickening + tibial osteophyte** |
| **MR arthrogram** | **Anterolateral gutter soft-tissue mass** · MRI **78.9% accuracy** for anterolateral soft-tissue impingement (Ferkel) |

![Dorsiflexed lateral: anterior tibial spur abutting talar neck](../../MCQ/attachments/q1012-a-ankle-tibia-spur.jpg)

![Axial MR arthrogram: anterolateral gutter soft-tissue impingement](../../MCQ/attachments/q4554-a-anterolateral-ankle-impingement-mr-t1.jpg)

**Diagnosis** — **meticulous physical exam** + oblique radiographs (Tol & van Dijk)

### Parma classification (anterior bony impingement)

| Type | Pattern |
|---|---|
| **A** | **Focal** |
| **B** | **Wide** |
| **C** | **Complex** |

**Prognosis (Parma)** — **isolated tibial spurs do better** · **concomitant OCD, older age, previous ankle fracture → worse**

![Parma classification: type A focal, B wide, C complex](../../MCQ/attachments/q210241-illa-parma-class.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Anterior ankle impingement
├─ First line → PT, activity / lifestyle change, NSAIDs, bracing
│      (steroid injection → soft-tissue impingement + synovitis pain)
├─ Failed nonop, bony spurs ± mild arthritis → arthroscopic debridement / cheilectomy
├─ Failed nonop, anterolateral soft tissue → arthroscopy + synovial debridement
└─ Severe arthritis → ankle arthrodesis (gold standard)
```

### Anterior bony impingement – arthroscopic debridement

**Rugby player, painful limited DF, tibial spur on dorsiflexed X-ray** — **arthroscopic tibial debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: chondral drilling · modified Broström · os trigonum excision · tibiotalar arthrodesis

**Impingement + mild arthritis** — **anterior ankle cheilectomy / debridement** (arthroscopic or open) · many still need **open approach** (Talusan)

**Wrestler, failed PT + NSAIDs + bracing, MRI capsular thickening + spur** — **arthroscopic debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: os trigonum excision (posterior) · percutaneous Achilles repair · more proprioceptive rehab (already failed) · loose body removal (none seen)

**Arthroscopy** — debride **bony abnormalities + inflamed synovium** · effective alternative to open · anteromedial: **97% return to prior sport** (Murawski & Kennedy)

**Technique** — **supine**, **external traction**, leg on padded bump · **knife cuts skin only → hemostat spread** for portals · **adequate view before burring** anterior tibia (protect dorsal NV bundle)

**Severe arthritis** — **ankle arthrodesis = gold standard** · **93–94% fusion with cancellous screws** (74% overall, 93% low risk) (OB) · **\~90% union** (Orthobullets, ankle arthritis) — read the options · **TAA** = technically demanding alternative

### Anterolateral soft-tissue impingement

**Military officer, old sprain, anterolateral pain at end-DF, stable drawer, failed nonop** — **ankle arthroscopy with synovial debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: Broström-Gould (anatomic ATFL + CFL shortening / reinsertion + inferior extensor retinaculum) · Chrisman-Snook (non-anatomic split peroneus brevis) · syndesmosis screw (diastasis) · loose body removal

**Outcome** — partial synovectomy + lateral-gutter scar debridement → **good–excellent at 2 yr** (Ferkel)

### Evidence-based indications for ankle arthroscopy

| Evidence (Glazebrook) | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Fair → recommended** | **Osteochondral lesions** · **ankle arthrodesis** |
| Indicated | **Anterior ankle impingement** (isolated bony) |
| **Limited** | Instability · septic arthritis · arthrofibrosis · **loose body removal** |
| **Not effective → not recommended** | **Debridement of diffuse degenerative cartilage (ankle arthritis)** |

## 6 Complications

| Portal / step | Structure injured |
|---|---|
| **Anterolateral portal** | **Superficial peroneal nerve** |
| **Anteromedial portal** | **Saphenous vein** |
| **Spur burring of anterior tibia** | **Dorsal neurovascular bundle** |

## X vs Y

| | **Anterior impingement** | **Posterior impingement** |
|---|---|---|
| Painful motion | **Forced dorsiflexion** | **Passive plantarflexion** |
| Bony cause | Tibial / talar neck spurs | **Os trigonum** (posterolateral talar process) |
| Sport | Soccer, rugby, wrestling, ballet | — |

| | **Anteromedial** | **Anterolateral** |
|---|---|---|
| Cause | **Tibia + talar neck exostoses** | **AITFL** on talus / **post-ATFL-sprain gutter fibrosis** |
| Imaging | Oblique X-ray spurs | MR arthrogram gutter tissue |
| Surgery | Arthroscopic spur + synovial debridement | **Arthroscopic synovial debridement** |

## How it's asked

**"Rugby player, painful limited DF, anterior tibial spur — surgery?"** → **arthroscopic tibial debridement**

**"Wrestler, failed nonop, MRI capsular thickening + spur?"** → **arthroscopic debridement**

**"NOT a source of anterior impingement pain?"** → **accessory ossification near posterolateral talar process (os trigonum)**

**"Old sprain, anterolateral pain at end-DF, stable drawer?"** → **ankle arthroscopy with synovial debridement**

**"Ankle arthroscopy indications EXCEPT?"** → **debridement of diffuse degenerative ankle cartilage**

**"Best plain view for anteromedial talar spurs?"** → **oblique**

**"Portal injuring SPN?"** → **anterolateral**

**"Real pain generator in bony impingement?"** → **impinged synovium + capsule** (spurs rarely touch)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Total Ankle Arthroplasty

*Foot & Ankle · 47 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Role** — **alternative to ankle arthrodesis for end-stage ankle OA** · aims to **preserve functional ROM** sacrificed by fusion

**Ankle OA** — typically **post-traumatic** · less frequent than hip / knee OA · nonop: gauntlet brace, PT, steroid injection, NSAIDs

### Implant generations

| Generation | Design | Problem / feature |
|---|---|---|
| **1st** | Highly **constrained / semi-constrained, two-component**, **cemented** both sides | **High loosening**, wide osteolysis, subsidence, mechanical failure |
| **2nd** | **Two-component fixed-bearing**, **polyethylene bearing** built into talar or tibial component | Conservative bone cuts · **cementless press-fit, porous coating** for ingrowth |
| **3rd** | Adds a third component: **independent polyethylene mobile-bearing meniscus** | Ligaments for stability · anatomic balancing · minimal resection |

**Three vs two components** — **no evidence three-component designs are better**

| Two-component | Three-component (mobile bearing) |
|---|---|
| **Agility** (cemented era; tibia titanium, central fins) | **STAR** (Scandinavian Total Ankle Replacement) |
| **INBONE** (tibial **IM stem**) | Mobility · Buechel-Pappas |
| **Salto Talaris** (**no modular polyethylene liner**, OB) · Eclipse | HINTEGRA · BOX |

**FDA-approved (Orthobullets)** — **STAR · INBONE · Agility · Salto Talaris**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Use |
|---|---|
| **WB AP + lateral ankle** | Pre-op: **extent of arthritis** · non-WB views **underestimate joint space narrowing** |
| **MRI** (pre-op) | **Osteonecrosis** (amount), bone loss, **subchondral cyst size** |
| **CT with metal artifact-reducing protocol** | **Best study for extent of osteolysis** / loosening / subsidence in painful TAA |
| Bone scan | Infection **or** aseptic loosening but **cannot differentiate** · high false-positive + false-negative |
| SPECT | **Not indicated** for PJI |
| Flexion–extension X-rays + gutter exam | Impingement, arthrofibrosis |

![Painful TAA — balloon osteolysis around the tibial component with talar subsidence](../../MCQ/attachments/q212922-a-screen-shot-2019-07-25-at-11-19-59-am.jpg)

### Diagnostic criteria – painful TAA & infection

**Painful TAA — first step** — **CBC, ESR, CRP** (rule out chronic infection) → aspiration + synovial fluid to confirm → then **CT (metal artifact-reducing)**

**Causes of failure** — **aseptic loosening**, **subsidence**, **impingement**, **arthrofibrosis**, **infection** · persistent pain after "successful" TAA is common

| Parameter | **Aseptic** (OB) | **PJI** (OB) |
|---|---|---|
| ESR / CRP | **19 mm/h / 1.1 mg/L** (normal) | **Elevated** |
| Serum WBC | **9K** | — |
| Synovial nucleated cells | **900 / mL, 44% PMN**, culture negative | **25,000 / µL, 85% PMN** · or **70K**, **gram-positive cocci in clusters** |
| Intra-op | — | **Purulence · > 5 PMN / hpf** |
| Next | **CT metal artifact-reducing** | **Explant + antibiotic spacer** |

| PJI timing | Definition | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Acute** | **< 4 wk** from index | **I&D + polyethylene exchange** (option) |
| **Chronic** | **> 4 wk** | **Explant + antibiotic spacer** (biofilm on metal) |

### Heterotopic ossification classification (Brooker-based) (TO)

| Class | Finding (TO) |
|---|---|
| **0** | **No HO** |
| **I** | **Islands of bone in soft tissue** about the ankle |
| **II** | **Bone spurs from tibia or talus, posterior joint space ↓ < 50%** |
| **III** | Spurs **↓ posterior joint space > 50%**, **non-bridging** |
| **IV** | **Bridging bone** continuous **tibia ↔ talus** |

### Periprosthetic fracture classification (Manegold, OB)

| Fracture type | Location | Prosthesis stability |
|---|---|---|
| **1 Intraoperative** | **A Medial malleolus** | **S Stable** |
| **2 Postoperative trauma** | B Lateral malleolus | **U Unstable** |
| **3 Postoperative stress** | C Tibia · D Talus | — |

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria – patient selection

| | **Orthobullets** | **TO** |
|---|---|---|
| **Indication** | **Unilateral or bilateral end-stage ankle OA** | Degenerative, **inflammatory** or post-traumatic arthritis |
| **Ideal patient** | **Older (middle–old), low demand**, reasonably mobile, no major comorbidity · **normal / low BMI** · **well-aligned, stable hindfoot** · good soft tissues | **Older, thin, low-demand**, **minimal deformity**, **retained ankle ROM** |
| **Contraindications** | **Active infection** · **peripheral vascular disease** · inadequate soft-tissue envelope · **Charcot arthropathy** · insufficient bone stock · **severe osteoporosis** · **osteonecrosis of the talus** · **peripheral neuropathy** | **Age < 50** · poor compliance · **heavy industrial labourer** · heavy smoker · **uncontrolled diabetes with neuropathy** · **significant ankle instability** · **angular deformity > 10–15°** · vascular insufficiency · **obesity > 250 lb** · significant bone loss · **osteonecrosis (AVN talus)** · **active or previous infection** |

**RA — NOT a contraindication (TO)** — arthrodesis used to be standard · arthroplasty now chosen to **preserve motion + ↓ stress on midfoot and subtalar joints**\
✗ Wrong options: AVN talus · ankle instability · heavy labourer · young age

**AVN talus (TO)** — **avascular, fragmented, collapsed talar body → not a candidate → arthrodesis**

**Diagnosis vs outcome** — **best for primary OA** · **worse with post-traumatic + inflammatory arthritis** (prior surgery, tissue laxity) (OB) · TO lists **inflammatory** among ideal indications — read the options

**Coronal deformity** — **> 10–15° = contraindication** (TO) · **> 15° varus / valgus** did as well as neutral when corrected, third-generation implants (OB, Queen) — read the options

**Young, active, heavy job** — **high stress on prosthesis + bone–implant interface → ↑ failure** → fuse

```
End-stage ankle OA, failed nonop
├─ Older, low demand, aligned hindfoot, good bone → TOTAL ANKLE ARTHROPLASTY
├─ Young / heavy labourer, isolated tibiotalar → TIBIOTALAR ARTHRODESIS
│      (direct anterior or arthroscopic → keeps later conversion to TAA)
└─ Rigid flatfoot (PTTI IV) + tibiotalar OA → PANTALAR ARTHRODESIS
       └─ Supple hindfoot, PTT strong, no forefoot deformity → TAA + realignment
```

### Patient selection & outcomes vs arthrodesis (deformity, flatfoot)

**49-yo construction worker, isolated tibiotalar OA, no subtalar pain** — **tibiotalar arthrodesis through direct anterior or arthroscopic approach** → allows **later conversion to TAA**\
✗ Wrong options: TAA (young, high demand) · TTC (no subtalar arthritis) · **medial malleolar osteotomy / transfibular approach** (prevents later conversion, ↑ wound complications after conversion) · repeat steroid

**Arthroscopic arthrodesis** — **< 15° coronal deformity** · **100% fusion** without graft (OB, Duan)

**PTTI stage IV, rigid hindfoot, tibiotalar OA, flatter despite Arizona brace** — **pantalar arthrodesis** (addresses arthritic joint + forefoot deformity)

| ✗ Option in stage IV rigid + ankle OA | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| **Medial slide calcaneal osteotomy + FDL transfer + LCL + Cotton + spring / deltoid recon** | Early disease; ignores tibiotalar OA |
| **Supramalleolar osteotomy** | Only when tilt comes from **distal tibia**; stage IV tilt = **deltoid insufficiency** |
| **TAA** | Deformity must be **reconstructed first** (staged or concurrent) |
| **Triple arthrodesis + deltoid reconstruction** | Only if **no ankle arthritis** (< 10° valgus) |

**Stage IV (Bluman)** — **rigid hindfoot → TTC or pantalar fusion** · **supple → realignment + deltoid reconstruction**

**Isolated TAA in valgus ankle with deltoid insufficiency (collapsing flatfoot, minimally correctable)** — **lateral polyethylene edge-loading + progressive lateral talar tilt**\
✗ Wrong options: durable neutral alignment + symmetric wear · medial gutter impingement from deltoid overconstraint · subtalar nonunion (no fusion done) · peroneal rupture from varus overcorrection

**Adjuncts to get plantigrade foot** — **deltoid reconstruction**, osteotomies, arthrodeses · **pain relief after tibiotalar injection = ankle is pain generator**

| Outcome | **TAA vs arthrodesis** |
|---|---|
| **Pain relief** | **Equivalent** |
| **Stairs (up + down)** | **TAA better** ✓ · better on **uneven surfaces** |
| ROM | **TAA greater** (✗ Wrong options: decreased ROM) |
| Gait | **TAA ↑ stride length**, gait velocity, **cadence** (✗ Wrong options: decreased stride length) |
| **Daily step count** | **Neither improves** |
| AOFAS / SF-36 | No difference (meta-analysis) |
| Reoperation / surgical complications | **Higher with TAA** |
| Adjacent joints | Arthrodesis → **premature OA of subtalar, TN, CC, NC, TMT, 1st MTP** (✗ Wrong options: knee) at 22 yr |

**Post-op ROM** — **pre-op ROM = best predictor** · minimal extra ROM gained · little high-quality TAA vs fusion evidence

**Survivorship** — **70–90% at 10 yr** · **> 80% at 5 yr if < 50 yo** (Orthobullets) · **70–98% at 3–6 yr, 80–95% at 8–12 yr** (OB, Easley) · **5-yr survival up to 60%, reoperation up to 30%** — worse than TKA / THA (OB) — read the options

### Surgical technique

**Approach** — **anterior, between tibialis anterior + EHL**

**Goal** — restore **mechanical alignment** (guides for tibia, talus ± fibula cuts) · **ligament balancing** · **stable, neutral, plantigrade** ankle + hindfoot · may need ligament reconstruction, tendon transfer, osteotomy, **heel cord lengthening**, arthrodesis

**Soft tissue** — **long incision** (↓ skin tension) · **thick flaps** · minimise retractors · **avoid tibialis anterior sheath** (prevents **bowstringing** + wound problems)

**Technical errors** — prosthesis **too lateral** · **too small → subsides** · uncorrected varus / valgus · replacing an ankle **too anteriorly subluxated**

### Failure – loosening/subsidence work-up & salvage (TTC arthrodesis)

```
Failed TAA
├─ Infection (erythema, drainage, ↑ ESR/CRP, pus) → STAGED: SPACER + IV ABX → FUSION
├─ Severe talar loss / arthritic STJ / subsidence into STJ → TTC FUSION
│      └─ Large defect → FEMORAL HEAD ALLOGRAFT + nail / plate / nail-plate
└─ Good residual talus + healthy STJ → ISOLATED ANKLE FUSION
       ├─ Small defect → shortening / cancellous graft + anterior plate
       └─ > 2 cm defect → structural iliac crest / distal tibial allograft
```

**4 key decisions (Berkowitz)** — **rule out infection** → **ankle only vs ankle + subtalar** → **which bone graft** → **which fixation**

**Include subtalar joint (TTC) if** — **insufficient talar bone stock** · **talar subsidence crosses STJ** · **pre-existing STJ arthrosis**

| Bone graft | Use |
|---|---|
| **Particulate cancellous** | **< 2 cm** talar bone loss |
| **Bulk / structural allograft** | **> 2 cm** bone loss |
| **Femoral head allograft** | **Graft of choice for large talar loss / TTC** |
| **Campbell technique** | **Tricortical iliac crest wedges** |
| Fresh-frozen distal tibial allograft | Structural option |

**Fixation** — **nail · plate · nail + plate**

**9 yr, talar subsidence into STJ, infection negative** — **TTC arthrodesis with structural (femoral head) allograft**\
✗ Wrong options: revise talar component (subsided not loose, no bone stock) · revise tibial (not loose) · revise both (early failure, poor talar purchase) · TTC without / cancellous graft (only < 2 cm)

![Talar component subsidence into the subtalar joint with talar bone loss](../../MCQ/attachments/q5754-c-fig-c.jpg)

**Fusion outcomes** — isolated tibiotalar fusion: **1 nonunion** vs **5** with ankle–hindfoot fusion · **subtalar nonunion = commonest complication** of ankle–hindfoot fusion → separate STJ approach · **arthrodesis after failed TAA worse than primary arthrodesis**

**Revision TAA preferred when** — **sufficient bone stock**, adequate soft tissue, **preserved ROM**, compliance → **one-stage revision** (long-stem)

| Salvage choice | When |
|---|---|
| **One-stage revision** | Preserved ROM + **sufficient bone stock** |
| **Two-stage revision** | **Established infection** |
| **Tibiotalar arthrodesis** | Extensive bone loss but **preserved talar stock + no subtalar arthritis** |
| **Tibiotalar + subtalar (TTC)** | **Insufficient talar stock** or **subtalar arthritis** |
| Below-knee / transtibial amputation | **Non-salvageable** only |

**Painful TAA, labs + aspirate negative, osteolysis** — **CT with metal artifact-reducing protocol**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · repeat aspiration · routine follow-up (subsidence worsens) · two-stage revision

**Persistent worsening pain since TAA, bone loss on CT** — **CBC, ESR, CRP** first\
✗ Wrong options: two-stage · one-stage · tibiotalar arthrodesis · tibiotalar + subtalar arthrodesis

### Periprosthetic infection

**Deep infection** — **0.5–3.5%** · **risk factors: prior ankle surgery, diabetes, wound healing problems > 14 days**, low pre-op scores

**Gold standard** — **two-stage revision of all components: explantation + cement spacer** → reimplantation or arthrodesis

**Reimplantation** — **rarely successful**: **0% early prosthesis salvage**, **16% reimplantation** (Myerson) · persistent infection, bone loss from debridement, recurrence risk

**Myerson series (19 infections)** — 3 revision arthroplasty · 6 arthrodesis · **7 permanent spacer** · 3 transtibial amputation

**Diabetic, 2 yr post-op, 5 wk symptoms, 70K WBC, GPC clusters** — **antibiotic spacer; reimplantation unlikely**\
✗ Wrong options: IV antibiotics + retention (only for suppression) · **I&D + poly exchange — biofilm** · spacer with successful reimplantation · amputation first

**Salto Talaris, 18 mo, ↑ CRP / ESR, 25,000 WBC 85% PMN** — **two-component explantation + antibiotic spacer**\
✗ Wrong options: oral antibiotics 6 wk · SPECT · poly exchange (no modular liner) · below-knee amputation

**Salvage order** — preserve joint → then limb: **permanent spacer, fusion, reimplantation** before **amputation**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Delayed wound healing** | **Most common** (Orthobullets) · **4–17%** |
| Superficial wound infection | — |
| **Deep infection** | **0.5–3.5%** → explant, antibiotics, reimplant or **salvage arthrodesis** |
| **Sensory deficit** | **Up to 21%** · anterior incision near **SPN + DPN** |
| **Intra-op fracture** | **Medial > lateral malleolus** |
| **Component loosening** | **Talar fails more than tibial** |
| **Subsidence** | → convert to fusion |
| **Osteolysis** | **Polyethylene wear** → large expansive cysts in tibia / talus · **CT metal artifact reduction** |
| **Heterotopic ossification** | **3.8–82%** (TO) |

### Intraoperative malleolar fracture – prevention

**Most frequent intra-op complication (TO)** — **fracture of the medial or lateral malleolus**\
✗ Wrong options: fracture of the talus dome

**Commonest cause of revision (TO)** — **aseptic loosening** (most series) · **arthrofibrosis / impingement** (some series) · **debridement of heterotopic bone** = commonest reoperation (some series)

**Major reported complications (TO)** — **wound dehiscence + heterotopic ossification** · TO marks "**wound dehiscence is the most common postoperative complication**" as false and "**HO is rare**" as false · Orthobullets: **delayed wound healing most common** — read the options

**Rate** — **4% periprosthetic fracture** (2% intra-op, 2% post-op) · **66% medial malleolus** · most occur **intra-operatively**

**Site** — **narrow bone bridge** between ankle joint and outer cortex of tibia / fibula

| Cause of malleolar fracture | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Plafond cut overextended medially / laterally** (more medial cut) | ↑ medial malleolus fracture |
| **Tibial cut too proximal** | Narrows bone bridge |
| **Oversized tibial component** | High compressive load on medial malleolus |
| **Ex-fix over-distraction / early removal** | Sudden bending moments on malleoli |
| Uncontrolled saw-blade excursion · tibial component too medial / lateral | Also risk factors (OB) |

**Prevention** — **prophylactic K-wire (or screw) pinning of the medial malleolus before the osteotomy cut** ✓

![Prophylactic pinning of the medial malleolus with the cutting jig in place](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12133-prophylactic-pinning-medial-malleolus.jpg)

![Unrecognised intra-operative medial malleolar fracture at 6 weeks](../../MCQ/attachments/q216271-a-fig-a.jpg)

### Postoperative heterotopic ossification grading (TO)

**Onset (TO)** — **as early as 3 months** · incidence **3.8–82%** · ↓ ROM, poor function

**Effect (TO)** — osteophytes → **malleolar impingement** → **pain on uneven terrain** → may need repeat surgery

**Risk (TO)** — **males 2× females** · large soft-tissue dissection · **bone debris left in field** · post-op haematoma · prolonged surgery · **wrong-sized components** · mispositioned components

**Spurs, posterior joint space ↓ < 50% (TO)** — **Grade 2**\
✗ Wrong options: 1 (islands) · 3 (> 50%) · 4 (bridging)

**Management (TO)** — **early active + active-assisted ROM** once confirmed → **surgical resection of mature HO** if symptomatic · exclude **component malalignment** (else recurs → **TAA revision**) · **indomethacin** prophylaxis works for THR, **doubtful for TAA**

## X vs Y

| | **TAA** | **Ankle arthrodesis** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | **Older, low demand, thin** | **Young, heavy labourer** |
| ROM / gait | **Preserved**, ↑ stride, cadence | ↓ |
| Stairs, uneven ground | **Better** | Worse |
| Pain relief | Equivalent | Equivalent |
| Reoperation | **Higher** | Lower |
| Adjacent joints | Protected | **Premature OA (subtalar → 1st MTP)** |
| Talar AVN | **Contraindicated** | Option |

| | **Acute PJI (< 4 wk)** | **Chronic PJI (> 4 wk)** |
|---|---|---|
| Treatment | **I&D + polyethylene exchange** | **Explant + antibiotic spacer** |
| Why | No mature biofilm | **Biofilm on metal** |

| | **Isolated tibiotalar fusion** | **TTC fusion** |
|---|---|---|
| Talus | **Good residual bone** | **Severe loss / subsidence into STJ** |
| Subtalar | **Healthy** | **Arthritic** |
| Graft | Cancellous (< 2 cm) / iliac crest | **Femoral head allograft** |
| Nonunion | Lower | **Higher (subtalar)** |

## How it's asked

**"9 yr after TAA, talar subsidence into STJ, infection negative — next?"** → **TTC arthrodesis with structural allograft**

**"Painful TAA, normal ESR/CRP, 900 cells, culture negative, osteolysis — next?"** → **CT with metal artifact-reducing protocol**

**"Worsening pain since TAA, bone loss — next step?"** → **CBC, ESR and CRP**

**"Chronic PJI, 70K WBC, GPC — treatment + outcome?"** → **antibiotic spacer; reimplantation unlikely**

**"Infected Salto TAA, 25,000 WBC — next?"** → **two-component explantation and antibiotic spacer**

**"True about TAA complications?"** → **commonest intra-op complication = medial or lateral malleolus fracture** (TO)

**"Reduces risk of intra-op medial malleolar fracture?"** → **medial malleolar pinning prior to osteotomy cut**

**"NOT a contraindication to TAA?"** → **rheumatoid arthritis** (TO)

**"Contraindication to TAA (TO list)?"** → **AVN talus** · **ankle instability** · **heavy industrial labourer** · **young age**

**"Rigid stage IV PTTI with tibiotalar OA?"** → **pantalar arthrodesis**

**"TAA vs arthrodesis function?"** → **improved ability to go up and down stairs**

**"49-yo construction worker, isolated tibiotalar OA?"** → **tibiotalar arthrodesis through a direct anterior or arthroscopic approach**

**"Isolated TAA in uncorrected valgus + deltoid insufficiency?"** → **lateral polyethylene edge-loading and progressive lateral talar tilt**

**"HO with spurs, posterior joint space ↓ < 50%?"** → **Grade 2** (TO)

**"Commonest complication of TAA?"** → **delayed wound healing** (Orthobullets)

**"Which component loosens more?"** → **talar**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Turf Toe

*Foot & Ankle · 48 / 48*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **hyperextension (hyperdorsiflexion) injury to the plantar plate + sesamoid complex** of the **hallux MTP joint**

**Epidemiology** — **contact sports on rigid / artificial turf** · **flexible cleats** · **football** (first described **1976**, collegiate players) · **up to 45% of NFL players** · **running backs + quarterbacks** · games > practice

**Surgery needed** — **< 2%** · **1.7%** of turf toe injuries → **< 5% need surgery** ✓

**Lesser toes** — great toe = commonest, but **plantar plate tears also occur in lesser toes** → **crossover toe**

### Anatomy – hallux MTP stabilisers

| Stabiliser | Components |
|---|---|
| Osseous | MT head – proximal phalanx articulation |
| **Tendons** | **FHB** (contains **tibial + fibular sesamoids**) · **abductor hallucis → medial sesamoid** · **adductor hallucis → lateral sesamoid** |
| **Ligaments** | **Medial + lateral collateral** · intermetatarsal · **intersesamoid** · sesamoid-phalangeal · accessory sesamoid |
| **Plantar plate** | Joint capsule · attaches to **transverse head of adductor hallucis, flexor tendon sheath, deep transverse intermetatarsal ligament** |

**Plantar plate** — **main stabiliser of the MTP joint**

![Hallux MTP ligaments — medial / lateral sesamoid-phalangeal, intersesamoid, accessory sesamoid, collateral ligaments](../../MCQ/attachments/q218687-illa-toe-1.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **forefoot fixed to ground** + **hallux MTP hyperextended** + **axial load on the heel** (ankle / hindfoot plantarflexed) → attenuation or tear of plantar capsuloligamentous complex

**Two patterns** — **axial load on a dorsiflexed great toe (35%)** · **landing on another player's foot → hyperdorsiflexion (33%)** · most after contact with surface or another player

**Tear site** — capsuloligamentous-sesamoid complex tears **off the proximal phalanx (phalangeal end), not the metatarsal**

![Turf toe mechanism — axial load on the heel with the hallux hyperextended](../../MCQ/attachments/q8920-illa-mechanism.jpg)

| Associated injury | Note |
|---|---|
| Varus / valgus hallux MTP injury | **Traumatic hallux valgus** = turf toe variant (**MCL** rupture) |
| **Sesamoid fracture** | ± diastasis |
| **Proximal sesamoid migration** | Grade III |
| Cartilage injury / loose body | Debride |
| **Proximal phalanx stress fracture** | Often overlooked |
| **Hallux rigidus** | **Late sequela** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — acute pain, stiffness, swelling · **inability to push off** · reduced agility · **pain at toe-off** · difficulty with **cutting drills**

**Exam** — **plantar swelling + ecchymosis** · **pain with passive toe extension / dorsiflexion** · **inability to hyperextend without pain** · gait: shortened time after heel rise

**Vertical Lachman** — **↑ dorsal / plantar laxity vs contralateral side** = plantar plate injury

**Lateral stress → excessive lateral deviation + lateral sesamoid subluxation** — **medial collateral ligament** rupture (label B)\
✗ Wrong options: A = IP joint · C = accessory sesamoid ligament · D = intersesamoid ligament · E = lateral sesamoid-phalangeal ligament

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Study | Finding / indication |
|---|---|
| **WB AP, lateral, oblique foot** | Usually **concentric** MTP (no dislocation seen) |
| **Sesamoid axial + forced dorsiflexion views** | **Sesamoid-to-joint distance vs contralateral** · **medial sesamoid displaced proximally** · sesamoid fracture |
| **Bone scan** | Negative X-ray + persistent pain, swelling, weak push-off → **↑ uptake at 1st MTP** · **proximal phalanx stress fracture** |
| **MRI** | **Most information** on plantar structures + cartilage · indicated for **abnormal X-ray**, **clinical partial / complete plantar plate tear**, + bone scan, persistent symptoms · **not routine** · rules out phalanx stress fracture |

**MRI in severe tear** — **plantar plate / intersesamoid ligament rupture** · osteochondral injury + contusion **plantar medial MT head** next to distal medial sesamoid · **dorsal capsule avulsion → slight plantar subluxation of proximal phalanx**

![Sagittal MRI — disruption of the hallux plantar plate](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob7011-27a-moved.jpg)

**Grade III X-ray** — **proximal retraction of sesamoids** vs normal side · MRI: **complete plantar plate disruption distal to the sesamoid**

![Grade III turf toe — left sesamoids retracted proximally vs normal right](../../MCQ/attachments/q5778-a-fig-a.jpg)

### Classification

| Grade | **Orthobullets** | **OB (clinical)** | Treatment (Anderson) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Sprain** of plantar plate | **Attenuation**, localised swelling, **minimal ecchymosis** | **Taping + early rehab**, early RTP |
| **II** | **Partial tear** | Partial tear, **moderate swelling**, **motion restricted by pain** | **Turf-toe plate / carbon-fibre orthosis** limiting extension, taping, RTP **\~2 wk** (often time off) |
| **III** | **Complete tear** | Complete disruption, **marked swelling / ecchymosis**, **hallux flexion weakness**, **frank MTP instability** | Nonop possible (**splint in plantarflexion**) · **direct primary repair** good in elite athletes, RTP **6–12 mo** |

**Evidence** — **fair** for nonop grade I–II · **insufficient** for operative grade III (Kadakia)

## 5 Management

```
Turf toe (most grade I–III)
├─ Acute → rest, ice, NSAIDs, immobilise in PLANTARFLEXION
├─ Grade I–II → TAPING + PT + RIGID / STIFF-SOLE ORTHOSIS
└─ Grade III / failed nonop
       ├─ Sesamoid retraction, capsular avulsion → CAPSULOLIGAMENTOUS REPAIR
       └─ Fragmented sesamoid → repair (screw / suture) or EXCISION
```

**Nonoperative (most injuries, grade I–III)** — rest, ice, **NSAIDs**, **taping**, **stiff-sole / rocker-bottom shoe**, walking boot · severe → **boot or short-leg cast 2–6 wk** · progressive motion once stable

**✗ Nonop traps** — **taping in acute phase** (vascular compromise with swelling) · **splinting in 5–10° dorsiflexion** (immobilise in **plantarflexion**) · **early motion in grade II–III**

### Operative criteria

| Indication for surgery (usually grade III) | Source |
|---|---|
| **Failed conservative treatment** | Orthobullets + OB |
| **Retraction of sesamoids** | Orthobullets + OB |
| **Sesamoid fracture with diastasis** · **diastasis of bipartite sesamoid** | Orthobullets + OB |
| **Traumatic bunion / traumatic hallux valgus** | Orthobullets + OB |
| **Loose fragments / bodies**, chondral injury | Orthobullets + OB |
| **Large capsular avulsion** · **vertical instability** | OB |
| Hallux toe deformity | Orthobullets |

**✗ Surgery traps** — **surgical repair as best initial treatment** of complete tear · **sesamoid excision as best initial treatment** (only failed nonop, depends on fragment size)

### Treatment – nonoperative vs capsuloligamentous repair

**Grade III, sesamoid retraction, failed nonop** — **capsuloligamentous repair**\
✗ Wrong options: **resection** (sesamoid fracture / fragmentation) · **tendon repair / tenotomy** (no tendon disruption or contracture) · **internal fixation** (no fracture)

**Running back, 1 wk, sesamoids aligned, MRI plate intact with signal (low–mid grade)** — **taping the first MTP + physical therapy + rigid orthotic**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan (MRI already done) · **1st MTP arthrodesis** / **lateral closing wedge osteotomy** (hallux varus options) · **sesamoidectomy** (sesamoid fracture)

**Technique** — **medial plantar incision** · **repair or excision of sesamoid** by fragmentation · **headless screw or suture** for sesamoid fracture · synovitis / OCD → **debridement or cheilectomy** · **abductor hallucis transfer** if plantar plate / flexors irreparable

**Post-op** — immediate **NWB** → progressive ROM + PT · **RTP 3–4 months** (Orthobullets) · **6–12 months** for grade III repair (OB, Anderson) — read the options

**Traumatic hallux valgus** — **74% return to pre-injury level at \~3.4 months** · **¼ never return**

### Lesser-toe plantar plate injury (crossover toe)

**Hyperextension injury 1 yr ago → 2nd toe crossover (varus)** — exam: **↑ laxity of MTP with dorsal / plantar directed force (vertical Lachman)**\
✗ Wrong options: pain improves with hyperextension (it worsens) · valgus instability = MCL deficiency

**Pathology** — **transverse plantar plate tears** in all crossover specimens · severe → **mid-substance + collateral ligament tears**, complete disruption (Coughlin anatomical grading)

**Treatment** — **activity + shoe-wear modification** first → **direct plantar plate repair** (plantar or dorsal approach) or **advancement through bone tunnels**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Hallux rigidus** | **Late sequela** → **cheilectomy vs arthrodesis** by severity |
| **Proximal phalanx stress fracture** | **May be overlooked** → bone scan / MRI |
| Career impact | **Devastating** for professional athletes |

## X vs Y

| Lesser MTP pain | Provocation |
|---|---|
| **Plantar plate tear** | **+ vertical Lachman**, pain **worse with hyperextension** |
| **Early Freiberg's** | Pain with **distraction** |
| **Late Freiberg's** | Pain with **axial loading / compaction** |
| **MCL deficiency** | **Valgus instability** |

| | **Turf toe (plantar plate)** | **Sesamoid fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Mechanism | Hyperextension + axial load | Same, ± diastasis |
| Imaging | Sesamoids **retracted proximally**, plate torn | Fracture line / fragments |
| Surgery | **Capsuloligamentous repair** | **Screw / suture repair or sesamoidectomy** |

## How it's asked

**"Hyperdorsiflexion, plantar ecchymosis, pain at toe-off, MRI plate torn — diagnosis?"** → **rupture of the plantar plate**\
✗ Wrong options: FHL tear · FHB tear · medial sesamoid fracture · MT head fracture

**"Lateral deviation on stress + lateral sesamoid subluxation — structure?"** → **medial collateral ligament (B)**

**"Crossover 2nd toe after old hyperextension — exam?"** → **increased laxity of the MTP joint with dorsal / plantar directed force**

**"Grade III with sesamoid retraction, failed nonop — next?"** → **capsuloligamentous repair**

**"Low–mid grade turf toe, MRI plate intact — next?"** → **taping the first MTP, physical therapy, rigid orthotic**

**"Turf toe treatment — true?"** → **less than 5% of injuries require surgery**
